### [1] [Creation of the Universe in Lorentzian Classical Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37748)
*Avipsa Chakrabarti,Avik Chakraborty,Martín Molina,Ayan Mukhopadhyay,Salomón Romero*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proposes 'cosmic creation gates'—Lorentzian gravitational junctions connecting multiple de Sitter regions—to model a finite-volume, geodesically complete beginning of the Universe without requiring smooth manifold structure; it analyzes volume evolution, fluid thermodynamics, linear perturbations (revealing dimensional dependence of mode survival), and initial-data fine-tuning, concluding that a finite past volume is non-generic and requires conditioning by gravitational dynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile the past incompleteness of inflationary spacetimes (per singularity theorems) with a physically well-defined beginning within Lorentzian gravity—bypassing the need for semiclassical or quantum gravity by relaxing smoothness via gravitational junctions.

Method: Constructs exact rotationally invariant junction solutions ('cosmic creation gates') of Einstein gravity with perfect-fluid timelike hypersurfaces joining n ≥ 2 locally de Sitter bulks in D ≥ 3 dimensions; analyzes geodesic completeness, global-time foliations, volume asymptotics, nonlinear backreaction deformations, and linearized perturbations (separating homogeneous/inhomogeneous sectors and counting propagating modes dimensionally).

Result: 1) Geodesically complete creation gates exist for w > −(D−3)/(D−2); 2) Past/future spatial volumes approach finite positive limits on the junction-adapted global-time foliation; 3) Deformed gates allow finite initial but unbounded future volume, with junction fluid always expanding and exhibiting cooling (w > 0) or heating (allowed w < 0); 4) Perturbation analysis shows: in D = 3, one hybrid sound + (n−1) relative transverse modes; in D ≥ 4, only the homogeneous hybrid sound mode survives; 5) Finite-volume beginning requires fine-tuned initial data; generic causal evolution yields unbounded future volume; 6) A broader oscillatory-bounded-past criterion is proposed, with gravitational equations selecting conditioned admissible histories.

Conclusion: A finite-volume cosmic beginning can be realized classically in Lorentzian gravity via timelike gravitational junctions—'cosmic creation gates'—without manifold smoothness; however, such beginnings are nongeneric and require fine-tuned initial conditions, while the dynamical evolution generically leads to infinite future volume, constraining viable cosmological models and highlighting the role of gravitational conditioning in history selection.

Abstract: The singularity theorems and the past incompleteness of inflationary spacetimes suggest that the beginning of the Universe lies beyond the domain of semiclassical gravity. We show that the creation of the Universe can nevertheless be understood within Lorentzian gravity by allowing gravitational junctions, so that the full spacetime need not be a smooth manifold. We construct rotationally invariant cosmic creation gates joining any finite number $n\geq2$ of locally de Sitter regions in $D\geq3$ spacetime dimensions at a timelike perfect-fluid junction with $P=w\rho$. For $w>-(D-3)/(D-2)$, they are geodesically complete in both temporal directions. The total spatial volume approaches finite, positive limits in the infinite past and future on the global-time foliation adapted to the junction fluid. We also construct exact deformed cosmic creation gates through nonlinear homogeneous backreaction that retain the initial volume while the future volume grows without bound. In these solutions, the junction fluid always expands, cooling for $w>0$ and heating for allowed $w<0$. Keeping the bulk regions locally de Sitter, we determine the linearized perturbations: in three dimensions, the generic inhomogeneous sector contains one hybrid sound mode and $n-1$ relative transverse modes; in four and higher dimensions, only the homogeneous hybrid sound mode survives. A finite-volume beginning requires fine-tuned homogeneous and inhomogeneous initial data, while causal response generically leads to unbounded future spatial volume. We also discuss a broader creation criterion allowing bounded past-volume oscillations. The gravitational equations determine conditioned admissible histories. We discuss implications for cosmology.

</details>


### [2] [Black Hole Entropy from Entangled Quantum Reference Frames](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37750)
*Ludovic Varrin*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes Schwarzschild black hole edge modes in the Donnelly–Freidel extended phase space, identifies them as dynamical SL(2,R) reference frames, relates two canonical gauge choices (Kuchař vs. Carlip–Teitelboim), quantizes the latter, and derives Bekenstein–Hawking entropy plus a universal log correction as entanglement entropy between exterior regions—without UV cutoff dependence.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the origin of black hole entropy as entanglement entropy by identifying and quantizing physical edge modes—specifically, dynamical reference frames at the bifurcation surface—within a covariant, gauge-invariant extended phase space framework.

Method: Uses the Donnelly–Freidel extended phase space formalism to model Schwarzschild edge modes as SL(2,R) frame degrees of freedom; identifies two equivalent gauge fixings of the same constrained system (mass/time-difference vs. area/rapidity); imposes causality via the semigroup SL⁺(2,R); applies harmonic analysis on SL⁺(2,R) using its Plancherel measure for quantization and entanglement entropy computation.

Result: The entanglement entropy between the two exterior regions equals the Bekenstein–Hawking area law S = A/(4ℓₚ²) plus a universal (−3/2) log(A/ℓₚ²) correction, both independent of UV cutoff; the relative rapidity emerges as the time shift in the crossed product construction.

Conclusion: Black hole entropy arises intrinsically from entanglement between exteriors due to causal, quantized SL⁺(2,R) edge modes—validating the gravitational entangling product framework and yielding a finite, cutoff-independent entropy with a robust logarithmic correction.

Abstract: In the extended phase space of Donnelly and Freidel, the edge modes of a Schwarzschild black hole are dynamical reference frames whose phase space is the cotangent bundle of $\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{R})$. Gluing the two exterior regions at the bifurcation point leaves a single canonical pair. Within the extended phase space, this pair can be described either by Kuchař's mass and difference of asymptotic times or by the area and the relative rapidity of the two frames, which are two gauge choices of the same system. The latter is the pair of Carlip and Teitelboim, and the relative rapidity is the time shift of the crossed product construction. We quantize the frames in this gauge and construct the gravitational entangling product of the two exteriors. Requiring the frames to be causal restricts them to the semigroup $\mathrm{SL}^+(2,\mathbb{R})$, whose Plancherel measure yields the Bekenstein--Hawking entropy as the entanglement entropy between the two exteriors, together with a logarithmic correction, both independent of the ultraviolet cutoff.

</details>


### [3] [Circuit complexity of coupled four-mode squeezed states in two-field inflation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38254)
*Tao Li,Hai-Bing Fu,Lei-Hua Liu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes Nielsen circuit complexity for coupled curvature and entropy perturbations in two-field inflation using a four-mode squeezed state formalism; complexity grows post-horizon crossing, and interfield coupling has only a small effect on the equal-weight Gaussian complexity measure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify quantum computational complexity of cosmological perturbations in multi-field inflation, specifically assessing how interfield coupling affects circuit complexity beyond the single-field case.

Method: Derives full quadratic Hamiltonian from the action for two-field inflation; solves three coupled differential equations for pair amplitudes of a four-mode squeezed state; computes two principal squeezing strengths; evaluates Nielsen complexity C_{2,k} = sqrt{2(r_{1k}^2 + r_{2k}^2)} for an equal-weight Gaussian circuit.

Result: Circuit complexity increases after horizon crossing in both coupled and uncoupled cases; interfield coupling modifies pair amplitudes and the four-mode state structure but induces only minor changes to C_{2,k} for the studied parameter space.

Conclusion: The equal-weight Gaussian circuit complexity is largely insensitive to interfield coupling details in two-field inflation, suggesting degeneracy among distinct four-mode squeezed-state structures; this serves as a baseline for extending Gaussian complexity analysis to more general multi-field models.

Abstract: We study the circuit complexity of coupled curvature and entropy perturbations in two-field inflation. We start from the quadratic action and derive the full Hamiltonian. The Hamiltonian includes all diagonal and off-diagonal quadratic terms. We then obtain three coupled equations for the pair amplitudes of the four-mode squeezed state. From these amplitudes, we calculate the two principal squeezing strengths. We use them to determine the Nielsen circuit complexity. For an equal-weight Gaussian circuit, the complexity takes the form $\mathcal{C}_{2,k}=\sqrt{2(r_{1k}^{2}+r_{2k}^{2})}$. It depends on the squeezing strengths but not explicitly on the squeezing angles. Our numerical results show that the complexity grows after horizon crossing in both coupled and uncoupled models. Interfield coupling changes the pair amplitudes and the four-mode squeezed state. However, its effect on the circuit complexity is small for the parameters considered. This result shows that different four-mode pair-amplitude matrices can yield similar values of the chosen equal-weight circuit complexity. The present calculation provides a reference for studying Gaussian circuit complexity in more general two-field inflationary models.

</details>


### [4] [The Non-Relativistic Limit of Five-Dimensional Boson Stars and Scale Invariance Violation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38606)
*Eduard Kim,Oleg Kondrashov,Emin Nugaev,Yakov Shnir*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies the non-relativistic limit of spherically symmetric boson stars in (4+1)D GR, revealing exact scale invariance in the Newtonian limit, which yields a critical configuration with fixed U(1) charge and zero binding energy; relativistic and gravitational corrections break this symmetry, lifting solution degeneracy and governing stability and energetics away from criticality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how scale invariance emerges in the non-relativistic limit of higher-dimensional boson stars and how its breaking by relativistic and gravitational effects determines physical properties like stability and the energy-charge relation.

Method: Analytical and comparative analysis of the (4+1)-dimensional Einstein-Klein-Gordon system in the non-relativistic limit; identification of scale-invariant Newtonian limit; comparison with the Wick-Cutkosky model to isolate and quantify relativistic and gravitational corrections.

Result: Exact scale invariance in the Newtonian limit yields a unique critical boson star with fixed global U(1) charge and vanishing binding energy; relativistic and gravitational corrections break scale invariance, lift degeneracy of solutions, and govern kinematic stability and deviations in the energy-charge curve.

Conclusion: Weak violation of scale invariance—induced by relativistic and gravitational effects—is essential for selecting physically stable boson star configurations and determining their observable properties beyond the critical limit.

Abstract: We investigate the non-relativistic limit of spherically symmetric boson stars in (4+1)-dimensional general relativity (GR). In this limit, the resulting Newtonian theory exhibits an exact scale invariance, leading to a critical boson star configuration with a fixed global U(1) charge and vanishing binding energy. We identify the origin of these properties in the restored scale symmetry and analyze how relativistic and gravitational corrections break this symmetry. By comparing the full (4+1)-dimensional Einstein-Klein-Gordon system with the Wick-Cutkosky model, we quantify the effects of these corrections on the energy-charge relation and the kinematic stability of the configurations. Our results demonstrate how weak violation of scale invariance lifts the degeneracy of the non-relativistic solutions and determines the behavior of boson stars away from the critical limit.

</details>


### [5] [Unifying energy-entropy bounds in semiclassical gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38713)
*Jackson R. Fliss,Andrew Rolph*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: The paper introduces Gravitational Energy-Entropy Conditions (GEECs), a new class of bounds that unify and strengthen the Quantum Focusing Conjecture (QFC) and Smeared Null Energy Condition (SNEC), while reducing to the classical Null Energy Condition (NEC).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the lack of mutual implication between QFC and SNEC, and their failure to reduce to the classical NEC, the authors aim to formulate a unified, stronger, and classically consistent bound on energy and entropy in semiclassical gravity.

Method: The authors define GEECs as stress-tensor-based bounds satisfying three criteria: implication of both QFC and SNEC, reduction to NEC in the classical limit, and compatibility with effective field theory. They construct an explicit family of GEECs and constrain its free parameters using test cases.

Result: A concrete, parametrized family of GEECs is constructed; it successfully implies QFC and SNEC, reduces to NEC classically, and passes key consistency checks in effective field theory.

Conclusion: GEECs constitute a robust, unifying framework for energy–entropy bounds in semiclassical gravity—stronger than prior conditions and physically well-motivated by EFT principles.

Abstract: We revisit bounds on energy and entropy in semiclassical gravity, aiming to strengthen and unify established bounds, in particular the quantum focussing conjecture (QFC) and the smeared null energy condition (SNEC). Both the QFC and SNEC have important applications and are the strongest in their respective hierarchies; however, neither implies the other, nor reduces to the null energy condition (NEC) in the classical limit. To that end, we define a new class of bounds dubbed gravitational energy-entropy conditions (GEECs) that imply both the QFC and SNEC, and reduce to the NEC classically. We construct an explicit family of GEECs by imposing the defining criteria on a broad set of stress tensor bounds, and constrain its remaining parameter space through test cases, in an effective field theory-inspired approach. Our GEECs are powerful candidate bounds on energy and entropy in semiclassical gravity.

</details>


### [6] [Holography and localization in fractional field theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38806)
*Gianluca Calcagni,Iván Salvador-García*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proposes two local dual formulations of fractional field theory—via a generalized Caffarelli–Silvestre extension (holographic bulk dual with two scalars) and via spectral localization (continuum-mass representation)—to handle nonlocality; the latter enables consistent fakeon-based quantization and classicization, facilitating comparison with quadratic quantum gravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome computational and conceptual difficulties arising from nonlocal operators in fractional field theory—particularly for quantizing gravity—by recasting it into equivalent local frameworks.

Method: 1) Generalization of the Caffarelli–Silvestre extension theorem to self-adjoint fractional field theories with multi-scale kinetic terms and nonlocal interactions (scalar-field example), yielding a holographic bulk dual with two local scalars; 2) Spectral localization: representing the nonlocal theory as a continuum of massive local fields, enabling fakeon prescription and classicization analogous to higher-derivative local theories.

Result: Two rigorous local dual representations of fractional field theory are constructed; spectral localization permits consistent unitary quantization via fakeons and bridges fractional quantum gravity with established techniques in quadratic quantum gravity.

Conclusion: Fractional field theory can be made tractable for quantum gravity by either dimensional extension (holographic duality) or mass-spectral reformulation (spectral localization); the latter is more flexible for incorporating interactions and aligning with causal, unitary quantization schemes like the fakeon program.

Abstract: Fractional field theory is a promising framework wherein to quantize the gravitational interaction but the presence of nonlocal operators can make its handling difficult. With the purpose of easing this problem, we recast the theory as a local dual in two ways. First, we generalize the Caffarelli--Silvestre extension theorem to self-adjoint fractional field theories with multi-scale kinetic terms and nonlocal interactions, taking the example of a scalar field. The latter is reinterpreted as a nonlocal boundary degree of freedom holographically dual to a local theory of two scalars living in a higher-dimensional bulk. However, inclusion of a local non-linear potential is not straightforward. We therefore propose an alternative picture where the nonlocal theory is not extended to a higher-dimensional space but is localized at the price of introducing a continuum of masses. This \emph{spectral localization} allows for a natural implementation of the fakeon prescription and the related classicization procedure, already employed in local theories with higher-derivative and complex ghosts. Applications to fractional quantum gravity and its comparison with quadratic quantum gravity are discussed.

</details>


### [7] [Gravitational Reconstruction in $AdS_{4}$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38887)
*David A. Lowe,Yiru Wang,Juanyi Yang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends gravitational perturbation reconstruction in AdS₄ from boundary stress tensors, using light-cone cuts and generalized functions to handle general spacelike momentum; it preserves FG gauge and normalizability, and connects to flat-space limits relevant for BMS and celestial holography.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the holographic reconstruction of linearized gravitational perturbations in AdS₄ from boundary data—specifically conserved, traceless stress tensors—to general boundary momenta (including zero-frequency limit), while maintaining gauge consistency (Fefferman–Graham) and normalizability, and to explore implications for flat-space holography (BMS, celestial).

Method: Uses conformally flat boundary metric; constructs reconstruction via boundary light-cone cuts for stress data with no spacelike Fourier support; employs explicit position-space generalized functions for general spacelike momentum; introduces boundary improvement terms to restore FG gauge without violating normalizability or altering the linearized Weyl tensor; derives convergence conditions via boundary kernel analysis.

Result: Complete reconstruction of normalizable linearized bulk perturbations from real, Lorentzian boundary stress data; explicit boundary kernels for general momentum (including ω → 0); gauge-restored (FG) solutions preserving physical observables; rigorous convergence criteria; derivation of controlled flat-space limits linking to BMS symmetry and celestial holography.

Conclusion: The reconstruction is robust across all admissible boundary stress data, respects holographic consistency (gauge, normalizability, observables), and provides a concrete framework bridging AdS₄ linear gravity with asymptotically flat holography—particularly for understanding soft theorems, memory effects, and celestial amplitudes.

Abstract: We reconstruct linearized gravitational perturbations in $AdS_{4}$ from a conserved, traceless stress tensor on a fixed conformally flat boundary metric, extending our earlier real Lorentzian construction. For admissible data with no spacelike Fourier support in a flat conformal frame, the physical response reduces to an integral over boundary light-cone cuts. Boundary improvements restore Fefferman--Graham (FG) gauge while preserving normalizability and the linearized Weyl tensor. We then reconstruct perturbations with general spacelike boundary momentum, including nonzero frequency and its zero-frequency limit, using explicit position-space generalized functions on real stress data. Boundary kernels are constructed for normalizable perturbations and sufficient conditions are derived for the reconstruction to converge. The results motivate controlled flat-space limits relevant to Bondi--Metzner--Sachs symmetries and celestial holography.

</details>


### [8] [Are Black Hole and Cosmological Horizon Thermodynamics Necessarily Inequivalent?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39048)
*Zachary J. Hoelscher*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates the thermodynamic equivalence between cosmological and black hole horizons in a toy universe with a non-rotating black hole and positive cosmological constant, focusing on the Nariai limit and parity-invariant QFT; it finds indistinguishable near-horizon physics semi-classically and proposes a shared microscopic origin for their entropies in this regime.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test a recent conjecture that cosmological and black hole horizon thermodynamics are generically inequivalent in UV-complete quantum gravity—motivated by structural disparities between string-theoretic black holes and cosmological horizons—and to seek a potential counterexample.

Method: Analyzes a toy model: a de Sitter–Schwarzschild spacetime in the Nariai limit (maximal black hole mass), assuming strict parity invariance of the quantum field theory; employs semi-classical gravity, neglecting quantum instability of the exact Nariai equilibrium, and compares local physics near the black hole and cosmological horizons.

Result: In the semi-classical Nariai limit with parity-invariant QFT, an observer cannot operationally distinguish physics near the black hole horizon from that near the cosmological horizon—indicating a local thermodynamic similarity, though not generic equivalence.

Conclusion: While not establishing universal equivalence, the result implies cosmological and black hole horizon thermodynamics cannot be entirely unrelated; thus, any consistent quantum gravity theory describing black holes in de Sitter space should provide a unified microscopic explanation for both entropies in this limiting regime.

Abstract: It has recently been argued that cosmological horizon thermodynamics and black hole thermodynamics are generically inequivalent in any consistent, UV complete theory of quantum gravity, where it was suggested that this could be falsified by the identification of a counterexample. This conjecture was motivated in part by noting that cosmological horizons do not incorporate many of the convenient features found in the black holes described by string theory. In this work, we consider as a toy model a universe with a single non-rotating black hole and a positive cosmological constant. We work in the Nariai limit where the mass of the black hole is taken to its maximum possible value, and where we also assert that the quantum field theory living on this background is strictly parity invariant. In the semi-classical regime where we neglect instability of the exact Nariai equilibrium under quantum fluctuations, we find that the observer cannot readily distinguish the physics in the neighborhood of the black hole horizon from the physics in the neighborhood of the cosmological horizon. This does not prove that the two are generically equivalent in any universe, though it does suggest that they cannot be entirely unrelated in general. We propose that any consistent theory of quantum gravity that aims to describe black holes in a universe with a positive cosmological constant should provide a related microscopic origin for the leading semiclassical cosmological entropy and black hole entropy in the neighborhood of the particular limit considered here.

</details>


### [9] [Absorption of bound supernova axion-like particles by the neutron star weakens the heating limits on their photon coupling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39402)
*Probit J Kalita,Subha Mitra,Bharat Kumar*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper revisits constraints on axion-like particles (ALPs) coupling to protons using neutron star reheating, showing that ALP absorption via inverse nucleon bremsstrahlung in cold neutron stars drastically suppresses heating from bound ALPs—except for those on nearly circular orbits outside the star—leading to revised, weaker but still significant exclusion limits on ALP mass (7.7–260 MeV) and photon coupling, sensitive to nuclear physics inputs (EoS, Δ(1232) resonance, bremsstrahlung rate) and requiring fine-tuned photon couplings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To correct the earlier analysis by Bose et al. [arXiv:2610.30255] by including ALP absorption—previously neglected—which dominates over decay for bound ALPs whose orbits intersect the neutron star, thereby reassessing viability of ALP-induced reheating and resulting astrophysical constraints.

Method: Self-consistent computation of protoneutron-star evolution, ALP production in core-collapse supernovae, general-relativistic bound-state orbits, and ALP absorption via inverse nucleon bremsstrahlung; systematic variation across 12 PNS evolutions, 3 bremsstrahlung rates (calibrated to SN energy loss), and EoSs with/without Δ(1232) isobars; inclusion of pion conversion and Δ-resonance effects on production vs. absorption.

Result: Absorption eliminates >99.99% of bound ALPs entering the star within minutes; surviving ALPs contribute only 10⁻⁸–10⁻⁴ of original heating estimate; PSR J2144−3933 excludes ALPs in mₐ ≈ 7.7–260 MeV, with lower gₐᵧ bound elevated by factor 280–700 due to absorption; Δ(1232) and pion conversion widen the excluded window; constraint sensitivity comparable for bremsstrahlung rate and final NS radius; viable parameter space requires cancellation between direct and pion-mixing-induced gₐᵧ.

Conclusion: Neglecting ALP absorption invalidates prior reheating-based limits; inclusion yields robust, physics-rich constraints highly dependent on nuclear many-body effects and stellar structure, narrowing but not eliminating a viable ALP window—whose detectability hinges on precise gₐᵧ cancellation and may already be constrained by supernova gamma-ray observations.

Abstract: Axion-like particles (ALPs) that couple to protons are produced in a core-collapse supernova, and the slow ones remain bound to the new neutron star. Bose, Ganguly, Kumar, and Raj [arXiv:2610.30255] proposed that the photons from the decay of this bound cloud reheat old neutron stars, and they derived strong limits on the ALP-photon coupling. They neglected the absorption of the ALPs by the star, but a bound ALP on an orbit that enters the star and reaches 50 km crosses it about $6\times 10^{18}$ times over the 333 Myr age of PSR J2144$-$3933. We show that inverse nucleon bremsstrahlung in the cold star absorbs every ALP whose orbit enters it within minutes, long before it decays, so only ALPs born outside the final star on nearly circular orbits survive; they carry $9\times 10^{-9} - 6\times 10^{-5}$ of the heating. We compute the protoneutron-star evolution, the production, the general-relativistic orbits and the absorption ourselves, for twelve protoneutron-star evolutions, three nucleon-bremsstrahlung rates normalized to the same supernova energy loss of light ALPs, and equations of state with and without $\Delta(1232)$ isobars. PSR J2144$-$3933 still excludes a window at $m_a\simeq 7.7-196$\ MeV, with a lower edge in the photon coupling $280-700$ times higher than without absorption. Pion conversion and the $\Delta(1232)$ resonance, which raise the production of surviving ALPs more than their absorption, widen this window to $m_a\simeq$ $7.7-260$\ MeV. The window depends at similar levels on the bremsstrahlung rate and on the radius of the final star. It requires a direct photon coupling that cancels the one induced by ALP-pion mixing, and existing supernova gamma-ray limits may already cover it.

</details>


### [10] [The Effective Kerr-Compton Tree Amplitude from Black Hole Perturbation Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40101)
*N. Emil J. Bjerrum-Bohr,Gang Chen,Konstantinos Papadimos,Nabha Shah,Chenliang Su*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: A new method constructs the tree-level Compton amplitude for gravitational-wave scattering off a Kerr black hole in post-Minkowskian EFT, using BH perturbation theory wave functions to fix contact terms; yields explicit amplitude up to sixth-order-in-spin, reveals new sectors and a leading dissipative contact term at sixth order, and reproduces known one-loop tail result.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome reliance on physical consistency constraints in prior amplitude constructions by directly anchoring contact terms to wave scattering functions from black hole perturbation theory, enabling systematic, unambiguous determination of higher-spin contributions in gravitational-wave scattering.

Method: Combines post-Minkowskian effective field theory with wave scattering functions from black hole perturbation theory to iteratively fix gravitational Compton amplitudes; employs on-shell constraints and matching to perturbative wave solutions to uniquely determine contact-term coefficients up to sixth order in spin.

Result: Explicit tree-level Compton amplitude for GW scattering off Kerr black hole up to O(a⁶) in spin; discovery of previously unknown amplitude sectors at fifth and sixth orders; identification of a leading dissipative contact term at O(a⁶); successful reproduction of the one-loop tail effect from BH perturbation theory.

Conclusion: The method establishes a robust bridge between EFT and black hole perturbation theory, enabling predictive, first-principles computation of high-spin gravitational amplitudes with manifest physical consistency and novel dissipative structures.

Abstract: We develop a new method to construct the tree-level Compton amplitude for gravitational-wave scattering off a Kerr black hole in post-Minkowskian effective field theory. Building on previous computations that required physical consistency of the amplitude, the method uses wave scattering functions from black hole perturbation theory to uniquely determine additional contact terms. We illustrate the iterative procedure by deriving explicit expressions up to sixth order in spin, including previously unknown sectors at the fifth and sixth orders in spin. The amplitude features complex transcendental structures and a leading dissipative contact term at sixth order in spin. We verify our result against known results in the literature and reproduce the one-loop tail result from black hole perturbation theory.

</details>


### [11] [Exact holographic superfluid at the Bogomolny point: transport, large charge, and Rényi entropies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40371)
*Siqing Yu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analytically solves the Abelian Higgs model in AdS₅ at the Breitenlohner-Freedman bound and Bogomolny coupling, yielding closed-form superfluid solutions across static, uncharged AdS₅ slicings (planar, global, hyperbolic); proves linear stability of nodeless probe condensates; and connects each solution to distinct CFT physics—finite-T superfluidity, lowest-energy charged state on ℝ×S³, and Rényi entropy black hole phases—with exact results for thermodynamics, hydrodynamics, scaling dimensions, and IR spectral features.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain exact, closed-form holographic superfluid solutions beyond numerical methods, and to unify the interpretation of the Abelian Higgs model across different AdS₅ slicings in terms of dual CFT phenomena—including finite-temperature superfluidity, extremal charged states, and charged Rényi entropies—while establishing rigorous stability.

Method: Analytical solution of the Abelian Higgs equations in the probe limit on static, uncharged AdS₅ backgrounds with maximally symmetric spatial sections, under the conditions m² = m²_BF and λ = λ_Bogomolny; linear stability analysis via perturbation theory around nodeless condensates; mapping to dual CFT observables using holographic renormalization and AdS/CFT dictionary.

Result: Exact closed-form condensate solutions for planar black brane (full T < T_c superfluid phase), global AdS₅ (lowest-energy charged state on ℝ×S³ with exact scaling dimension Δ(Q)), and hyperbolic slicing (homogeneous hairy black holes for charged Rényi entropies, with nonvanishing condensate at entangling surface); exact Gorter-Casimir behavior, sound speed/attenuation, and identification of extra IR towers beyond superfluid EFT.

Conclusion: The special parameter choice (m² = m²_BF, λ = λ_Bogomolny) renders the probe-limit Abelian Higgs model exactly solvable across all maximally symmetric AdS₅ slicings, yielding physically distinct yet analytically controlled dual CFT states; linear stability holds universally for nodeless condensates in standard quantization with λ ≥ 0 and no superflow; the solutions expose rich IR structure not captured by effective superfluid descriptions.

Abstract: The Abelian Higgs model in AdS$_5$, in the probe limit, is a standard holographic model of a strongly coupled superfluid, usually solved numerically. When the scalar mass saturates the Breitenlohner-Freedman bound and the quartic coupling takes the Bogomolny value, its condensed phase can be found in closed form on every static, uncharged AdS$_5$ background with maximally symmetric spatial sections. We prove that it is linearly stable, as is any nodeless probe-limit condensate in the standard quantization with a nonnegative quartic coupling and no superflow. On each slicing it answers a different question about the dual conformal field theory. On the planar black brane it is the superfluid at all temperatures below $T_c$: the superfluid fraction follows the Gorter-Casimir two-fluid law exactly, and the speed and the attenuation of sound are found in closed form. In global AdS$_5$ it describes a state of charge $Q$ on $\mathbb{R}\times S^3$, arguably the lowest-energy one, and gives its scaling dimension in closed form at all $Q$, classically in the bulk. Its excitations include towers that the superfluid effective theory does not describe; they come from an infrared fixed point of the probe sector. The hyperbolic slicing gives in closed form a homogeneous hairy phase of the black holes that compute the charged Rényi entropies of a ball. Its condensate does not vanish at the entangling surface. When sourced by operators inserted there, the phase sets in at Rényi index exactly one at zero chemical potential, as found for a neutral scalar.

</details>


### [12] [Holographic duals of JT gravity at finite cutoff: the classical case](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40391)
*Andreas Belaey,Thomas G. Mertens,Lorenzo Russo,Alex Tarana*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper formulates JT gravity with finite-cutoff boundaries and end-of-the-world branes using the first-order BF formalism, dynamically reduces it to a 1D boundary theory, geometrically identifies boundary phase space degrees of freedom, and derives a closed-form holographic Schwarzian description encoding the embedding of the finite-cutoff curve in AdS₂.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe bulk locality and the fundamental structure of spacetime in JT gravity by incorporating realistic finite-cutoff boundaries and end-of-the-world branes, moving beyond infinite-cutoff or purely boundary-only treatments.

Method: First-order BF formulation of JT gravity; dynamical dimensional reduction to a one-dimensional boundary system; symplectic analysis to identify geometric phase space variables; derivation of the finite-cutoff holographic Schwarzian action in phase space.

Result: A geometric characterization of boundary phase space degrees of freedom; a closed-form phase-space Schwarzian action describing the embedding of finite-cutoff curves in AdS₂; a consistent framework unifying bulk geometry, boundary dynamics, and holography at finite cutoff.

Conclusion: Finite-cutoff JT gravity admits a precise geometric and phase-space holographic description via the Schwarzian, revealing how boundary embeddings encode bulk locality—paving the way for canonical quantization and deeper insights into emergent spacetime.

Abstract: We formulate JT gravity with finite-cutoff boundaries and end-of-the-world branes in the first-order BF formulation. We dynamically reduce the BF model into a one-dimensional system living at the boundaries, generalizing earlier constructions. We obtain in particular a geometrical understanding of the phase space degrees of freedom associated to each of these boundaries. We leverage these results to probe bulk locality and the underlying structure of spacetime. Within our framework, we are able to derive a closed-form expression for the holographic Schwarzian description of JT gravity at finite-cutoff in phase space. The resulting degrees of freedom describe the embedding of the finite-cutoff curve in AdS$_2$. A quantization of these systems is presented in a companion paper.

</details>


### [13] [Gravity between two masses beyond Newton's law](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40462)
*Zhengwen Liu,Xing-Bo Yuan*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the static two-body gravitational interaction in general relativity up to 12th order in Newton's constant G for arbitrary mass ratios, and derives an all-orders closed-form expression for the first backreaction correction beyond the test-body (Schwarzschild) limit—using a stationary interaction-energy formulation, exact one-body resummation, stationarity constraints, and integrated response identities to expose hidden analytical structure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the long-standing analytical challenge of extracting the static two-body interaction from Einstein's non-linear field equations—specifically, to go beyond the test-mass approximation and capture full mass-ratio dependence and gravitational self-interaction (backreaction) in the static sector, which serves as the purest probe of gravitational non-linearity.

Method: Formulates gravity as a stationary interaction energy problem; employs exact one-body background (e.g., Schwarzschild metric) to resum one body’s self-interaction; exploits stationarity to reduce required field components; uses integrated response identities to uncover cancellations among coupled gravitational modes; combines complementary perturbative expansions (e.g., post-Minkowskian and mass-ratio series) to reconstruct full mass dependence at finite G-order.

Result: Static two-body interaction energy computed explicitly from G⁹ through G¹² for arbitrary mass ratio; closed-form all-orders expression for the leading backreaction correction beyond the test-body limit (i.e., first deviation from geodesic motion in a fixed Schwarzschild background); systematic identification of cancellation mechanisms enabling high-order analytic control.

Conclusion: The static gravitational two-body problem—despite its non-linearity—admits unexpected analytical simplicity when recast via stationary interaction energy and response identities; these results unify infinitesimal-probe and comparable-mass regimes, and provide foundational inputs for high-precision gravitational-wave modeling and effective-one-body theory.

Abstract: Newton's law of universal gravitation describes the attraction between two masses through an inverse-distance potential. In Einstein's theory gravity gravitates: each mass reshapes the spacetime geometry through which the other acts. More than a century later, extracting the two-body interaction from this coupled geometry remains a fundamental analytical challenge. Even without motion or radiation, the static sector of the interaction harbours an infinite hierarchy of field couplings: the purest laboratory for gravitational non-linearity. Here we determine the static interaction from ninth to twelfth order in Newton's constant for arbitrary mass ratios, and derive a closed expression for the first backreaction beyond the Schwarzschild test-body limit to all orders in the gravitational coupling. We achieve both advances by formulating the problem around the stationary interaction energy. An exact one-body background resums one source's self-interaction, stationarity reduces the fields required from the other, and integrated response identities reveal cancellations across the coupled modes. Complementary expansions then resolve the complete mass dependence at finite order. Together, these results bridge the gap between an infinitesimal probe and comparable masses, exposing hidden analytical simplicity within Einstein's theory and providing essential building blocks for modelling orbital dynamics and gravitational-wave generation.

</details>


### [14] [On Geometric Observables in Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40615)
*Manthos Karydas,Murat Kologlu,Allic Sivaramakrishnan*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs coordinate-invariant, gravitationally dressed distance and time observables in perturbative quantum gravity, computes their leading graviton corrections in correlators, and shows their asymptotic limits yield memory and Shapiro time delay operators—bridging local geometric measurements with celestial holography.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically define and compute quantum corrections to classical geometric observables (e.g., distance, time) in perturbative quantum gravity, addressing the lack of exploration of graviton-induced modifications to such fundamental quantities.

Method: Constructs finite-distance/time observables using gravitational dressing to ensure diffeomorphism invariance; computes leading-order graviton contributions to their correlation functions in perturbative QG; takes asymptotic (null-infinity) limits to recover memory and Shapiro delay operators.

Result: Explicit construction of dressed geometric observables; derivation of their graviton-corrected two- and higher-point correlators; demonstration that asymptotic limits reproduce known infrared effects (gravitational memory and Shapiro time delay) as operator identities.

Conclusion: Geometric observables can be consistently defined and quantized in perturbative quantum gravity via gravitational dressing, and their asymptotic behavior provides a concrete operational link between bulk spacetime measurements and boundary/celestial data.

Abstract: Measures of geometry like distance and time are staples of classical gravity. While geometric quantities arise broadly in quantum gravity, their graviton corrections are less explored. In this paper, we study geometric observables in perturbative quantum gravity. We form distance and time observables that model measurements, including in the presence of spacetime fluctuations. Gravitational dressing ensures these constructions are coordinate invariant. Then we show how these finite-distance observables become memory and Shapiro time delay operators upon taking asymptotic limits. We compute leading graviton effects in correlators of geometric observables throughout. Altogether, our results identify a class of geometric observables that link experiments and operations on the celestial sphere.

</details>


### [15] [The Effective Field Theory Origin of the Fuzzy Sphere Method](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38702)
*Mykola Dedushenko*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces a 'mother theory' framework on $S^2 \times \mathbb{R}$ to systematically derive fuzzy sphere Hamiltonians for (2+1)D CFTs; it constructs Ising and easy-plane ferromagnet descendants, obtains exact $z=2$ criticality and one-magnon solutions, and outlines broader applications.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a systematic, local, and UV-complete framework for constructing fuzzy sphere Hamiltonians—bypassing ad hoc lowest-Landau-level constructions—and to unify relativistic and non-relativistic critical phenomena (e.g., $z=1$ and $z=2$ scaling) within a single geometric and field-theoretic setting.

Method: Constructs local 3D Euclidean field theories ('mother theories') on $S^2 \times \mathbb{R}$ with charged fermions coupled to scalar mediators ($\phi$ or $\Phi$); integrates out massive modes (higher Landau levels, fermions, or mediator) to obtain either fuzzy-sphere lattice Hamiltonians (LL-projected) or effective EFTs; analyzes symmetry breaking, critical flows, and exact solvability via spectral analysis.

Result: 1) Ising mother theory yields fuzzy Ising, Gaussian, and Lee-Yang descendants; 2) Complex-mediator mother theory yields an easy-plane fuzzy ferromagnet with spontaneous $U(1)$ breaking, pseudo-Goldstone mode, and analytically accessible $z=2$ quantum critical point; 3) Exact one-magnon eigenstates are found at criticality; 4) Framework generalizes to broader class of fuzzy lattice models.

Conclusion: The mother-theory approach unifies the construction of fuzzy sphere Hamiltonians with conventional EFT reasoning, enables analytical control over non-relativistic criticality ($z=2$), reveals exact solvability structures, and opens a systematic pathway to engineer and classify quantum critical points on noncommutative geometries.

Abstract: Fuzzy sphere Hamiltonians for (2+1)D conformal field theories are usually written directly on the lowest Landau level. We introduce a framework organizing them as descendants of local ``mother theories'' on $S^2\times\mathbb R$. We start with the example of Ising mother theory built from two charged Dirac fermions coupled to a real mediator $\phi$. Integrating out $\phi$ and the higher Landau levels produces the fuzzy Hamiltonian, while integrating out the fermions gives a conventional local $\phi$ effective field theory flowing to the Ising point. Mild modifications of the same theory reach the Gaussian point (tricritical route) and the Lee-Yang continuation. Exploring the space of mother theories, we next promote $\phi$ to a complex field $\Phi$ and construct a new example, whose descendant is an easy-plane ferromagnet on the fuzzy sphere. Its spontaneous symmetry breaking phase gives a pseudo-Goldstone relativistic scalar, similar to the model introduced by Y.-C. He, while its critical point is non-relativistic, providing the first example of $z=2$ scaling on the fuzzy sphere. Remarkably, it admits exact one-magnon solutions, and the $z=2$ critical point can be found analytically. We then outline a rich landscape of further applications of our approach.

</details>


### [16] [Holomorphic fermions in $\text{AdS}_{2}$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37762)
*Calvin Y.-R. Chen,Lukas W. Lindwasser*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper identifies conserved symmetric, traceless spinor-tensors for Majorana fermions on 2D AdS with tuned masses, links them to holomorphic/anti-holomorphic half-integer spin currents, fully parameterises the classical phase space using arbitrary (anti-)holomorphic functions, reveals a hidden conformal symmetry via conformal Killing spinor-tensors, and shows reduced chiral algebras compared to flat-space counterparts.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover hidden symmetries and algebraic structures of massive Majorana fermions on two-dimensional Anti-de Sitter space, particularly exploring connections to conformal field theory and chiral algebras.

Method: Analysis of conserved spinor-tensors in 2D AdS; use of complex coordinates and Laurent mode expansions; classical phase space parameterisation via (anti-)holomorphic functions; covariant reformulation using conformal Killing spinor-tensors; comparison of chiral algebras with flat-space theory.

Result: Existence of symmetric, traceless conserved spinor-tensors; holomorphic/anti-holomorphic half-integer spin currents with CFT-like mode expansions; complete phase space parameterisation by arbitrary (anti-)holomorphic functions; covariant description via conformal Killing spinor-tensors indicating hidden conformal symmetry; chiral algebras strictly smaller than those in flat-space theory.

Conclusion: Massive Majorana fermions on 2D AdS exhibit rich conformal-like structures—despite curvature and mass—revealing an emergent hidden conformal symmetry encoded in conformal Killing spinor-tensors and yielding constrained chiral algebras relative to flat space.

Abstract: On two-dimensional Anti-de Sitter space, Majorana fermions with a discrete set of finely tuned, non-zero mass parameters possess conserved spinor--tensors that are symmetric and traceless. In complex coordinates, these correspond to pairs of holomorphic and anti-holomorphic half-integer spin currents and admit Laurent series mode expansions reminiscent of standard two-dimensional conformal field theories. We exploit this fact in order to fully parameterise the classical phase space associated with the equation of motion in terms of arbitrary (anti-)holomorphic functions. We further show that this parameterisation can be written covariantly in terms of a conformal Killing spinor--tensor, hinting at a hidden conformal symmetry. We also show they possess chiral algebras which are strict subalgebras of the one present in the corresponding theory on flat space.

</details>


### [17] [Quiver Lab -- A Computing Tool for Quiver Gauge Theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37766)
*Zhenghao Zhong*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Quiver Lab is a browser-based tool for visualizing quivers and analyzing moduli spaces of 3D N=4 supersymmetric theories, computing Hilbert series, plethystic logarithms, refinements, and Hasse diagrams.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide an accessible, interactive platform for studying the geometry and dynamics of 3D N=4 supersymmetric gauge theories via quiver representation and moduli space analysis.

Method: Implements computational algebraic geometry and representation-theoretic algorithms in a web interface, supporting both guided (Light) and advanced (Expert) user workflows for computing Higgs/Coulomb branch data and Hasse diagrams.

Result: Enables efficient computation and visualization of Hilbert series, plethystic logarithms, supported refinements, and Decay/Fission Hasse diagrams for 3D N=4 quiver gauge theories.

Conclusion: Quiver Lab bridges theoretical physics and computational tools, facilitating exploration and verification of moduli space structures in supersymmetric field theories.

Abstract: Quiver Lab is a browser-based workspace for drawing quivers and exploring the moduli spaces of three-dimensional $\mathcal{N} = 4$ theories. It computes Higgs and Coulomb branch Hilbert series, plethystic logarithms, supported refinements, and Decay and Fission Hasse diagrams. This note explains the Light and Expert workflows and the main computational methods

</details>


### [18] [Go with the flow(s): radial de Sitter holography and $T^2 (+ Λ_2)$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37776)
*Matthew J. Blacker*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs Wheeler-DeWitt states localized on Schwarzschild-de Sitter spacetime, develops a slice-independent worldtube holographic framework in the static patch, and establishes a dictionary linking it to cut-off holography frameworks ($T^2$ and $T^2 + \Lambda_2$), thereby providing a bulk realization of $T^2 + \Lambda_2$ for dS₄ black holes; it identifies the worldtube’s average Hamiltonian as the seed energy and shows that recovering the full $T^2 (+ \Lambda_2)$ spectrum requires sacrificing slice independence.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge bulk gravitational physics in de Sitter space with candidate boundary duals by constructing concrete, localized quantum gravitational states and holographic frameworks that respect the causal structure of the static patch.

Method: Constructs midisuperspace Wheeler-DeWitt wavefunctionals localized on Schwarzschild-de Sitter geometry; defines a slice-independent worldtube holographic setup in the static patch; derives a dictionary mapping worldtube observables (especially the average Hamiltonian) to $T^2$ and $T^2 + \Lambda_2$ cut-off CFT deformations; analyzes consistency conditions and trade-offs between slice independence and spectral matching.

Result: A bulk-realized $T^2 + \Lambda_2$ holographic description for 4D de Sitter black holes; identification of the worldtube average Hamiltonian as the seed energy for $T^2 (+ \Lambda_2)$; demonstration that exact spectral recovery necessitates slice dependence, revealing a fundamental tension between locality in time and spectral fidelity in de Sitter holography.

Conclusion: The worldtube framework provides a geometrically natural, static-patch-compatible holographic setup for dS black holes, but full equivalence with $T^2 (+ \Lambda_2)$ requires breaking manifest slice independence—highlighting an intrinsic trade-off between background independence and boundary spectral precision in de Sitter holography.

Abstract: What can we learn about a putative dual to de Sitter spacetime from the bulk? Conversely, what can a candidate dual theory teach us about the bulk? In this work, we construct midisuperspace Wheeler-DeWitt states that are localised on the Schwarzschild-de Sitter spacetime. Using these states, we construct a slice-independent worldtube holographic framework in the static patch. We then construct a dictionary between this worldtube proposal and both the $T^2$ and $T^2 + \Lambda_2$ frameworks for cut-off holography. In doing so, we provide a bulk realisation of $T^2+ \Lambda_2$ for dS$_4$ black holes. We identify the average Hamiltonian of our worldtube proposal as the seed energy of the $T^2 (+ \Lambda_2)$ theories. To recover the $T^2 (+ \Lambda_2)$ energy spectrum as the average Hamiltonian of a worldtube theory, one must make the bulk framework slice-dependent, and we discuss the consequences of this trade-off.

</details>


### [19] [Differential Equations for Dressed "in-in" Correlators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37856)
*Ivo Sachs*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends differential equation and Integration-by-Parts (IBP) techniques to cosmological in-in correlators of conformally coupled scalars, treating them as dressed flat-space amplitudes; it demonstrates algorithmic formulation and functional insights using bubble-triangle and exchange diagrams.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop systematic, algorithmic tools for analyzing cosmological in-in correlators—particularly for conformally coupled scalars—by leveraging the structural similarity to flat-space amplitudes and known computational methods like IBP and differential equations.

Method: Extends the differential equations method and Integration-by-Parts identities from flat-space scattering amplitudes to cosmological in-in correlators in de Sitter space, specifically for conformally coupled scalar fields; applies the formalism to bubble-triangle and exchange diagrams.

Result: Derives powerful differential equations for these correlators that constrain and characterize the class of special functions (e.g., polylogarithms, elliptic integrals) they evaluate to; shows the equations can be constructed algorithmically.

Conclusion: The extended formalism provides a robust, computationally tractable framework for studying cosmological correlators, bridging flat-space amplitude techniques with inflationary observables and enabling systematic classification of their analytic structure.

Abstract: We extend the formalism of differential equations and Integration-by-Parts identities, to cosmological in-in correlators of conformally coupled scalar fields, that can be formulated as dressed flat space amplitudes. These differential equations turn out to be quite powerful for extracting information about the class of functions which the corresponding amplitudes integrate to. Furthermore, they can be formulated algorithmically. Here we illustrate this for the cosmological bubble- triangle and exchange diagram.

</details>


### [20] [Near-horizon BF bounds for the $t^+$ KK tower on extremal AdS$_5$ black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37908)
*Xiao-Long Liu,Zheng Miao,Yuezhang Tang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the charged Breitenlohner–Freedman (BF) bound for horizon harmonics in the type IIB $t^+$ Kaluza–Klein tower on extremal charged AdS$_5$ black holes with equal rotations, using 5D gauged supergravity and linearized exceptional field theory; it identifies regimes—BPS, $\Omega_H > 1$, and $\Phi_H > 1$—where modes respectively satisfy, violate, or marginally pass the bound, with violation detectable only beyond low-level truncations and influenced by charge–spin discreteness and large-radius spin scaling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine stability of extremal charged AdS$_5$ black holes with equal rotations against scalar perturbations from the type IIB $t^+$ Kaluza–Klein tower, specifically assessing when near-horizon modes violate the charged BF bound—a necessary condition for instability in AdS backgrounds.

Method: Analyzes the scalar wave equation derived from maximal five-dimensional gauged supergravity (level-zero) and linearized exceptional field theory (higher levels); examines sign changes of a quadratic form in charge and angular velocity; studies mode behavior across parameter space: BPS curve ($\Omega_H = \Phi_H = 1$), $\Omega_H > 1$, and $\Phi_H > 1$; considers level truncation, spin dependence, and large-radius asymptotics.

Result: All modes satisfy the charged BF bound precisely on the BPS curve; for $\Omega_H > 1$, every black hole has at least one violating mode, but no finite level/spin truncation captures all violations; for $\Phi_H > 1$, BF-passing modes lie in a narrow strip governed by charge–spin quantization; at large radius and fixed rotation, a level-4 mode with spin $\\propto$ radius violates the bound even when all U(2) singlets satisfy it.

Conclusion: The near-horizon charged BF bound is generically violated away from the BPS curve, revealing subtle dependence on KK level, spin, and charge quantization; however, full radial stability—including backreaction and higher-derivative effects—remains undetermined.

Abstract: We study the charged Breitenlohner--Freedman (BF) bound for all horizon harmonics of the type IIB $t^+$ Kaluza--Klein tower on extremal charged AdS$_5$ black holes with equal rotations. The scalar equation follows from maximal five-dimensional gauged supergravity at level zero and the linearized exceptional-field-theory equations at higher levels. For aligned charge and rotation, a quadratic form in charge and spin changes signature on the curve of supersymmetric (BPS) black holes, where every mode satisfies the bound. Above the curve, where $\Omega_H>1$, every black hole has a violating mode, although no finite truncation in level or spin detects violation at every such black hole. Below the curve, where $\Phi_H>1$, the BF-passing set is confined to a thin strip and controlled by charge and spin discreteness. At large radius and fixed rotation, a level-four mode with spin proportional to the radius violates the bound even where all U(2) singlets satisfy it. These results concern the near-horizon BF bound of one tower in two-derivative supergravity; full radial stability remains open.

</details>


### [21] [Quotient Quiver Subtraction -- Product Groups](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38052)
*Sam Bennett,Amihay Hanany,Guhesh Kumaran*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces quotient quiver subtraction as a combinatorial method to gauge emergent U(n)×U(n′) and SU(n)×SU(n′) Coulomb branch global symmetries in 3d N=4 theories, using brane constructions and symplectisation of unitary nodes; verified via examples and linked to Class S and maximal implosion theories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically gauge emergent Coulomb branch global symmetries—specifically U(n)×U(n′) and SU(n)×SU(n′)—in 3d N=4 gauge theories, translating gauging into a tractable combinatorial operation on quivers.

Method: Uses Type IIB brane systems to implement quotient quiver subtraction: a two-step gauging procedure involving symplectisation (replacing unitary gauge nodes with symplectic ones) and subsequent quotienting to realise the desired global symmetry gauging.

Result: A constructive, brane-realised algorithm for gauging U(n)×U(n′) and SU(n)×SU(n′) emergent symmetries; explicit quiver examples confirm consistency; established connections to Class S theories and 3d realisations of maximal implosion spaces.

Conclusion: Quotient quiver subtraction provides a precise, geometrically grounded framework for gauging emergent Coulomb branch symmetries in 3d N=4 theories, unifying and extending recent combinatorial and brane-based techniques.

Abstract: Quotient quiver subtraction translates the gauging of Coulomb branch global symmetries into a combinatoric operation on 3d $\mathcal{N}=4$ gauge theories. This work uses brane systems to introduce a systematic method to gauge $\textrm{U}(n)\times\textrm{U}(n')$ and $\textrm{SU}(n)\times\textrm{SU}(n')$ emergent Coulomb branch global symmetries. Similar to other recently-introduced techniques, the gauging operation is a two-step process; in this case, it involves the symplectisation of unitary gauge factors into symplectic groups. A host of explicit examples are used to verify the procedure, and connections are made to Class $\mathcal{S}$ theories and three-dimensional theories realising maximal implosion spaces.

</details>


### [22] [Covering Surfaces, Conical-Defect Continuation, and Multi-Entropy at Arbitrary Genus](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38208)
*Chen-Te Ma*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Derives normalized geometric factors for permutation-twist correlators at arbitrary genus; links real part of conical-defect exponent to covering-space anomaly and imaginary part to Euler-characteristic Weyl phase; extends normalization, computes higher replica corrections, specifies holographic limit condition; shows large c alone insufficient for full entanglement determination.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically normalize permutation-twist correlators across arbitrary genus and clarify the geometric and anomaly structure underlying multi-entanglement entropy in holographic CFTs.

Method: Uses hypergeometric uniformization for three uniformly ramified branch points; analytically continues conical-defect exponents; generalizes to arbitrary numbers of branch values; computes higher-order replica-index corrections; imposes consistency conditions for holographic duality.

Result: Normalized geometric factors expressed via real (anomaly) and imaginary (Euler-Weyl) parts of continued conical-defect exponents; explicit higher replica corrections to geometric multi-entropy; precise extra condition—beyond large central charge—for holographic limit; counterexample showing reducible conformal/topological model where large c fails to fix entanglement fully.

Conclusion: Geometric multi-entropy is governed by both modular anomaly and topological Weyl phase; holography requires additional constraints beyond large-c scaling; normalization must account for genus-dependent branching structure and replica analytic continuation.

Abstract: We derive normalized geometric factors for permutation-twist correlators at arbitrary genus. For three uniformly ramified branch values, hypergeometric uniformization shows that the real part of the continued conical-defect exponent equals the covering-space anomaly factor. Its imaginary part is an Euler-characteristic Weyl phase. We extend the normalization to additional branch values, derive higher replica-index corrections to the geometric multi-entropy, and state the extra condition required for a holographic limit. A reducible conformal/topological example demonstrates why large central charge alone does not fix the complete entanglement result.

</details>


### [23] [Entropically favored scalar hair on AdS black branes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38226)
*Alex Buchel*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: The paper constructs neutral AdS₅ black branes with stable, entropically favored scalar hair via a quadratic scalar–Gauss–Bonnet coupling; the UV operator dimension remains fixed at Δ=3, and fully backreacted solutions are obtained with only a quadratic scalar potential—discovered via neural-network-guided model search.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To find a simple, physically viable holographic model where scalar hair forms spontaneously in black brane backgrounds and is thermodynamically preferred over the symmetric (hairless) phase—addressing stability, backreaction, and UV consistency.

Method: Constructs asymptotically AdS₅ black brane solutions in Einstein–scalar–Gauss–Bonnet gravity with a quadratic scalar potential; employs neural-network-assisted exploration of coupling space to identify minimal, stable hairy configurations; analyzes thermodynamic dominance via entropy comparison.

Result: Identifies a minimal model where neutral AdS₅ black branes support source-free, fully backreacted scalar hair stabilized by quadratic scalar–Gauss–Bonnet coupling; the hairy phase has higher entropy than the symmetric phase; UV operator dimension remains Δ=3, preserving standard holographic interpretation.

Conclusion: A simple, consistent holographic model for spontaneous symmetry breaking in thermal states is achieved—demonstrating that entropically favored scalar hair can emerge without fine-tuned potentials or higher-derivative operators beyond Gauss–Bonnet, enabled by ML-guided theory discovery.

Abstract: We construct neutral AdS$_5$ black branes with source-free scalar hair that is entropically favored over the symmetric phase. A quadratic scalar--Gauss--Bonnet coupling destabilizes the thermal state while leaving the ultraviolet operator dimension fixed at $\Delta=3$. The fully backreacted solutions require only a quadratic scalar potential. We describe how a neural-network search led to this simple model.

</details>


### [24] [Tachyon and massless sectors of the bosonic string effective action](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38364)
*Mohammad R. Garousi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper identifies a minimal, T-duality-invariant basis for the bosonic string effective action with tachyon and massless fields, separates couplings into massless and tachyon sectors via field redefinition, fixes all six-derivative massless-sector couplings, leaves two tachyon-sector couplings undetermined (one fixed by four-tachyon S-matrix), and verifies agreement with sphere-level S-matrix expansions up to six-momentum order.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a consistent, T-duality-invariant effective action for bosonic string theory including the tachyon, where non-derivative tachyon terms appear only in the potential and all other couplings involve covariant derivatives—enabling systematic constraint via T-duality upon circle reduction.

Method: Imposing T-duality invariance (Buscher rules plus higher-derivative corrections) on the effective action after circle reduction; performing field redefinitions to isolate metric/dilaton/tachyon dependence; matching against sphere-level open- and closed-string S-matrix elements (two tachyons + two massless, and four tachyons) at six-momentum order.

Result: Complete determination of all six-derivative couplings in the massless sector; identification of two T-dual tachyon-sector couplings, one of which (the four-tachyon term) has its coefficient fixed uniquely by the four-tachyon S-matrix at six-momentum order; full agreement between the leading-order action plus massless-sector couplings and the two-tachyon–two-massless S-matrix expansion at that order.

Conclusion: T-duality and S-matrix consistency jointly fix the structure of the bosonic string effective action up to six derivatives: the massless sector is fully determined, while the tachyon sector retains limited freedom—only the four-tachyon coupling is constrained by S-matrix data, confirming the separation of dynamics into geometric (massless) and tachyonic sectors.

Abstract: Recently, it has been observed that in certain schemes of the bosonic string effective action, the non-derivative tachyon appears only in the tachyon potential, while all other couplings involve covariant derivatives of the tachyon and massless fields. Within these schemes, a minimal basis can be found whose coupling constants are constrained by T-duality upon circle reduction. Using this constraint, one obtains sets of couplings at each order of \(\alpha'\) that are invariant under Buscher rules supplemented by higher-derivative corrections for all base-space fields except the tachyon. In this paper, we observe that consistency of these T-dual sets with S-matrix elements requires an appropriate field redefinition, such that each set becomes invariant under Buscher rules supplemented by higher-derivative corrections for all base-space fields except the metric, dilaton, and tachyon. This separates the couplings into a massless sector and a tachyon sector. We determine the six-derivative couplings in the massless sector, with all coupling constants fixed, and find two T-dual couplings in the tachyon sector with arbitrary coupling constants. We show that the leading-order action and the massless-sector couplings exactly reproduce the expansion of the sphere-level S-matrix element of two tachyons and two massless vertex operators at six-momentum order, and that the coupling constant of the four-tachyon coupling in the tachyon sector is fixed by the expansion of the four-tachyon S-matrix element at six-momentum order.

</details>


### [25] [The Topological String on the Quintic to Genus 83 and the Rank-Two D4-D2-D0 Indices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38473)
*Michael Hecht*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes high-genus (2–83) quintic Gopakumar-Vafa invariants up to degree 44 using holomorphic anomaly equations, resolving holomorphic ambiguities and polar D4-D2-D0 coefficients via wall-crossing and modularity constraints—yielding complete GV, PT, and DT data through degree 32.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically compute high-genus Gopakumar-Vafa invariants for the quintic Calabi–Yau threefold, where direct enumerative methods fail and holomorphic ambiguities obstruct reconstruction of topological string amplitudes.

Method: Integration of holomorphic anomaly equations constrained by rank-one modularity of Gieseker invariants and rank-two charge conjugation; solution of coupled boundary/surplus wall-crossing equations; computer-assisted extension of wall crossing to close recursion; use of low-genus input and mock modularity assumptions for extrapolation.

Result: Quintic GV invariants fully determined for genera 2–83 and degrees ≤32; partial results up to degree 44 and genus 216; 22/36 polar rank-two D4-D2-D0 coefficients fixed unconditionally; conditional candidates obtained for genera 84–85 and remaining polar coefficients using mock modularity.

Conclusion: Holomorphic anomaly + wall-crossing + modularity provide a powerful, computationally tractable framework for high-genus GV invariants on the quintic, substantially extending known enumerative data and revealing deep interplay between modular forms, BPS state counting, and topological string theory.

Abstract: We compute quintic Gopakumar-Vafa invariants for genera 2-83 and degrees up to 44 by integrating the holomorphic anomaly equations. Under rank-one modularity for the required Gieseker invariants and rank-two charge conjugation, a coupled system of boundary and surplus wall-crossing equations fixes the holomorphic ambiguities and 22 of the 36 polar rank-two D4-D2-D0 coefficients, without assuming any rank-two index. A computer-assisted extension of wall crossing supplies additional equations and the boundary datum needed to close the recursion through genus 83. With known low-genus input, the GV data are complete through degree 32 and yield PT and DT tables. Selected higher-genus wall-crossing invariants reach genus 216; 33 entries require additional hypotheses. Retrospectively selected extrapolations give conditional candidates at genera 84 and 85 and, with rank-two mock modularity, for the remaining polar coefficients.

</details>


### [26] [Resurgence and Modularity in the First Painlevé Equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38584)
*Kohei Iwaki,Noam Tamarin*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes an explicit connection between nonlinear Stokes maps for the first Painlevé equation and modular transformations of its two-parameter tau function, showing that the composition of three Stokes maps equals the modular S∘T action; it identifies rotations (8π/5 and 2π/5) with T- and S-modular actions respectively, computes associated parameter transformations and tau-function multiplicative factors—including normalization—and shows single-valuedness after a full 2π rotation is restored via Abel’s five-term identity for the Rogers dilogarithm.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the global analytic structure of the first Painlevé transcendent and its tau function around infinity, particularly how monodromy (encoded in Stokes phenomena) relates to modular symmetry of the tau function.

Method: Combines exact WKB analysis and topological recursion assumptions; tracks Stokes multipliers of the associated isomonodromy system while evolving the homology basis of the elliptic spectral curve under rotations of the independent variable; identifies Borel directions and composes nonlinear Stokes maps S_{−π}, S_0, S_π; matches rotational parameter transformations to modular generators S and T; computes tau-function prefactors using Rogers dilogarithm identities.

Result: Derives explicit parameter transformation for the modular S-action under 2π/5 rotation (new result); confirms known 8π/5 → T-action; computes full multiplicative factors—including normalization constants—in tau-function transformation laws; verifies single-valuedness of both Painlevé transcendent and tau function after 2π monodromy via cancellation governed by Abel’s five-term identity for the Rogers dilogarithm.

Conclusion: The nonlinear Stokes monodromy of the first Painlevé equation is faithfully represented by SL(2,ℤ) modular action on the two-parameter tau function, with concrete rotational realizations and precise prefactor calculus anchored in dilogarithmic identities—establishing a deep bridge between resurgence, isomonodromy, and modular geometry.

Abstract: For the first Painlevé equation, we relate three nonlinear Stokes maps associated with a full turn around infinity to modular transformations of the two-parameter tau function through the identity $\boldsymbol S_\pi\circ\boldsymbol S_0\circ \boldsymbol S_{-\pi}=\mathsf S\circ\mathsf T$. Here the subscripts label Borel directions, while $\mathsf S$ and $\mathsf T$ denote the modular generators. Working under the analytic assumptions of the topological recursion and exact WKB constructions, we compare Stokes multipliers of the associated isomonodromy system while tracking the homology basis of its elliptic spectral curve under rotations of the independent variable. For the sectors considered, we identify the known parameter transformation induced by an $8\pi/5$ rotation with the modular $T$-action and derive a new explicit parameter relation realizing the $S$-action for a $2\pi/5$ rotation. We also determine the multiplicative factors in the corresponding tau-function relations, including their normalization constants. Together, these results give an explicit realization of single-valuedness after one full turn around infinity for both the Painlevé transcendent and its tau function, with the tau-function prefactors cancelling by Abel's five-term identity for the Rogers dilogarithm.

</details>


### [27] [Conformal Spectrum of (Super) Chiral Ising and XY Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38614)
*Oleg Antipin,Jahmall Bersini,Jacob Hafjall,Giulia Muco,Francesco Sannino*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the scaling dimension spectrum of composite operators in critical Gross-Neveu-Yukawa and NJL-Yukawa models at next-to-leading order semiclassically, resolves Lorentz structure, and obtains a closed-form spectrum at SUSY-enhanced parameter values—supporting emergent superconformal symmetry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test and provide evidence for emergent superconformal symmetry in strongly coupled quantum field theories—specifically in chiral Ising and chiral XY universality classes—by computing operator spectra beyond leading order.

Method: Next-to-leading order semiclassical computation of scaling dimensions of composite operators; analysis of Lorentz representation structure; evaluation at parameter values where infrared supersymmetry is expected.

Result: Full operator spectrum resolved with Lorentz structure; closed-form expression for scaling dimensions at SUSY points; operators organized into superconformal multiplets.

Conclusion: The semiclassical spectrum organizes naturally into superconformal multiplets at SUSY parameters, strengthening evidence for emergent superconformal dynamics in these Yukawa-type critical models.

Abstract: We determine the spectrum of scaling dimensions of composite operators in the critical Gross-Neveu-Yukawa and Nambu-Jona-Lasinio-Yukawa models through a next-to-leading order semiclassical computation. Their infrared fixed points belong to the chiral Ising and chiral XY universality classes, respectively. At next-to-leading order, we resolve the Lorentz structure of the operator spectrum. For the parameter values at which supersymmetry is expected to emerge in the infrared, we obtain a closed-form expression encoding the full spectrum within the semiclassical methodology. The resulting organization of operators into superconformal multiplets provides further evidence for emergent superconformal dynamics.

</details>


### [28] [Bootstrapping Wilson lines in Critical Bosonic QED$_3$ Part 1: Large-$N$ endpoint correlators and CFT data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38663)
*Jun Maeda,Toshiki Onagi,Soichiro Shimamori*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes conformal Wilson line observables in critical bosonic QED₃ with N scalar flavors at large N, using diagrammatic four-point functions and endpoint crossing; it determines defect CFT data (endpoint dimension, g-function, OPE coefficients) and finds bulk-defect spectral agreement in all non-singlet channels to O(1/N).


<details>
