  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically characterize the conformal defect CFT defined by a Wilson line in critical bosonic QED₃, especially its non-singlet sector, and test bulk-defect spectral correspondence beyond singlet channels.

Method: Combines perturbative diagrammatic computation of four-point correlators (including mixed correlators with Hubbard–Stratonovich fields) with exact endpoint crossing symmetry constraints formulated covariantly under SU(N); works to first subleading order in 1/N for fixed integer charge q.

Result: Closed-form expressions for four-endpoint and mixed two-bulk-two-endpoint correlators; exact endpoint scaling dimension Δₑₙd(q), defect g-function g(q), and dimensions & OPE coefficients of lowest non-singlet Wilson-line primaries; bulk and defect spectra agree in all non-singlet SU(N) channels to O(1/N), including OPE-weighted anomalous dimension averages at degenerate levels.

Conclusion: Endpoint crossing + large-N diagrammatics provides a robust framework for defect CFT data in vectorial QED₃; the observed bulk-defect spectral matching in non-singlet sectors suggests a deeper integrability or duality structure underlying the Wilson line defect.

Abstract: We study the conformal Wilson line with general charge $q$ in critical bosonic QED$_3$ with $N$ complex scalar flavors by combining diagrammatic four-point calculations with endpoint crossing. We formulate the endpoint crossing equations with the full $\mathrm{SU}(N)$ flavor structure, keeping positivity manifest. For arbitrary positive integer charge $q$ held fixed at large $N$, we obtain closed-form expressions for four-endpoint correlators and mixed correlators involving two bulk Hubbard--Stratonovich fields through first subleading order in $1/N$. These results determine the endpoint dimension, the defect $g$-function, and the dimensions and endpoint OPE coefficients of the lowest non-singlet Wilson-line primaries. Moreover, combining the diagrammatic correlator with endpoint crossing, somewhat surprisingly, we find agreement between the bulk and Wilson-line spectra in every non-singlet flavor channel through order $1/N$. At degenerate levels, this agreement concerns the OPE-weighted averages of anomalous dimensions probed by the endpoint correlator. For unit charge, we independently reproduce these averages from the known bulk higher-spin spectrum.

</details>


### [29] [Topological interfaces between universes and generalized families of 2d QFTs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38680)
*Pinak Banerjee,Yuhan Gai,Alonso Perez-Lona,Daniel Robbins,Subham Roy,Eric Sharpe,with an appendix by Xingyang Yu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies 2D quantum field theories with topological point operators that decompose into 'universes', using multi-fusion categories to characterize their finite symmetries and classify inter-universe topological interfaces into three types (I: invertible, II: non-invertible but symmetry-preserving, III: no interface—disconnected universes). It derives inherited symmetries for abelian orbifolds, constructs Type III pairs via 2-group extensions and RG flows, and proposes a correspondence with generalized QFT families.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically understand how 2D QFTs with topological point operators decompose into disconnected 'universes', and to classify the possible topological interfaces between them using categorical symmetry structures beyond ordinary groups—specifically, multi-fusion categories—as natural generalizations of fusion categories used for non-invertible symmetries.

Method: Uses multi-fusion category theory to formalize finite symmetries of decomposing 2D QFTs; defines an action functor encoding trivially acting symmetries and inter-universe topological interfaces; classifies universe pairs by interface invertibility (Types I–III); computes symmetries for orbifolds with trivially acting abelian normal subgroups; constructs Type III examples via 2-group extensions and RG flows; draws analogies with equivariant/categorical families of QFTs.

Result: A complete classification of inter-universe topological interfaces into Types I (invertible), II (non-invertible but symmetry-preserving), and III (no interface, i.e., fully disconnected universes); explicit derivation of full inherited symmetry for abelian orbifolds—yielding only Types I/II; construction of Type III pairs from 2-group extensions and UV-complete RG flows; proposal of a precise correspondence between interface types and generalized QFT families (equivariant ↔ Type I, categorical ↔ Type II, discrete-parameter families ↔ Type III).

Conclusion: Multi-fusion categories provide the correct categorical framework for finite symmetries of decomposing 2D QFTs; the trichotomy of interface types reflects deep structural properties of the symmetry category; Type III universes—previously elusive—arise naturally from extended symmetry structures (e.g., 2-groups) or RG flow embeddings, and align with discrete-parameter generalized QFT families.

Abstract: Two-dimensional quantum field theories with topological point operators decompose into universes. Just as fusion categories have played an important role in understanding non-invertible symmetries, multi-fusion categories play an analogous role for the symmetries of such decomposing theories. We describe a finite symmetry as a multi-fusion category together with its action functor, which keeps track of trivially acting symmetries and of the topological interfaces between universes. Relative to a chosen symmetry, we classify pairs of universes by the topological interfaces it contains, which reflect the structure of the multi-fusion category: universes in different indecomposable summands are joined by no interface (Type~III), while within a summand they are related by invertible (Type~I) or only non-invertible (Type~II) interfaces. For orbifolds with a trivially acting abelian normal subgroup, we derive the full inherited symmetry, which characterizes the operators between universes found case by case by Robbins, Sharpe and Vandermeulen and yields pairs of Types~I and~II but never of Type~III. We construct Type~III pairs instead from extensions of $2$-group type and from renormalization group flows out of a common ultraviolet theory. Finally, we propose a correspondence with the generalized families of quantum field theories of Brennan and Intriligator, in which equivariant families correspond to Type~I pairs and categorical ones to Type~II pairs whose gauging preserves the symmetry, family anomalies appear as universes carrying different symmetry-protected topological phases, and Type~III pairs can realize families over a discrete parameter. An appendix by Xingyang Yu works out the higher-charge Schwinger model as a Lagrangian example.

</details>


### [30] [Collective Bootstrap for Matrix Quantum Mechanics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38716)
*Xianlong Liu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces a collective bootstrap framework for large-N matrix quantum mechanics using loop space of single-trace coordinate moments, reformulating the energy minimization as a semidefinite program to obtain rigorous lower bounds on planar ground state energies; it achieves high accuracy across one- to three-matrix models and extends feasibility to BFSS/BMN bosonic sectors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome limitations of conventional moment methods in large-N matrix quantum mechanics—particularly handling momentum-containing moments and nonlinear constraints—by working exclusively in loop space of coordinate moments, enabling higher truncations and scalability to multi-matrix models including BFSS/BMN.

Method: Develops a collective bootstrap in loop space of single-trace coordinate moments; uses loop joining/splitting for kinetic constraints, applies virial theorem and Schur complement, relaxes quadratic loop products, and recasts constrained energy minimization as a semidefinite program (SDP); employs sequential SDP heuristic enforcing planar factorization to estimate ground state energy and loop expectations.

Result: Yields certified lower bounds on planar ground state energy at each truncation; achieves close agreement with analytic solutions and master field optimization for 1-, 2-, and 3-matrix models; enables higher word-length truncations than momentum-inclusive methods; demonstrates viability for BFSS/BMN bosonic sectors.

Conclusion: The loop-space collective bootstrap provides a scalable, systematically improvable, and numerically robust framework for non-perturbative study of large-N matrix quantum mechanics, unifying rigorous bounding with practical estimation while avoiding auxiliary momentum variables.

Abstract: We develop a collective bootstrap framework for large $N$ matrix quantum mechanics formulated directly in the loop space of single trace coordinate moments. Loop joining and splitting operations organize the kinetic constraints systematically and avoid treating momentum containing moments as independent variables. Employing the virial theorem, a Schur complement, and a relaxation of quadratic products of loops, we recast the nonlinear constrained optimization of the collective energy into a semidefinite program, which yields a lower bound on the planar ground state energy at every truncation. A sequential semidefinite optimization heuristic then searches for configurations satisfying planar factorization and estimates the ground state energy and loop expectation values. Applications to one-, two-, and three-matrix models exhibit close agreement with analytic solutions and master field optimization. By working exclusively with coordinate moments, the framework permits truncations at higher word length and offers a promising approach to models with more matrices, including the bosonic sectors of the BFSS and BMN models.

</details>


### [31] [Elliptic master integrals and the one-loop cosmological wavefunction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38725)
*Amara McCune,Matthew D. Schwartz*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes one-loop cosmological wavefunction coefficients for conformally coupled scalars, showing the triangle coefficient is polylogarithmic (dilogarithmic) despite elliptic master integrals, due to cancellation of elliptic periods; in contrast, the box coefficient is non-polylogarithmic and involves elliptic integrals and K3 surface periods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve whether loop-level cosmological wavefunction coefficients inherit ellipticity from their underlying Feynman integrals—specifically, whether the one-loop triangle and box coefficients for a conformally coupled scalar are elliptic or polylogarithmic.

Method: Bootstrap approach leveraging analyticity, locality, and flat-space amplitude constraints; residue analysis of loop integrands; threshold discontinuity computation; closed-form evaluation of elliptic and K3-period integrals.

Result: The one-loop triangle wavefunction coefficient is rational in external momenta and expressible in dilogarithms—elliptic periods cancel due to the residue being a rational flat-space amplitude; the one-loop box coefficient is not polylogarithmic—its threshold discontinuity requires elliptic integrals, and for regular-tetrahedron kinematics, its integrand involves periods of a K3 surface.

Conclusion: Ellipticity of master integrals does not imply ellipticity of physical observables like wavefunction coefficients; cancellations enforced by consistency conditions (e.g., flat-space limit, locality) can promote elliptic structures to polylogarithmic ones—or conversely, reveal deeper geometric structures (e.g., K3) where no such simplification occurs.

Abstract: Loop corrections to the cosmological wavefunction involve similar integrals to those required for scattering amplitudes. For the one-loop triangle of a conformally coupled scalar, the master integrals are elliptic, and so the triangle wavefunction coefficient was expected to be elliptic as well. Using a bootstrap approach, we compute this triangle coefficient exactly and show that it is not: the elliptic periods cancel, and the flat-space wavefunction coefficient is a combination of dilogarithms. The cancellation occurs because the residue of the loop integrand on the elliptic sector is a flat-space amplitude, rational in the loop momentum. The one-loop box, by contrast, is not polylogarithmic, even with a single external leg at each site. Its threshold discontinuity involves elliptic integrals, which we also give in closed form. For regular-tetrahedron momenta the periods of a K3 surface enter its loop integrand.

</details>


### [32] [From pq-webs to magnetic quivers and back I: dictionary and deformations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38769)
*Sebastiano Garavaglia,Sara Pasquetti,Palash Singh*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes an exact parameter dictionary linking 5D N=1 SCFTs (engineered by (p,q)-brane webs) to their 3D N=4 magnetic quivers, specifically for the N+SU(N)_0^{k−1}+N box quiver; it validates the mapping via deformations (masses, weak coupling, VEVs) using the superconformal index and squashed-sphere partition function, enabling quantitative reconstruction of 5D physics from 3D observables.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a precise, testable correspondence between five-dimensional N=1 superconformal field theories and their three-dimensional N=4 magnetic quivers—enabling quantitative control over deformations and infrared dynamics that are otherwise inaccessible in 5D.

Method: Derivation and verification of an exact parameter dictionary mapping 5D flavor masses and instanton couplings to 3D FI parameters; deformation analysis via superconformal index (5D) and squashed-sphere partition function (3D), incorporating Higgsing, decoupling, and IR theory identification.

Result: A validated exact dictionary enabling faithful translation of mass deformations, weak-coupling limits, and monopole VEVs between 5D SCFTs and their magnetic quivers; demonstration that 3D partition function techniques reliably capture 5D index responses under arbitrary deformations.

Conclusion: Magnetic quivers are promoted from qualitative classification tools to quantitative computational frameworks for 5D N=1 SCFTs: once the dictionary is fixed, exact 3D observables fully encode and reconstruct deformed 5D physics, allowing bidirectional navigation of the deformation space.

Abstract: This is the first of two companion papers in which we study five-dimensional $\mathcal{N}=1$ theories engineered by $(p,q)$ five-brane webs and their associated three-dimensional $\mathcal{N}=4$ magnetic quivers. We focus on the $N+\mathrm{SU}(N)_0^{k-1}+N$ box quiver theories. We propose an exact parameter dictionary relating the five-dimensional flavour masses and instanton couplings to the Fayet-Iliopoulos parameters of the corresponding strong-coupling magnetic quiver. We test this dictionary through a broad class of deformations, including mass deformations, weak-coupling limits, and vacuum expectation values. On the five-dimensional side, we implement these flows directly at the level of the superconformal index. The dictionary fixes the corresponding FI deformations and monopole VEVs. We analyse them in the magnetic-quiver squashed-sphere partition function using a new method that tracks Higgsing, decoupled sectors, and infrared theories. Our results promote magnetic quivers to tools for the quantitative study of how the five-dimensional superconformal index responds to deformations. Once the parameter dictionary is known, exact three-dimensional observables can be used to reconstruct the corresponding five-dimensional physics and to navigate the full deformation network in both directions.

</details>


### [33] [From pq-webs to magnetic quivers and back II: distributional indices and 5d theories breaking bad](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38770)
*Sebastiano Garavaglia,Sara Pasquetti,Palash Singh*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper explores nilpotent moment-map VEV deformations in 5D N=1 SCFTs engineered by (p,q) five-brane webs, linking excessive Higgsing to distributional superconformal indices—akin to 'bad/broken' 3D theories—and provides a five-brane web interpretation via Hanany-Witten moves and tessellations of generalized toric diagrams.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how nilpotent Higgs branch deformations in 5D N=1 SCFTs (engineered by (p,q) five-brane webs) map to 3D magnetic quiver dynamics, especially under excessive Higgsing, and to unify the geometric (web/toric) and algebraic (moment-map/VEV) descriptions across dimensions.

Method: Uses the exact parameter dictionary between 5D N+SU(N)_0^{k-1}+N theories and their 3D magnetic quivers (established in the companion paper); analyzes superconformal index behavior under nilpotent moment-map VEVs; interprets results via Hanany-Witten transitions in (p,q) five-brane webs and tessellations of generalized toric diagrams.

Result: Excessive Higgsing leads to distributional 5D superconformal indices, analogous to bad/broken 3D theories; Hanany-Witten moves encode the Higgsing cascade; inequivalent 5D UV frames correspond to distinct tessellations of the generalized toric diagram.

Conclusion: The interplay between nilpotent Higgsing, brane dynamics, and toric geometry reveals a deep structural correspondence between 5D SCFTs and their 3D magnetic quivers, with distributional indices signaling loss of standard IR description—highlighting universality of 'badness' across dimensions.

Abstract: This is the second of two companion papers in which we study five-dimensional $\mathcal{N}=1$ theories engineered by $(p,q)$ five-brane webs and their associated three-dimensional magnetic quivers. Leveraging the exact parameter dictionary between the $N+\mathrm{SU}(N)_0^{k-1}+N$ theories and their magnetic quivers at infinite coupling, proposed in the first paper, we investigate nilpotent moment-map VEV deformations and their three-dimensional counterparts. We show that, when the Higgsing is excessive, the five-dimensional superconformal index becomes distributional, mirroring a characteristic feature of bad and broken theories in three dimensions. We also develop a five-brane web interpretation of these results, identifying the Hanany-Witten moves associated with the Higgsing cascade and relating inequivalent frames to distinct tessellations of the generalised toric diagram.

</details>


### [34] [Generalized Symmetries of QED$_4$ on Non-Spin Manifolds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38907)
*Michele Del Zotto,Vivek Saxena*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes generalized symmetries and anomalies in massless QED on oriented non-spin 4-manifolds, where the photon is a spin^c connection and the path integral sums over spin^c structures; it identifies a w₂-twisted magnetic 1-form symmetry, an order-two mixed magnetic-gravitational anomaly classified by reduced oriented bordism (detected on the Wu manifold), and shows its removal requires summing over spin^c structures; it further explores gravitational and electromagnetic responses of chiral defects, framing anomalies in spin^c Hall TQFT, and cancellation via noninvertible WRT layers — all illustrated on ℂℙ².


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how generalized symmetries—particularly magnetic 1-form symmetry—behave in massless QED when spacetime lacks a spin structure, necessitating spin^c geometry; to characterize the resulting interplay between electromagnetic, gravitational, and topological degrees of freedom, especially anomalies arising from summing over spin^c structures.

Method: Uses differential cohomology and spin^c geometry to formulate QED on oriented non-spin 4-manifolds; applies bordism theory (specifically reduced oriented bordism Ω₄^SO) to classify mixed anomalies; computes index-theoretic chiral anomalies via the spin^c Dirac operator; constructs anomaly inflow arguments with spin^c Hall TQFT and Witten-Reshetikhin-Turaev (WRT) topological field theories; verifies predictions explicitly on the complex projective plane ℂℙ².

Result: Identifies a w₂-twisted 1-form magnetic symmetry whose 2π rotation acts as a gravitational sign tied to the normal bundle; discovers an order-two mixed magnetic-gravitational anomaly obstructing ordinary symmetry interpretation, classified by Ω̃₄^SO and detected on the Wu manifold; shows the anomaly vanishes in fixed spin^c sectors (via counterterm) but persists upon summing over spin^c structures; finds rational chiral defects exhibit coupled gravitational–electromagnetic response; reveals a residual framing anomaly after spin^c Hall TQFT cancellation, resolved by stacking a neutral, noninvertible WRT layer; confirms all features on ℂℙ².

Conclusion: The global structure of QED on non-spin manifolds is governed by spin^c geometry and generalized symmetries twisted by w₂; the mixed magnetic-gravitational anomaly is not intrinsic to a single sector but emerges from the sum over spin^c structures, reflecting deep topological constraints; chiral defects and topological responses are inherently gravitational–electromagnetic–framing entangled, demanding noninvertible TQFTs for consistent anomaly cancellation—establishing a refined framework for quantum gauge theory on general oriented 4-manifolds.

Abstract: Generalized symmetries are sensitive to the tangential structure of spacetime. We study this interplay for massless QED on oriented non-spin four-manifolds, where the photon becomes a spin$^c$ connection and the path integral sums over spin$^c$ structures. The shifted flux quantization extends the magnetic 1-form symmetry into a $w_2$-twisted 1-form symmetry: its $2\pi$ rotation acts as a gravitational sign, fixed by the normal bundle of the surface the operator wraps. The obstruction to treating it as an ordinary magnetic 1-form symmetry is an order-two mixed magnetic-gravitational anomaly, which is classified by reduced oriented bordism and detected on the Wu manifold. In a fixed spin$^c$ sector, this anomaly is removable by a counterterm, so it is a property of the sum over spin$^c$ structures. We discuss its consequences for gapped, Coulomb, and Higgs phases. The chiral anomaly is governed by the index of the spin$^c$ Dirac operator, so rational chiral defects carry gravitational as well as electromagnetic responses. A spin$^c$ Hall topological field theory cancels the electromagnetic part but leaves a framing anomaly. At the level of anomaly inflow, this is canceled for every rational angle by stacking a neutral, generally noninvertible Witten-Reshetikhin-Turaev layer. The same framing subtlety affects the chiral defects of QED on curved spin manifolds. The complex projective plane illustrates each of these features explicitly.

</details>


### [35] [Deformed Amplituhedra and Interpolating Anomalous Dimensions on the Coulomb Branch](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39052)
*Francesco Calisto,Subramanya Hegde,Martín Lagares,Elia Mazzucchelli*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends the deformed Amplituhedron to general massive four-point kinematics in the Coulomb branch of N=4 SYM, uses copositivity to constrain the two-loop integrand to double-box topologies, computes analytic results via 4D differential equations, and shows that the walking anomalous dimension is fully determined by cusp and octagon data up to two loops.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically construct and compute planar four-point amplitudes on the Coulomb branch with generic massive kinematics—beyond previously studied limits—and to understand the role of the 'walking' anomalous dimension as an interpolation between cusp and octagon regimes.

Method: Extends the deformed Amplituhedron using a six-parameter Gram matrix; imposes copositivity on its inverse to fix real geometry and integrand structure; specializes to a three-parameter massive configuration; evaluates one- and two-loop integrals via four-dimensional differential equations; expresses results in terms of multiple polylogarithms; analyzes multiscale IR limits for exponentiation and anomalous dimension interpolation.

Result: Copositivity uniquely selects the two planar double-box numerators at two loops; analytic expressions for one- and two-loop amplitudes are obtained; exponentiation holds through two loops; the two-loop coefficients in the all-loop walking anomalous dimension ansatz are fixed; evidence emerges that the full walking anomalous dimension is determined solely by cusp and octagon data.

Conclusion: The deformed Amplituhedron framework, augmented by copositivity, successfully governs multi-massive scattering geometry and integrand selection; the walking anomalous dimension is not independent but encoded in the cusp and octagon limits, suggesting a deeper unification of infrared dynamics across mass scales in N=4 SYM.

Abstract: We study planar four-point scattering amplitudes on the Coulomb branch of $\mathcal N=4$ super Yang--Mills theory, focusing on multi-parameter families of massive kinematics. We extend the deformed Amplituhedron construction of \cite{Arkani-Hamed:2023epq,Flieger:2025ekn} to general external kinematics characterized by a six-parameter embedding-space Gram matrix. We show that copositivity constraints on the inverse Gram matrix control the real geometry and restrict the numerator of the two-loop canonical form to the span of the two planar double-box structures expected for the scattering amplitude. We then specialize to a three-parameter massive configuration and evaluate the relevant one- and two-loop integrals using differential equations directly in four dimensions, obtaining analytic expressions in terms of multiple polylogarithms. We investigate multiscale infrared limits of the proposed two-loop amplitude and find exponentiation through two loops. The kinematic family contains the limits governed by the cusp and octagon anomalous dimensions and interpolates between them through the walking anomalous dimension introduced in~\cite{Alday:2026cpk}. Our result reproduces the one-loop interpolation and fixes the two-loop coefficients in their proposed all-loop ansatz. Interestingly, our results suggest that the walking anomalous dimension may be entirely determined through all loop orders by the cusp and octagon anomalous dimensions.

</details>


### [36] [Regularized Master-Field Approximation for Mass-Deformed Supersymmetric Yang-Mills Matrix Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39101)
*Yue Lei,Reishi Maeta*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper applies the regularized master-field approximation (RMA) to mass-deformed supersymmetric Yang–Mills matrix models with 3, 4, and 10 matrices, demonstrating its ability to quantitatively reproduce fuzzy-sphere classical solutions and their quantum destabilization—particularly via Dirac zero modes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the efficacy of the regularized master-field approximation (RMA) for capturing non-perturbative quantum effects—including symmetry breaking and instability—in large-$N$ supersymmetric matrix models that admit fuzzy-sphere classical vacua.

Method: Application of the RMA—a large-$N$, stochastic, reduced-model computational technique—to three mass-deformed supersymmetric Yang--Mills-type matrix models (with 3, 4, and 10 bosonic matrices); analysis of emergent geometry, stability of fuzzy-sphere configurations, and role of fermionic zero modes via spectral properties of the Dirac operator.

Result: RMA successfully reproduces fuzzy-sphere solutions and their quantum breakdown across all three models; Dirac zero modes are identified as key destabilizing agents in specific parameter regimes.

Conclusion: The RMA is a robust and efficient tool for studying geometric emergence and quantum stability in supersymmetric large-$N$ matrix models, with implications for holography and non-perturbative formulation of quantum field theory.

Abstract: The regularized master-field approximation (RMA) is an efficient computational method for large-$N$ reduced matrix models, including those with multiple matrices or complex integration weights. In this work, we apply the RMA to mass-deformed supersymmetric Yang--Mills-type matrix models with three, four, and ten matrices. All three models admit fuzzy-sphere configurations as classical solutions. We show that the RMA quantitatively reproduces these fuzzy-sphere solutions and their breakdown, and that zero modes of the Dirac operator can destabilize fuzzy-sphere configurations in certain cases.

</details>


### [37] [virasoro_blocks: Software for the 2d bootstrap](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39106)
*David Poland*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: virasoro_blocks is software for high-precision, pole-reduced computation of Virasoro and related conformal blocks—optimized for numerical bootstrap—enabling sharp bounds on scalar gaps and average exchanged dimensions in 2D CFTs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard Zamolodchikov recursion yields Virasoro blocks with many Kac poles, hindering their use in semidefinite programming (SDP) for the numerical conformal bootstrap; there is a need for stable, high-accuracy, pole-minimized representations valid at arbitrary central charge—including singular cases like minimal models and c=1.

Method: The software implements optimal interpolation to reduce the number of Kac poles while preserving accuracy; supports arbitrary-precision arithmetic; computes Virasoro, global, N=1 superconformal, torus character, and torus one-point blocks; applies SDP-based numerical bootstrap to derive bounds.

Result: Produces high-fidelity, low-pole conformal blocks usable in SDP; recovers scalar gap bounds approaching the Liouville theory window [(c−1)/16, (c−1)/12]; derives structured bounds on average exchanged dimension in identical-scalar four-point functions, with known 2D CFTs lying on or near the boundaries.

Conclusion: virasoro_blocks bridges analytic conformal block theory and numerical bootstrap practice by enabling robust, high-precision, pole-suppressed evaluation across diverse 2D CFT settings—including singular central charges—thereby sharpening bootstrap constraints and revealing new structural insights into the space of 2D CFTs.

Abstract: We introduce virasoro_blocks, software that computes Virasoro conformal blocks at any central charge in a form suitable for the numerical bootstrap. At each finite order in the elliptic nome, Zamolodchikov's recursion gives the block as a positive prefactor times a rational function of the exchanged weight. However, its many Kac poles make it difficult to use in semidefinite programming approaches to the bootstrap. In this software we use optimal interpolation to reduce the number of poles while keeping high accuracy. The software works in arbitrary precision and handles singular central charges, including the minimal models and $c=1$. It also computes global blocks, $N=1$ superconformal blocks, torus characters, and torus one-point blocks. We illustrate it with two applications. Assuming only scalar Virasoro primaries, we compute bounds on the scalar gap that approach the window of Liouville solutions between $(c-1)/16$ and $(c-1)/12$. We also bound the average exchanged dimension in four-point functions of identical scalars at several central charges. These bounds have a rich structure, and many known 2d CFTs lie on or near their boundary.

</details>


### [38] [Ortho-symplectic interface line operators in $\mathcal{N}=4$ SYM theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39207)
*Yasuyuki Hatsuda,Tadashi Okazaki*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates line operators at half-BPS interfaces in 4d N=4 SYM with orthogonal/symplectic gauge groups, classifies them for NS5- and D5-type interfaces, proposes S-duality between Wilson and vortex lines at interfaces, and verifies the duality via matching line defect half-indices.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the behavior and classification of line operators at half-BPS interfaces in 4d N=4 SYM with orthogonal and symplectic gauge groups, and to test S-duality predictions in this setting.

Method: Classification of line operators at NS5- and D5-type interfaces based on electric/magnetic charges; proposal of S-duality maps between Wilson and vortex lines; computation and comparison of line defect half-indices as non-perturbative evidence.

Result: Line operators are systematically labeled by electric/magnetic charges depending on interface type; S-duality conjectures between Wilson and vortex lines are formulated; matching of line defect half-indices provides strong evidence for the proposed dualities.

Conclusion: The classification and duality proposals are consistent with S-duality expectations; the agreement of half-indices supports the validity of the dualities at orthogonal/symplectic half-BPS interfaces in N=4 SYM.

Abstract: We study the line operators at the half-BPS interfaces in 4d $\mathcal{N}=4$ super Yang-Mills (SYM) theories of orthogonal and symplectic gauge groups. For the NS5-type interfaces, line operators are labeled by pairs of electric or magnetic charges under the gauge groups on either side of the interface, whereas for the D5-type interfaces they are labeled by electric or magnetic charges under the single gauge group preserved by the interface. We propose dualities between the Wilson lines and vortex lines at the interfaces under the action of S-duality. As highly non-trivial evidence of the dualities on the junctions of the line operators and the interfaces, we find matching of the line defect half-indices for the dual pairs.

</details>


### [39] [Schwinger--Keldysh worldline effective field theory for quantum black holes: dissipation, noise and decoherence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39243)
*Hajime Kobayashi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper develops a Schwinger-Keldysh worldline effective field theory for quantum black holes, unifying dissipation, fluctuations, and decoherence in a real-time influence functional; it derives Planck-suppressed stochastic forces for Schwarzschild binaries, ensures momentum conservation, reproduces known horizon-induced decoherence scaling, and outlines extensions to non-equilibrium and microscopic quantum structure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a unified real-time framework for quantum gravitational open-system effects—specifically dissipation, fluctuations, and decoherence—in black hole physics, bridging semiclassical gravity with quantum measurement and noise in curved spacetime.

Method: Schwinger–Keldysh worldline effective field theory with a real-time influence functional; causal response and symmetric noise kernels constrained by positivity and Kubo–Martin–Schwinger (KMS) symmetry; Hubbard–Stratonovich transformation to represent noise as Gaussian stochastic sourcing; absorptive matching to horizon physics; application to post-Newtonian Schwarzschild binary and spatial superposition decoherence.

Result: Derivation of complete relative-acceleration noise at leading post-Newtonian order that is Planck-suppressed, respects center-of-mass momentum conservation, and encodes both direct and metric-mediated stochastic forces; recovery of Danielson–Satishchandran–Wald horizon-induced decoherence scaling for quadrupolar superpositions; demonstration that response and noise remain independent matching data beyond thermal equilibrium.

Conclusion: The Schwinger–Keldysh worldline EFT provides a consistent, causal, and thermodynamically compliant framework for quantum black hole dynamics, enabling systematic treatment of noise and decoherence from horizons and supporting future extensions to quantum microstructure and gravitational interferometry.

Abstract: We formulate a Schwinger--Keldysh worldline effective field theory for quantum black holes that treats dissipation, fluctuations, and decoherence within one real-time influence functional. The reduced dynamics is encoded in causal response and symmetric noise kernels. Their spectra obey positivity constraints, while thermal Kubo--Martin--Schwinger symmetry relates them through the fluctuation--dissipation relation. A Hubbard--Stratonovich representation turns the noise kernel into a Gaussian source driving the resolved metric and worldline. For a leading post-Newtonian Schwarzschild binary, direct and metric-mediated stochastic forces combine into the complete relative-acceleration noise at this order, preserve center-of-mass momentum balance, and remain Planck suppressed. The same noise kernel gives the quadrupolar decoherence functional for a supported spatial superposition and reproduces the Danielson--Satishchandran--Wald horizon-induced scaling after absorptive matching. Away from thermal equilibrium, response and noise remain independent matching data. We discuss extensions to microscopic quantum structure in the worldline kernels, matching to spatially extended coarse-grained descriptions, and gravitational splitting.

</details>


### [40] [Quantum field theory with scale-dependent weighted measures: nonlinear momentum representations, vacuum fluctuations, and causal structure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39285)
*S. González-Salud,R. Cartas-Fuentevilla*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper revisits free quantum field construction using momentum-space weighted measures with an independent energy scale, yielding a family of theories parameterized by a dimensionless spectral function; it includes the Euclidean Snyder algebra case, derives key quantum field properties (equal-time algebra, vacuum fluctuations, two-point functions), establishes UV finiteness conditions, analyzes nonlocal causality via convolution of the Pauli-Jordan function, and provides an interacting extension and numerical illustrations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize free quantum field theory beyond standard momentum-space measures by introducing an independent energy scale and spectral weighting, thereby accommodating Planck-scale modifications—such as those from quantum gravity or noncommutative geometry—while recovering standard QFT at low energies.

Method: Constructs free quantum fields via momentum-space integration weighted by a spectral function dependent on a new energy scale; employs observer-covariant timelike four-velocity to define Lorentz-covariant weights; computes equal-time commutators, conserved observables, vacuum expectation values, and two-point functions for the complex Klein-Gordon field in arbitrary spatial dimension; analyzes UV behavior and causal structure through convolution of the Pauli-Jordan distribution.

Result: Derives explicit expressions for the equal-time algebra, conserved weighted charges, vacuum fluctuations, and translation-invariant two-point functions; identifies necessary and sufficient ultraviolet conditions for finiteness of these quantities; reveals a nonlocal causal structure encoded in a spatial convolution kernel; recovers Euclidean Snyder algebra as a special case; provides numerical profiles of kernels and amplitudes and sketches an interacting extension.

Conclusion: Momentum-space spectral weighting with an independent energy scale provides a consistent, flexible framework for deformed free quantum fields that maintains covariance, enables UV regularization, modifies causality nonlocally, and naturally incorporates structures from quantum spacetime models like Snyder space.

Abstract: We reconsider the construction of free quantum fields through momentum-space weighted measures, introducing an independent energy scale that restores the conventional theory at low momenta. The resulting family is characterized by a dimensionless spectral function and includes, as a distinguished case, the spatial weight associated with the Euclidean Snyder algebra. We discuss its relation to modified dispersion relations and nonlinear momentum coordinates, and introduce an observer-covariant spectral weight defined using a timelike four-velocity. For the complex Klein-Gordon field we obtain the equal-time algebra, conserved weighted observables, vacuum fluctuations, and two-point functions in arbitrary spatial dimension. The ultraviolet conditions for their finiteness are established, and the nonlocal causal structure is analyzed in terms of a spatial convolution of the ordinary Pauli-Jordan distribution. An illustrative interacting extension and numerical profiles of the kernels and amplitudes are also presented.

</details>


### [41] [Does SO(2,2) symmetry lead to an additional U(1) symmetry in 5-dimensional Einstein-scalar theories?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39400)
*Saeedeh Sadeghian*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper challenges a conjecture about the uniqueness of SO(2,2)-symmetric solutions in 5D Einstein-scalar gravity by constructing an explicit counterexample using Lie symmetry analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the conjecture in Sadeghian et al. (2014) that SO(2,2) symmetry plus a scalar field in five dimensions implies an additional U(1) symmetry (and hence uniqueness), which remains unproven.

Method: Lie symmetry method for solving partial differential equations applied to the 5D Einstein-scalar system with SO(2,2) symmetry.

Result: An explicit non-axisymmetric solution depending on both θ and ψ coordinates is constructed, violating the presumed U(1) symmetry and thus falsifying the conjecture.

Conclusion: The conjectured U(1) enhancement and associated uniqueness of SO(2,2)-invariant 5D Einstein-scalar solutions does not hold in general; symmetry enhancement is not guaranteed in the presence of a scalar field.

Abstract: Solving Einstein equations including the matter fields in dimensions higher than four is computationally challenging. However, the existence of symmetries facilitates these calculations. Among these symmetries, we can mention the SO(2,2) symmetry that arises in the near-horizon geometries of extremal black holes with vanishing horizon area in four dimensions \cite{EVH/CFT} and higher dimensions \cite{Sadeghian:2015}. Solutions of Einstein theories including scalar and Maxwell fields with SO(2,2) symmetry have been classified in four, five, and higher dimensions \cite{Sadeghian:2014}. For the Einstein-Maxwell-Lambda theory, a theorem states that SO(2,2) symmetry leads to an additional U(1) symmetry and therefore unique axisymmetric solutions. Nevertheless, in the presence of a scalar field, this symmetry group is not large enough in five dimensions to guarantee solution uniqueness, and the additional U(1) symmetry remains a conjecture in \cite{Sadeghian:2014}. Here, we study the Einstein equations of theories with a five-dimensional scalar field using the Lie symmetry method for solving partial differential equations. We challenge the conjecture in \cite{Sadeghian:2014} and find a solution that depends on both $\theta$ and $\psi$ coordinates as a counterexample to that conjecture.

</details>


### [42] [Carrollian CFTs and the Light Front](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39450)
*Arjun Bagchi,Kinjal Banerjee,Sourish Banerjee,Meghaa AK*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a bridge between 4d lightfront physics in asymptotically flat spacetimes and 3d Carrollian CFTs via the isomorphism between 4d Poincaré and 3d conformal Carroll algebras, constructs new Carroll CFT representations relevant for massive bulk scattering, and computes two-point functions to map between the two frameworks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify lightfront descriptions of asymptotically flat spacetimes with Carrollian holography by exploiting the algebraic isomorphism between 4d Poincaré and 3d conformal Carroll algebras, and to extend Carroll CFTs to describe massive scattering.

Method: Leverages Lie algebra isomorphism between 4d Poincaré and 3d conformal Carroll algebras; constructs novel unitary representations of Carroll CFTs inspired by lightfront quantization; computes Carroll-invariant two-point correlation functions; establishes an explicit operator/state map between 3d Carroll CFT data and 4d lightfront fields in AFS.

Result: New massive representations of 3d Carroll CFTs are constructed; Carroll-invariant two-point functions are derived; a concrete dictionary mapping 3d Carroll CFT operators to 4d lightfront fields in asymptotically flat spacetimes is established.

Conclusion: The isomorphism between 4d Poincaré and 3d conformal Carroll algebras provides a rigorous foundation for identifying lightfront dynamics in asymptotically flat spacetimes with Carrollian CFTs, enabling a holographic description of massive scattering amplitudes.

Abstract: Carrollian symmetries play an important role in building holographic duals to asymptotically flat spacetimes (AFS). This construction hinges on the isomorphism between the (asymptotic) isometries of AFS and the conformal Carroll algebra. Carroll symmetries also naturally arise on the lightfront, as co-dimension one subalgebras in the Poincare algebra written in lightcone coordinates. In this paper, building on the isomorphism between four dimensional (4d) Poincare and 3d conformal Carroll algebras, we make a connection between physics on the lightfront and Carrollian CFTs. Using insights from the lightfront literature, we construct new representations of Carroll CFTs that connect to massive bulk scattering. We compute two-point functions and build a map between 3d Carroll CFTs and 4d lightfront theories living in AFS.

</details>


### [43] [Large-$N$ limit of chiral quiver theories: free energy, supersymmetric indices and operator counting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39569)
*Edoardo Colombo,Hyojoong Kim,Nakwoo Kim,Hoseob Shin*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: The paper presents the first analytic solution to the large-N saddle point equations for the S^3 free energy of a chiral N=2 Chern-Simons-matter theory with an M-theory dual, resolving long-range force cancellation and confirming N^{3/2} scaling; it matches holographic predictions for all trial R-charges and extends to superconformal and topologically twisted indices, supporting the operator counting conjecture beyond non-chiral cases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the long-standing tension between matrix model long-range forces and the expected N^{3/2} scaling of the free energy in chiral N=2 Chern-Simons-matter theories with M-theory duals, and to test the operator counting conjecture beyond non-chiral quivers.

Method: Analytic solution of large-N saddle point equations for the S^3 free energy; analysis of long-range force cancellation in the matrix model; extension to superconformal and topologically twisted indices on S^2×S^1; finite-N numerical validation across multiple R-charge assignments.

Result: Exact analytic large-N free energy matching holographic prediction for all trial R-charges; confirmation of N^{3/2} scaling via long-range force cancellation; agreement with operator counting method; consistent behavior in Cardy and large-N limits of indices; numerical support at finite N.

Conclusion: The analytic solution confirms consistency of chiral quiver gauge theories with their M-theory duals, validates the operator counting conjecture for chiral cases, and establishes universality of the saddle point governing multiple supersymmetric observables.

Abstract: We find the first analytic solution to the large-$N$ saddle point equations for the $S^3$ free energy of a chiral $\mathcal N=2$ Chern-Simons-matter theory with an M-theory dual. Focusing on the chiral quiver dual to AdS$_4\times Q^{1,1,1}/\mathbb Z_{\rm k}$, we show how the long-range forces of the matrix model cancel at large $N$, resolving a long-standing tension with the expected $N^{3/2}$ scaling. The resulting free energy precisely matches the holographic prediction for all assignments of trial R-charges. Our results extend to the superconformal and topologically twisted indices on $S^2\times S^1$, whose large-$N$ and Cardy limit is determined by the same saddle. The solution agrees with the predictions of the operator counting method, giving strong evidence for this conjecture beyond non-chiral quivers. We support the analytic solution with a finite-$N$ numerical analysis for multiple assignments of R-charges, extending recent numerical results obtained for this quiver.

</details>


### [44] [Superstring Field Theory with Double Background Independence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39594)
*Chris Hull*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends a recently constructed superstring field theory by introducing a symmetric formulation between two decoupled sectors—physical and shadow superstrings—and generalizes it to allow independent background CFTs and marginal deformations for each sector.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve background dependence in superstring field theory and enable consistent treatment of multiple string sectors with independent conformal backgrounds.

Method: Constructs a symmetric action for the two-sector superstring field theory and generalizes it using separate background conformal field theories, ensuring invariance under independent marginal deformations of each background.

Result: A generalized superstring field theory is obtained where physical and shadow sectors can be expanded around distinct CFT backgrounds, and the full theory remains invariant under marginal deformations of either background independently.

Conclusion: The generalized theory achieves greater flexibility and background independence, providing a framework for studying dualities and sector-coupling mechanisms in superstring field theory without shared-background constraints.

Abstract: A recently constructed superstring field theory has two decoupled sectors, the physical superstring and a shadow sector with a second copy of the superstring. These are both expanded around the same background conformal field theory and the theory is independent of marginal deformations of this common background. A form of the action is presented that is symmetric between the two sectors. A generalisation of this theory is given in which the two sectors can be expanded around two different backgrounds, and the theory is independent under separate marginal deformations of the two background conformal field theories.

</details>


### [45] [Majorana Neutrino Physics and Matter-Antimatter Asymmetry in Type IIA String Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39804)

Main category: hep-th
