<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To realize Majorana neutrino masses and the observed baryon asymmetry (via leptogenesis) within a concrete, consistent quantum gravity framework—specifically, Type IIA string theory—while respecting swampland constraints and matching low-energy phenomenology.

Method: Constructs a semi-realistic $T^6/(\\mathbb{Z}_2 \\times \\mathbb{Z}_2)$ orientifold compactification with intersecting D6-branes; identifies Euclidean D2-brane instantons carrying Sp(2) Chan-Paton symmetry as the source of both the dimension-5 Weinberg operator and the right-handed neutrino mass term for the seesaw mechanism; fixes flavour vectors by phenomenological matching (not full CFT computation); applies swampland constraints (e.g., on instanton action and reheating temperature); performs parameter scans and benchmark analysis.

Result: The model realizes seesaw-dominated neutrino masses with normal ordering; reproduces measured $\\Delta m^2_{21}, \\Delta m^2_{31}$; yields baryon asymmetry $\\eta_B \\approx 8.6 \\times 10^{-11}$ (within factor ~3 of observed value); predicts effective Majorana mass $\\langle m_{\\beta\\beta} \\rangle = 2.594\\,\\text{meV}$; shows mass hierarchy is accommodated but not uniquely predicted.

Conclusion: A self-consistent, string-derived origin for neutrino masses and leptogenesis is possible in this orientifold setup, serving as an existence proof; however, predictive power is limited by reliance on matching rather than full top-down computation, and the mass hierarchy remains input rather than output.

Abstract: We study Majorana neutrino masses and the matter-antimatter asymmetry in Type IIA string theory on a $T^6/(\mathbb{Z}_2 \times \mathbb{Z}_2)$ orientifold with intersecting D6-branes. Euclidean D2-instantons with $\text{Sp}(2)$ Chan-Paton symmetry generate both the seesaw mechanism and the Weinberg operator. The flavour vectors are not computed from a compactification but fixed by matching, so our results are an existence proof. Matching the measured mass-squared differences selects the seesaw-dominated case with normal ordering. Swampland criteria constrain the instanton action and the reheating temperature. A benchmark example reproduces the neutrino parameters, gives $\eta_B \approx 8.6 \times 10^{-11}$, within an order of magnitude of observation, and predicts $\langle m_{\beta \beta} \rangle =2.594 \text{meV}$. A parameter scan shows the mass hierarchy is accommodated rather than predicted.

</details>


### [46] [On functional classes in higher-spin theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39851)
*Yu. A. Tatarenko*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a rigorous functional-space framework for the higher-spin algebra A and its twisted adjoint module A^{tw} in d-dimensional Vasiliev theory, enabling a well-defined free-level factorization over ideals; it links admissible function classes to locality—showing spin-local expressions are finite while certain non-local ones diverge due to A^{tw} properties.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguities in ideal factorization within Vasiliev's higher-spin gravity by providing a precise functional realization of the higher-spin algebra A and its twisted adjoint module A^{tw}, thereby addressing foundational issues tied to star-product closure, module structure, and locality.

Method: Imposes algebraic consistency conditions: closure of A under the Moyal star product, module compatibility of A^{tw} under star action, and auxiliary analytic constraints; identifies admissible function spaces satisfying these, drawing on Soloviev's prior work for A, and analyzes convergence/divergence behavior of local vs. non-local expressions in this setting.

Result: A properly defined free-level factorization procedure over ideals is achieved; admissible function classes for A and A^{tw} are characterized by simple algebraic conditions; divergence of certain non-local expressions is attributed to structural properties of A^{tw}, whereas spin-local expressions remain well-defined and finite.

Conclusion: The functional-space characterization of A and A^{tw} provides a mathematically sound basis for ideal factorization in Vasiliev theory and clarifies the locality problem: spin-locality is sufficient for finiteness, while generic non-locality leads to divergence—highlighting the critical role of module structure in quantum consistency.

Abstract: The factorization over the ideal in the $d$-dimensional Vasiliev theory requires an accurate description of the higher-spin algebra $\mathcal{A}$ and its twisted adjoint module $\mathcal{A}^{tw}$ as certain functional spaces. It is shown that the conditions of $\mathcal{A}$ to be closed under action of the Moyal star product, and of $\mathcal{A}^{tw}$ to be a star-product $\mathcal{A}$-module along with some auxiliary conditions lead to properly defined free-level factorization procedure. The conditions on admissible functions have simple algebraic form; in the case of $\mathcal{A}$ the analogous functional space has been considered by Soloviev \cite{Soloviev:2013iba}. The relation of the obtained functional classes to the locality problem in the Vasiliev theory is discussed. In particular it is observed that certain non-local expressions diverge due to properties of $\mathcal{A}^{tw}$ while the spin-local expressions are well-defined.

</details>


### [47] [Initial conditions leave IR imprints in quantum cosmology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39965)
*Manishankar Ailiga,Akash Mondal,Gaurav Narain*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the one-loop wavefunction of the Universe for ℝ×S³ geometries in Lorentzian signature, analyzing how an infinite family of covariant boundary actions—parameterized by c and ω—affect saddle-point structure, perturbative stability, UV regularization, and IR sensitivity of the gravitational path integral.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguities in the gravitational path integral's boundary conditions for cosmological wavefunctions, particularly how covariant boundary terms influence saddle selection, perturbative consistency, and UV/IR behavior of the wavefunction on ℝ×S³.

Method: Lorentzian gravitational path integral quantization on ℝ×S³; identification of lapse-scale-factor-fluctuation configuration space; imposition of boundary conditions via infinite-family covariant boundary actions parameterized by (c, ω); saddle-point analysis of lapse and scale factor; one-loop determinant computation with careful treatment of UV divergences and pole shifting; counterterm renormalization for UV finiteness; infrared asymptotics analysis.

Result: Dominant initial momenta constrain ω to half-integers; four c-dependent dominant conjugate momentum branches emerge—two de Sitter, one Hartle-Hawking, one tunnelling; only dS and HH saddles admit well-behaved perturbations; c acts as a UV regulator and shifts poles of the one-loop determinant off the real q_f axis for dS; UV-finite no-boundary and dS wavefunctions obtained via counterterms; IR-divergences and boundary-choice sensitivity revealed.

Conclusion: The choice of covariant boundary action—encoded in c and ω—critically determines viable semiclassical saddles, perturbative stability, UV regularization, and IR structure of the Lorentzian wavefunction; the parameter c plays a dual role as regulator and pole shifter, establishing a concrete link between boundary conditions and quantum cosmological predictions.

Abstract: We investigate the gravitational path integral of $\mathbb{R} \times S^3$ geometries in Lorentzian signature along with the metric fluctuations which live on the $S^3$ hypersurfaces. Each metric configuration is characterised by lapse, scale factor and fluctuations, and is weighted by the exponential of the Einstein-Hilbert action. A particular boundary requirement for the scale-factor is achievable via an infinite family of covariant boundary actions, each restricting the boundary behaviour of the fluctuation field which cannot be arbitrarily chosen. The paper explores the effect of this infinite family of covariant boundary actions on the wavefunction of Universe to one-loop. These boundary actions are characterized by parameters $c$ and $\omega$. Dominant contribution of initial momentum in the path-integral restricts $\omega$ to be half-integer, leading to an infinite family of $c$-dependent conjugate momenta. In special case for fixed $\omega$, four such $c$-dependent dominant branches of conjugate momenta emerge: two lead to deSitter, one leads to Hartle-Hawking and one to the tunnelling Universe. For all these $c$-dependent initial conjugate momenta, the lapse-saddles are relevant, however only, one of deSitter saddle and Hartle Hawking saddle have well-behaved perturbations. The one-loop wavefunction at these well-behaved saddles is computed systematically by isolating the UV-divergences, where the covariant boundary parameter $c$ acts as a UV-regulator and also helps in shifting the poles of one-loop determinant, away from the real $q_f$ axis for dS saddles, where $q_f$ is the final size of the Universe. UV-finite wavefunction for no-boundary and dS is obtained by adding suitable counterterms. The infrared behaviour of these wavefunctions show not only IR-divergences but also sensitivity toward the initial boundary choices.

</details>


### [48] [$\mathcal{N}=4$ Super Yang-Mills Flux Tube in AdS](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40156)
*Victor Gorbenko,Orr Sela,Brian R. Zielazienski*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies a supersymmetric Wilson line on the boundary of N=4 SYM in AdS₄ as a holographic model for confining flux tubes, bridging weak/strong coupling regimes via defect spectrum and superconformal index analysis, and proposes an operator map identifying the worldsheet axion with a two-particle state—supporting smooth spectral interpolation from gauge theory to string worldsheet.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a non-integrable yet supersymmetric holographic model that interpolates between perturbative gauge theory and confining string dynamics, by deforming N=4 SYM with a boundary (half-space) that breaks conformal symmetry but preserves enough supersymmetry to constrain dynamics and model flux tubes.

Method: Analyzes the defect CFT defined by a supersymmetric Wilson line on the boundary of AdS₄; computes the defect spectrum at weak and strong coupling in the planar limit; evaluates the superconformal index; establishes a map between boundary gauge-theory operators and bulk worldsheet excitations of the dual holographic string.

Result: A consistent operator map is established, notably identifying the worldsheet axion as a two-particle bound state at strong coupling; the spectrum smoothly interpolates from exponential growth (gauge-theory regime) to Cardy-like growth (string worldsheet regime); integrability is broken while SUSY remains sufficient to control dynamics.

Conclusion: The boundary-deformed N=4 SYM with a supersymmetric Wilson line provides a controllable, non-integrable, supersymmetric framework for studying spectral reorganization in confining theories, realizing a holographic duality between defect operators and string worldsheet degrees of freedom.

Abstract: We study a supersymmetric Wilson line on the boundary of $\mathcal{N}=4$ super Yang-Mills theory in AdS$_4$, or equivalently on a half-space, as a model for exploring the connection between holographic strings and confining flux tubes. The boundary breaks four-dimensional conformal symmetry and is expected to remove integrability, bringing this system closer to a confining string while preserving enough supersymmetry to constrain its dynamics. Working in the planar limit, we analyze the defect spectrum at weak and strong coupling and compute the superconformal index, thereby relating the perturbative gauge-theory description to the worldsheet excitations of a long holographic string. These complementary perspectives allow us to propose a map between operators in the two regimes, under which the worldsheet axion, in particular, becomes a two-particle state at strong coupling. The resulting picture supports a smooth interpolation from an exponentially growing gauge-theory spectrum to the Cardy-like spectrum of a string worldsheet, providing a non-integrable supersymmetric model for investigating the spectral reorganization expected in confining gauge theories.

</details>


### [49] [A density calculus for quantum field theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40174)
*Tanay Kibe,Pratik Roy*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces Haagerup noncommutative $L^p$ spaces to quantum field theorists, extending density matrix calculus—including fractional powers and Schatten norms—to type III von Neumann algebras lacking a trace; it constructs these spaces via crossed products, clarifies Haagerup densities and the canonical functional for expectations, and applies them to compute sandwiched Rényi divergence for chiral current coherent states—verifying monotonicity and the Rényi quantum null energy condition for all $\alpha > 1$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To equip quantum field theorists with tools to handle noncommutative $L^p$ spaces in type III settings (e.g., local algebras in QFT), where standard trace-based methods fail due to absence of an intrinsic trace.

Method: Constructs Haagerup $L^p$ spaces first concretely for matrix algebras—explicitly exhibiting the crossed product, its trace, and dual action—then generalizes to arbitrary von Neumann algebras; emphasizes representation of normal states by Haagerup densities and distinguishes the crossed-product trace from the canonical Haagerup functional used for expectation values.

Result: Explicit computation of sandwiched Rényi divergence for coherent excitations of a chiral current in 2D CFT; verification of monotonicity under restriction and satisfaction of the Rényi quantum null energy condition for all real $\alpha > 1$.

Conclusion: Haagerup $L^p$ spaces provide a rigorous, computationally tractable framework for information-theoretic quantities in algebraic QFT with type III algebras, enabling concrete calculations and verification of fundamental physical principles like energy conditions and data processing inequalities.

Abstract: These notes provide an introduction to Haagerup noncommutative $L^p$ spaces for quantum field theorists. The aim is to explain how the familiar calculus of density matrices, fractional powers, and Schatten norms extends to local observable algebras of type~III, where an intrinsic trace is unavailable. We develop the construction first for a matrix algebra, making the crossed product, its trace, and the dual action explicit, before passing to general von Neumann algebras. Particular attention is paid to the representation of normal states by Haagerup densities and to the distinction between the crossed-product trace and the canonical Haagerup functional used to compute expectation values. As a concrete application, we calculate the sandwiched Rényi divergence for a coherent excitation of a chiral current, verifying monotonicity under restriction and the Rényi quantum null energy condition at every real order $\alpha>1$. The presentation assumes familiarity with quantum mechanics and elementary quantum field theory, while introducing the operator-algebraic concepts needed for the construction and calculation.

</details>


### [50] [Quantum curves of 6d SCFTs on a -3 curve](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40203)
*Minsung Kim,Sung-Soo Kim,Xin Wang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs quantum Seiberg-Witten curves as elliptic difference equations for half-BPS defects in 6d N=(1,0) SCFTs on a −3 curve, using defect partition functions on ℝ⁴×T² computed via generalized blowup equations; applied to su(3), g₂, so(N) theories and their Higgsing trees, plus Z₂-twisted circle compactification of su(3).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically incorporate half-BPS defects into the quantum Seiberg-Witten geometry of 6d N=(1,0) SCFTs—particularly those engineered by −3 curves—and extend the Nekrasov–Shatashvili framework to elliptic (i.e., T²-dependent) settings with codimension-2 and -4 defects.

Method: Computes defect partition functions on ℝ⁴×T² using generalized blowup equations; takes the Nekrasov–Shatashvili limit (ħ→0 with one Ω-background parameter fixed); identifies the resulting holomorphic blocks as solutions to elliptic difference equations—the proposed quantum Seiberg-Witten curves.

Result: Quantum Seiberg-Witten curves are formulated as explicit elliptic difference equations for minimal su(3), g₂, and so(N) theories linked via Higgsing trees; the Z₂-twisted compactification of su(3) yields a distinct quantum curve reflecting orbifold monodromy.

Conclusion: Defect partition functions encode quantum-corrected Seiberg-Witten geometry in elliptic integrable systems; the framework unifies defect physics, blowup equations, and quantum spectral curves across a class of 6d SCFTs and their lower-dimensional descendants.

Abstract: We study half-BPS defects and quantum Seiberg-Witten curves in 6d $ \mathcal{N}=(1,0) $ SCFTs on a $ -3 $ curve. We compute the partition functions in the presence of codimension-4 and codimension-2 defects on $ \mathbb{R}^4 \times T^2 $ using generalized blowup equations. We then propose quantum Seiberg-Witten curves as elliptic difference equations satisfied by the Nekrasov-Shatashvili limits of the defect partition functions. We apply this construction to the minimal $ \mathfrak{su}(3) $ gauge theory, and to the $ \mathfrak{g}_2 $ and $ \mathfrak{so}(N) $ gauge theories on a Higgsing tree whose endpoint is the minimal $ \mathfrak{su}(3) $ theory. We also investigate defects and the quantum curve of the $ \mathfrak{su}(3) $ gauge theory compactified on a circle with a $ \mathbb{Z}_2 $ twist.

</details>


### [51] [Competition and coexistence of baryonic condensates in AdS hardwall model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40244)
*Akash Singh*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates coexistence vs. mutual exclusion of diquark and dibaryon condensates in cold dense QCD using a backreacted 5D AdS hardwall model with two charged scalars coupled to a baryon-number U(1)_B gauge field.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether multiple baryon-number breaking order parameters (diquark and dibaryon condensates) can coexist or are mutually exclusive in cold dense QCD.

Method: Five-dimensional AdS hardwall model with full gravitational and gauge backreaction; two charged scalar fields dual to operators with baryon charges q_i and scaling dimensions Δ_i; systematic variation of Δ₂ and q₂ while fixing q₁=2, Δ₁=3; analysis across confined/deconfined phases in the μ_B–T plane.

Result: Diquark and dibaryon condensates coexist only within a narrow window of Δ₂, which widens with increasing q₂ and vanishes when the two operators become identical; outside this window, only one condensate forms; coexistence persists in both confined and deconfined phases; at low T, confined phase with both condensates is thermodynamically preferred, separated by a narrow band of deconfined phase with single condensate.

Conclusion: Coexistence of diquark and dibaryon condensates is possible but highly constrained in parameter space and phase structure; their competition is governed by operator quantum numbers (charge and dimension), and thermodynamic preference depends on temperature and confinement status.

Abstract: Cold dense QCD admits several order parameters for baryon-number breaking, among them diquark and dibaryon condensates. We ask whether they exclude one another or coexist, using a five-dimensional AdS hardwall model with full backreaction in which two charged scalars, dual to operators of charges $q_i$ and dimensions $\Delta_i$, couple to a common $U(1)_B$ gauge field. With the diquark-like operator fixed at $q_1=2$, $\Delta_1=3$, both operators condense only within a narrow window of $\Delta_2$, which widens with $q_2$ and closes when the operators coincide. Outside the window, only a single condensate forms. The structure persists in both confined and deconfined phases. In the $\mu_B$-$T$ plane, confined matter with both condensates is preferred at low temperature, below a narrow band of deconfined matter with one condensate.

</details>


### [52] [$\mathcal{N}=2$ conformal higher-spin actions in harmonic superspace](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40274)
*Evgeny Ivanov,Nikita Zaigraev*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs linearized 4D, N=2 Weyl-type off-shell actions in harmonic superspace for superconformal higher-spin gauge multiplets, building on prior work on N=2 supergravity; it establishes superconformal and linearized gauge invariance, introduces geometric principles—Grassmann harmonic analyticity for prepotentials/transformations and Grassmann half-analyticity for invariant actions/super-Weyl tensors—and shows that consistent linearized spin-s gauge invariance on generic N=2 supergravity backgrounds requires Weyl-flatness.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the geometric harmonic superspace framework from N=2 conformal supergravity to superconformal higher-spin gauge theories, enabling systematic construction of off-shell, gauge- and superconformally invariant actions and laying groundwork for nonlinear generalizations.

Method: Harmonic superspace formalism with Grassmann harmonic analyticity (for defining prepotentials and their gauge/superconformal transformations) and Grassmann half-analyticity (for constructing invariant actions and generalized N=2 super-Weyl tensors); verification of linearized gauge invariance and superconformal invariance; analysis of consistency of higher-spin gauge symmetry on arbitrary N=2 supergravity backgrounds.

Result: Explicit linearized off-shell Weyl-type actions for N=2 superconformal higher-spin multiplets; full characterization of prepotential structure and transformation laws via Grassmann harmonic analyticity; invariant action construction and super-Weyl tensor definition via Grassmann half-analyticity; proof that linearized spin-s gauge invariance holds only on N=2 Weyl-flat super-backgrounds without modifying gauge transformations or harmonic zero-curvature equations.

Conclusion: The harmonic superspace formulation provides a robust, geometrically transparent framework for linearized N=2 superconformal higher-spin theories, unifying structural features under analyticity principles and constraining viable backgrounds—pointing toward nonlinear extensions and deeper connections between superconformal symmetry, geometry, and higher-spin dynamics.

Abstract: We construct linearized $4D, \mathcal{N}=2$ Weyl-type off-shell actions in harmonic superspace for the superconformal higher-spin gauge multiplets introduced in arXiv:2404.19016. This work is a followup of our arXiv:2511.16325, where an analogous construction was carried out for conformal $\mathcal{N}=2$ supergravity. We check the superconformal invariance of the new actions and their linearized gauge invariance. As the characteristic features of our formulation, the structure of the underlying prepotentials and their gauge and superconformal transformations are entirely determined by the fundamental \textit{principle of Grassmann harmonic analyticity}, while the construction of the invariant actions and of the generalized $\mathcal{N}=2$ super-Weyl tensors relies upon the \textit{principle of Grassmann half-analyticity}. This provides not only a new geometric framework for the linearized $\mathcal{N}=2$ superconformal gauge higher-spin theories, but also suggests possible ways of promoting them to the full nonlinear level. We consider a generalization to an arbitrary $\mathcal{N}=2$ supergravity background and show that the linearized spin-$\mathbf{s}$ gauge invariance, without radical modifications of the gauge transformation law and/or harmonic zero-curvature equations, is consistent only with $\mathcal{N}=2$ Weyl-flat super-backgrounds.

</details>


### [53] [Bounds on the Diagonal Degree of the Biadjoint Scalar Amplitude Matrix](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40355)
*Freddy Cachazo,Rajbir Longia*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper disproves the existence of a critical n_c beyond which the biadjoint scalar theory matrix M_n contains a full-rank diagonal submatrix; instead, the largest non-singular diagonal submatrix (diagonal degree Δ_n) is exponentially smaller than the CHY rank (n−3)!, satisfying exp(−1.5n) ≤ Δ_n/(n−3)! ≤ exp(−0.01n+O(1)).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether a critical n_c exists such that for all n > n_c, the biadjoint scalar matrix M_n contains a diagonal submatrix of maximal CHY rank (n−3)!, motivated by minimizing terms in the KLT formula.

Method: Analytical combinatorial and asymptotic analysis of the biadjoint scalar partial amplitude matrix M_n; derivation of rigorous exponential upper and lower bounds on the diagonal degree Δ_n — the size of the largest non-singular diagonal submatrix — using properties of orderings, permutation structure, and matrix singularity constraints.

Result: Proof that Δ_n/(n−3)! decays exponentially as n → ∞: specifically, exp(−1.5n) ≤ Δ_n/(n−3)! ≤ exp[−0.01n + O(1)]; thus no such critical n_c exists.

Conclusion: The conjectured existence of asymptotically full-rank diagonal submatrices in M_n is false; the diagonal degree is exponentially suppressed, fundamentally limiting simplifications of the KLT relation via diagonal selections.

Abstract: Biadjoint scalar theory partial amplitudes, $m_n(\alpha,\beta)$, define an $(n-1)!/2\times (n-1)!/2$ matrix, $M_n$, with rows and columns labeled by orderings. The CHY construction of $M_n$ implies that its rank is $(n-3)!$. Invertible submatrices of maximal rank enter in the KLT construction relating graviton amplitudes to gluon amplitudes. Motivated by finding the KLT formula with the smallest number of terms, one of the authors posed the question of whether there is a critical $n_c$ such that for all $n>n_c$ there exists a diagonal submatrix of $M_n$ of rank $(n-3)!$. In this work we obtain bounds on the size of the largest non-singular diagonal submatrix of $M_n$, which we call its diagonal degree $\Delta_n$, and we answer the question in the negative. We prove that the diagonal degree is exponentially suppressed relative to the CHY rank: $\Delta_n/(n-3)! = \exp(-\Theta (n))$ as $n\to \infty$. More explicitly, we establish the bounds $\exp(-1.5n)\leq\Delta_n/(n-3)!\leq \exp \left[ -0.01n+O(1)\right]$.

</details>


### [54] [Breaking of integrability in elliptic deformations of AdS3 strings with mixed flux](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40386)
*Ian Le Meur,Fiona K. Seibold*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs elliptic deformations of the AdS₃×S³×T⁴ superstring with mixed NSNS/RR fluxes in type IIB supergravity, finds that generic elliptic deformations break worldsheet integrability (due to inelastic 2→2 scattering), and shows that the Wess-Zumino term—integrability-preserving in rational/trigonometric cases—destroys integrability in the elliptic case.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically explore whether elliptic deformations of the AdS₃×S³×T⁴ superstring with mixed flux preserve classical integrability—a key property underlying exact solvability and AdS/CFT integrability—and to clarify the role of the Wess-Zumino term across different deformation classes.

Method: Constructs elliptic sigma models on deformed AdS₃ and S³ with B-field couplings and mixed NSNS/RR flux; analyzes integrability via existence of a flat Lax connection and computation of tree-level worldsheet S-matrix; examines elasticity of 2→2 scattering and checks satisfaction of the classical Yang-Baxter equation.

Result: Generic elliptic deformations yield inelastic 2→2 amplitudes, violating integrability; only special parameter choices (identified by Lax flatness) restore elasticity and satisfy the classical Yang-Baxter equation; crucially, the Wess-Zumino term—benign in rational/trigonometric limits—breaks integrability for elliptic deformations.

Conclusion: Elliptic deformations of AdS₃×S³×T⁴ with mixed flux are generically non-integrable at the classical worldsheet level; integrability is recovered only in restricted subsectors, and the Wess-Zumino term plays a qualitatively distinct, obstructive role in the elliptic case compared to rational or trigonometric ones.

Abstract: We construct elliptic deformations of the $AdS_3 \times S^3 \times T^4$ superstring supported by mixed NSNS and RR fluxes in type IIB supergravity and investigate their integrability. The NSNS sector includes a Wess-Zumino term, and imposing that the theory preserves 8 supersymmetries also requires the presence of a B-field coupling the deformed $AdS_3$ and $S^3$ factors. We explore integrability from two different perspectives: through the construction of a flat Lax connection and the computation of the tree-level worldsheet S-matrix. For generic elliptic parameters and NSNS flux, we find inelastic $2 \rightarrow 2$ amplitudes, violating the elasticity required by integrability. In the integrable cases as identified by the Lax analysis, these inelastic amplitudes vanish and the tree-level S-matrix satisfies the classical Yang-Baxter equation. In particular, the addition of a Wess-Zumino term, which preserves integrability in the rational and trigonometric models, breaks integrability for elliptic deformations.

</details>


### [55] [A Simplified Born Rule for Cosmological Correlators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40412)
*Fan Zhu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proves universal cancellation of double-boundary poles in equal-time in-in cosmological correlators in flat spacetime, leading to a simplified Born rule expressed via embedded wavefunction coefficients; for Tr(ϕ³) theory with conformal coupling, it introduces the Born polytope—a geometric object whose canonical function reproduces normalized correlators and enables a stringy integral representation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguities in the analytic structure of cosmological correlators arising from double-boundary poles in the in-in formalism, and to uncover deeper geometric and combinatorial structures underlying the Born rule in quantum cosmology.

Method: Analytic proof of double-boundary pole cancellation at the integrand level (prior to spatial loop integration); derivation of embedded wavefunction coefficients; construction of forest-cut expansion for Tr(ϕ³); definition and characterization of the Born polytope via its facet structure and canonical form; derivation of a stringy integral formula from polytope geometry.

Result: Universal cancellation of double-boundary poles for arbitrary graphs and local derivative interactions; formulation of a simplified Born rule in terms of embedded wavefunction coefficients; uniform forest-cut expansion for conformally coupled Tr(ϕ³); explicit construction of the Born polytope whose canonical function matches normalized correlators; emergence of a stringy formula for correlators from the same geometry.

Conclusion: The analytic structure of cosmological correlators is governed by robust combinatorial and geometric principles—encoded in the Born polytope—that unify wavefunction-based quantum cosmology with modern amplitudes methods, revealing a hidden simplicity beneath the in-in formalism.

Abstract: Cosmological correlators are natural observables of the primordial universe and are related to wavefunction coefficients by the Born rule. For equal-time in-in correlators in flat spacetime, we prove that all double-boundary poles cancel for arbitrary graphs, including in theories with local derivative interactions. These cancellations occur before integration over spatial loop momenta and yield a $\textit{simplified Born rule}$ in terms of what we call embedded wavefunction coefficients. The resulting expansion reveals which physical poles can occur together while keeping the interaction dependence explicit. For conformally coupled $\operatorname{Tr}(\phi^3)$ theory in flat spacetime, the simplified Born rule takes the form of a uniformly weighted forest-cut expansion. From its pole combinatorics, we construct what we call the $\textit{Born polytope}$, whose canonical function reproduces the normalized correlator for arbitrary graphs after matching its facet supports to the physical energies. The same geometry also yields a stringy formula for correlators.

</details>


### [56] [Generalised $Ω$-backgrounds in twisted $N=4$ super Yang-Mills](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40465)
*Elisa Iris Marieni,Itamar Yaakov*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces rigid supergravity backgrounds for 4d N=4 super Yang-Mills that generalize topological twists and the Omega-deformation, deriving twisted supersymmetry variations for the Weyl and matter multiplets across all three inequivalent topological twists.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the framework of topological twisting and Omega-deformation from N=2 to N=4 super Yang-Mills by constructing compatible rigid supergravity backgrounds.

Method: Derivation of twisted supersymmetry variations for the N=4 Weyl multiplet and matter multiplets coupled to rigid bosonic supergravity backgrounds, classified by the three inequivalent topological twists.

Result: Explicit twisted supersymmetry transformations for both Weyl and matter multiplets in each of the three topological twists; identification of generalized rigid supergravity backgrounds enabling deformations beyond standard topological twists.

Conclusion: The constructed backgrounds provide a unified geometric framework for deforming N=4 SYM analogous to the Omega-background in N=2 theories, enabling new exact computations and insights into the interplay between supersymmetry, topology, and deformation parameters.

Abstract: We introduce rigid supergravity backgrounds for 4d $\mathcal{N}=4$ super Yang-Mills which deform its topological twists and generalise the $\Omega$-deformation of $\mathcal{N}=2$ gauge theories. We derive the twisted supersymmetry variations of the $\mathcal{N}=4$ Weyl multiplet, and the $\mathcal{N}=4$ matter multiplet coupled to a rigid bosonic background, for each of the three inequivalent topological twists. Other, more exotic backgrounds are also discussed.

</details>


### [57] [Localization in Seven-Dimensional Supergravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40514)
*Florian Gaar,Michèle Levi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends relative equivariant localization to 7D maximal Cartan-gauged supergravity to compute supersymmetric observables—specifically the AdS₇ black-hole action—directly from fixed-point data on an auxiliary even-dimensional filling, bypassing explicit solution construction; it reformulates the Euclidean action and Killing-spinor equations using field strengths and spinor bilinears adapted to Majorana reality, incorporates massive 3-form self-duality to fix the anomaly class and higher-form fixed-point datum, and derives the entropy extremization condition (linear chemical-potential constraint + nonlinear charge relation) matching Lorentzian horizon extremality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the difficulty of solving 7D supergravity explicitly due to its unique spinor-bilinear geometry and massive higher-form dynamics, and to access holographic observables of 6D superconformal theories via systematic localization without requiring full bulk solutions.

Method: Extends relative equivariant localization to 7D maximal Cartan-gauged supergravity; formulates Euclidean action and Killing-spinor equations in terms of field strengths; constructs a Majorana-adapted bilinear system; uses massive 3-form self-duality to fix quartic anomaly class and determine higher-form fixed-point datum from Abelian moment maps; performs localization at a single fixed point of the auxiliary filling; proves gauge-covariant Killing-spinor invariance under combined supersymmetric Killing flow and R-symmetry; derives linear chemical-potential constraint and extremizes constrained entropy functional.

Result: Recovers the general supersymmetric AdS₇ black-hole action purely from fixed-point data without extending the solution; establishes gauge-covariant Killing-spinor invariance; derives the linear chemical-potential constraint and the nonlinear charge relation required for real positive stationary entropy, which is equivalent to Lorentzian horizon extremality for spherical black holes.

Conclusion: Relative equivariant localization is successfully generalized to 7D maximal supergravity, enabling efficient computation of supersymmetric observables—including black-hole thermodynamics—from algebraic and geometric fixed-point data, circumventing the need for explicit solutions while preserving physical consistency with Lorentzian extremality and anomaly constraints.

Abstract: Seven-dimensional supergravity provides holographic access to strongly coupled six-dimensional superconformal theories through its highest-dimensional supersymmetric AdS vacua. Its unique spinor-bilinear geometry and massive higher-form dynamics make systematic analysis and solution-finding distinctively challenging. Relative equivariant localization can obtain supersymmetric observables in odd dimensions from fixed-point data on an auxiliary even-dimensional filling, without an explicit solution. We extend this approach to seven dimensions in maximal Cartan-gauged supergravity with all gauge fields active. We formulate the Euclidean action and Killing-spinor equations in terms of field strengths, and construct a bilinear system adapted to the change in spinor reality conditions from symplectic-Majorana to Majorana. On the non-null gauged active spinor branch, independent, oppositely R-charged spinors are paired by Majorana conjugation on the same complex background. One algebraic gauging correction and two algebraic bilinear projections suffice to extend the equal-charge sector to independent Cartan charges, and the underlying efficient reorganization also applies in the Lorentzian theory. Massive 3-form self-duality fixes the quartic anomaly class and determines the higher-form fixed-point datum from Abelian moment maps. Localization at the single fixed point of the filling recovers the general supersymmetric AdS$_7$ black-hole action, without extending the solution. We prove gauge-covariant Killing-spinor invariance under the combined supersymmetric Killing flow and R-symmetry action, deriving the linear chemical-potential constraint. Extremizing the constrained entropy functional and requiring real positive stationary entropy yields the nonlinear charge relation within the Euclidean analysis, equivalent to Lorentzian horizon extremality for the known spherical black-hole family.

</details>


### [58] [Thermodynamics of Swirling Black Holes and Kerr-Taub-NUT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40529)
*Ernesto Frodden,Diego Hidalgo,Francisco Manzano*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes the first law of black hole thermodynamics for Kerr swirling (KS), accelerated Kerr swirling (AKS), and Kerr-Taub-NUT (KTN) black holes, using the surface charge method adapted for spacetimes with conical singularities; it shows entropy deviates from A/4 for KS/KTN due to string-like defects and topology, but conforms to A/4 for regularized AKS.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously derive the first law for black hole solutions with non-standard asymptotics and string-like defects (conical singularities), where conventional mass/angular momentum definitions and entropy formulas fail.

Method: Adapted surface charge method based on Noether’s theorem to compute quasi-local, finite mass and angular momentum for KS, KTN, and AKS spacetimes; avoids reliance on flat asymptotics or ad hoc stringy terms; cross-validates AKS mass via Christodoulou-Ruffini formula.

Result: First law holds for all three spacetimes; entropy equals A/4 only for defect-free AKS; for KS and KTN, entropy differs due to topological effects of conical singularities; integrated charges are finite and symmetry-derived without auxiliary string contributions.

Conclusion: Standard black hole thermodynamics—particularly the A/4 entropy rule—requires modification in spacetimes with string-like defects; a new geometrical framework accounting for topology and conserved charges is needed for such black holes.

Abstract: We establish the first law of black hole thermodynamics for the recently discovered Kerr swirling (KS) and accelerated Kerr swirling (AKS) black hole solutions. We also revisit the first law for the Kerr-Taub-NUT (KTN) spacetime. Guided by Noether's original insight that global symmetries give rise to conservation laws, we employ and adapt the surface charge method for computing the associated charges (mass and angular momentum) in the presence of spacetime string-like defects (conical singularities) along the axes of both KS and KTN black holes. We obtain finite integrated charges in a quasi-local manner, without relying on the non-flat asymptotic regions characteristic of these spacetimes. We then establish a first law without introducing independent stringy terms unrelated to symmetries. For both KS and KTN, the quantities that play the role of the black hole entropy differ from the simple geometrical rule of one-fourth of the horizon area. This mismatch arises because we keep first-law charges associated with conservation laws. It indicates that standard formulas may not be naively used for black holes with string-like defects; they have a different topology and suggest the need for a new geometrical framework. In stark contrast, for the AKS spacetime, which can be regularized outside the singularity and lacks string-like defects, we find that the charges are consistent with a first law having the usual one-fourth of the area entropy. We first determine the mass using the Christodoulou-Ruffini formula and then check its agreement with the result obtained from the surface charge method.

</details>


### [59] [Gluons and Knots](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.10673)
*Alfredo Guevara*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proposes a topological interpretation of the scattering amplitude for a single minus-helicity gluon with many plus-helicity gluons, linking it to 2D null Wilson loops, 3D positive transverse links, and HOMFLY-PT polynomial corners via skein and Reidemeister relations; it rigorously derives the formula using Lean-formalized DAGs and validates it against Catalan-number predictions in 'star' kinematics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover a precise topological and geometric structure underlying MHV-like gluon amplitudes, bridging scattering physics, knot theory, and quantum field/string theory.

Method: Interprets wall-crossing and the Weinberg soft theorem as skein relations and Reidemeister moves; constructs a state-counting description from corners of HOMFLY-PT polynomials; employs Lean-formalized dependency graph (DAG) for rigorous derivation, assuming Reidemeister III invariance.

Result: Derives a novel, topologically grounded formula for the amplitude; confirms exact agreement with Catalan numbers in 'star' kinematics; establishes a conjectured connection to topological field and string theory.

Conclusion: The single-minus gluon amplitude is topological—piecewise constant in kinematic chambers, governed by wall-crossing and Yangian symmetry—and admits a rigorous, knot-theoretic formulation validated both formally and numerically.

Abstract: We argue that the scattering amplitude of a single minus helicity gluon with arbitrarily many positive helicity gluons is topological. The amplitude is constant in chambers of kinematic space, its resonances are located at walls and its factorization is determined by wall-crossing. Yangian invariance dictates the amplitude to be determined by a 2d null Wilson loop that can be lifted into a positive and transverse 3d link. By reinterpreting wall-crossing and the Weinberg soft theorem as skein relations and Reidemeister moves of the subknots, we construct a state-counting description consisting of adding the `corners' of their HOMFLY-PT polynomials. Beyond the classical limit, we argue that this provides a conjectured but precise connection to topological field and string theory. The new formula is rigorously derived via a Lean-formalized dependency graph (DAG) as detailed in a companion paper, laying out the precise assumptions including Reidemeister III invariance. It is further tested exhaustively, including novel `star' kinematics where the amplitude and state count are shown to become Catalan numbers, in agreement with knot theory literature.

</details>

# gr-qc [[Back]](#toc)

### [60] [Validity Domains for Semiclassical Time in Quantum Cosmology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37696)
*Gianpaolo Rosati,Sergio L. Cacciatori*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper clarifies the distinct validity conditions for using semiclassical gravity as a temporal reference in quantum cosmology: gravitational WKB validity, Born–Oppenheimer adiabaticity on configuration space, and sharpness against quantum fluctuations—showing they define non-overlapping domains, even in the simplest FRW–scalar minisuperspace model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguities in defining a reliable semiclassical time variable in quantum gravity, particularly whether mere existence of an oscillatory WKB gravitational branch suffices for temporal reference—addressing conceptual conflations between WKB validity, adiabatic separation, and quantum decoherence.

Method: Formulates the Born–Oppenheimer adiabatic condition directly on gravitational configuration space via the Hamilton–Jacobi vector field (bypassing premature introduction of WKB time); computes and compares domain-specific control parameters—WKB parameter, configuration-space adiabatic parameter, and sharpness criterion—in a closed FRW minisuperspace with homogeneous massive scalar field; applies uniform Airy approximation at the turning point to resolve divergences and assess parametric scaling.

Result: Demonstrates explicit inequivalence: near the gravitational turning point, the standard WKB parameter diverges while the configuration-space adiabatic parameter vanishes as O(M^{-1/3}); Airy regularization removes the divergence but preserves the hierarchy—adiabatic control remains parametrically weaker. Hence D_t = D_WKB ∩ D_ad ∩ D_sharp is strictly smaller than any individual domain.

Conclusion: A semiclassical gravitational branch being oscillatory (WKB-valid) does not guarantee its suitability as a temporal reference; reliable WKB time requires simultaneous satisfaction of WKB validity, configuration-space adiabaticity, and quantum sharpness—making the intersection D_t the true domain of temporal reliability.

Abstract: We investigate the domain in which a semiclassical gravitational WKB flow can serve as a quantitatively controlled temporal reference for subordinate quantum degrees of freedom. Rather than introducing WKB time at the outset, we formulate the Born--Oppenheimer adiabatic condition directly on gravitational configuration space using the Hamilton--Jacobi vector field. This separates adiabatic control from the local WKB validity of the gravitational branch and from the sharpness of the candidate reference against quantum fluctuations. For a single selected semiclassical branch, these requirements define the validity domain $D_t = D_{\mathrm{WKB}} \cap D_{\mathrm{ad}} \cap D_{\mathrm{sharp}}$, with an additional decoherence condition when coherent branches this http URL a closed Friedmann--Robertson--Walker minisuperspace coupled to a homogeneous massive scalar mode, the distinction is explicit: approaching the gravitational turning point from the oscillatory region, the ordinary WKB control parameter diverges while the configuration-space adiabatic parameter tends to zero. A uniform Airy treatment of the turning point removes the formal WKB divergence while preserving the inequivalence: the corresponding local control parameter remains of order unity, whereas the configuration-space adiabatic parameter is parametrically suppressed as $O(M^{-1/3})$.Thus Born--Oppenheimer adiabaticity and gravitational WKB validity define inequivalent domains even within the simplest minisuperspace model. We conclude that the existence of an oscillatory semiclassical branch is not sufficient for a reliable WKB temporal description: semiclassical temporal reliability is instead determined by the overlap of the validity domains supporting the reference flow.

</details>


### [61] [Classical traversability of a carbon BTZ wormhole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37704)
*Valerie Tynianska,Alfredo Iorio*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies classical traversability of a 2D carbon membrane wormhole analog derived from a BTZ black hole via reflection-symmetric doubling; massless Dirac quasiparticles follow null geodesics, and traversability is governed by a sharp angular momentum threshold determined by the throat radius.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore classical traversability in a condensed-matter analogue of a spatial wormhole—realized on a curved graphene-like membrane—bypassing quantum gravity complications while retaining geometric fidelity.

Method: Constructs an ultrastatic, reflection-symmetric wormhole geometry from the exterior region of a non-rotating BTZ black hole; models massless Dirac quasiparticles as spinorial light rays following null geodesics; solves exact geodesic equations to analyze trajectory behavior near the throat.

Result: Identifies a binary traversability threshold set by conserved angular momentum relative to the minimum throat radius; reveals logarithmic divergence in angular evolution for near-critical trajectories, leading to multiple windings around the throat before escape.

Conclusion: Classical traversability in this analogue wormhole is purely geometric and deterministic, governed by null geodesic structure; the results provide a rigorous ray-optical foundation for future mesoscopic (e.g., transport or interferometric) experiments.

Abstract: We investigate the classical traversability of an analogue spatial wormhole geometry realized on a two-dimensional curved carbon membrane. The background is obtained by implementing a reflection-symmetric doubling technique on the exterior spatial sector of a non-rotating Bañados-Teitelboim-Zanelli black hole, effectively replacing the event horizon with a smooth traversable throat. Within this framework, massless Dirac quasiparticles propagate as spinorial light rays whose kinematics are governed exclusively by the metric curvature, forcing them to track the null geodesics of the ultrastatic spacetime. By evaluating the exact geodesic equations, we demonstrate that classical traversability is dictated by a sharp binary threshold in the conserved angular momentum, where the minimum throat radius acts as a centrifugal potential barrier that separates traversing paths from reflecting ones. We show that near-critical trajectories experience a logarithmic divergence in their angular evolution, causing the quasiparticles to execute multiple spatial windings around the throat before escaping. These classical ray-tracing configurations establish the necessary geometric backbones for future mesoscopic investigations.

</details>


### [62] [Thermodynamic and structural properties of the thin atmosphere of a white dwarf](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37708)
*Riccardo Fantoni*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes thermodynamic and structural properties of Coulomb plasma (jellium) in the thin atmosphere of a white dwarf using path integral Monte Carlo simulations embedded in a general relativistic stellar model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To accurately model the physical conditions—especially quantum and strong-coupling effects—in the dense, low-temperature, high-gravity atmospheres of white dwarfs, where standard approximations fail.

Method: Path integral Monte Carlo (PIMC) method coupled with a general relativistic description of the white dwarf spacetime; treats electrons as quantum particles and ions classically or as fixed background (jellium), accounting for Coulomb interactions and gravitational redshift effects.

Result: Quantitative thermodynamic (e.g., internal energy, pressure, specific heat) and structural (e.g., pair correlation functions, static structure factor) properties of the jellium plasma under white-dwarf atmospheric conditions (high density ~10^4–10^6 g/cm³, low temperature ~10^4–10^5 K, strong gravity).

Conclusion: PIMC within general relativity reveals significant quantum and correlation effects on plasma behavior in white dwarf atmospheres—deviations from classical or mean-field models are substantial, necessitating ab initio quantum many-body methods for accurate spectral and cooling modeling.

Abstract: We determine thermodynamic and structural properties of the Coulomb plasma (jellium) in the thin atmosphere of a white dwarf using the path integral Monte Carlo method within a general relativity description of the star.

</details>


### [63] [Draining Without Emptying a Stringy Black Hole: Repetitive Electric Penrose Process](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37713)
*Huai-Fan Li,Xiong-Ying Guo,Ke Wang,Hong Lei*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates the repetitive electric Penrose process in the GMGHS black hole, showing that energy extraction ROI can exceed 100% but efficiency stays ≤50%, with most extracted energy increasing the black hole's irreducible mass; iteration termination is governed by escape and negative-energy particle existence conditions, and final state retains nonzero charge and mass.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the feasibility and limits of repetitive energy extraction via the electric Penrose process in the charged dilatonic GMGHS black hole—contrasting with RN black holes—and to determine how charge depletion, particle parameters, and black hole initial conditions affect extraction performance.

Method: Analytical derivation of effective potential behavior under repeated scattering; identification of two physical iteration-termination conditions (escape condition for particle 0 and existence condition for negative-energy particle 1); numerical simulation of energy return on investment (ROI) and utilization efficiency across varying particle decay radii, charge-to-mass ratios, specific energies, and initial black hole charges.

Result: Effective potential remains invariant across iterations in GMGHS (unlike RN); escape condition is charge-independent, while negative-energy particle existence requires sufficiently large black hole charge; ROI > 100% achievable but energy utilization efficiency ≤ 50%; extractable energy predominantly increases irreducible mass; smaller decay radius and larger |q1/m1| improve both ROI and efficiency; higher incident particle specific energy lowers ROI but leaves efficiency unchanged; for non-extremal initial black holes, ROI increases monotonically with initial charge, while efficiency peaks then declines; final state retains nonzero charge and mass.

Conclusion: The repetitive electric Penrose process in the GMGHS black hole is sustainable but self-limiting: it cannot fully discharge the black hole, and its energetic gain (ROI) does not reflect true thermodynamic efficiency, as most extracted energy contributes to irreducible mass growth—highlighting a fundamental trade-off between extractable energy and black hole irreversibility.

Abstract: This paper studies the repetitive electric Penrose process in the Gibbons-Maeda-Garfinkle-Horowitz-Strominger (GMGHS) black hole under the optimal condition for maximum energy extraction. Unlike the Reissner-Nordström (RN) black hole, the effective potential curve of the incident particle in the GMGHS black hole remains unchanged during the iterations. The iteration termination conditions are determined by two aspects: the escape condition requires that particle 0 can overcome the effective potential peak and escape to infinity, and this condition is independent of the black hole charge; the existence condition for negative-energy particle 1 requires the black hole charge to be sufficiently large, indicating that the repetitive process cannot completely consume the charge of the GMGHS black hole to zero. Numerical simulations show that the return on investment of energy extraction can exceed $100\%$, but the energy utilization efficiency does not exceed $50\%$, and most of the extractable energy is converted into an increase in the irreducible mass of the black hole. In addition, a smaller decay radius and a larger absolute value of the charge-to-mass ratio of particle 1 can improve the return on investment and energy utilization efficiency of energy extraction; the larger the specific energy of the incident particle, the smaller the return on investment of energy extraction, while the energy utilization efficiency remains unchanged; for an initially non-extremal black hole, as the initial charge increases, the return on investment of energy extraction increases monotonically, while the energy utilization efficiency first increases and then decreases. After the iterations end, the black hole still retains extractable energy, and neither the charge nor the mass vanishes.

</details>


### [64] [On a model of quantum quadratic gravity in 7 + 1-dimensional spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37780)
*Vsevolod Chistiakov,Artur Mingaleev*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies a quadratic gravity model extended with higher-derivative curvature terms, identifies a perfect-square action for specific couplings, reduces it in FLRW cosmology to a reparameterization-invariant variable, maps it via nonlinear transformation to a Gaussian (Wiener) path integral, and finds that the transformation Jacobian becomes field-independent only in 7+1 dimensions—enabling a formal Wiener-measure representation of quantum expectation values.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a well-defined, mathematically tractable path integral formulation for quantum cosmology in higher-derivative gravity, leveraging geometric invariance and stochastic quantization techniques.

Method: Performs Hamiltonian reduction in spatially flat FLRW cosmology to obtain a reparameterization-invariant dynamical variable; introduces a nonlinear field redefinition mapping the reduced action to a Gaussian form; analyzes the functional Jacobian of this transformation under time discretization to identify dimension-dependent simplifications.

Result: The transformation Jacobian becomes independent of the dynamical field only in $7+1$ spacetime dimensions; consequently, expectation values of observables in the reduced model admit a formal representation as Wiener integrals.

Conclusion: The $7+1$-dimensional case uniquely allows a consistent Wiener-measure-based path integral quantization of this quadratic gravity model after reduction and field redefinition, suggesting dimensional selectivity in stochastic approaches to quantum gravity.

Abstract: We consider a quadratic gravity model supplemented by the terms $\nabla_{\mu}R\nabla^{\mu}R$ and $(\nabla^{2}R)^2$. For specific relations among the coupling constants, the action takes a perfect-square form. In the spatially flat FLRW model, we perform a reduction to a dynamical variable invariant under time reparameterizations. We construct a nonlinear change of variable that transforms the reduced action into the Gaussian action associated with the Wiener measure. An analysis of the transformation Jacobian within a time discretization singles out the case of $7+1$-dimensional spacetime, in which the Jacobian of the discrete transformation under consideration is independent of the dynamical variable. On this basis, we propose a formal representation of expectation values of observables in the reduced model as integrals with respect to the Wiener measure.

</details>


### [65] [Primordial Gravitational Waves in Loop Quantum Cosmology: Regularization Ambiguities and Initial State Dependence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37790)
*Mah Noor Jamil,Parampreet Singh*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes the primordial tensor power spectrum in loop quantum cosmology (LQC) and its modified versions (mLQC-I/II), using hybrid and dressed metric prescriptions with a Starobinsky potential; it analyzes the tensor-to-scalar ratio r(k), distinguishes initial-state-dependent vs. independent features, identifies scale-dependent oscillatory behavior near the scale-invariant regime, and assesses implications for CMB B-mode observables.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate how quantum gravity effects near the cosmological bounce in loop quantum cosmology influence observable inflationary predictions—particularly the tensor power spectrum and tensor-to-scalar ratio—and to disentangle physical imprints of the bounce from artifacts of initial conditions or regularization.

Method: Numerical and analytical evolution of tensor perturbations across the quantum bounce in standard LQC, mLQC-I, and mLQC-II; use of de Sitter mode functions (dressed metric) and Hankel-function solutions (hybrid) as initial states on the emergent contracting de Sitter branch in mLQC-I; computation of scale-dependent r(k) = P_T/P_S to isolate bounce features; propagation of spectra to CMB B-mode angular power spectrum.

Result: For N_e = 60–65, the pivot-scale r agrees with standard Starobinsky inflation; r(k) shows slow variation in the scale-invariant regime and characteristic oscillations near it—those near the scale-invariant regime are adiabatic-order-independent, unlike low-k spikes; infrared modes retain memory of the bounce and pre-bounce branch, preserving regularization dependence; resulting B-mode spectra are within reach of next-generation CMB experiments.

Conclusion: Quantum gravitational corrections from the bounce imprint distinctive, potentially observable signatures in the tensor spectrum—especially via scale-dependent oscillations in r(k)—that are robust against initial-state choices in certain regimes, offering a pathway to test LQC with future CMB B-mode measurements.

Abstract: We compute the primordial tensor power spectrum in standard loop quantum cosmology and in the modified models mLQC-I and mLQC-II, evolving the tensor perturbations across the bounce in the hybrid and dressed metric prescriptions with a Starobinsky potential. In mLQC-I, the emergent de Sitter contracting branch requires the use of dedicated initial states. We use de Sitter mode function in the dressed metric approach and a Hankel-function solution in the hybrid approach. For inflationary e-foldings $N_e = 60$ and $65$, the pivot-scale tensor-to-scalar ratio agrees with the value of standard inflation with this potential. The scale-dependent ratio $r(k)=\mathcal{P}_T/\mathcal{P}_S$ cancels effects common to the two sectors and separates features that are independent of the initial state from those that depend on it. It varies slowly in the scale-invariant regime and oscillates around $r_\star$ at smaller $k$. The oscillations next to the scale-invariant regime do not depend on the adiabatic order, unlike the peaks and spikes at lower $k$. For a given initial state, the regularization dependence survives in the infrared, where long-wavelength modes retain a record of the bounce and of the pre-bounce branch. We propagate the normalized spectra to the CMB $B$-mode spectrum and discuss observational prospects.

</details>


### [66] [Inverse design of scalar-tensor EFT from odd-parity effective potentials](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37915)
*Kazufumi Takahashi,Yuki Watanabe*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces an inverse-design method for scalar-tensor gravity EFTs, reconstructing background and EFT parameters from a given odd-parity effective potential—exemplified by Pöschl-Teller and Regge-Wheeler potentials—and links ringdown phenomenology to fundamental EFT parameters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge black hole ringdown observations (e.g., quasinormal modes) with underlying scalar-tensor effective field theory by enabling controlled reconstruction of viable EFTs from phenomenologically motivated potentials.

Method: Inverse design: starting from a prescribed odd-parity effective potential (e.g., Pöschl-Teller or Regge-Wheeler), solve the mapping relations between that potential and the background geometry + EFT coefficients for static, spherically symmetric spacetimes with timelike scalar gradient; employ analytic solutions for QNMs where possible.

Result: Explicit EFT realizations of Pöschl-Teller potentials (with exact QNM spectra) and both standard and parametrically deformed Regge-Wheeler potentials within scalar-tensor EFT; derivation of explicit mappings from potential deformations to EFT operator coefficients.

Conclusion: The inverse-design framework enables physically interpretable, observation-driven construction of scalar-tensor EFTs; it provides a rigorous pathway to translate constraints on black hole potential deformations into bounds on fundamental EFT parameters.

Abstract: We develop an inverse-design approach to the effective field theory (EFT) of perturbations in scalar-tensor gravity around static and spherically symmetric backgrounds with a timelike scalar-field gradient, taking the odd-parity effective potential as input and reconstructing compatible background and EFT data. As analytically tractable examples, we construct explicit realizations of the Pöschl-Teller potential, for which the quasinormal-mode spectrum can be obtained in closed form. We then study the Regge-Wheeler potential of general relativity and its parametrized deformations, showing how both can be realized within the scalar-tensor EFT. In particular, the latter realizations provide a route to interpreting phenomenological constraints on potential deformations from black hole ringdown observations in terms of the underlying EFT data.

</details>


### [67] [Quantum gravity phenomenology in quadrupolar Bose-Einstein condensates: Finite-temperature thermodynamics and projected bounds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37930)
*Asma Tahar Taiba,Abdelaali Boudjemaa*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how the generalized uncertainty principle (GUP)—a candidate quantum gravity signature—affects thermodynamic properties of quadrupolar Bose-Einstein condensates, finding that quadrupole-quadrupole interactions combined with GUP (especially LQGUP) induce significant, measurable corrections to critical temperature, condensed fraction, and equation of state.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe quantum gravity phenomenology by identifying experimentally accessible signatures in finite-temperature many-body systems—specifically, using quadrupolar BECs as sensitive probes of GUP-induced modifications to quantum mechanics.

Method: Analytical thermodynamic analysis within second quantization; incorporation of modified density of states from quadratic (QGUP) and linear-quadratic (LQGUP) generalized uncertainty principles; calculation of corrections to condensed fraction, critical temperature, equation of state, and related observables; estimation of experimental sensitivity via fractional temperature resolution constraints.

Result: GUP induces non-negligible corrections to all key thermodynamic quantities; LQGUP generally yields larger deviations than QGUP, but magnitude depends on parameters and perturbative validity; projected upper bounds on GUP deformation parameters are derived from assumed temperature resolution; QQIs enhance GUP sensitivity.

Conclusion: Quadrupolar BECs constitute a viable and promising platform for testing GUP and constraining quantum gravity models via precision thermometry of many-body observables, motivating future work with realistic experimental parameters.

Abstract: We study the thermodynamics of quadrupolar Bose-Einstein condensates in the realm of the generalized uncertainty principle (GUP) for the quest of understanding and testing quantum gravity (QG). Using both quadratic GUP (QGUP) and linear-quadratic GUP (LQGUP) which incorporate modified density of states, we calculate corrections to the condensed fraction, critical temperature, equation of state and other thermodynamic quantities. We find that the intriguing interplay of quadrupole-quadrupole interactions (QQIs) and the GUP may strongly modify the finite-temperature properties of the condensate, with the LQGUP generally predicting larger corrections than the QGUP, although their relative magnitudes depend on the chosen parameters and the validity of the perturbative expansion. We further estimate the sensitivity of the critical temperature to GUP-induced effects by imposing an assumed fractional temperature resolution which provides projected upper bounds on the GUP deformation parameters. Our findings demonstrate that quadrupole-quadrupole interacting Bose gases provide a promising platform for testing GUP-induced deviations from standard quantum mechanics and for probing QG phenomenology through measurements of finite-temperature many-body observables. They also motivate future studies using realistic system-specific parameters.

</details>


### [68] [Apparent horizon mergers in analytic intial data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37938)
*Federico Elizondo Lopez,Ivan Booth*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates that parameter evolutions of Brill-Lindquist-deSitter initial data reproduce key features of marginally outer trapped surface (MOTS) evolution observed in black hole mergers—features more complex than the standard 'pair-of-pants' event horizon picture—establishing this analytic initial data as a valuable testbed for MOTS and merger geometry studies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify a tractable, analytic initial data set that captures the intricate evolution of marginally outer trapped surfaces (MOTS) during black hole mergers—beyond the simpler 'pair-of-pants' event horizon topology—thereby enabling controlled theoretical and numerical exploration of MOTS dynamics and spacetime geometry.

Method: Analysis of parameter-dependent evolutions of Brill-Lindquist-deSitter initial data, comparing the resulting MOTS behavior (e.g., formation, merging, topology changes) with known features from prior numerical studies of black hole mergers by Pook-Kolb, Hennigar & Booth.

Result: Parameter evolutions of Brill-Lindquist-deSitter initial data qualitatively reproduce the complex MOTS evolution observed in full numerical black hole mergers—including nontrivial birth, growth, interaction, and coalescence patterns—despite being based on a highly symmetric, analytic setup.

Conclusion: Brill-Lindquist-deSitter initial data serves as a useful analytic testing ground for studying MOTS evolution and spacetime geometry in black hole mergers, bridging conceptual simplicity and physical richness.

Abstract: Five years ago Pook-Kolb, Hennigar and Booth published a series of three papers exploring the evolution of apparent horizons and other marginally outer trapped surfaces during a black hole merger. This was seen to be much more intricate than the well-known "pair-of-pants" merger associated with event horizons. In this paper we show that parameter evolutions of Brill-Lindquist-deSitter initial data mimic many of the features of that evolution. Hence, this analytic intial data is a useful testing ground for ideas about marginally outer trapped surface evolution and spacetime geometry during mergers.

</details>


### [69] [Systematic High Overtone Expansion of Kerr Quasinormal Modes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38019)
*Yasuyuki Hatsuda*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a systematic high-overtone WKB expansion for Kerr quasinormal modes on normal angular branches, leveraging a genus-one spectral curve and elliptic periods to compute coefficients to arbitrary order, with numerical and exact-WKB validation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a systematic, analytically tractable method for computing high-overtone quasinormal mode (QNM) frequencies of Kerr black holes—particularly on normal angular branches—where standard WKB or numerical methods become cumbersome or intractable.

Method: Derives large-frequency expansion of the angular eigenvalue via polynomial recursion; reformulates the radial Teukolsky equation to reveal that all WKB corrections live on a fixed genus-one spectral curve; expresses higher-order quantum periods solely in terms of two fundamental elliptic periods; computes expansion coefficients recursively.

Result: Obtains arbitrary-order high-overtone expansions for scalar, electromagnetic, and gravitational QNMs on normal angular branches; numerically verifies accuracy against direct solutions of angular and radial equations; matches scalar-mode results with exact WKB analysis for extremal Kerr.

Conclusion: The spectral geometry of the problem is fully encoded in a genus-one curve, enabling efficient, analytic high-overtone expansions; this framework unifies treatment across spin weights and provides a foundation for deeper connections between black hole QNMs, integrability, and resurgent asymptotics.

Abstract: We develop a systematic method to compute the high overtone expansion of Kerr quasinormal modes on normal angular branches to arbitrary order. We first obtain the expansion of the angular eigenvalue at large frequencies by a simple polynomial recursion. Using this result in an alternative form of the radial Teukolsky equation, we find that all the WKB corrections are defined on a fixed genus-one spectral curve, and that the quantum periods at the second and higher orders are expressed in terms of only two elliptic periods. This allows us to determine the expansion coefficients order by order. We confirm that the truncated expansions reproduce quasinormal frequencies obtained by solving the angular and radial equations directly, for scalar, electromagnetic and gravitational fields. We also compare the scalar results with the exact WKB analysis of extremal Kerr black holes.

</details>


### [70] [Physical Effects in the Vicinity of Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38068)
*Alex Koek*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This thesis explores phenomena within classical general relativity with implications for cosmological and black hole observations, particularly in the context of the emerging gravitational wave astronomy era.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate classical general relativistic phenomena whose insights may inform modern cosmological and black hole observations, especially as observational astronomy transitions from electromagnetic to gravitational-wave-based detection.

Method: Theoretical analysis within the framework of classical general relativity; no specific numerical or experimental methods are detailed in the abstract.

Result: No explicit results are stated in the abstract; the work is positioned to yield insights relevant to cosmology and black hole physics.

Conclusion: General relativity remains foundational and fertile ground for generating new physical insights, especially in light of the gravitational-wave observational revolution.

Abstract: General relativity, a theory that is already over a century old, is still the best theory that we have to describe the universe at energy scales where only the gravitational force is important. It has stood tall to any observational tests thrown at it since its inception, and it has remarkable predictive power when it is utilized to study and model astrophysical problems. It also continues to surprise us with its ability to generate new problems and insights that render themselves useful in the study of modern physics. This is especially highlighted by the transition of modern observational astronomy away from an electromagnetic radiation epoch, and into a new gravitational wave epoch. This thesis devotes its study to phenomena that can be analyzed within the framework of classical general relativity, but whose results may contain interesting implications for cosmological and black hole observations. (Full abstract can be found in document).

</details>


### [71] [Gravitational baryogenesis in Gauss-Bonnet braneworld cosmology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38188)
*Ian B Whittingham*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper explores gravitational baryogenesis in a Gauss-Bonnet–modified Randall-Sundrum type II braneworld, identifying viable expansion regimes (GB and GR) where the curvature time-derivative condition $\dot{R}<0$ holds, computing the baryon asymmetry $\eta(T)$, and showing that observed asymmetry can be reproduced for specific parameter ranges of bulk curvature scale $\mu$ and GB coupling $\beta$, unlike standard GR where $\eta=0$ for radiation domination.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether gravitational baryogenesis—requiring $\dot{R} < 0$—can successfully generate the observed baryon asymmetry in a Gauss-Bonnet–enhanced Randall-Sundrum type II braneworld, where cosmic expansion exhibits three distinct regimes with differing $H(\rho)$ dependencies.

Method: Analytical derivation of the Hubble evolution across GB, RS, and GR regimes; evaluation of the Ricci scalar time derivative $\dot{R}$ to identify regimes satisfying the baryogenesis condition; computation of the baryon-to-entropy ratio $\eta(T)$ using the gravitational baryogenesis formula adapted to the modified background; parameter-space scanning constrained by inflationary bounds on $\mu$ and $\beta$.

Result: Gravitational baryogenesis is viable only in the GB and GR regimes ($\dot{R}<0$); the RS regime ($\dot{R}>0$) suppresses asymmetry. The predicted $\eta(T) \propto \mu (1+w)(1-w)(1+1/\beta)$ yields observed asymmetry for $\beta \sim 10^{-3}–10^{-2}$ at $\mu = 10^{12}$ GeV and $\beta \sim 10^{-2}–10^{-1}$ at $\mu = 10^{13}$ GeV; $\mu = 10^{14}$ GeV overproduces $\eta$. Crucially, $\eta \neq 0$ even for $w = 1/3$, unlike in GR.

Conclusion: Gauss-Bonnet braneworld cosmology provides a viable framework for gravitational baryogenesis, with parameter-tuned agreement to observational constraints—demonstrating that higher-curvature gravity in extra-dimensional settings can resolve the vanishing-asymmetry problem of standard GR-based gravitational baryogenesis.

Abstract: The present study investigates the feasibility of gravitational baryogenesis in a Gauss-Bonnet (GB) modified Randall-Sundrum (RS) type II braneworld cosmology. The modified expansion rate of the Universe in this scenario evolves through three distinct expansion regimes, the GB regime at early times where the expansion rate $H \sim \rho^{1/3}$ falls more slowly than the standard expansion law $H\sim \rho^{1/2}$ of GR, then the RS type era with an enhanced expansion $H \sim \rho$, before finally reducing to the standard expansion law in the low energy limit.
