Gravitational baryogenesis depends on $\dot{R}$ and requires $\dot{R} <0$. This condition is satisfied for the GB and GR regimes. However, $\dot{R}>0$ for the RS regime and successful baryogenesis requires either that the decoupling temperature occurs during the GB regime or that the Universe makes a direct transition from the GB regime to the GR regime. The GB baryon asymmetry $\eta(T)$ is proportional to $\mu (1+w)(1-w)(1+1/\beta)$, where $\mu$ is the energy scale $\mu \equiv 1/l$ associated with the bulk curvature scale $l$, $\beta \equiv 4 \alpha \mu^{2}$, where $\alpha$ is the GB coupling, and $w$ is the equation of state parameter $p=w \rho$. Studies of inflation in GB-braneworld cosmology place constraints on the allowed ranges of the parameters $\mu$ and $\beta$. Guided by these, the asymmetry has been calculated for ranges of $(\mu, \beta, w)$ and it is found that the observed values can be obtained for a value of $\beta$ within the range $(10^{-2} - 10^{-3})$ for $\mu =10^{12}$ GeV, and within the range $(10^{-1}- 10^{-2})$ for $\mu =10^{13}$ GeV. All values of $\eta$ for $\mu=10^{14}$ are too high. In contrast to GR where $\eta$ is zero for a radiation dominated Universe ($w=1/3$), it is non-zero in GB braneworld cosmology.

</details>


### [72] [Axion-angle dependence of the rotational and tidal properties of PNJL quark stars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38343)
*Safiqul Islam,Ayan Banerjee,Javlon Rayimbaev,Safyan Mukhtar*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies how a spatially uniform axion angle θ (0 to π) affects rotational and tidal properties of quark stars using an axion-extended PNJL equation of state; it finds significant θ-dependent changes in maximum mass, moment of inertia (+13% at 1.338 M⊙), and tidal deformability (342 → 547), yet universal I-Love-Q and Ī–C relations hold to within 2.7% and 0.4%, respectively.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify how a fixed, uniform axion field—parameterized by the axion angle θ—affects macroscopic observables of self-bound quark stars, particularly rotational (moment of inertia, quadrupole) and static tidal (deformability, mass shift) properties, and to assess universality of neutron-star-like relations under axionic modifications of the EOS.

Method: Solves the TOV and perturbative rotational/tidal equations in general relativity for five values of θ ∈ [0, π] using an axion-extended Polyakov-loop Nambu–Jona-Lasinio (PNJL) equation of state; includes finite-surface-density corrections to rotational mass shift and static tidal response; computes leading-order moment of inertia Ī, second-order spin-induced quadrupole moment Q, and dimensionless tidal deformability Λ̄; evaluates I-Love-Q and Ī–C relations across sequences.

Result: Maximum static mass increases from 1.68 to 1.95 M⊙ with θ; at M = 1.338 M⊙, Ī rises by 13% and Λ̄ increases from 342 to 547; differences in Ī between adjacent θ samples exceed numerical interpolation/table-resolution sensitivities; I-Love-Q relation holds to 0.4% max deviation, Ī–C to 2.7%; predicted binary tidal deformability curves remain within GW170817’s 90% credible region.

Conclusion: While stellar properties (M_max, Ī, Λ̄) exhibit strong, non-negligible dependence on the axion angle θ within this EOS family, normalized universal relations (I-Love-Q, Ī–C) retain high fidelity (<3% deviation), suggesting robustness of EOS-insensitive modeling even under axion-modified strong-interaction physics.

Abstract: We investigate how a fixed, spatially uniform axion angle modifies the rotational and static tidal properties of self-bound quark stars in general relativity, using an axion-extended Polyakov-loop Nambu--Jona-Lasinio equation of state. We vary $\theta$ from $0$ to $\pi$ while holding the remaining model parameters fixed, and compute the leading-order moment of inertia and the second-order spin-induced quadrupole moment and mass shift. The calculation includes finite-surface-density contributions to the rotational mass shift and static tidal response. Across this range, the static maximum mass increases from $1.68$ to $1.95\,M_\odot$, remaining below the lower edge of the quoted $1\sigma$ timing interval for PSR~J0740+6620. At $M=1.338\,M_\odot$, the moment of inertia increases by $13\%$ and the dimensionless tidal deformability rises from $342$ to $547$. Over the common mass range examined, the differences in the moment of inertia between adjacent sampled angles exceed the tested interpolation and table-resolution sensitivities. Despite these changes, joint fits to the five sequences describe the $\bar I$--$C$ and I-Love-Q relations with maximum departures of $2.7\%$ and $0.4\%$, respectively, over the fitted domains. The predicted binary tidal-deformability curves lie within the estimated 90\% credible region of the adopted GW170817 posterior over the mass-ratio ranges examined. Within this EOS family, stellar properties vary appreciably with $\theta$, while the normalized quantities admit accurate joint fits across the five sequences.

</details>


### [73] [Hamiltonian Analysis of the Newer General Relativity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38372)
*Chen-Yi Sia,Tee-How Loo,Avik De*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper conducts a Hamiltonian analysis of Newer General Relativity—a five-parameter class of quadratic nonmetricity theories—classifying them into nine types based on primary constraint structure, deriving their ADM formulation, and computing degrees of freedom.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically understand the constraint structure and physical degrees of freedom in Newer General Relativity, a class of symmetric teleparallel gravity theories with quadratic nonmetricity, by extending Hamiltonian methods beyond standard GR and Teleparallel Equivalent of GR (TEGR).

Method: Systematic Dirac–Bergmann Hamiltonian analysis: derivation of the ADM decomposition for symmetric teleparallel geometry; identification of conjugate momenta and primary constraints; construction of canonical and primary Hamiltonians; classification into nine types via constraint algebra; consistency analysis to determine secondary constraints and first-/second-class status; counting of physical degrees of freedom.

Result: Nine distinct constraint-structure types are identified; for each, full constraint classification (first/second class), consistency conditions, and number of local physical degrees of freedom (0, 1, or 2) are explicitly determined; the ADM formulation is rigorously established for Newer General Relativity.

Conclusion: The physical content of Newer General Relativity is highly parameter-sensitive: only specific subsets of the five-parameter space yield propagating degrees of freedom (up to two), while others are either topological or over-constrained; this classification provides a foundation for viability studies (e.g., cosmology, gravitational waves) and distinguishes viable extensions from ghost/tachyon-prone models.

Abstract: We present a systematic Hamiltonian analysis of the five-parameter class of theories known as Newer General Relativity. Quadratic nonmetricity theories of this type can be divided into nine types according to the structure of their primary constraints. After reviewing the framework of symmetric teleparallel gravity, we derive the ADM formulation of Newer General Relativity, discuss the existing classification and perform the Hamiltonian formulation of the theory. For each type, we compute the conjugate momenta, identify the primary constraints, and construct both the canonical and primary Hamiltonians. We then determine the constraint structure and analyze the resulting physical degrees of freedom in each type.

</details>


### [74] [Quasinormal Modes of a Rotating Hořava Black Hole: Two Perturbative Approaches to Nonseparability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38428)
*Wen-Di Guo,Wentao Liu,Yu-Xiao Liu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes scalar quasinormal modes of rotating black holes in Hořava gravity using two novel approximations that preserve the exact Kerr limit; it finds distinct, spin-dependent corrections to real and imaginary mode frequencies due to Lorentz violation, with ~2% real-frequency angular correction at high spin and nonmonotonic, projection-sensitive damping.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe strong-field gravity and test Lorentz violation via quasinormal modes (QNMs) of black holes in Hořava gravity—a candidate quantum gravity theory with built-in Lorentz violation in the UV—requiring accurate treatment of nonseparable scalar wave equations induced by rotation.

Method: Two complementary approximation schemes: (I) slow-rotation expansion applied only to the Lorentz-violating (nonseparable) part while retaining exact Kerr background geometry; (II) small-η biorthogonal spheroidal projection, where η is the Lorentz-violating parameter. Both methods employ residual-controlled numerical continuation and symmetric finite differences for eigenvalue solving, validated against a Kerr continued-fraction benchmark and an independent radial spectral method.

Result: Axisymmetric QNMs serve as exact null tests confirming Kerr consistency. For corotating fundamental scalar modes: real frequency increases monotonically with spin; at a/M = 0.9, both methods yield ~2% angular correction to real frequency; imaginary (damping) frequency is nonmonotonic and highly sensitive to angular projection due to internal cancellation effects.

Conclusion: Rotation couples strongly to Lorentz violation in Hořava gravity, imprinting distinctive, measurable signatures in black-hole ringdown spectra—particularly in the angular dependence and nonmonotonic damping behavior—enabling future gravitational-wave tests of quantum gravity phenomenology.

Abstract: Quasinormal modes (QNMs) provide a powerful probe of strong-field gravity. We study fundamental scalar quasinormal modes of an asymptotically flat rotating black hole in the low-energy sector of Hořava gravity, whose exact scalar equation is nonseparable due to rotation-induced Lorentz violation. We develop two complementary approximations that both preserve the exact Kerr sector: Method I applies a slow-rotation approximation only to the nonseparable Lorentz-violating part, whereas Method II employs a small-$\eta$ biorthogonal spheroidal projection, with $\eta$ the Lorentz-violating parameter. Using residual-controlled continuation and symmetric finite differences, validated by a Kerr continued-fraction benchmark and an independent radial spectral method, we compute the linear frequency response. Axisymmetric modes provide an exact Kerr null test. For the corotating modes studied, the real-frequency response is positive and increases with spin. At $a/M=0.9$, the two methods reveal a roughly $2\%$ angular correction to the real response, while the imaginary response is nonmonotonic and highly sensitive to angular projection due to internal cancellation. These results highlight the distinct rotational signatures of Lorentz violation in black-hole ringdown.

</details>


### [75] [Exact Rotating Wormholes in Five Dimensions and Their Exceptional-Point Instability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38659)
*Jose Luis Blázquez-Salcedo,Luis Manuel González-Romero,Fech Scen Khoo,Burkhard Kleihaus,Jutta Kunz,Pablo Navarro Moreno*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs rotating 5D Ellis–Bronnikov wormholes with equal angular momenta, solves the metric equations exactly via an $A_2$ Toda system, and reveals a second-order exceptional point in radial perturbations where unstable modes transition from purely imaginary to complex—signifying oscillatory (not stabilized) instability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguities in prior analyses of radial stability of rotating wormholes, particularly whether rotation stabilizes or merely modifies the instability; and to obtain exact solutions beyond static or perturbative approximations.

Method: Exact closed-form integration of the 5D Einstein–scalar field equations for reflection-symmetric wormholes with equal angular momenta; reduction to an open $A_2$ Toda system; construction of elementary tau-function solutions; cohomogeneity-one linear stability analysis including spectral decomposition near exceptional points.

Result: Discovery of a second-order exceptional point at |J|/A ≈ 0.100628 where two purely imaginary unstable eigenfrequencies coalesce; beyond it, the mode pair becomes complex-conjugate with positive imaginary part (oscillatory instability), not stabilization; growth rate strongly suppressed in rapid-rotation limit.

Conclusion: Rotation does not eliminate radial instability of Ellis–Bronnikov wormholes but transforms it into an oscillatory instability via an exceptional-point transition—highlighting the necessity of complex spectral analysis beyond real-eigenvalue approximations.

Abstract: We give an independent closed-form construction of rotating five-dimensional Ellis--Bronnikov wormholes with equal angular momenta and analyze the radial instability of the reflection-symmetric family. The nontrivial coupled metric equations reduce exactly to an open $A_2$ Toda system. The general two-ended solution is expressed in terms of elementary Toda tau functions; the symmetric subclass reduces to a particularly simple one-parameter family interpolating between the static wormhole and the extremal equal-spin Myers--Perry geometry. On this exact background we revisit the cohomogeneity-one perturbation problem. Besides the ordinary unstable mode of the static wormhole, rotation renders the descendant of its non-normalizable zero mode normalizable. The two purely imaginary unstable frequencies coalesce at $|J|/A=0.100628$, where their eigenvectors coalesce as well, identifying a second-order exceptional point. Beyond it the pair continues as $\omega_\pm=\pm\omega_R+\mathrm i\omega_I$ with $\omega_I>0$: the apparent loss of the radial instability found in the original real-eigenvalue treatment is therefore not stabilization, but a transition to an oscillatory instability. We follow this branch deep into the rapidly rotating regime, where its growth rate becomes strongly suppressed.

</details>


### [76] [Resonance correction to time-dependent finite-size effects in Effective Field Theory of binary black hole inspiral](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38668)
*Shani Avitan,Ram Brustein*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes how resonant excitation of ultra-compact objects (UCOs) enhances tidal deformation effects in binary inspirals, using point-particle EFT; it finds a leading 2.5PN resonant correction to the gravitational-wave phase—distinct from the 5PN static case—and shows all finite-size terms scale with a negative static electric quadrupole Love number $k_2$, supporting resonant signatures as optimal probes of black hole internal structure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test whether astrophysical black holes possess internal structure (i.e., are ultra-compact objects rather than vacuum GR black holes) by probing non-vanishing tidal Love numbers and resonant tidal excitations during binary inspirals, which would imprint distinctive features in gravitational-wave phase evolution.

Method: Employs point-particle effective field theory (EFT) for compact binaries in circular orbits; models the UCO's internal electric quadrupole as a harmonic oscillator, integrates out the internal degree of freedom to generate time-dependent finite-size operators, and computes their coefficients and post-Newtonian scaling—including resonant vs. static contributions.

Result: The static electric quadrupole Love number $k_2$ is negative; all finite-size tidal operators are proportional to $k_2$; resonant excitation generates a leading 2.5 post-Newtonian (PN) correction to the orbital energy flux and gravitational-wave phase, whereas the non-resonant (static) counterpart enters only at 5 PN; explicit numerical coefficients for the induced EFT operators are derived.

Conclusion: Resonant tidal excitation provides a significantly enhanced, observationally accessible signature—orders of magnitude stronger than static tides—for distinguishing ultra-compact objects from black holes in GR, making it one of the most promising avenues for probing black hole microstructure with gravitational-wave astronomy.

Abstract: General Relativity predicts that black holes lack an internal structure and that their tidal Love numbers vanish. However, if astrophysical black holes were instead ultra-compact objects (UCOs) with internal structure, their tidal Love numbers would no longer vanish. Then, during a binary inspiral, their tidal deformation could transfer orbital energy to the UCO, increasing the orbital period and leaving an observable imprint on the phase of the emitted gravitational waves. As we discussed previously, the effect is enhanced if one binary companion is resonantly excited. Here we use the point-particle effective field theory framework to calculate this enhancement. We consider circular orbits and focus on excitations of the internal electric quadrupole, modelling the UCO spectrum as a harmonic oscillator spectrum. However, our methods could be straightforwardly extended to more complicated situations. Integrating out the internal quadrupole induces a series of time-dependent finite-size operators with specific numerical coefficients, which we calculate. We find that the static electric quadrupole Love number $k_2$ is negative, and that all finite-size terms are proportional to $k_2$. We further show that resonant excitation induces a leading 2.5 Post-Newtonian term, compared with the 5 Post-Newtonian term in the time-independent case. Our results confirm the expectation that resonant excitation during a binary inspiral offers one of the best ways to detect whether astrophysical black holes can undergo tidal deformation and, if they do, learn about their internal excitation spectrum.

</details>


### [77] [Gravitational field of spinors with torsion and matter$-$antimatter asymmetry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38738)
*Nikodem Popławski*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper extends general relativity to Einstein–Cartan theory by incorporating spacetime torsion required by angular momentum conservation for Dirac particles; it shows that torsion induces a nonlinear, charge-conjugation-asymmetric Dirac equation, yields negative (positive) torsional energy for matter (antimatter), and predicts solitonic particle solutions with intrinsic matter–antimatter energy asymmetry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile the conservation of total (orbital + spin) angular momentum for a free Dirac particle in curved spacetime, which necessitates nonzero spacetime torsion — thereby extending general relativity to the Einstein–Cartan theory.

Method: Uses spinor connection formalism to derive the torsion-modified Dirac equation for a spherically symmetric spinor in its own gravitational field; computes the energy–momentum tensor including torsional contributions; derives the full coupled Einstein–Cartan–Dirac equations; analyzes charge conjugation properties and ground-state energetics.

Result: The Dirac equation becomes cubic and nonlinear due to torsion; the torsional energy contribution is negative for fermions and positive for antifermions; ground-state energy of matter is lower than that of antimatter; the coupled equations admit particle-like soliton solutions exhibiting matter–antimatter energy asymmetry.

Conclusion: Spacetime torsion, mandated by angular momentum conservation, fundamentally breaks matter–antimatter symmetry at the level of single-particle quantum field dynamics in gravity, offering a geometric origin for baryon asymmetry and supporting stable, localized solitonic fermionic states.

Abstract: The conservation law for the orbital plus spin angular momentum of a free Dirac particle in curved spacetime requires that the affine connection has the antisymmetric part: the torsion tensor, which extends general relativity to the Einstein$-$Cartan theory of gravity. In spacetime with torsion, the Dirac equation becomes a nonlinear, cubic equation in the spinor wave function. Under charge conjugation, the nonlinear term changes sign relative to the mass term. A Dirac spinor and its charge conjugate therefore satisfy different field equations. We use the spinor connection formalism to derive the Dirac equation for a spherical spinor in its centrally symmetric gravitational field with torsion. We determine the energy$-$momentum tensor for this spinor and show that the energy contribution from torsion is negative for matter particles and positive for antimatter particles. Consequently, the ground state of a fermion has a lower energy than the ground state of the corresponding antifermion. We also derive the Einstein$-$Cartan$-$Dirac equations for this spinor; they indicate the existence of particle-like soliton solutions exhibiting matter$-$antimatter energy asymmetry.

</details>


### [78] [Isospectrality breaking in the axial quasinormal modes of Reissner-Nordström black holes in dynamical Chern-Simons gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38742)
*Abhishek Rout*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how odd-parity perturbations of Reissner-Nordström black holes are modified in dynamical Chern-Simons (dCS) gravity, revealing broken axial–polar isospectrality due to pseudoscalar–gravitational–electromagnetic coupling; it computes quasinormal-mode shifts analytically and numerically, identifying critical charge thresholds, scaling laws, and near-extremal suppression.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how parity-violating modifications—specifically dynamical Chern-Simons gravity—affect black hole perturbations and stability, particularly whether and how axial–polar degeneracy (isospectrality) in GR is broken for charged black holes.

Method: Derives linearized field equations for odd-parity perturbations in dCS gravity; identifies finite-charge couplings between the pseudoscalar field and axial gravitational/electromagnetic modes; diagonalizes the Einstein–Maxwell sector; computes quasinormal-mode frequency shifts using exterior complex scaling (for photon-sphere modes) and Frobenius shooting (for near-extremal zero-damping modes).

Result: The pseudoscalar perturbation couples exclusively to the axial sector, breaking axial–polar isospectrality; coupling kernel is (Mr−Q²)Δ/r⁸; electromagnetic-led and pseudoscalar modes become exactly degenerate at critical charge Q_c; off-resonance shifts scale as ζl³ (Schwarzschild) or ζl²/Q (charged); near extremality, zero-damping mode shift is suppressed linearly in (1−Q²/M²); for l=2 Schwarzschild fundamental mode: βδω = −0.7733 − 0.3946i.

Conclusion: Dynamical Chern-Simons gravity induces qualitatively new coupling structures in charged black hole perturbations, leading to spectral breaking with characteristic charge-dependent scalings, resonant degeneracies, and distinct near-extremal behavior—providing observable signatures for testing dCS gravity via black hole spectroscopy.

Abstract: We study odd-parity perturbations of Reissner-Nordström black holes in dynamical Chern-Simons gravity. The dCS action is parity invariant because $\vartheta\,{}^{*}RR$ is the product of two pseudoscalars; nevertheless, the pseudoscalar perturbation has odd parity and enters only the axial sector at linear order and so lifts the axial--polar isospectrality of general relativity. We derive the finite-charge couplings between the pseudoscalar and the axial gravitational and electromagnetic perturbations directly from the linearized field equations. Both directions are governed by the radial kernel $(Mr-Q^2)\Delta/r^8$, and at nonzero charge the pseudoscalar couples to $\Psi+\chi\Psi_e$, with $\chi=2Q/[r\sqrt{(l-1)(l+2)}]$. After diagonalizing the Einstein--Maxwell sector, we compute branch-resolved quasinormal-mode shifts with exterior complex scaling for the photon-sphere modes and Frobenius shooting for the near-extremal zero-damping modes. For the Schwarzschild $l=2$ fundamental mode we obtain $\beta\,\delta\omega=-0.7733-0.3946\,i$ (units $M=\alpha=1$). Off resonance, the gravitational-led shift scales as $\zeta\,l^3$ at $Q=0$ but as $\zeta\,l^2/Q$ at fixed nonzero charge and large $l$. The electromagnetic-led branch becomes exactly degenerate with the pseudoscalar mode at $Q_c=2M\sqrt{l(l+1)+1}/[l(l+1)+2]$, where the pseudoscalar potential coincides with the polar partner of the electromagnetic-led potential; there the breaking is $\mathcal{O}(\sqrt\zeta)$, $M|\Delta\omega|\simeq2.4\sqrt\zeta$ for $l=2$, and the shift reverses sign across $Q_c$. Near extremality the photon-sphere shifts remain finite, whereas the non-degenerate fundamental gravitational-led zero-damping mode is suppressed, $\delta\omega/\omega\simeq6.0\times10^{3}\,\zeta\,(1-Q^2/M^2)$ for $l=2$.

</details>


### [79] [Bosonic Dark Matter Cores Can Screen Scalar-Tensor Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38746)
*Jorge Castelo Mourelle,Saeed Fakhry,Néstor Ortiz*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates scalarized fermion-boson stars (FBSs) in massless Damour-Esposito-Farèse gravity, showing that ultralight bosonic dark matter catalyzes spontaneous scalarization, enables stable configurations with dark-matter cores satisfying multi-messenger mass-radius constraints, and suppresses scalar charges—potentially evading current observational bounds—while uniquely predicting high-mass scalarized FBSs as a testable signature.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether scalarized fermion-boson stars (FBSs) are observationally viable in scalar-tensor gravity, particularly in light of multi-messenger astrophysical constraints on neutron star mass and radius, and to explore the interplay between spontaneous scalarization and ultralight bosonic dark matter.

Method: Numerical construction of static, spherically symmetric FBS solutions in massless Damour-Esposito-Farèse gravity, using the MPA1 nuclear equation of state for fermionic matter and a self-interacting complex scalar field (ultralight bosonic DM); models constrained within a multi-messenger-motivated mass-radius window (e.g., NICER, gravitational-wave events).

Result: 1) Bosonic dark matter catalyzes spontaneous scalarization; 2) Energetically favored scalarized FBSs possess DM cores compatible with strong scalar coupling and observed radius bounds; 3) DM cores suppress scalar charges, mimicking massive scalar field effects and easing tension with fifth-force and binary-pulsar constraints; 4) Scalarized FBSs appear exclusively at high masses (>~2.1 M⊙), offering a distinctive observational signature.

Conclusion: Scalarized FBSs in massless DEF gravity are astrophysically viable when incorporating ultralight bosonic dark matter, with DM cores playing a dual structural and screening role—enhancing theoretical consistency with current observations and providing a falsifiable high-mass prediction.

Abstract: We examine the observational viability of scalarized fermion-boson stars (FBSs) in massless Damour-Esposito-Farèse gravity, using the MPA1 nuclear equation of state and a self-interacting complex scalar field playing the role of an ultralight bosonic dark matter model. Constraining stellar models within a multi-messenger-motivated mass-radius window, we obtain three main results: First, bosonic dark matter acts as a catalyst for spontaneous scalarization. Second, energetically favored configurations are scalarized FBSs featuring bosonic dark matter cores that reconcile strongly coupled regimes with radius bounds. Third, dark matter cores tend to suppress scalar charges, which mimics the effect of a massive gravitational scalar field and could contribute to evade observational constraints. Additionally, the exclusive prediction of scalarized FBSs at high masses suggests a way to test this theoretical scenario.

</details>


### [80] [Fermion-Boson Stars in Scalar-Tensor Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38747)
*Jorge Castelo Mourelle,Saeed Fakhry,Néstor Ortiz*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper numerically constructs static, spherically symmetric mixed fermion-boson stars in massless Damour-Esposito-Farèse scalar-tensor gravity and shows they can undergo spontaneous scalarization; it maps how bosonic mass, fermionic pressure, and bosonic amplitude jointly determine scalarized configurations (e.g., bosonic halos vs. cores) and identifies the critical coupling for scalarization onset.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore whether spontaneous scalarization—a strong-field nonlinear phenomenon previously studied in purely fermionic or bosonic compact objects—can occur in hybrid stars composed of both fermionic (neutron-like) and bosonic (scalar-field) matter, and to characterize the resulting parameter-dependent scalarized structures.

Method: Numerical solution of the coupled Einstein-scalar-fluid field equations for static, spherically symmetric configurations in the massless Damour-Esposito-Farèse theory; fermions modeled as a perfect fluid, bosons as a massive free complex scalar field; systematic parameter scans over bosonic mass, central fermionic pressure, and central bosonic amplitude; construction of mass-radius sequences and determination of critical coupling via bifurcation analysis.

Result: Mixed fermion-boson stars exhibit spontaneous scalarization; rich morphology emerges—including fermion-dominated stars with extended bosonic halos and boson-dominated cores embedded in fermionic envelopes—depending on parameter combinations; critical coupling for scalarization onset is quantified and shown to vary nontrivially with bosonic mass and central amplitudes.

Conclusion: Spontaneous scalarization is viable and structurally diverse in hybrid fermion-boson stars, demonstrating that bosonic fields significantly enrich the space of scalarized compact objects beyond pure neutron or boson star paradigms; this opens new avenues for testing scalar-tensor theories with multimessenger astrophysical observations.

Abstract: In the massless Damour-Esposito-Farèse scalar-tensor theory of gravity, we numerically construct static, spherically symmetric star models composed of a mixture of fermionic and bosonic matter, interacting purely gravitationally. The fermionic sector is modeled as a perfect fluid, while the bosonic sector is described by a massive, free, complex scalar field. We demonstrate that mixed fermion-boson stars can undergo spontaneous scalarization, and we systematically investigate the role played by the bosonic field across different regimes of bosonic mass, central fermionic pressure, and central bosonic amplitude. Our analysis reveals a complex interplay among the parameters, giving rise to a variety of scalarized configurations, ranging from fermionic matter embedded within extended bosonic halos to bosonic cores confined within fermionic matter. We examine sequences of solutions, in particular mass-radius surfaces, and we determine the critical coupling parameter for the onset of scalarization.

</details>


### [81] [Constructing likelihoods for a non-Gaussian gravitational-wave background](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38765)
*Tore Boybeyi,Vuk Mandic,Joseph Romano*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper reformulates stochastic gravitational-wave background (SGWB) analysis from stellar-mass compact binaries using the characteristic function of multi-detector data, unifying existing non-Gaussian searches and enabling systematic construction of new approximate sufficient statistics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The stochastic gravitational-wave background from stellar-mass compact binaries is intrinsically non-Gaussian due to its discrete, finite-merger origin; standard Gaussian cross-correlation methods are suboptimal, necessitating a principled framework for optimal non-Gaussian detection.

Method: The authors recast the SGWB inference problem in terms of the characteristic function (Fourier transform of the probability density) of the joint detector data distribution; they derive how existing search statistics emerge as asymptotic or approximative limits of this characteristic function and construct new statistics via controlled truncations or expansions of it.

Result: A unified theoretical framework is established: (i) known non-Gaussian searches (e.g., hierarchical Bayesian, burst-like, or higher-moment methods) are shown to correspond to specific approximations of the characteristic function; (ii) new, tunable approximate sufficient statistics are explicitly constructed and validated on an exactly solvable toy model.

Conclusion: The characteristic function formalism provides a rigorous, flexible, and general foundation for designing optimal or near-optimal searches for non-Gaussian astrophysical stochastic backgrounds, bridging and extending current methodologies.

Abstract: The stochastic gravitational-wave background from stellar-mass compact binaries is non-Gaussian since it is made up of a finite number of individual mergers. Over the years, several methods have been developed to search for this background beyond the standard cross-correlation statistic. We reformulate the data analysis problem for such astrophysical backgrounds in terms of the characteristic function of the data in multiple detectors, and show that the existing searches arise as different limiting cases. We also show that new searches can be constructed through different approximations of the characteristic function, which gives a concrete way to engineer suitable approximate sufficient statistics. To illustrate this framework in the context of a simple example, we use the formalism proposed here to analyze an exactly solvable toy model.

</details>


### [82] [Unifying stochastic and deterministic signals: a practical search for the intermittent binary black-hole background](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38766)
*Tore Boybeyi,Vuk Mandic,Joseph Romano*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes a novel likelihood-based search method for detecting the intermittent gravitational-wave background from subthreshold binary black-hole mergers, unifying stochastic and deterministic approaches via higher-order generalized overlap reduction functions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The unresolved population of subthreshold BBH mergers generates an intermittent gravitational-wave background that is guaranteed to exist but remains undetected; current stochastic and deterministic searches are suboptimal because they neglect inter-frequency correlations induced by individual mergers.

Method: A truncated likelihood ratio fully marginalized over extrinsic parameters, representing detector data via approximate sufficient statistics—higher-order cross-moments weighted by generalized overlap reduction functions (ORFs) that extend the standard ORF to capture inter-frequency correlations from individual mergers.

Result: The method captures nearly all Bayesian evidence and population information in those higher-order generalized ORFs; validation on one year of simulated Advanced LIGO data shows a catalog-informed analysis can infer the peak of the BBH redshift distribution.

Conclusion: Standard likelihood functions ignoring inter-frequency correlations are inherently suboptimal; the proposed unified, likelihood-based search enables more sensitive and informative detection of the intermittent GW background from unresolved BBH mergers.

Abstract: Over the past decade, the Advanced LIGO, Advanced Virgo, and KAGRA detectors have detected nearly 400 compact binary mergers, most of which are stellar-mass binary black-hole (BBH) mergers. Nevertheless, most mergers in the Universe are too weak to be individually detected at current sensitivity levels. Since a BBH merger in band is much shorter than the average time between successive mergers, the population of these subthreshold events gives rise to an intermittent background of gravitational waves (GWs). This GW background is guaranteed to exist, but it has not yet been detected with current stochastic or deterministic searches. Here we propose a fast and accurate likelihood-based search for this background, which unifies the standard model-agnostic cross-correlation search and a fully deterministic, template-based search in a single likelihood. Our search is based on a truncated likelihood ratio, which is fully marginalized over the extrinsic parameters of the individual mergers, and which represents the data in two detectors as an expansion in terms of approximate sufficient statistics. These statistics are higher-order cross-moments of the data, weighted by geometrical factors that generalize the standard overlap reduction function (ORF) used in cross-correlation searches. We show that almost all the Bayesian evidence and information on the BBH merger population comes from correlations between frequency bins induced by individual mergers and is captured in the higher-order generalized ORFs. Consequently, any likelihood function that does not take these inter-frequency correlations into account is necessarily suboptimal. We validate our search on one year of simulated Advanced LIGO data, and find that a catalog-informed analysis, which uses information from both the detected and unresolved mergers, can infer the peak of the redshift distribution of the population of BBH mergers.

</details>


### [83] [The General-Relativistic Limit of f(R) Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38795)
*Philippe G. LeFloch,Yue Ma*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proves the convergence of f(R) gravity coupled to a massless scalar field to general relativity (Einstein-massless-scalar theory) in the small-data, asymptotically Euclidean regime near Minkowski spacetime, establishing global existence, geodesic completeness, and uniform convergence of metrics, matter, and effective curvature under a limiting family of f(R) Lagrangians.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously justify general relativity as the low-energy/weak-curvature limit of f(R) gravity — a prominent class of modified gravity theories — by controlling the singular limit where the effective scalar field mass diverges and its evolution degenerates to an algebraic constraint.

Method: Employs the Euclidean-hyperboloidal foliation method and adapted wave coordinates to treat a quasilinear wave-Klein-Gordon system; combines oscillatory estimates, weighted interior/exterior energy bounds, and propagation estimates for deviation from the Einstein trace relation; handles nonlinearities violating the classical null condition.

Result: Global existence and future causal geodesic completeness for the full f(R)-scalar system; uniform convergence (including finitely many derivatives) of physical/conformal metrics and scalar field to the Einstein-massless-scalar development; convergence of the rescaled effective field (nonlinear reparametrization of R) to the scalar curvature of the limiting spacetime.

Conclusion: The Einstein-Hilbert theory is the robust, stable, and globally well-posed relativistic limit of f(R) gravity with massless scalar matter for small, well-prepared asymptotically Euclidean data — resolving analytical challenges posed by high-frequency oscillations, large derivatives, and the degenerate massive-to-algebraic transition of the effective field.

Abstract: We establish the general-relativistic limit of f(R) gravity coupled to a massless scalar field for small, asymptotically Euclidean, well-prepared Cauchy data near Minkowski spacetime. We prove global existence and future causal geodesic completeness, with uniform estimates for the metric, matter, and rescaled effective field as an admissible family of modified Lagrangians f(R) approaches the Einstein-Hilbert Lagrangian R. The physical and conformal metrics and the matter field converge to the limiting Einstein-massless-scalar development, uniformly on the entire future spacetime, together with finitely many space and time derivatives. The effective field, defined by a nonlinear reparametrization of the physical scalar curvature, converges to the scalar curvature of the limiting Einstein development. The main analytical difficulty is that the effective-field mass tends to infinity, while its evolution equation becomes an algebraic trace relation. Small field amplitudes may coexist with high-frequency oscillations and comparatively large derivatives, whose quadratic contributions must be controlled uniformly. Using the Euclidean-hyperboloidal foliation method and adapted wave coordinates, we analyze a quasilinear wave-Klein-Gordon system whose nonlinearities do not satisfy the classical null condition. We combine oscillatory estimates, weighted interior and exterior energies, and propagation estimates for the discrepancy from the Einstein trace relation. Quantitative convergence on compact regions and uniform spacetime decay yield global convergence.

</details>


### [84] [Integrable singularities obstruct Cauchy horizons and mass inflation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38826)
*Francisco Tello-Ortiz*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper challenges the universality of the Poisson-Israel-Ori mass-inflation mechanism—widely seen as supporting strong cosmic censorship—by identifying a class of static, spherically symmetric black-hole interiors with integrable central singularities (ρ ∼ A/r², A > 1/(8π)) that inherently lack Cauchy horizons due to a strictly positive compactness gap (q = 2m/r > 1/2) throughout the trapped region; this gap ensures finite blueshift, bounded in-out coupling, and suppression of mass inflation under null radiation and counter-streaming dust models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the robustness of strong cosmic censorship by examining whether mass inflation—the canonical instability of Cauchy horizons—is unavoidable for all physically admissible black-hole interiors, particularly those violating standard regularity assumptions but satisfying energy conditions and integrability.

Method: Analytical study of spherically symmetric anisotropic black-hole interiors with marginally divergent but integrable density ρ ∼ A/r²; derivation of monotonicity properties of compactness q = 2m/r under NEC and dynamical screening; application of generalized Vaidya and counter-streaming null dust models; exact analysis of the Misner-Sharp mass evolution and its coupling term 2/(r|f|); comparison with the Dray-'t Hooft-Redmount (DTR) relation and Ori’s thin-shell model.

Result: A strictly positive compactness gap (q > 1/2) persists throughout the trapped region, preventing any second zero of the lapse and thus excluding Cauchy horizons; under NEC and screening, 2m/r is non-decreasing along both future null directions; blueshift time remains finite (not divergent); the DTR coupling coefficient 2/(r|f|) is uniformly bounded; mass growth is a priori bounded and e-folding rate is luminosity-limited—not governed by inner-horizon surface gravity.

Conclusion: Integrable-singularity black-hole interiors constitute a physically consistent class for which the Poisson-Israel-Ori mass-inflation mechanism is structurally inoperative, implying that strong cosmic censorship may not be dynamically enforced via mass inflation in all realistic gravitational collapse scenarios.

Abstract: Black-hole interiors with an inner (Cauchy) horizon are unstable through the Poisson-Israel-Ori mass-inflation mechanism, governed by the Dray-'t Hooft-Redmount relation and widely regarded as a dynamical realization of strong cosmic censorship. We show that static, spherically symmetric anisotropic interiors supported by a marginally divergent but integrable density $\rho\sim A/r^2$, with $A>1/(8\pi)$ and mild screening at finite radius, are incompatible with this mechanism. The compactness $q=m/r$ decreases monotonically from a super-critical central value to $1/2$ at the unique event horizon, producing a strictly positive compactness gap throughout the trapped region and excluding any second zero of the lapse. This gap enters the dynamics quantitatively. (i) Under the null energy condition and a dynamical screening condition, $2m/r$ is non-decreasing along both future null directions of a trapped domain, so the gap propagates under nonlinear spherically symmetric evolution. (ii) For ingoing radiation (generalized Vaidya), the gap bounds the blueshift: the advanced time spent in the gapped region is finite, whereas it diverges at a Cauchy horizon. (iii) For counter-streaming null dust, the radiative part $\mu$ of the Misner-Sharp mass obeys exactly $\partial_u\partial_v\mu=2\,\partial_u\mu\,\partial_v\mu/(r|f|)$, of which the DTR relation is the thin-shell form; the in-out coupling $2/(r|f|)$ is uniformly bounded on the interior precisely when the gap is positive. This yields an a priori mass bound and, in the Ori thin-shell model, an e-folding rate bounded by the influx luminosity instead of tending to the inner-horizon surface gravity. Integrable-singularity interiors thus realize a class of sources for which the Poisson-Israel-Ori amplification channel is structurally inoperative in the models in which mass inflation was originally established.

</details>


### [85] [Probing Scalar Polarization with Next-Generation Gravitational-Wave Detectors: The Roles of Detector Geometry and Source Inclination](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38956)
*Sk Md Adil Imam,Macarena Lagos*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper forecasts how next-generation gravitational-wave detectors (Cosmic Explorer and Einstein Telescope) will improve constraints on scalar polarizations in gravitational waves using binary neutron star multi-messenger events, highlighting the critical roles of detector geometry and electromagnetic (EM) priors—especially on orbital inclination—in tightening bounds on the scalar amplitude parameter α_B.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test beyond-GR gravity by constraining non-tensor (scalar) polarizations in gravitational waves using multi-messenger observations of binary neutron stars.

Method: Bayesian parameter estimation forecasts on simulated binary neutron star injections in next-generation detectors (CE, ET) and O5 LIGO-Virgo; incorporates EM-informed priors on sky position, luminosity distance, and orbital inclination; uses the parametrized post-Einsteinian (PPE) formalism with parameters α (tensor amplitude), α_B (scalar amplitude), and β (tensor–scalar phase).

Result: ET improves α_B constraints ≈2× over 2×CE due to its triangular geometry; EM-based inclination prior tightens α_B bounds by ≈56× vs. isotropic prior for a GW170817-like event; EOS assumptions also affect α_B inference but less dominantly than inclination priors.

Conclusion: Detector geometry and high-quality EM external information—particularly on orbital inclination—are essential, complementary levers (alongside sensitivity) for robust scalar-polarization tests in the next decade.

Abstract: Gravitational-wave observations of compact binaries offer a direct way to test whether gravity admits polarizations beyond the two tensor modes of general relativity. We perform a forecast to assess how well future multi-messenger events will constrain the presence of scalar polarizations in gravitational waves. Performing binary neutron star injections, we forecast next-generation constraints on three modified gravity parameters in the PPE formalism: $\alpha$ (tensor amplitude), $\alpha_B$ (scalar amplitude) and $\beta$ (tensor and scalar phase). Cosmic Explorer (CE) and Einstein Telescope (ET) each tighten every parameter by 1--2 orders of magnitude over an O5 LIGO-Virgo network, with ET's triangular geometry giving a persistent $\approx2\times$ improvement over 2$\times$CE on the scalar-mode amplitude $\alpha_B$ in both a GW170817-like and a second, representative BNS. We show that, if the sky position and luminosity distance are known from EM observations, adding an EM-based prior on the orbital inclination $\iota$ narrows the $\alpha_B$ constraint by $\approx56\times$ relative to an isotropic inclination prior, for a GW170817-like event. We also assess how EM inclination priors as well as assumptions on the neutron star equation of state may affect the inference on the scalar amplitude $\alpha_B$. Overall, these results indicate that detector geometry and the availability of well-placed EM external information are both important levers --- alongside raw network sensitivity --- for next decade scalar-polarization tests.

</details>


### [86] [Early-time tails in Kerr black hole ringdown](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39058)
*Ansh Gupta,Naritaka Oshita*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper quantifies the branch-cut contribution from the Green's function tail in black hole ringdown waveforms, showing it reaches ~10% of the quasinormal mode (QNM) amplitude at ringdown onset for Gaussian wave packets with ω₀ ∼ 1/M, with weak spin dependence in the tail itself but stronger spin dependence in QNM excitation; this implies non-negligible impact on spectroscopic accuracy near ringdown onset.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether the nonoscillatory branch-cut tail—often neglected in ringdown modeling—contaminates early-time ringdown analyses and affects black hole spectroscopy accuracy.

Method: Within linear perturbation theory, the authors compute and compare the branch-cut and quasinormal mode contributions to the waveform generated by localized Gaussian wave packets scattering off a Kerr black hole; they define ringdown onset as the earliest time when both contributions jointly reconstruct the full waveform, and evaluate relative amplitudes as functions of mass M, frequency ω₀, and spin.

Result: For ω₀ ∼ 1/M, the branch-cut amplitude is ~10% of the QNM amplitude at ringdown onset, with only mild spin dependence; the unconvolved tail waveform is nearly spin-independent, implying spin dependence in the relative amplitude arises predominantly from QNM excitation efficiency.

Conclusion: The branch-cut contribution is non-negligible near ringdown onset and may bias black hole spectroscopy, especially in high-precision or early-time analyses; models assuming pure QNM superposition require careful validation against full Green’s function structure.

Abstract: Black hole ringdowns are modelled as a superposition of quasinormal modes (QNMs), although the branch cut of the Green's function produces a nonoscillatory tail that may contaminate early-time ringdown analyses. Within linear perturbation theory, we quantify this branch-cut contribution for the scattering of localised Gaussian wave packets by a Kerr black hole with mass $M$. Defining the onset of ringdown as the earliest time at which the QNM and branch-cut contributions reconstruct the waveform, we evaluate the branch-cut amplitude relative to the QNM amplitude. For wave packets with a characteristic frequency $\omega_0\sim 1/M$, the branch-cut amplitude is typically of order $10\%$ of the QNM amplitude at ringdown onset with only a mild dependence on spin. We further find that the tail waveform before convolving with a source is almost independent of spin. The spin dependence of the relative amplitude therefore resides primarily in the excitation of the QNMs. Our results suggest that the branch-cut contribution may affect the accuracy of black hole spectroscopy, particularly near the onset of ringdown.

</details>


### [87] [Complete quasinormal modes of Type-D black holes. II. Extremal Kerr black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39095)
*Changkai Chen,Xiaohua Zhang,Zhoujian Cao,Jiliang Jing*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a double-confluent Heun (HeunD) method to compute quasinormal-mode (QNM) spectra of extremal Kerr black holes directly—without extrapolation—by matching horizon-ingoing and infinity-outgoing solutions while enforcing angular regularity. It reveals expanded QNM spectra on both conventional and unconventional Riemann sheets, traces Schwarzschild QNMs through spin continuation to extremality, identifies novel behavior near the superradiant bound (e.g., vanishing damping for m<0 Class I modes), and confirms spectral completeness and sheet assignments via scattering amplitudes and contour analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the long-standing challenge of computing complete, physically consistent quasinormal-mode spectra for extremal Kerr black holes—where standard perturbative or extrapolative methods fail due to singular coordinate behavior and breakdown of separation of variables—and to unify extremal Kerr QNMs with the previously established Schwarzschild spectrum within a coherent analytic framework.

Method: Develops a double-confluent Heun (HeunD) formulation of the radial Teukolsky equation at extremality; employs uniform asymptotic matching of horizon-ingoing and infinity-outgoing solutions under angular regularity constraints; uses analytic continuation across frequency branch cuts and in black-hole spin parameter to connect Schwarzschild and extremal Kerr spectra; validates frequencies and Riemann-sheet assignments via independent scattering-amplitude calculations and contour-integral spectral counting.

Result: Direct computation of complete extremal Kerr QNM spectra—including previously hidden modes on unconventional Riemann sheets; discovery that unconventional Class I modes (m<0) approach negative superradiant bounds with zero damping at extremality; identification of interspersed finite-damping modes invalidating single overtone cutoff models; verification of spectral completeness in bounded domains; resolution of Riemann-sheet structure and critical accumulation behavior near superradiant thresholds.

Conclusion: The HeunD method successfully integrates extremal Kerr into a unified analytic framework for black-hole QNMs, demonstrating how Schwarzschild mode families reorganize—rather than smoothly deform—into distinct extremal sequences with rich spectral topology, including multi-sheeted structure and accumulation phenomena governed by superradiant physics.

Abstract: We develop a double-confluent Heun (HeunD) method that extends our construction of complete quasinormal-mode (QNM) spectra to extremal Kerr black holes. Matching the horizon-ingoing and infinity-outgoing radial solutions together with angular regularity yields the frequencies directly at extremality, without extrapolation from nonextremal spins. For gravitational perturbations, we obtain an expanded spectrum on both conventional and unconventional Riemann sheets, including additional QNMs revealed by analytic continuation across frequency branch cuts. Continuation in spin connects the extremal modes to our complete Schwarzschild spectrum, including its unconventional branches. We show for the first time that unconventional Class I sequences with $m<0$, continued from unconventional Schwarzschild modes, approach negative superradiant bounds with vanishing damping in the extremal limit. We also identify finite-damping modes interspersed among sequences approaching zero damping, showing that a single overtone cutoff cannot describe the transition to extremality. Independent scattering-amplitude calculations verify representative frequencies and their sheet assignments, while a contour count establishes spectral completeness within a bounded frequency domain. Uniform matching near the superradiant bound further distinguishes critical accumulation from isolated extremal QNMs and resolves the associated Riemann-sheet structure. These results bring extremal Kerr into our unified treatment of black-hole QNM spectra and reveal how the Schwarzschild mode families reorganize in the extremal limit.

</details>


### [88] [Gravitational lensing of gravitational waves by a Kerr black hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39181)
*Jian-hua He*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper simulates gravitational wave lensing by a Kerr black hole using a novel (1+1)D time-domain mode-sum method with hyperboloidal foliation and field redefinition; it finds that strong-field lensing alters the ringdown but not inspiral/merger, validating Schwarzschild approximations for current detector searches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To accurately model strong-field gravitational lensing of GWs by rotating (Kerr) black holes—beyond geometric optics—and assess whether non-spinning (Schwarzschild) approximations suffice for detecting lensing signatures with ground-based interferometers.

Method: A (1+1)-dimensional time-domain mode-sum approach with localized hyperboloidal foliation and static field redefinition to ensure stable, bounded-characteristic-speed evolution on a uniform tortoise grid; incident GWs constructed via plane-wave Weyl tensor projection onto Kinnersley tetrad, extended to arbitrary incidence/polarization using spin-dyad rotations and Wigner D-matrix transformations; evolution of 68,000 coupled (j,m) spin-weighted spherical harmonic modes.

Result: Kerr black holes act as diffraction-limited thick lenses: prompt pulse has spin-independent transverse Gaussian profile; lensing coda encodes spin; binary coalescence shows strong ringdown modification but inspiral/merger waveforms in Kerr vs. Schwarzschild differ negligibly (mismatch < 2.2×10⁻⁵).

Conclusion: Schwarzschild models are highly accurate and computationally efficient surrogates for searching strong-field GW lensing signatures with current ground-based detectors, as lensing-induced deviations are confined to the post-merger ringdown.

Abstract: We simulate the gravitational lensing of gravitational waves (GWs) by a Kerr black hole using a $(1+1)$-dimensional time-domain mode-sum approach. To enable efficient explicit time evolution, we implement a localized hyperboloidal foliation combined with a static field redefinition, ensuring bounded characteristic speeds across an extended, uniform tortoise-coordinate grid. Incident GW initial data are constructed by projecting a plane-wave Weyl tensor onto the Kinnersley tetrad. Through local spin-dyad rotations and Wigner $D$-matrix transformations, our framework accommodates both idealized wave packets and realistic binary coalescence signals at arbitrary incidence and polarization angles. To accurately resolve the lensed waveforms, we evolve over $68,000$ coupled $(j,m)$ modes. Our results reveal that a Kerr black hole acts as a diffraction-limited thick lens, focusing GWs into a forward beam. For idealized wave packets, the primary prompt pulse exhibits a spin-independent transverse Gaussian profile, whereas the subsequent lensing coda is sensitive to the black hole's spin. For binary coalescence signals, strong-field lensing substantially modifies the post-merger ringdown. However, the lensed inspiral and merger phases in Kerr and Schwarzschild backgrounds are virtually identical, yielding waveform mismatches below $2.2\times10^{-5}$ across various inclinations and polarizations. These findings demonstrate that non-spinning Schwarzschild models provide an accurate and computationally economical surrogate to search for strong-field lensing signatures with current ground-based detectors.

</details>


### [89] [Energy-flux noise induced by imperfections of the Gaussian beam in the Li-Baker HFGW detector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39248)
*Lin-Lin Yang,Hao Yu,Pan-Pan Wang,Jin Li,Cheng-Gang Shao*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes energy-flux noise in the Li-Baker high-frequency gravitational wave detector caused by Gaussian beam imperfections modeled as higher-order Laguerre-Gaussian modes; it finds cross-polarized LG modes dominate noise, imposes stringent power-fraction limits (e.g., σ₀₁ < 6.0×10⁻⁴⁹), and proposes a polarization filter to suppress noise while preserving signal.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify and mitigate energy-flux noise induced by Gaussian beam imperfections in the Li-Baker high-frequency gravitational wave (HFGW) detector, which threatens detection sensitivity.

Method: Analytical derivation of noise-to-signal ratio for co- and cross-polarized Laguerre-Gaussian (LGₙₗ) beam modes modeling Gaussian beam imperfections; Monte Carlo simulations to estimate cumulative noise from multiple cross-polarized modes.

Result: Higher-order co-polarized LG modes produce zero tangential energy-flux noise at optimal detection angles (φ = π/2 or 3π/2); cross-polarized LG modes induce significant noise—individual modes require σₙₗ < 10⁻⁴⁶, with LG₀₁ most restrictive (σ₀₁ < 6.0×10⁻⁴⁹); total cross-polarized power must satisfy σ_total < 10⁻⁴⁹.

Conclusion: Polarization filtering is essential to retain only the co-polarized Gaussian beam component, effectively suppressing energy-flux noise without compromising signal integrity—providing concrete design guidance for noise control in Li-Baker HFGW detectors.

Abstract: This paper investigates the energy-flux noise induced by imperfections of the Gaussian beam (GB) in the Li-Baker high-frequency gravitational wave (HFGW) detector. Modeling these imperfections with higher-order Laguerre-Gaussian (LG) beams, we analytically derive the resulting noise-to-signal ratio for both co- and cross-polarized $\mathrm{LG}_{nl}$ modes. It is found that higher-order co-polarized LG beams generate no tangential energy-flux noise at the optimal detection plane ($\phi=\pi/2$ or $3\pi/2$), whereas higher-order cross-polarized LG beams introduce non-negligible noise. Specifically, for an individual higher-order cross-polarized $\mathrm{LG}_{nl}$ mode under our representative parameters, maintaining the noise-to-signal ratio below unity typically requires the power fraction to satisfy $\sigma_{nl}<10^{-46}$, with the $\mathrm{LG}_{01}$ mode imposing the most stringent constraint of $\sigma_{01}<6.0\times10^{-49}$. Monte Carlo simulations suggest that the total power fraction $\sigma_{\mathrm{total}}$ of multiple higher-order cross-polarized $\mathrm{LG}_{nl}$ modes needs to remain below $10^{-49}$. Consequently, we propose employing a polarization filter to retain only the co-polarized component of the GB adopted in the practical experiment, thereby suppressing the noise while preserving signal integrity. These results provide theoretical guidance for noise control in the Li-Baker HFGW detector.

</details>


### [90] [Scalar-induced gravitational waves in spatially covariant gravity with two tensorial degrees of freedom](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39252)
*Jia-Xi Feng,Yang Yu,Yan-Heng Yu,Xian Gao,Qing-Guo Huang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates scalar-induced gravitational waves (SIGWs) in a spatially covariant gravity model with a k-essence scalar field, showing that finite-σ effects and cubic extrinsic-curvature interactions modify the SIGW energy spectrum—even when tensor quadratic action matches GR.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how modified scalar dynamics and non-linear interactions affect scalar-induced gravitational waves (SIGWs) in a spatially covariant gravity framework where the tensor sector mimics GR at quadratic order during radiation domination.

Method: Derives scalar transfer functions at finite σ ≡ C₂N̄ and in the large-|σ| limit; constructs the SIGW source function; incorporates a cubic extrinsic-curvature interaction that alters the source without changing background or quadratic actions; computes fractional energy density Ω_GW for monochromatic and log-normal primordial curvature power spectra.

Result: Finite-σ effects suppress the SIGW spectrum across broad scales compared to the σ → ∞ limit; in that limit, the cubic interaction suppresses Ω_GW at small wavenumbers and enhances it at larger wavenumbers.

Conclusion: Modified scalar dynamics and higher-order gravitational interactions can significantly reshape the SIGW energy spectrum even when the linear tensor propagation is indistinguishable from GR—highlighting the diagnostic power of SIGWs for probing beyond-GR physics in the scalar sector.

Abstract: We study scalar-induced gravitational waves (SIGWs) in a spatially covariant gravity model with two tensorial degrees of freedom, minimally coupled to a $k$-essence scalar field. For the parameter choice considered during radiation domination, the quadratic tensor action coincides with that of general relativity (GR). We derive the scalar transfer functions at finite $\sigma\equiv C_2\bar N$ and in the large-$|\sigma|$ limit. We then construct the source function of SIGWs. We further examine a cubic extrinsic-curvature interaction that modifies the source function of SIGWs without affecting the background or quadratic actions. We calculate the fractional energy density of SIGWs for monochromatic and log-normal primordial curvature power spectra. We find that, for the positive values of $\sigma$ studied, finite-$\sigma$ effects suppress the spectrum over a broad range of scales relative to the result in the $\sigma \rightarrow \infty$ limit. In this limit, the cubic interaction suppresses the spectrum at small wavenumbers and enhances it at larger wavenumbers. These results show that modified scalar dynamics and non-linear interactions can alter the fractional energy density of SIGWs even when the quadratic tensor action is identical to that of GR.

</details>


### [91] [The seeds and silhouettes of optimal gravitational shielding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39262)
*Bruno Le Floch,Philippe G. LeFloch*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs complete vacuum initial data sets with prescribed asymptotically flat exterior geometry—including gluing two Schwarzschild exteriors with small (possibly negative) masses—allowing arbitrarily slow decay and nonzero second fundamental form; it uses localized seed data, constraint error control, harmonic/super-harmonic corrections, and a relative ADM energy-momentum notion to freely control charges, even beyond standard ADM definitions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a constructive theory of optimal localization for vacuum initial data sets beyond harmonic decay, enabling flexible gluing (e.g., Schwarzschild exteriors) while controlling physical charges like ADM mass—even for slowly decaying or non-ADM data.

Method: Constructs localized seed data sets with constraint errors decaying to any finite order; applies projection and stability theorems from companion papers; introduces harmonic and super-harmonic corrections; defines a relative ADM energy-momentum for slowly decaying data; adjusts seeds via harmonic-decaying or compact transverse-traceless terms to control charges.

Result: Existence of complete vacuum initial data sets with prescribed asymptotically flat exterior geometry (including glued Schwarzschild regions), controllable relative and total ADM energy-momentum, arbitrary slow radial decay, and nonzero second fundamental form; establishes a constructive framework for optimal localization beyond harmonic decay.

Conclusion: The construction provides a robust, flexible, and physically meaningful method to generate vacuum initial data with tailored asymptotics and conserved charges—even when standard ADM quantities are undefined—thereby extending the scope of geometric gluing and constraint solving in general relativity.

Abstract: We construct complete vacuum initial data sets with prescribed asymptotically flat exterior geometry, including the gluing of two Schwarzschild exterior regions with small masses (which need not be positive). The construction allows for arbitrarily slow leading decay and nonzero second fundamental form. For leading terms with a fixed radial power, we first construct localized seed data sets whose constraint errors decay to any prescribed finite order, then apply the projection and stability theorems of our companion papers. The resulting vacuum data sets differ from seeds by harmonic terms controlled by Hamiltonian and momentum silhouettes, and by super-harmonic corrections. This difference of harmonic terms is measured by a relative notion of ADM energy-momentum, which is defined even for slowly-decaying data sets that do not admit an ADM energy-momentum. Upon adjusting seed data sets by either harmonic-decaying terms or compact transverse-traceless terms, we freely control this relative ADM charge and, when it exists, the total ADM charge. In particular, when gluing Schwarzschild metrics the total ADM mass can be specified independently of the Schwarzschild mass parameters. Together with our companion papers, these results establish a constructive theory of optimal localization beyond harmonic decay.

</details>


### [92] [Radial pulsations of gravitationally decoupled anisotropic self-bound stars with a generalized linear equation of state](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39346)
*D. Santana,C. Usca,J. Andrade,J. O. Cedeño*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs anisotropic self-bound stellar models via gravitational decoupling (MGD), imposes a generalized linear equation of state, obtains closed-form solutions for specific η values, and analyzes radial stability under two distinct anisotropy-preserving material prescriptions—finding all 24 configurations linearly stable but with eigenvalue dependence on compactness and prescription choice.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how different material responses to anisotropy affect radial stability in self-bound stars, despite identical equilibrium configurations—addressing the gap between static anisotropy and its dynamical implications.

Method: Gravitational decoupling in the minimal geometric deformation (MGD) framework; Tolman IV temporal potential; generalized linear EoS $P_r = \eta(\rho - \rho_s)$; central regularity and Schwarzschild matching to fix geometry; two anisotropy-preserving prescriptions for perturbations (fixed layer-wise anisotropy vs. fixed $\Delta/(\rho+P_r)$); full metric perturbation analysis yielding fundamental mode and three overtones.

Result: Closed-form solutions for $\eta = 1, 1/2, 1/3, 1/4$ (including MIT bag model at $\eta=1/3$); all 24 perturbed configurations (12 backgrounds × 2 prescriptions) yield positive fundamental squared frequencies → linear radial stability; fundamental eigenvalue decreases monotonically with compactness within each family; prescription choice induces directionally varying shifts in eigenvalues; tangential tension and failure of tangential profile diagnostic limit physical viability of some configurations.

Conclusion: Identical anisotropic self-bound equilibria can support distinct radial oscillation spectra depending on the assumed microphysical response to anisotropy; analytical MGD models provide tractable benchmarks for stability studies; stability does not guarantee physical acceptability—kinematic and thermodynamic diagnostics remain essential.

Abstract: We construct anisotropic self-bound stellar configurations through gravitational decoupling in the minimal geometric deformation framework and investigate their stability against linear radial perturbations. Retaining the Tolman IV temporal potential, we impose the generalized linear equation of state $P_r=\eta(\rho-\rho_s)$ on the complete matter distribution. Central regularity and Schwarzschild matching determine the radial geometry and relate the surface density to the compactness and stellar radius. Closed-form solutions are obtained for $\eta=1,1/2,1/3,1/4$, including the MIT bag radial relation when $\eta=1/3$ and $\rho_s=4B$. Since the equilibrium anisotropy does not determine its dynamical response, we introduce two material prescriptions that preserve either the anisotropy of each fluid layer or its ratio to $\rho+P_r$. Including metric perturbations, we compute the fundamental mode and three overtones for twelve equilibrium configurations under both prescriptions. All 24 background response combinations have positive fundamental squared frequencies, establishing linear radial stability within the adopted closures. The fundamental eigenvalue decreases with compactness within each sampled family, while changing the anisotropic response produces shifts whose direction depends on the background. Tangential tensions in several configurations and the failed tangential profile diagnostic of the maximally stiff family limit their physical interpretation. These results provide analytically tractable self-bound backgrounds and demonstrate that identical equilibrium configurations can support distinct radial spectra depending on their anisotropic material response.

</details>


### [93] [Emergence of an acoustic black hole in a lattice model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39369)
*Antonino Flachi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs an acoustic black hole in a lattice-Boltzmann model without imposing an acoustic metric a priori; the emergent geometry is inferred from steady-state flow. A horizon forms, verified via Mach profile and phonon ray tracing, and ringdown matches approximate quasinormal modes of a draining bathtub—deviations arise from cell-scale horizon structure. Two horizon models are compared: a reflective boundary on ideal geometry (2 free parameters) and absorption at the sonic point in the local frame (no free parameters); the latter better fits horizon behavior and non-rotating spectrum. Superradiance is theoretically present due to emergent geometry and horizon absorption, but not observed dynamically; its realization in this lattice model remains unresolved. Lattice effects include fourfold sonic surface anisotropy and cell-scale-determined surface gravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To realize and characterize an acoustic black hole in a fully microscopic, discrete lattice-Boltzmann setting—without presupposing an acoustic metric—to probe horizon physics, quasinormal modes, and superradiance in a controllable, emergent-gravity analog system.

Method: Numerical simulation of a draining vortex in a lattice-Boltzmann fluid; extraction of effective acoustic metric from steady-state velocity field; phonon ray tracing and perturbative ringdown analysis; comparison of two horizon models—(i) reflective boundary on ideal draining-bathtub metric, fitted with reflectivity modulus and phase; (ii) absorption at the sonic point in the local rest frame, parameter-free; spectral analysis for superradiant amplification.

Result: A well-defined acoustic horizon emerges self-consistently; ringdown frequencies approximate those of the ideal draining bathtub, with deviations attributable to finite-cell-scale horizon structure; the local-absorption model outperforms the reflective model in fitting both horizon location and non-rotating QNM spectrum; superradiant amplification is predicted on the measured metric but not observed dynamically; lattice-induced fourfold anisotropy and cell-scale-dependent surface gravity are quantified.

Conclusion: Acoustic black hole phenomenology—including horizons, quasinormal ringing, and superradiance—can emerge from discrete, collision-based hydrodynamics without continuum metric input; however, lattice-scale structure fundamentally modifies horizon absorption and symmetry, necessitating beyond-continuum modeling; dynamic observation of superradiance remains an open challenge in such toy models.

Abstract: We build an acoustic black hole in a lattice model: a draining vortex evolved by the local collide-and-stream rule of a lattice-Boltzmann model, with no acoustic metric imposed, the geometry being measured from the steady state. A horizon emerges, first located from the measured Mach profile and then confirmed by phonon ray tracing on the measured metric, and the ringdown after a perturbation follows approximately the quasinormal frequencies of the draining bathtub. The residual departures are traced to the cell-scale structure of the horizon, which is not an ideal absorber. We model it in two ways: as a reflective horizon on the ideal bathtub, with two free parameters (the modulus and phase of the reflectivity), and as the sonic point of the realised flow absorbing in its own frame, with no adjustable parameter. The second describes the horizon and the non-rotating spectrum best. Superradiance has two ingredients: the emergent geometry, which amplifies in a loss-free calculation on the measured metric, and the horizon, whose absorption frame sets the width and strength of the amplified band but leaves no trace in the ringdown. Both models are used to examine superradiance, which has not been observed in the present toy model: whether the lattice hole superradiates dynamically remains open. The lattice leaves its own imprint as well: a fourfold anisotropy of the sonic surface and a surface gravity set by the cell-scale structure of the horizon.

</details>


### [94] [Horizon Residues, Matter-Trace Winding and Test-Scalar Modes in f(Q,T) Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39370)
*Wen-Xiang Chen,M. Zeeshan Gul*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes a magnetic black hole solution in quadratic non-metricity gravity with a matter-trace coupling f(Q) = Q + αQ² + 16πλT. It finds that, despite a non-zero matter trace ∝ r⁻⁸, the non-metricity scalar Q vanishes on this branch, rendering the quadratic coupling α sterile; the metric acquires a λ-dependent r⁻⁶ correction, and the matter trace’s logarithmic derivative yields a topological winding number of −8 around the singularity—but zero around the horizon—indicating the winding number encodes divisor structure rather than governing backreaction; a degeneracy between λ and the non-linear electrodynamics parameter is identified.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate how matter trace couplings affect local horizon remnants and hairy black hole solutions in quadratic non-metricity gravity, particularly whether topological features (e.g., winding numbers of the matter trace) govern physical observables like temperature or backreaction.

Method: Constructs an exact magnetic black hole solution in coincident Cartesian Kerr–Schild coordinates within f(Q) = Q + αQ² + 16πλT gravity coupled to polynomial non-linear electrodynamics; derives field equations, Euclidean temperature, horizon structure, master scalar equation, and gauge shifts; analyzes topological properties of the matter trace via its logarithmic derivative and contour integrals.

Result: The non-metricity scalar Q vanishes identically on the magnetic branch; the matter trace T ∝ r⁻⁸; the metric receives a λ-dependent r⁻⁶ correction C = ξ(1 − 4λ)q⁴/10; the quadratic coupling α has no effect (sterile); the logarithmic derivative of T yields winding number −8 around the singularity but 0 around the horizon; temperature and scalar propagation remain continuous in λ; a degeneracy emerges between λ and the non-linear electromagnetic coefficient.

Conclusion: The winding number of the matter trace is a topological divisor invariant—not a dynamical driver—and does not supersede the trace in determining backreaction; the analysis reveals a parameter degeneracy and confirms the decoupling of α on this branch, highlighting the subtlety of matter–geometry coupling in non-metricity theories.

Abstract: We extend the analysis of local horizon remnants in quadratic non-metricity gravity to a specific matter trace theory with the functional form f(Q) = Q + alpha Q^2 + 16 pi lambda T.
