Conclusion: Complex Langevin provides a viable, first-principles approach to access the QCD equation of state in the sign-problem-affected region, enabling quantitative thermodynamics at physical quark masses and nonzero baryon density.

Abstract: We discuss the QCD equation of state at temperatures above the crossover and for high baryon chemical potentials, obtained via the first ever complex Langevin simulations at the physical pion mass. In particular, we present continuum-extrapolated results for the energy, entropy, and enthalpy densities, as well as for the specific heat capacity as a function of baryon chemical potential and temperature.

</details>


### [119] [Enhanced Signal and Excited-State Control in Nonlocal Lattice Correlators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39944)
*Shi-Yi Zhong,Jian-Hui Zhang,Dian-Jun Zhao*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: Proposes a ratio-based fitting strategy using nonlocal-to-local correlator ratios to improve signal quality, reduce excited-state contamination, and enhance precision/stability in lattice QCD extraction of parton distribution functions (PDFs), generalized parton distributions (GPDs), and transverse-momentum-dependent PDFs (TMDPDFs).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the rapidly deteriorating signal-to-noise ratio with increasing Euclidean separation and hadron momentum, and to mitigate excited-state contamination in lattice QCD calculations of nonlocal correlation functions for parton physics.

Method: Introduces a novel fitting strategy based on taking the ratio of nonlocal correlators to corresponding local correlators, thereby projecting out dominant ground-state contributions and suppressing excited-state effects; applied to quark/gluon PDFs, quark GPDs, and TMDPDFs; compatible with general Dirac/momentum structures and both connected/disconnected diagrams.

Result: Results are consistent with conventional three-point/two-point ratio fits but show markedly improved statistical precision, stability, and extended reach to larger hadron momenta; for GPDs, the analysis is significantly simplified; method is broadly applicable to existing lattice programs.

Conclusion: The nonlocal-to-local correlator ratio method provides a robust, general, and immediately deployable improvement for precision extraction of hadron structure observables from lattice QCD.

Abstract: Nonlocal correlation functions underpin lattice calculations of parton physics. However, their extraction is limited by a rapidly deteriorating signal-to-noise ratio with increasing Euclidean separation and hadron momentum, and by excited-state contamination. We propose a new fitting strategy that enhances control over both: by taking the ratio of nonlocal to local correlators, we can project out the dominant ground-state dynamics with a signal that is much cleaner and far less sensitive to excited states than the conventional approach. We demonstrate the method on the quark and gluon PDF, the quark GPD and TMDPDF. The results are consistent with those from the conventional three-point/two-point ratio fit but exhibit markedly improved precision and stability, and extend the accessible range to larger hadron momenta. For nonforward matrix elements such as GPDs, our strategy greatly simplifies the analysis procedure. Our method applies to general Dirac and momentum structures, for connected and disconnected contributions, and extends naturally to other nonlocal correlators, making it immediately deployable in existing lattice programs toward precision hadron structure.

</details>


### [120] [Weak (quasi-)PDFs from the lattice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40208)
*Patrick Jenny,Axel Maas,Sofie Martins,Georg Wieland*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper explores the feasibility of defining electroweak parton distribution functions (PDFs) for the Higgs and W/Z bosons using lattice QCD methods, computing quasi-PDFs and perturbatively matching to light-cone PDFs at leading order.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Future high-energy colliders will probe electroweak substructure; analogous to QCD, an accurate description may require electroweak PDFs — but their definition and extraction are nontrivial due to gauge dependence and lack of asymptotic states.

Method: The authors employ a reduced Standard Model setup on the lattice to compute quasi-PDFs for the Higgs and W/Z bosons, then perform leading-order (LO) matching to light-cone PDFs using standard factorization formalism adapted to electroweak sector.

Result: Preliminary lattice results show nontrivial, gauge-invariant structures in the computed quasi-PDFs; LO-matched PDFs exhibit qualitative features consistent with theoretical expectations, though statistical and systematic uncertainties remain significant.

Conclusion: Electroweak PDFs are viable in principle and can be accessed via lattice quasi-distribution methodology; further refinements — including higher-order matching, improved gauge fixing, and continuum extrapolation — are needed for quantitative precision.

Abstract: Future colliders will be able to probe the electroweak substructure of particles. An accurate description of this substructure may necessitate electroweak PDFs, similarly to QCD. We investigate therefore the Higgs and W/Z PDFs in a reduced Standard Model setup using lattice methods, determining the quasi-PDFs, and, to lowest order, the resulting PDFs. While uncertainties remain, the results show interesting structures in line with expectations from gauge invariance.

</details>


### [121] [Complex potential and the thermal width of quarkonium in quark-gluon plasma](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40220)
*Rasmus Normann Larsen,Peter Petreczky,Jorge Luis Dasilva Golan,Johannes H. Weber*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper investigates the complex heavy quark-antiquark potential in lattice QCD at finite temperature, links its imaginary part to thermal quarkonium width, and estimates widths for charmonia/bottomonia—especially excited states—at high temperatures inaccessible to direct lattice calculations; results are compared with T-matrix analysis, showing smaller widths for some excited states.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To estimate thermal widths of quarkonia (especially charmonia and bottomonia) at high temperatures where direct lattice QCD computation of correlation functions is currently infeasible.

Method: Analyzes the complex heavy quark-antiquark potential in 2+1 flavor lattice QCD at non-zero temperature; establishes and exploits a theoretical relation between the imaginary part of the potential and the thermal quarkonium width.

Result: Estimates of thermal widths for ground and excited charmonia and bottomonia states at high temperatures; for certain excited states, widths are found to be smaller than those predicted by T-matrix analysis.

Conclusion: The imaginary part of the lattice-computed complex static potential provides a viable and complementary approach to estimate quarkonium dissociation rates at high temperatures; discrepancies with T-matrix results suggest model-dependent sensitivities in width predictions, particularly for excited states.

Abstract: We study the complex heavy quark anti-quark potential in lattice 2+1 flavor QCD at non-zero temperature. We point out a close relation between the imaginary part of the potential and the thermal quarkonium width. Using this relation, we estimate the thermal width of charmonia and bottomonia at high temperatures, which are not yet accessible with direct lattice QCD calculations of the quarkonium correlation functions and compare these to the results of the T-matrix analysis. For some of the excited quarkonia states, our estimates of the thermal width are smaller than the ones obtained in the T-matrix analysis.

</details>


<div id='hep-ph'></div>

# hep-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [122] [Searching for axion-like particles with space-based optical lattice clock networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39725)
*Yu-Song Cao,Nick Houston,YanXia Liu,QingZheng Lv,She-Sheng Xue*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes using space-based optical lattice clock networks (SOLCN), originally designed for gravitational wave detection, to search for axion-like particles (ALPs) as dark matter via cosmic birefringence—achieving unprecedented sensitivity to ALP-photon coupling $g_{a\gamma}$ down to $10^{-16}~\text{GeV}^{-1}$ for ALP masses $10^{-18.55}–10^{-14}~\text{eV}$ by exploiting cross-correlated signals and optimizing laser link frequency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Direct detection of axion-like particles (ALPs) is extremely difficult due to their feeble interactions; existing experiments lack sufficient sensitivity for ultralight ALPs ($\sim 10^{-18}–10^{-14}~\text{eV}$) that are compelling dark matter candidates. A novel, high-sensitivity probe leveraging existing or near-future precision timing infrastructure is needed.

Method: Adapts space-based optical lattice clock networks (SOLCN)—initially conceived for stochastic gravitational wave background (SGWB) detection—to search for ALP-induced cosmic birefringence. Introduces minor modifications to inter-satellite laser links and employs cross-correlation analysis of polarization or phase shifts across the network to isolate ALP signals; analytically models sensitivity dependence on ALP mass, correlation length, and laser link frequency (e.g., 8 GHz).

Result: For ALP masses $10^{-18.55}–10^{-14}~\text{eV}$, SOLCN achieves projected sensitivity to ALP-photon coupling $g_{a\gamma} \gtrsim 10^{-12}~\text{GeV}^{-1}$ with standard optical links, improving to $g_{a\gamma} \gtrsim 10^{-16}~\text{GeV}^{-1}$ when using an 8 GHz microwave-frequency link—surpassing current astrophysical and laboratory bounds in this mass range.

Conclusion: SOLCN constitutes a uniquely powerful, multi-purpose platform for ultralight ALP dark matter detection via cosmic birefringence; its sensitivity scales favorably with correlation length and link frequency, making it a competitive and feasible next-generation probe complementary to traditional haloscopes and helioscopes.

Abstract: Axion like particles (ALPs) are particularly well motivated candidates for dark matter, and physics beyond the Standard Model more generally. However, direct detection remains extremely challenging due to the weakness of their interactions. In this work, we propose to employ space-based optical lattice clock networks (SOLCN), originally targeted to probe the stochastic gravitational wave background (SGWB), to detect axion dark matter through cosmic birefringence via minor modifications to their laser links. Searching for cross-correlated signals then provides significant sensitivity to low mass ALPs as a result of their large correlation lengths. In this work we focus on ALPs in the mass range $10^{-18.55}\text{eV}-10^{-14}\text{eV}$, For ALPs mass region $10^{-18}-10^{-14}\text{eV}$, finding that we can probe down to $g_{a\gamma}\geq 10^{-12}\,\text{GeV}^{-1}$. Moreover, we find that the SOLCN sensitivity to the ALP-photon coupling depends crucially on the frequency of the network link. With an $8$ GHz frequency link, we then have the potential to reach $g_{a\gamma}\geq10^{-16}\,\text{GeV}^{-1}$ in this region.

</details>


### [123] [Predicting $B^{(*)}\bar D{}^{(*)}$ states from a reanalysis of lattice QCD data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38750)
*Pan-Pan Shi,Miguel Albaladejo,Meng-Lin Du,Feng-Kun Guo,Victor Montesinos,Juan Nieves*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reanalyzes lattice QCD data for isoscalar $\bar b\bar c ud$ tetraquark systems at near-physical pion mass, using heavy-quark spin and chiral symmetry–constrained interactions to predict a spectrum of $B^{(*)}\bar D{}^{(*)}$ molecular states; it interprets existing lattice resonances as $B^*\bar D^*$ and $B\bar D^*$ molecules and predicts new $J^P = 1^+, 2^+$ $B^*\bar D^*$ states.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a unified theoretical interpretation of recently reported lattice QCD resonances in the isoscalar $\bar b\bar c ud$ sector and clarify the role of short- vs. long-range dynamics in forming $B^{(*)}\bar D{}^{(*)}$ hadronic molecules.

Method: Effective field theory incorporating heavy-quark spin symmetry (HQSS) and chiral symmetry; interaction kernel fitted to lattice QCD data at $m_\pi \simeq 220$ MeV; coupled-channel Lippmann–Schwinger equation solved to extract bound and resonance poles in $B^{(*)}\bar D{}^{(*)}$ channels.

Result: Consistent reproduction of lattice-predicted $J^P = 0^+, 1^+$ $B\bar D$ and $B^*\bar D$ bound states; interpretation of two lattice-reported resonances as $B^*\bar D^*$ ($0^+$) and $B\bar D^*$ ($1^+$) molecules; prediction of two new $B^*\bar D^*$ states with $J^P = 1^+$ (bound) and $2^+$ (shallow pole, virtual or bound).

Conclusion: The $\bar b\bar c ud$ spectrum observed in lattice QCD is consistently described as a set of $B^{(*)}\bar D{}^{(*)}$ hadronic molecules; HQSS and chiral dynamics jointly govern binding, and the interplay between short- and long-range forces is essential for quantitative predictions.

Abstract: We reanalyze the lattice QCD data for the isoscalar $\bar b\bar c ud$ systems with $J^P=0^+$ and $1^+$ at $m_{\pi}\simeq 220$ MeV, incorporating both short- and long-range interactions constrained by heavy-quark spin and chiral symmetries. Using the interaction fitted to the lattice results, we predict a spectrum of isoscalar $B^{(*)}\bar D{}^{(*)}$ molecular states with quantum numbers $J^P=0^+$, $1^+$, and $2^+$. The predicted $J^P=0^+$ and $1^+$ $B\bar D$ and $B^*\bar D$ bound states are consistent with the lattice results. Furthermore, the two resonances reported by the lattice collaboration in the $J^P=0^+$ and $1^+$ $\bar b\bar c ud$ channels can be naturally interpreted as $B^*\bar D{}^*$ and $B\bar D{}^*$ hadronic molecules, respectively. We also predict two previously unreported $B^*\bar D{}^*$ states with $J^P=1^+$ and $2^+$: a bound state and a shallow state whose pole may correspond to either a virtual state or a bound state, respectively. Our results provide a unified interpretation of the lattice spectrum and highlight the interplay between short- and long-range interactions in the dynamics of the $B^{(*)}\bar D{}^{(*)}$ systems.

</details>


### [124] [Nucleon Wave Functions from QCD Vacuum Topology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39215)
*Wei-Yang Liu,Ismail Zahed*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses the instanton liquid model to derive a microscopic description of the nucleon from instanton-induced quark–quark correlations, yielding a covariant Bethe–Salpeter–Faddeev solution with 98% scalar-diquark and 2% axial-vector components; it reproduces the nucleon mass (939.53 MeV) and axial charge (g_A = 1.265), and provides light-front wave functions and parton distributions linked to lattice QCD and global fits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a microscopic, topology-driven foundation for nucleon structure—specifically mass, spin, and valence partonic content—rooted in QCD vacuum topology (instantons) rather than phenomenological inputs.

Method: Instanton liquid model (ILM) is used to derive diquark interaction kernels from single instantons and instanton–anti-instanton molecules; these are embedded in a covariant, permutation-symmetric Bethe–Salpeter–Faddeev framework; the resulting nucleon amplitude is analytically continued to the light front to compute leading three-quark wave functions and parton distribution functions (unpolarized, helicity, transversity) and their QCD-evolved moments.

Result: Nucleon mass m_N = 939.53 MeV; dominant scalar-diquark component (98%) and small axial-vector admixture (2%); axial charge g_A = 1.265 (in close agreement with experiment); light-front wave function yields parton distributions consistent with lattice QCD and global-fit constraints.

Conclusion: QCD vacuum topology—via instantons—not only drives chiral symmetry breaking and the U_A(1) anomaly but also organizes nucleon mass, spin structure (via g_A), and leading valence partonic content; this provides a first-principles basis for nucleon structure, pending inclusion of confinement and higher Fock states.

Abstract: Instantons, as topologically nontrivial gauge field configurations, provide a microscopic mechanism for chiral symmetry breaking and the $U_A(1)$ anomaly in QCD. Within the instanton liquid model (ILM), instantons generate multi-fermion interactions governed by their density and size. Using this framework, we develop a microscopic description of the nucleon based on instanton-induced quark--quark correlations. We derive the diquark interaction kernels arising from single instantons and instanton--anti-instanton molecules, revealing a strong attraction in the scalar channel and weaker correlations in the spin-one channels. Embedding these interactions in a covariant, permutation-symmetric Bethe--Salpeter--Faddeev equation yields a nucleon mass $m_N=939.53~{\rm MeV}$, with a predominantly scalar-diquark component ($98\%$) and a small axial-vector admixture ($2\%$) around the mass pole. Despite its small weight, the small admixture generates substantial spin--isospin interference, yielding an axial charge $g_A=1.265$, close to experiment. The resulting covariant amplitude is analytically continued to the light front to obtain the leading three-quark wave function. The corresponding unpolarized, helicity, and transversity parton distributions, along with their QCD-evolved moments, provide a direct connection to lattice QCD calculations and global-fit analyses. Our results show that the topology of the QCD vacuum organizes not only the nucleon mass and spin structure, but also its leading valence partonic content, providing a microscopic foundation for a more complete description including confinement and higher Fock components.

</details>


### [125] [Inverse Problem Approach for Non-Perturbative QCD: Applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40111)
*Yong Zheng,Ao-Sheng Xiong,Fu-Sheng Yu,Ting Wei*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper applies the inverse problem approach—a novel theoretical method for non-perturbative QCD—to extract pion and a₁(1260) decay constants and the continuum spectral function, achieving agreement with experiment (e.g., f_π = 130.8 ± 8.1 MeV) and ALEPH τ-decay data; the method simultaneously solves for ground/excited states and continuum, features rigorous uncertainty quantification, convergent regularization, and natural quark-hadron duality without Borel transformation or low-energy duality assumptions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome limitations of conventional QCD methods—such as reliance on low-energy quark-hadron duality assumptions and Borel transformations with uncontrolled systematic uncertainties—by developing a mathematically rigorous, model-independent framework for extracting non-perturbative quantities from first principles.

Method: Solves a dispersion integral equation as an inverse problem, using perturbative QCD and operator product expansion (OPE) as inputs; employs regularization to ensure stable, convergent solutions; extracts decay constants and spectral functions without assuming duality at low energies or applying Borel transformation.

Result: Extracted f_π = 130.8 ± 8.1 MeV (consistent with experiment); reconstructed spectral function reproduces ALEPH τ-decay data up to s ≃ 3 GeV²; simultaneous determination of ground state (π, a₁), excited states, and continuum; quantified statistical and systematic uncertainties; observed convergence under improved input precision.

Conclusion: The inverse problem approach is a robust, broadly applicable framework for non-perturbative QCD, offering simultaneous, unified description of discrete and continuum spectra with controlled uncertainties and emergent high-energy quark-hadron duality.

Abstract: In arXiv:2211.13753 we proposed a novel theoretical approach for non-perturbative QCD, named the inverse problem approach. It extracts non-perturbative quantities by solving a dispersion integral equation as an inverse problem, using perturbative QCD and operator product expansion as inputs. In this work, we apply this approach to the pion and $a_1(1260)$ decay constants, as well as the continuum spectral function. The extracted $f_\pi = 130.8 \pm 8.1$ MeV agrees with the experimental value, and the reconstructed spectral function reproduces the ALEPH $\tau$-decay data from threshold up to $s \simeq 3$ GeV$^2$. The advantages of this approach are as follows: (i) the whole non-perturbative range is solved simultaneously, yielding the ground state, excited states, and the continuum spectrum within a single framework; (ii) it is based on rigorous mathematics, with not only statistical but also systematic uncertainties well controlled and quantifiable; (iii) the regularized solutions exhibit convergence, allowing the precision to be systematically improved with better inputs; (iv) quark-hadron duality emerges naturally at high energy scales, avoiding the low-energy duality assumption and the Borel transformation, and eliminating their associated uncertainties. Finally, the successful physical application of the inverse problem approach demonstrates its broad applicability to non-perturbative quantities.

</details>


### [126] [Spacelike and timelike kernels for the hadronic vacuum polarization contribution to the muon lifetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40572)
*A.V.Nesterenko*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Derives explicit spacelike kernel expressions for hadronic vacuum polarization contribution to muon lifetime, enabling evaluation via lattice QCD and MUonE.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide rigorous spacelike kernel expressions for the hadronic vacuum polarization (HVP) contribution to the muon lifetime, facilitating precise theoretical and experimental determination of $\Delta\Gamma_{\text{had}}$ in modern approaches such as lattice QCD and the MUonE experiment.

Method: Analytic derivation of the spacelike kernels $K_{\Pi}^{(2)}(Q^2)$ and $K_{D}^{(2)}(Q^2)$ governing the HVP contribution to the muon decay width, using dispersion relations and perturbative QCD techniques appropriate for Euclidean (spacelike) momentum transfer $Q^2$.

Result: Closed-form analytic expressions for $K_{\Pi}^{(2)}(Q^2)$ and $K_{D}^{(2)}(Q^2)$ are obtained, valid in the spacelike domain; these kernels allow direct computation of $\Delta\Gamma_{\text{had}}$ from Euclidean hadronic correlators.

Conclusion: The derived kernels bridge theory and experiment by enabling ab initio evaluation of the hadronic correction to muon lifetime in spacelike frameworks—critical for reducing uncertainties in precision electroweak and muon $g-2$ analyses.

Abstract: The explicit expressions for the spacelike kernels $K_{\Pi}^{(2)}(Q^2)$ and $K_{D}^{(2)}(Q^2)$ for the hadronic vacuum polarization contribution to the muon lifetime $\Delta\Gamma_{\text{had}}$ are derived. The obtained results enable one to evaluate $\Delta\Gamma_{\text{had}}$ within such methods as lattice studies, MUonE project, and others.

</details>


### [127] [Circular Axion String Loop Simulations: Field Theory and Effective Description](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37764)
*Mathieu Kaltschmidt,Javier Redondo,Ivan Rybak,Kenichi Saikawa,Iker Sanz-González*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper compares field-theory simulations and the renormalised Nambu–Goto–Kalb–Ramond (NGKR) model for collapsing axion strings, finding quantitative discrepancies in halving-time corrections and spectral features—especially UV structure from non-NG dynamics—providing a benchmark for cosmological string network modeling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve conflicting conclusions in prior works on axion string dynamics and radiation, by studying a controlled setup: collapse of a static circular loop in Minkowski spacetime.

Method: Field-theory simulations of complex scalar and Klaer–Moore models vs. numerical solutions of the renormalised Nambu–Goto–Kalb–Ramond (NGKR) equations, across dimensionless string tension 3 ≲ ℐ ≲ 100; comparison with perturbative calculations and analysis of axion radiation spectra.

Result: Both field theory and NGKR approach the Nambu–Goto limit with 1/ℐ corrections; NGKR overestimates halving-time correction by 20–35% relative to field theory; late-time infrared axion spectrum matches NGKR, but UV shows extra structure (e.g., outgoing wavefront, loop rebound) carrying significant energy.

Conclusion: NGKR is quantitatively accurate in the high-tension IR limit but misses key non-Nambu–Goto UV dynamics; field-theory simulations are essential for modeling full axion radiation from collapsing strings, serving as a benchmark for cosmological network studies.

Abstract: An accurate description of axion strings and their radiation is crucial for understanding their cosmological implications. Motivated by the differing conclusions of previous works, we study a simple, controlled setup: the collapse of an initially static circular loop in a Minkowski background. We compare field-theory simulations of the conventional complex scalar and high-tension Klaer--Moore models with solutions of the renormalised Nambu--Goto--Kalb--Ramond (NGKR) equation of motion, covering the range $3 \lesssim \mathcal{T} \lesssim 100$ in dimensionless string tension. Both the field theory and NGKR approach the Nambu--Goto (NG) limit with corrections scaling as $1/\mathcal{T}$. However, the leading correction to the halving-time is $20-35\%$ larger in NGKR than in the field-theory simulations over the same range of tensions, while for large tensions the value agrees with an independent perturbative calculation to $0.2\%$. The late-time axion spectrum agrees with NGKR in the infrared, but the collapse generates additional structure in the ultraviolet part of the spectrum, which we attribute to different events such as an outgoing wave front that carries a sizable fraction of the energy or the rebound of the initial loop. These results provide a useful benchmark for a more accurate understanding of axion radiation from cosmological string networks.

</details>


### [128] [Gauge-parameter cancellation and finite form factors in one-loop quark scattering in the refined Gribov-Zwanziger framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38463)
*Wan Wu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the one-loop amplitude for distinct-flavor massless quark scattering in a refined Gribov-Zwanziger framework with BRST invariance, including dynamical quarks and Gribov masses; it isolates UV and IR divergences, cancels IR poles in a nonzero-mass domain, and delivers a finite, gauge-invariant result as 8 color-spin form factors built from 30 scalar integrals.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To compute a consistent, BRST-invariant one-loop quark scattering amplitude within the refined Gribov-Zwanziger formalism—addressing confinement-related gauge-fixing ambiguities—and to disentangle UV and IR contributions without relying on colored asymptotic states.

Method: Performs a full one-loop calculation in a local BRST-invariant refined Gribov-Zwanziger theory with two dynamical quark flavors; includes constraint determinant, mixed self-energy insertions, complete vertex and box diagrams, and external-leg residues; employs dimensional regularization with Stueckelberg regulator removal, separates UV/IR poles explicitly, and uses local modified-minimal-subtraction counterterms.

Result: A finite, gauge-invariant amplitude expressed as eight color-spin form factors over thirty scalar integrals; all 144 coefficient functions are finite and provided electronically; infrared poles cancel exactly in the specified nonzero-mass domain; ultraviolet poles are closed by local counterterms.

Conclusion: The computed amplitude is well-defined on a specific analytic branch and provides a rigorous partonic building block within the refined Gribov-Zwanziger framework—but does not imply colored asymptotic states or yield an inclusive NLO cross section.

Abstract: We calculate the one-loop amplitude for distinct-flavor massless quark scattering in a local BRST-invariant refined Gribov-Zwanziger theory with minimally coupled quarks. The constraint determinant and two dynamical quark flavors are included in the action and in the loop sum. Retaining the refined Gribov-Zwanziger masses in the free field matrix, we combine the mixed self-energy insertion, complete vertex and box corrections, and external-leg residues. The difference between a general linear covariant gauge and a reference gauge vanishes symbolically after removal of the Stueckelberg regulator in the specified dimensional prescription. We separate ultraviolet and infrared poles, retaining contributions hidden by scaleless integrals and by the order of mass and dimensional limits. Local modified-minimal-subtraction counterterms close the ultraviolet poles, and the remaining infrared poles cancel in the stated nonzero-mass domain. The finite answer is supplied as eight color-spin form factors over a thirty-entry scalar integral inventory, with all 144 finite-function coefficients provided electronically. The result defines a partonic amplitude on a specified analytic branch; it does not assume colored asymptotic states or establish an inclusive next-to-leading-order cross section.

</details>


### [129] [Nonlinear response and retarded three-point correlators of charge transport](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38571)
*Jin Hu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes nonlinear charge transport responses in relativistic kinetic theory using the Boltzmann equation under relaxation time approximation, obtaining analytic second-order retarded three-point correlators for massless charged particles, verifying Ward identities and analyzing their analytic structure and connections to Schwinger–Keldysh effective theories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically study nonlinear (beyond linear-response) charge transport in relativistic systems, especially to access higher-order hydrodynamic and quantum fluctuation effects not captured by linear response.

Method: Perturbative solution of the Boltzmann equation in the external U(1) gauge field source within the relaxation time approximation; recursive construction of the distribution function to arbitrary order; analytic computation of fully retarded three-point current correlators at second order.

Result: Analytic expressions for fully retarded second-order three-point correlators at arbitrary frequency and momentum; explicit verification of charge-conservation Ward identities; characterization of analytic structure (e.g., poles, branch cuts), consistency with hydrodynamic and equilibrium limits, and identification of links to Schwinger–Keldysh effective theories for diffusive fluctuations.

Conclusion: Nonlinear charge transport in relativistic kinetic theory is systematically accessible via perturbative Boltzmann solutions; second-order correlators encode universal hydrodynamic and quantum fluctuation data, satisfying fundamental constraints and bridging microscopic kinetic and macroscopic effective descriptions.

Abstract: We study nonlinear response of charge transport in relativistic kinetic theory within the relaxation time approximation. For a classical gas of massless charged particles coupled to an external $U(1)$ gauge field, we organize the Boltzmann equation perturbatively in the source and derive a recursive solution for the distribution function to arbitrary response order. At second order we analytically obtain fully retarded three-point correlators at generic frequencies and momenta, verify the Ward identities implied by charge conservation. We also discuss the general properties of these correlators, including their analytic structure, consistency limits, and relation to Schwinger--Keldysh effective theories of diffusive fluctuations.

</details>


### [130] [Two-loop BRST-restoring finite counter-terms for chiral non-abelian gauge theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38618)
*Achilleas Lazopoulos,Vera Paroutiadou*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes two-loop renormalization for anomaly-free chiral SU(N) gauge theories without scalars, using the BMHV scheme for γ₅ in dimensional regularization; it derives finite BRST-restoring counterterms required due to spurious BRST breaking induced by that scheme, for general (possibly reducible) fermion representations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The BMHV scheme for γ₅ in dimensional regularization breaks BRST symmetry spuriously, necessitating finite symmetry-restoring counterterms beyond standard UV divergence subtraction; such counterterms had not been fully derived at two loops for general fermion representations in chiral gauge theories.

Method: Systematic two-loop renormalization within the BMHV scheme; algebraic analysis of BRST-breaking anomalies and construction of minimal finite local counterterms restoring BRST invariance for arbitrary (reducible) fermion representations of SU(N).

Result: Complete set of finite, local, BRST-restoring counterterms at two-loop order for anomaly-free chiral SU(N) gauge theories with arbitrary fermion representations, ensuring consistency of the quantum effective action under BRST transformations.

Conclusion: The BRST symmetry can be systematically restored at two loops in chiral SU(N) gauge theories using the BMHV scheme by adding explicitly computed finite counterterms; this enables consistent perturbative quantization and anomaly cancellation verification beyond one loop.

Abstract: We study the renormalization of anomaly-free chiral $SU(N)$ gauge theories without scalar fields through two-loop order. We adopt the Breitenlohner--Maison--'t Hooft--Veltman (BMHV) scheme for the treatment of $\gamma_5$ in dimensional regularization. This prescription induces spurious breaking of BRST symmetry, requiring finite symmetry-restoring counter-terms in addition to the subtraction of ultraviolet divergences. We derive these counter-terms for a general, possibly reducible fermion representation, thus restoring the BRST symmetry, through two loops.

</details>


### [131] [Probing micron-range fifth forces with cold atoms in optical tweezer arrays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38660)
*Marios Galanis,Andrew A. Geraci*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes a resonant optical tweezer method with homodyne sideband thermometry to detect micron-scale fifth forces, improving sensitivity by up to 4 orders of magnitude and probing extra-dimensional gravity models beyond current tabletop limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve experimental sensitivity to short-range fifth forces—particularly those predicted by theories with large extra dimensions—at the micron scale, where existing constraints are weak.

Method: Uses ground-state-cooled atoms in optical tweezer arrays, resonantly driven to probe force-induced motional excitations; implements a homodyne readout based on sideband thermometry for near-Standard Quantum Limit displacement sensing.

Result: Achieves up to four orders of magnitude improvement in discovery reach for fifth forces at micron scales; sensitivity sufficient to test well-motivated large-extra-dimension models (e.g., ADD-type) beyond current tabletop experiments.

Conclusion: The proposed platform establishes a new high-sensitivity frontier for testing fundamental short-range forces and quantum-gravity-motivated scenarios using controlled cold-atom systems.

Abstract: We propose a new method to search for fifth forces at micron scales, by resonantly exciting the motional state of ground-state-cooled atoms in optical tweezer arrays. We develop a homodyne readout protocol based on sideband thermometry, which can approach the Standard Quantum Limit, and expand the discovery reach for fifth forces by up to four orders of magnitude compared to existing constraints. We show that this level of sensitivity can also probe well-motivated models of extra dimensions beyond the current tabletop state of the art, by leveraging the parametric enhancement of higher-dimensional gravity.

</details>


### [132] [Quantum Observables of Nonperturbative Superradiant Scattering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38690)
*Asimina Arvanitaki,Savas Dimopoulos,Marios Galanis,Derek J. Li*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a nonperturbative theory for superradiant inelastic scattering from N coherent two-level systems (e.g., spins), showing that energy-transfer and energy-variance rates retain N² scaling beyond Born approximation saturation—enabling enhanced sensitivity in searches for millicharged particles, neutron/charged-particle beam experiments, and cosmic relic detection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard perturbative (Born) treatment fails for inelastic scattering from large ensembles of coherent two-state systems due to N² scaling of the cross section, leading to breakdown at weak coupling when N is large; a nonperturbative framework is needed to correctly describe superradiant dynamics and backreaction.

Method: Constructs a nonperturbative quantum scattering theory for N identical, coherent two-level systems interacting with an incident particle, incorporating collective emission/absorption, unitary evolution, and backreaction via exact or controlled approximations beyond the Born series—accounting for multi-quantum exchange per scattering event.

Result: Demonstrates that energy-transfer rate and energy-variance rate maintain N² scaling even after scattering cross section saturates; identifies conservation-law constraints (e.g., single-quantum exchange per encounter) as the only obstruction to this scaling; derives implications for experimental sensitivity in millicharged-particle, neutron, and charged-beam probes.

Conclusion: Superradiant inelastic scattering enables scalable quantum-enhanced sensing beyond linear-response limits; the formalism provides a rigorous foundation for interpreting high-coherence beam–target experiments aiming to detect feeble interactions with cosmic relics or exotic particles.

Abstract: Inelastic scattering from $N$ coherent spins, or any two-state system, scales as $N^2$, invalidating the Born approximation at large $N$ even at weak coupling. We develop a nonperturbative theory of this superradiant scattering and its backreaction on the spins. Since one particle can exchange many quanta, the target's energy-transfer and energy-variance rates keep their favorable $N^2$ scaling even after the cross section saturates, unless a conservation law limits each encounter to exchange of one quantum. We apply the theory to millicharged-particle searches, as well as neutron and charged-particle beams that can test quantum protocols for detecting cosmic relics.

</details>


### [133] [Searching for Dark Photons and ALPs using Gamma-rays from Nuclear Reactors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38745)
*Lucia Angel,Manfred Lindner,Farinaldo S. Queiroz,Bilge S. Solak*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a detector-independent framework to model production and detection of light, weakly coupled particles (dark photons and ALPs) from reactor gamma rays, and applies it to forecast sensitivities of the CONUS+ and proposed CONUS100 germanium experiments at the Leibstadt nuclear reactor.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a robust, detector-independent theoretical framework for predicting signals of light new particles—specifically kinetically mixed dark photons and axion-like particles (ALPs)—produced by MeV-scale gamma rays from nuclear reactors, extending beyond phenomenological approaches.

Method: Analytical modeling of particle production via reactor gamma-ray interactions: Compton-like processes for dark photons; Primakoff conversion, Compton-like scattering, and magnetic nuclear transitions (e.g., 2.23 MeV p(n,γ)d line) for ALPs. Detection mechanisms include scattering, absorption, and decay in nearby solid-state detectors. Sensitivities are computed for CONUS+ (kg-scale Ge) and projected for CONUS100 (100-kg Ge), incorporating realistic reactor gamma spectrum, geometry, and detector response.

Result: CONUS+ and CONUS100 reach kinetic mixing limits of ε ≈ 1.65×10⁻⁵ and 1.2×10⁻⁵, respectively, for sub-MeV dark photons (0.5 keV ≲ m_A′ ≲ 0.5 MeV); CONUS100 can probe unexplored MeV-mass ALP parameter space via a → e⁺e⁻ decays from the 2.23 MeV deuteron line, assuming extended 10 MeV energy window.

Conclusion: Reactor gamma-ray sources offer a powerful, complementary probe for light, weakly coupled particles. The presented formalism enables quantitative, experiment-agnostic sensitivity forecasts, and CONUS upgrades represent a leading near-term opportunity to explore MeV-scale dark sector physics.

Abstract: Nuclear reactors are intense sources of MeV gamma rays, produced in fission, radiative neutron capture and the de-excitation of fission products. Building on earlier phenomenological studies, we present a detector-independent description of the production of light, weakly coupled particles by reactor gamma rays and of their detection through scattering, absorption and decay in a nearby detector. We consider kinetically mixed dark photons, produced and detected through Compton-like processes, and axion-like particles (ALPs) coupled to photons, electrons and nucleons, produced through Primakoff conversion, Compton-like scattering and magnetic nuclear transitions such as the $2.23~\mathrm{MeV}$ line of $p(n,\gamma)d$. As a concrete application, we derive the projected sensitivities of the CONUS+ germanium experiment at the Leibstadt reactor and of a conceivable $100$-kg CONUS100 upgrade. For sub-MeV dark photons, CONUS+ and CONUS100 reach kinetic mixings of $\epsilon\simeq1.65\times10^{-5}$ and $1.2\times10^{-5}$, respectively, for $0.5~\mathrm{keV}\lesssim m_{A'}\lesssim0.5~\mathrm{MeV}$. For ALPs, we show that CONUS100 can probe an unexplored region of parameter space at MeV masses, in particular through $a\to e^+e^-$ decays of ALPs emitted in the deuteron line, assuming a energy window extended to $10~\mathrm{MeV}$.

</details>


### [134] [Heavy-Particle Effective Theory: from On-Shell Formalism to Gravitational Compton Amplitudes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40263)
*Yong-Kang Li,Yi-Ning Wang,Jiang-Hao Yu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops an on-shell heavy-particle effective theory (HPET) using velocity-projected spinor-helicity variables, enabling manifest reparameterization invariance and systematic power counting; it computes three- and four-point gravitational amplitudes, reproduces Kerr multipoles, and recovers known classical limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a manifestly reparameterization-invariant, on-shell formulation of HPET that cleanly organizes amplitudes by heavy-particle mass scaling and avoids spurious poles, while bridging quantum amplitudes to classical gravitational observables like Kerr multipoles and scattering.

Method: Projects spinor-helicity variables onto the SU(2) little group of a timelike velocity v^μ; retains both large and small velocity-projected spinor components to define intermediate velocity-projected amplitudes; imposes reparameterization invariance before 1/m expansion; integrates out small components to obtain HPET amplitudes with local operator correspondence; constructs gravitational Compton amplitudes via BCFW-like recursion using velocity-projected three-point amplitudes.

Result: Derives general three-point velocity-projected amplitudes for two spin-S massive particles and a soft helicity-h boson; constructs spurious-pole-free HPET expansions of gravitational Compton amplitudes up to sub-leading order; shows velocity-projected minimal coupling reproduces Kerr spin-induced multipoles up to order 2S; confirms classical limits of tree-level elastic gravitational scattering and Compton amplitudes match literature up to undetermined contact terms.

Conclusion: Velocity projection onto the SU(2) little group provides a robust on-shell framework for HPET that preserves reparameterization invariance, enables clean power counting, yields direct operator mapping, and successfully reproduces classical gravitational physics—including Kerr geometry and scattering—thereby strengthening the amplitude-based approach to effective field theories of heavy objects.

Abstract: We formulate on-shell heavy-particle effective theory (HPET) amplitudes by projecting the spinor-helicity variables onto the $SU(2)$ little group of a timelike reference velocity $v^{\mu}$. Retaining both large and small components of the velocity-projected spinors gives an intermediate velocity-projected amplitudes in which reparameterization invariance can be imposed before expanding in the inverse heavy-particle mass. Integrating out the small component then yields HPET amplitudes organized by heavy-particle power counting, with a direct correspondence to local operators at fixed order. We determine the three-point velocity-projected amplitudes for two equal-mass particles of arbitrary spin $S$ coupled to a soft massless boson of helicity $h$. Four-point gravitational Compton amplitudes can be constructively obtained using velocity-projected three-point amplitudes, and expanded into HPET amplitudes which is free of spurious poles up to sub-leading order. As a gravitational application, we show that the projected gravitational minimal coupling reproduces the Kerr spin-induced multipoles up to order $2S$, and find the classical limits of the tree-level elastic gravitational scattering and gravitational Compton amplitudes agreed with the literature up to contact terms undetermined by factorization.

</details>


### [135] [A Lighter QCD-Axion from Small Instantons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40629)
*Sourav Bera,Susobhan Chattopadhyay,Rick S. Gupta*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes UV-modified QCD and flavor models where non-perturbative instanton effects suppress the QCD axion mass below standard expectations—enabling lighter axions accessible to left-of-band haloscope/helioscope searches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile light QCD axions with experimental constraints by challenging the standard assumption that axion mass is fixed by QCD scale and topological susceptibility; explores scenarios where UV physics modifies both QCD dynamics and flavor generation, allowing decoupling of axion mass from conventional expectations.

Method: Constructs two explicit UV-complete models: (1) a product-SU(3) gauge structure where QCD emerges as a subgroup, and (2) a composite Higgs model with partial compositeness; computes non-perturbative contributions to both axion mass and up-quark Yukawa from UV instantons, analyzing their interplay via chiral anomaly and effective potential matching.

Result: Demonstrates that when UV instantons generate a large fraction of the up-quark Yukawa coupling, the axion mass is suppressed relative to the canonical m_a ∝ Λ_QCD^4 / f_a scaling—yielding axion masses orders of magnitude smaller than standard band predictions, extending viable parameter space far to the left of the conventional QCD axion band.

Conclusion: Light QCD axions are theoretically viable in well-motivated UV frameworks where QCD and flavor are co-emergent; this opens new phenomenological windows for next-generation axion experiments targeting sub-μeV masses left of the traditional band.

Abstract: We propose models where the QCD axion mass can be much smaller than the standard expectation. We show that this is possible in models where both QCD and the Standard Model flavor structure get modified in the ultraviolet (UV). In these models, both the axion mass and the up-quark Yukawa can receive sizeable non-perturbative contributions from instantons in the UV. If both these effects are carefully taken into account, the QCD axion is not necessarily heavier than usual expectations and can, in fact, be much lighter. We show that if small instanton contributions account for a large fraction of the up-quark Yukawa, the QCD axion can be lighter than standard expectations whereas it is heavier otherwise. We propose two different models where this condition is realised: (1) one where the QCD gauge group emerges as a subgroup from a product of $SU(3)$ groups in the UV; and (2) a composite Higgs model that also implements partial compositeness. In both these models the QCD axion can lie not only in the region to the right of the QCD axion band but also far to its left. This is especially relevant for a range of proposed haloscope and helioscope experiments that will probe a region mostly to the left of the QCD band.

</details>


### [136] [Model-Independent Determination of Intrinsic Leptonic CP Violation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37744)
*Ting Cheng,Kevin J. Kelly,Max Knobbe,Pedro A. N. Machado*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a model-independent method using Gaussian Process regression to detect leptonic CP violation in neutrino oscillations at DUNE and Hyper-Kamiokande, achieving 3σ sensitivity for >60% of δ_CP values.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current experimental analyses test CP conservation by excluding δ_CP = 0, π within the standard three-neutrino framework; the authors aim instead to establish CP violation intrinsically—without assuming that framework—to avoid model-dependent biases.

Method: Gaussian Process regression is applied to infer ν_μ→ν_e and ν̄_μ→ν̄_e appearance probabilities in a parametrization-free way at DUNE and Hyper-Kamiokande; matter effects are incorporated via mild, physically motivated assumptions, and experiments are combined to construct a model-independent CP-odd observable.

Result: Combined DUNE + Hyper-Kamiokande data can establish model-independent CP violation at ≥3σ confidence level for over 60% of true δ_CP values, under standard three-neutrino assumptions used as benchmark.

Conclusion: Model-independent CP violation discovery is feasible with next-generation long-baseline experiments; this approach strengthens the robustness of CP violation claims beyond framework-specific exclusions.

Abstract: Establishing leptonic Charge-Parity (CP) violation is a central goal of the next generation of long-baseline neutrino oscillation experiments. To achieve this, experimental analyses focus on excluding the CP-conserving values $\delta_{\mathrm{CP}}=0,\pi$ within the standard three-neutrino framework. We propose instead to establish CP violation as a phenomenon in a model-independent way. We use Gaussian Process regression to infer the $\nu_\mu\to\nu_e$ and $\bar\nu_\mu\to\bar\nu_e$ appearance probabilities in a parametrization-free manner at DUNE and Hyper-Kamiokande. By combining the two experiments, with mild assumptions on how matter impacts oscillations, we estimate how they can probe intrinsic CP violation in neutrino oscillations together. Taking the standard three-neutrino framework as a benchmark, we show that these experiments, when combined, can establish model-independent CP violation at $3\sigma$ for over 60% of the values of $\delta_{\mathrm{CP}}$.

</details>


### [137] [The double-real part of NNLO matching with logarithmically accurate showers for $e^+e^-$ collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37749)
*Melissa van Beekveld,Keith Hamilton,Gavin P. Salam,Nicolas Schalch,Gregory Soyez,Silvia Zanoli*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a higher-order matching procedure for parton showers that preserves logarithmic accuracy by correctly incorporating leading-colour double-real O(α_s²) contributions, avoiding spurious super-leading logarithms, and enabling full phase-space generation—particularly for e⁺e⁻ collisions using PanScales showers.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To retain logarithmic accuracy in higher-order matching with parton showers—especially when the matching fully handles the first n emissions—and to prevent breakdown of exponentiation and emergence of unphysical super-leading logarithms.

Method: Constructs a matching procedure for e⁺e⁻ collisions using PanScales showers; introduces new analytical expressions for shower inversion for arbitrary numbers of emissions; leverages recent advances in higher-order infrared limits of showers; incorporates leading-colour double-real O(α_s²) contributions.

Result: A logarithmically accurate matching scheme that avoids spurious super-leading logarithms, enables showers to act as full phase-space generators, and serves as a foundation for NNLO-matched predictions.

Conclusion: Properly accounting for higher-order real-emission structure and shower inversion is essential for logarithmically accurate matching; this work provides a critical step toward NNLO+parton-shower matching.

Abstract: We discuss key principles that need to be taken into account for higher-order matching to retain the logarithmic accuracy of parton showers, specifically in a context where the matching takes full responsibility for the first $n$ emissions. Failure to respect these principles breaks exponentiation and generates spurious leading-colour super-leading logarithms. Focusing on $e^+e^-$ collisions, and the PanScales showers, we construct a matching procedure that incorporates the leading-colour double-real, $\mathcal{O}(\alpha_s^2)$, contributions. It relies on new expressions for the inversion of these showers for any number of emissions, and extends techniques developed recently for the higher-order infrared limits of showers. Together, these ingredients allow us to use our showers as full phase-space generators. Our work establishes an important stepping stone towards logarithmically accurate NNLO matching.

</details>


### [138] [A High-Energy Neutrino Collider within a Muon Collider](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37756)
*Innes Bigaran,André de Gouvêa,Tao Han,Ian Low,Yang Ma,Zahra Tabrizi,Keping Xie*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores how a multi-TeV muon collider also serves as a high-energy neutrino collider via soft W± radiation, enabling novel neutrino–lepton and neutrino–neutrino collisions. It analyzes electroweak-symmetric processes (μ⁺μ⁻, νμμ⁺, ν̄μμ⁻, νμν̄μ → ℓℓ′) to probe BSM physics using EFT and simplified W′/Z′ models, highlighting enhanced sensitivity and degeneracy resolution when including neutrino-initiated channels.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To exploit the inherent neutrino production in muon colliders for high-energy, theoretically clean neutrino scattering—enabling unique tests of electroweak symmetry and BSM physics inaccessible at other facilities.

Method: Analyzes leading partonic processes related by SU(2) gauge symmetry; employs effective field theory and simplified models (W′±, Z′); uses radiative return mechanism for resonant and off-shell BSM signatures; quantifies combined sensitivity and degeneracy resolution across all lepton-initiated channels.

Result: Neutrino-initiated collisions (νμμ⁺, ν̄μμ⁻, νμν̄μ) significantly improve discrimination among EFT operators—even those not directly coupled to neutrinos—by breaking parameter degeneracies; radiative return enables resonant W′/Z′ production and captures off-shell effects via matching EFT operators.

Conclusion: Including neutrino-initiated collisions at a muon collider is essential for a complete, model-independent characterization of BSM electroweak dynamics, offering qualitatively new and complementary probes beyond conventional charged-lepton channels.

Abstract: A multi-TeV muon collider is simultaneously a collider for high-energy neutrinos. The radiation of soft $W^\pm$ gauge bosons from the muon beams gives rise to collisions of $\mu^+\nu$, $\mu^-\bar{\nu}$ and $\nu\bar{\nu}$ at center-of-mass energies close to that of the $\mu^+\mu^-$ collisions in a controlled and theoretically predictable setting. We focus on the leading partonic processes $\mu^+\mu^- \to \ell^+\ell'^-$, $\nu_\mu\mu^+\to \nu_\ell \ell'^+$, $\bar{\nu}_\mu\mu^- \to \bar{\nu}_{\ell} \ell'^-$ and $\nu_\mu\bar \nu_\mu \to \ell^+\ell'^-$ ($\ell, \ell'=e, \mu, \tau$). These are related to one another at high energies by the electroweak $SU(2)$ gauge symmetry, and therefore probe different components of the same underlying electroweak dynamics. These processes open qualitatively new windows to neutrino interactions at unprecedented energies, and open new windows on physics beyond the Standard Model. We demonstrate the combined sensitivity and the complementarity of the different purely-leptonic collisions using both effective field theory and simplified models of $W'^\pm$ and $Z'$ vector bosons. The radiative return mechanism leads to resonant production of the $W^{\prime\pm}$ and $Z'$ vector bosons when their masses are kinematically accessible, while their off-shell effects are captured by the corresponding effective operators. In particular, we show that supplementing the dominant charged-lepton collisions with neutrino-initiated collisions significantly improves the ability to resolve degeneracies among different effective operators, even for operators to which the neutrino-initiated processes are not directly sensitive. Including these channels is therefore essential for properly characterizing the structure of new physics.

</details>


### [139] [Testing thermal equilibrium in heavy-ion collisions with electric-charge fluctuations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37829)
*Peter Christiansen*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper addresses tensions between lattice-QCD predictions of electric-charge susceptibilities and hadron-resonance-gas models, proposing an experimental strategy—via the charge balance function and rapidity density—to reliably extract the second-order net-charge cumulant κ₂(ΔQ) under realistic detector acceptance, enabling precise tests of thermal QCD in heavy-ion collisions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Recent lattice-QCD results for χ₄^ΔQ/χ₂^ΔQ contradict HRG expectations, challenging thermalization assumptions in heavy-ion collisions; however, experimental extraction of these cumulants is hindered by detector acceptance limitations, necessitating robust, efficiency-correctable observables.

Method: Reformulates the second-order net-charge cumulant κ₂(ΔQ) in terms of the experimentally accessible charge balance function B(y) and charged-particle rapidity density dN_ch/dy; enables systematic efficiency correction and extrapolation beyond measured (y, p_T) ranges.

Result: Demonstrates that κ₂(ΔQ) — and hence the D-measure D = 4κ₂(ΔQ)/⟨N_ch⟩ — can be reconstructed from event-averaged, extrapolatable observables, providing a controlled experimental pathway to confront lattice-QCD predictions.

Conclusion: The proposed reformulation establishes a complementary, acceptance-robust experimental framework for testing thermal QCD via net-charge fluctuations; it lays groundwork for extending the method to fourth-order cumulants, contingent on improved theoretical and experimental control.

Abstract: Relativistic heavy-ion collisions exhibit several phenomena commonly associated with the formation of a thermalized quark--gluon plasma. However, the observation of similar phenomena in small systems, and their non-thermal microscopic descriptions there, raises questions about this assumption. Recent continuum-extrapolated lattice-QCD calculations of the second- and fourth-order electric-charge susceptibilities, $\chi_{2}^{\Delta Q}$ and $\chi_{4}^{\Delta Q}$, reveal a striking tension with hadron-resonance-gas expectations, particularly for $\chi_{4}^{\Delta Q}$. As the ratio $\chi_{4}^{\Delta Q}/\chi_{2}^{\Delta Q}$ is experimentally measurable with net-charge cumulants, this opens up completely new ways to test whether thermal QCD applies in central heavy-ion collisions. However, achieving a measurement that can be interpreted with high precision requires further work on both the phenomenological and experimental sides. Here, the focus is on an experimental counterpart to recent phenomenological work on the $D$-measure of net-charge fluctuations: how restricted detector acceptance in rapidity and transverse momentum can be controlled experimentally. We demonstrate this for the second-order net-charge cumulant, $\kappa_2(\Delta Q)$, by rewriting it in terms of the charge balance function and the charged-particle rapidity density. Both are event-averaged observables for which experimental efficiency corrections and extrapolations into unmeasured kinematic regions can be studied systematically. This provides a complementary experimental route for connecting measurements of $D = 4\kappa_2(\Delta Q)/\langle N_{ch} \rangle$ to theory and a possible starting point for extending the same strategy to the fourth-order cumulant.

</details>


### [140] [Meson-baryon final-state interactions in the $ψ(3686)\to pK^-K_S^0\barΞ^0$ decay](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37922)
*F. C. Sobrinho,W.-T. Lyu,E. Oset*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the charmonium decay ψ(3686) → p K⁻ K̄⁰ Ξ̄⁰ using SU(3) symmetry and final-state interactions, identifying three SU(3)-singlet amplitudes, incorporating resonant contributions (Λ(1520), Λ(1670), Σ(1430)), and achieving a good fit to six invariant-mass distributions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore SU(3) symmetry constraints and final-state interaction effects in multi-body charmonium decays—specifically ψ(3686) → B B̄ M M̄—as a complementary probe to simpler B B̄ M decays, enabling resonance identification and dynamics disentanglement.

Method: SU(3) group-theoretic construction of all possible baryon-antibaryon–meson–meson singlets; selection of S-wave dominant terms contributing to p K⁻ K̄⁰ Ξ̄⁰; inclusion of D-wave Λ(1520) and coupled-channel effects from Λ(1670) and Σ(1430) via final-state interactions; simultaneous fit to six invariant-mass spectra.

Result: Three independent SU(3)-singlet amplitudes identified with distinct weights; successful description of all six invariant-mass distributions; evidence for Λ(1520) (dominant), small Λ(1670) in K⁻p, and significant Σ(1430)-induced distortion in K̄⁰p spectrum.

Conclusion: Charmonium decays into B B̄ M M̄ provide a rich, symmetry-constrained environment to isolate and study hyperon resonances and final-state dynamics, validating SU(3) as a predictive tool and extending insight beyond conventional B B̄ M channels.

Abstract: We study the decay $\psi(3686)\to p K^- \bar K^0 \bar\Xi^0$, treating the $\psi(3686)$ as a $c\bar c$ state and therefore as a singlet under SU(3) transformations in the light-quark sector. We consider all possible combinations of a baryon, an antibaryon, and two pseudoscalar mesons that form an SU(3) singlet, retaining the terms that can lead to the $p K^- \bar K^0 \bar\Xi^0$ final state through $S$-wave final-state interactions. Three independent SU(3)-singlet structures are identified and assigned different weights. We also include the contribution of the $\Lambda(1520)$ in the meson-baryon $D$-wave. A fit to the data provides a good description of all six invariant-mass distributions. In addition to the $\Lambda(1520)$ contribution, we find a small contribution from the $\Lambda(1670)$ in the $K^-p$ invariant-mass distribution, generated by final-state interactions, and a contribution from the $\Sigma(1430)$, which produces a significant distortion of the $\bar K^0p$ invariant-mass distribution relative to phase space. This study demonstrates the value of investigating charmonium decays into a baryon-antibaryon pair and two pseudoscalar mesons, complementing the more commonly studied decays into a baryon-antibaryon pair and a single pseudoscalar meson.

</details>


### [141] [Baryon-Number Violating $Λ_c$ Decays from Light-Cone Sum Rules](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37987)
*Ali Mohamed*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies baryon-number-violating exclusive decays Λc → πχ̄ and Λc → Kχ̄, where χ is a light SM-singlet fermion carrying baryon number; form factors are computed using light-cone sum rules (including twist-2 to twist-4 two-particle and factorisable four-particle contributions), and branching fractions are estimated at ~10⁻⁷–10⁻⁶, assessable by STCF, Belle II, and FCC-ee.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe new physics via baryon-number-violating decays with missing energy—specifically, dimension-six operators coupling three quarks to a light invisible fermion χ that preserves total baryon number but induces apparent violation in the visible sector.

Method: Computed hadronic form factors for Λc → π/K transitions using light-cone sum rules with pion/kaon distribution amplitudes; included leading-order two-particle contributions up to twist four and factorisable four-particle terms; employed two distinct Λc interpolating currents to quantify current dependence; used collider-based single-operator benchmarks to estimate branching fractions.

Result: Branching fractions of order 10⁻⁷ over sizeable regions of phase space, with uncertainties extending up to ~10⁻⁶ in some scenarios; results are consistent with but more precise than prior chiral perturbation theory and QCD factorisation estimates.

Conclusion: Λc → πχ̄ and Λc → Kχ̄ provide viable, theoretically controlled probes of baryon-number-violating new physics; upcoming experiments (STCF, Belle II, FCC-ee) can test significant portions of the predicted branching fraction range.

Abstract: Baryon-number-violating decays with missing energy probe new interactions connecting Standard-Model quarks to light invisible fermions. Such signatures can arise from dimension-six operators coupling three quarks to an SM-singlet fermion $\chi$, which escapes detection. With a suitable baryon-number assignment for $\chi$, total baryon number is conserved while its violation is apparent in the visible sector. We study the exclusive decays $\Lambda_c\to\pi\bar\chi$ and $\Lambda_c\to K\bar\chi$, determining the required form factors using light-cone sum rules with pion and kaon distribution amplitudes. The calculation includes leading-order two-particle contributions up to twist four and factorisable four-particle terms. Two $\Lambda_c$ interpolating currents are employed to assess the current dependence. Using single-operator benchmarks based on collider bounds, we obtain branching fractions of order $10^{-7}$ over sizeable parts of the physical region, with uncertainty envelopes reaching $10^{-6}$ in some scenarios. These are indicative estimates within the retained light-cone expansion. We compare with existing chiral-perturbation-theory and QCD-factorisation estimates and discuss experimental prospects. Future data from STCF and Belle II could probe part of the predicted range, while FCC-ee could reach smaller branching fractions.

</details>


### [142] [Vector- and tensor-meson resonance contributions to $χ_{cJ}\to PPV$ decays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38178)
*Yuan-Guo Xu,Qin-Ze Song,Bo Lan,Ru-Min Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies light vector- and tensor-meson resonance contributions to three-body charmonium decays χ_{cJ} → P₁P₂V using a quasi-two-body framework and SU(3) flavor symmetry; it compares narrow-width approximation (NWA) and finite-width effects (FWE), highlighting dominant resonance bands (e.g., ππρ, πK̄K*, K̄Kφ) and identifying K̄Kω and η′KK* as clean probes of tensor resonances T → PV.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically quantify and prioritize vector- and tensor-meson resonance contributions in χ_{cJ} → P₁P₂V decays, especially where kinematic closure necessitates finite-width treatment for tensor resonances, enabling informed experimental amplitude analyses.

Method: Quasi-two-body factorization combined with SU(3) flavor relations for underlying two-body subprocesses; includes both narrow-width approximation (NWA) and finite-width effects (FWE) — the latter crucial for off-shell tensor resonances (T → PV) near threshold.

Result: Predicted resonance band strengths span several orders of magnitude; largest contributions in ππρ, πK̄K*, and K̄Kφ; K̄Kω and η′KK* dominantly sensitive to tensor resonances, offering clean access to T → PV dynamics; band-by-band strengths serve as priors for future amplitude analyses.

Conclusion: Finite-width effects are essential for reliable interpretation of tensor-resonance contributions in χ_{cJ} three-body decays; identified channels provide targeted opportunities to isolate and study tensor meson properties and SU(3) breaking patterns.

Abstract: Light vector- and tensor-meson resonance contributions to the three-body decays $\chi_{cJ}\to P_1P_2V$ are studied in a quasi-two-body framework combined with SU(3) flavor relations for the two-body subprocesses, covering both the $\chi_{c0,1,2}\to VV\to P_1P_2V$ and $\chi_{c1,2}\to TP\to P_1P_2V$. Both the narrow-width approximation (NWA) and the finite-width effects (FWE) treatment are considered, the latter being essential for tensor subdecays that are kinematically closed at the nominal mass and contribute only through the high-mass tail of the resonance. The predicted resonance bands span several orders of magnitude, with the largest ones in the $\pi\pi\rho$, $\pi K\bar K^{*}$, and $K\bar K\phi$ final states, whereas the $K\bar K\omega$ and $\eta'KK^{*}$ channels are dominated by the tensor band and thus provide a clean window onto the $T\to PV$ tensor resonances. The band-by-band strengths and their ordering are intended as priors and channel priorities for future invariant-mass and amplitude analyses, and can be compared with the measured totals of several channels.

</details>


### [143] [Spectra and strong decays of open charm-bottom $c\bar{b}q\bar{q}$ tetraquarks in an unequal-mass Born-Oppenheimer framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38217)
*Bowen Kang,Xi Xia,Tao Guo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies the mass spectra and strong decays of S-wave open charm-bottom tetraquarks (c̄bq̄q) using the Born-Oppenheimer approximation with unequal-mass heavy quark treatment; predicts masses with flavor-dependent shifts and hyperfine splittings for J^P = 0^+, 1^+, 2^+; estimates partial widths for two-body open-flavor decays; provides benchmarks for theory and experiment.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend previous studies on hidden-heavy-flavor tetraquarks to open charm-bottom (c̄bq̄q) systems, accounting for unequal heavy-quark masses and improving general applicability of the computational method.

Method: Born-Oppenheimer approximation with independently optimized heavy-light orbitals; construction of effective potential; color-singlet tetraquark states formed by coupling two color octets; color-spin recoupling and phenomenological decay prescription for partial width estimation.

Result: Predicted mass spectra for J^P = 0^+, 1^+, 2^+ c̄bq̄q tetraquarks showing systematic flavor-dependent shifts and characteristic hyperfine splittings; estimated partial widths for selected two-body open-flavor strong decays; identified channel-dependent dominant decay modes.

Conclusion: The framework successfully describes open charm-bottom tetraquark spectra and decays, offering quantitative predictions that serve as theoretical benchmarks and guidance for experimental searches at facilities like LHCb and Belle II.

Abstract: We investigate the mass spectra and strong decay channels of $S$-wave open charm-bottom $c\bar{b}q\bar{q}$ ($q = u,d,s$) tetraquarks within the Born-Oppenheimer approximation. Extending our previous study of hidden heavy flavor tetraquarks, we account for the unequal-mass heavy quark sources and construct the effective potential based on independently optimized heavy-light orbitals. This makes the computational method more generally applicable. For the quantum numbers $J^P=0^+$, $1^+$, and $2^+$, we have obtained the spectra of the open charm-bottom $c\bar{b}q\bar{q}$ tetraquarks corresponding to color-singlet states constructed by coupling two color octets. The predicted masses exhibit systematic flavor-dependent shifts and characteristic hyperfine splittings. We also estimate partial widths for selected two-body open flavor decay channels using color-spin recoupling and a phenomenological decay prescription. The resulting channel-dependent decay modes, together with comparisons to other theoretical predictions, provide useful benchmarks for future theoretical investigations and experimental searches for open charm-bottom tetraquarks.

</details>


### [144] [Tracing Electromagnetic Response through Charge Splitting of Directed Flow in the Quark-Gluon Plasma](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38363)
*Ankit Kumar Panda,Zhu Meng,Long-Gang Pang,Xin-Nian Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses a (3+1)D viscous hydrodynamic model with electromagnetic (EM) source terms and Cooper–Frye corrections to explain the beam-energy-dependent sign reversal and amplitude change of the charge-split directed flow Δv₁ in heavy-ion collisions. It attributes the sign reversal to competition between electric and magnetic field-induced momentum distortions, governed by QGP electrical conductivity—consistent with lattice-QCD estimates—and predicts mass-dependent Δv₁ for π, K, p to constrain EM relaxation times.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the experimentally observed sign reversal and energy dependence of charge-split directed flow Δv₁ ≡ v₁(h⁺) − v₁(h⁻) across RHIC (200 GeV) and LHC (5.02 TeV), and to establish it as a quantitative probe of QGP’s electromagnetic response and spacetime evolution.

Method: (3+1)-dimensional viscous hydrodynamics incorporating: (i) an electromagnetic source term in the bulk QGP evolution, and (ii) EM corrections to Cooper–Frye freeze-out; electrical conductivity is treated as a tunable parameter varying between RHIC and LHC energies.

Result: The model reproduces the sign reversal at midrapidity and amplitude change of Δv₁ between Au+Au (200 GeV) and Pb+Pb (5.02 TeV); the reversal arises from conductivity-mediated competition between E- and B-field-driven momentum distortions; mass-dependent predictions for π, K, p Δv₁ are provided.

Conclusion: Charge-split directed flow Δv₁ is a robust, quantitative probe of QGP’s electromagnetic conductivity, relaxation times, and spatiotemporal evolution—bridging EM field dynamics, transport properties, and hadronic observables.

Abstract: Experimentally observed charge splitting of directed flow, $\Delta v_1\equiv v_1(h^+)-v_1(h^-)$, exhibits both a sign reversal at midrapidity and the amplitude change from Au+Au collisions at $\sqrt{s_{\rm NN}}=200$ GeV to Pb+Pb at $\sqrt{s_{\rm NN}}=5.02$ TeV. We study this behavior within a unified $(3+1)$-dimensional viscous hydrodynamic model that incorporates an electromagnetic source term in the bulk evolution of the quark-gluon plasma (QGP) and an electromagnetic correction to the Cooper--Frye particlization. The beam-energy dependence of $\Delta v_1$ can be described by varying the effective electrical conductivity between RHIC and LHC energies, with values consistent with the conductivity scale indicated by lattice-QCD calculations. We find that the sign reversal emerges from the competition between momentum distortions induced by the electric and magnetic field which is controlled by the conductivity during the QGP evolution. The model further predicts the mass-dependent charge splittings for identified pions, kaons, and protons, providing further constraints on their respective relaxation times and the corresponding electromagnetic interaction. These results establish the charge splitting of directed flow as a quantitative probe of the electromagnetic response and the space-time evolution of the QGP.

</details>


### [145] [Gluon gravitational form factors of the proton from near-threshold charmonium photoproduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38505)
*Chentao Tan,Zhun Lu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates gluon contributions to the proton's gravitational form factors (GFFs) using a light-front spectator model calibrated to near-threshold J/ψ photoproduction data, yielding GFFs consistent with lattice QCD and experimental cross sections; it further derives 2D light-front gluon distributions of mass, angular momentum, pressure, and shear force, as well as Galilean energy density and pressure anisotropy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Near-threshold J/ψ photoproduction measurements motivate investigation of gluon contributions to the proton's gravitational form factors (GFFs) — key probes of its internal energy-momentum structure in QCD.

Method: Light-front spectator model where the proton is treated as an active gluon plus a spectator particle; model parameters fixed via global fit to differential cross section data for J/ψ photoproduction; gluon GFFs and mechanical properties computed from this framework.

Result: Extracted gluon GFFs agree reasonably with recent lattice QCD results; total cross section prediction matches available experimental data; 2D light-front gluon distributions for mass, angular momentum, pressure, shear force, Galilean energy density, radial/tangential/isotropic pressures, and pressure anisotropy are obtained.

Conclusion: The gluonic sector plays a significant role in the proton’s mechanical structure; the light-front spectator model, constrained by J/ψ photoproduction, provides a viable phenomenological approach to access gluon GFFs and their associated spatial and mechanical interpretations.

Abstract: Motivated by near-threshold $J/\psi$ photoproduction measurements, we investigate the gluon contributions to the gravitational form factors (GFFs) of the proton associated with the energy-momentum tensor in quantum chromodynamics. We calculate the gluon GFFs and the corresponding mechanical properties of the proton within a light-front spectator model, in which the proton is described as an active gluon accompanied by a spectator particle. The model parameters are determined from a global fit to the differential cross section for $J/\psi$ photoproduction. The resulting gluon GFFs are in reasonable agreement with recent lattice QCD determinations. We further calculate the total cross section and find reasonable agreement with the available experimental data. Using the extracted gluon GFFs, we investigate the two-dimensional light-front gluon distributions inside the proton, such as the mass, angular momentum, pressure, and shear force distributions. We also study the gluonic contributions to the two-dimensional Galilean energy density, radial and tangential pressures, isotropic pressure, and pressure anisotropy.

</details>


### [146] [Inelastic Kinetic Decoupling in WIMP Leptogenesis: A Minimal $Z_4$ Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38531)
*An Liu,Hong-Hao Zhang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores a Z4-symmetric Standard Model extension where a Dirac singlet fermion χ serves as dark matter; its inelastic annihilations drive WIMP leptogenesis, and early kinetic decoupling—induced by a mass gap in χL ↔ χ̄Ψ conversions—significantly affects relic abundance and B−L asymmetry evolution, with implications for indirect detection via Fermi-LAT.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
