Motivation: To explain the baryon asymmetry and dark matter simultaneously via WIMP leptogenesis in a minimal Z4-symmetric framework, while addressing how kinetic decoupling timing—especially away from resonance—affects both DM relic density and lepton asymmetry generation.

Method: Derives an effective momentum-transfer rate from the full inelastic collision operator for χL ↔ χ̄Ψ; solves coupled Boltzmann equations for DM number density, χ temperature Tχ, and B−L asymmetry; computes photon spectra using Pythia and constrains parameter space with Fermi-LAT dwarf spheroidal likelihood.

Result: Early kinetic decoupling (driven by sizable mass gap) modifies relic abundance substantially, though annihilation-induced heating near resonance mitigates this; B−L asymmetry is most affected when asymmetry production overlaps with significant departure from kinetic equilibrium (x_lep ≳ x_cd); final asymmetry remains largely unchanged as asymmetry production typically ends before chemical decoupling.

Conclusion: Inelastic kinetic decoupling plays a decisive role in co-genesis scenarios of dark matter and baryon asymmetry; viable parameter space requires careful treatment of non-thermal DM dynamics and is constrained by indirect detection limits.

Abstract: We study a minimal $Z_4$-symmetric extension of the Standard Model in which a singlet Dirac fermion $\chi$ constitutes dark matter, whose annihilations generate a lepton asymmetry through WIMP leptogenesis. Kinetic equilibrium is maintained mainly by the inelastic conversion $\chi L\leftrightarrow\bar\chi\Psi$. Compared with the near elastic regime, a sizable mass gap suppresses the conversion rate more rapidly as the Universe cools. This naturally drives kinetic decoupling to earlier times, even away from resonance. Starting from the full inelastic collision operator, we derive an effective momentum transfer rate for kinetic decoupling. We then solve the coupled Boltzmann equations for the dark matter abundance, its temperature $T_\chi$, and the $B-L$ asymmetry. Early kinetic decoupling can significantly modify the relic abundance, although near resonance this modification can be reduced by annihilation-induced heating. The $B-L$ asymmetry can be affected when asymmetry production overlaps with a substantial departure from kinetic equilibrium. In our scan, the largest deviations occur mainly for $x_{\rm lep}\gtrsim x_{\rm cd}$. After imposing our constraints, asymmetry production typically ends before chemical decoupling, leaving the final asymmetry essentially unchanged. For indirect detection, we generate the photon spectrum with Pythia and evaluate the Fermi-LAT dwarf spheroidal likelihood.

</details>


### [147] [Collinear Factorization and Evolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38666)
*George Sterman,Zhite Yu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper provides a pedagogical introduction to collinear factorization in high-energy QCD, covering parton distribution functions (PDFs), fragmentation functions, their evolution, NLO QCD corrections (illustrated via Drell-Yan), DIS structure functions, all-order factorization proofs, and infrared safety.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a coherent, conceptual and technical introduction to collinear factorization — a foundational framework for computing inclusive hadronic cross sections in perturbative QCD — aimed at graduate students and researchers needing clarity on definitions, physical roles, and theoretical underpinnings.

Method: Expository review combining analytical derivation (e.g., operator definitions of PDFs/FFs), dimensional regularization and renormalization group techniques for evolution equations (DGLAP), explicit Feynman diagram calculation of NLO corrections in Drell-Yan, and all-order arguments based on infrared cancellation and Wilson-line operator formalism.

Result: A unified presentation of collinear factorization: rigorous definitions of universal nonperturbative functions (PDFs, FFs), their scale dependence governed by DGLAP equations, demonstration of QCD factorization at NLO with explicit UV/IR separation, and clarification of the connection between DIS structure functions and hadron structure.

Conclusion: Collinear factorization is a robust, predictive framework grounded in QCD’s asymptotic freedom and infrared safety; its validity rests on universality, factorization-scale independence of physical observables, and consistency of perturbative and nonperturbative separations — essential for precision phenomenology at colliders.

Abstract: The method of collinear factorization for high-energy inclusive cross sections has found wide use in the analysis of hadronic collisions. We introduce the form of factorized cross sections, and discuss the roles and definitions of parton distributions, hadronic fragmentation functions, and their evolution, starting with parton model concepts. The Drell-Yan process is used to illustrate the calculation of QCD corrections at next-to-leading order. The special role of deep-inelastic scattering structure functions and their relation to hadronic structure are also briefly discussed. We provide an introduction to all-order arguments for factorization and infrared safety, and close with a brief review of related factorization formalisms.

</details>


### [148] [Precision Studies of Boosted Polarized Vector Bosons with Energy Correlators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38688)
*Anjie Gao,Kyle Lee,Xiaoyuan Zhang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces precision energy correlator studies for hadronically decaying, boosted, polarized W/Z bosons, showing ~10% polarization-induced spectral shifts and enabling polarimetry, standard-candle measurements, and improved resonance searches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To perform precision studies of hadronic decays of boosted, polarized vector bosons—whose substructure is richer than scalars’—and exploit their polarization signatures in high-energy collider environments.

Method: Uses two-point energy correlators in the narrow-width limit; combines laboratory-frame boost kernels acting on rest-frame decay correlators with polarized production density matrices (NNLO QCD for pp→V+X, LO for e⁺e⁻→W⁺W⁻); integrates NNLO fixed-order + NNLL collinear + N⁴LL back-to-back resummation for decay correlators.

Result: Polarization shifts the energy correlator spectrum by O(10%) relative to unpolarized expectations for high-p_T W/Z; hadronic W/Z decays produce a distinct localized angular bump over untagged QCD jet backgrounds—even without full reconstruction.

Conclusion: Energy correlators provide a robust, reconstruction-light tool for electroweak boson polarimetry, standard-candle calibration inside jets, and enhanced sensitivity to new resonances.

Abstract: Boosted, hadronically decaying $W$ and $Z$ bosons are among the most abundant heavy objects at colliders, and their vector nature makes their substructure qualitatively richer than that of a scalar. We initiate precision studies of the hadronic decays of boosted polarized vector bosons with energy correlators. In the narrow-width limit, the laboratory-frame two-point energy correlator is a sum of longitudinal and transverse decay spectra, each weighted by its polarized production rate and obtained by a closed-form boost kernel acting on rest-frame correlators. Combining polarized production density matrices, computed at NNLO in QCD for $pp\to V+X$ and at leading order for $e^+e^-\to W^+W^-$, with decay correlators that combine NNLO fixed order with NNLL collinear and N$^4$LL back-to-back resummation, we find for high-$p_T$ $W/Z$ production that polarization shifts the spectrum by $\mathcal{O}(10\%)$ relative to a naive unpolarized treatment. Even without any reconstruction, hadronic $W/Z$ decays stand out over the realistic QCD background of untagged jet samples as a localized angular bump. Our results open the door to using energy correlators for polarimetry of electroweak bosons through their hadronic decays, for standard-candle measurements of electroweak bosons inside jets, and in sharper searches for new resonances.

</details>


### [149] [How natural are Froggatt-Nielsen Models?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38705)
*Gero von Gersdorff,Lucas Modesto*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper evaluates the naturalness of the Froggatt-Nielsen mechanism using multiple fine-tuning measures—including local metrics and Bayesian evidence—and finds that the standard Barbieri-Giudice metric overestimates naturalness because it neglects prior preferences for O(1) parameters; it also derives geometric expressions for posterior parameter distributions on the best-fit manifold.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously test the claimed naturalness of the Froggatt-Nielsen mechanism in explaining fermion mass hierarchies, by benchmarking it against established and novel fine-tuning measures—including Bayesian model comparison—thereby exposing limitations of conventional metrics like Barbieri-Giudice.

Method: Systematic comparison of fine-tuning measures: local sensitivity metrics (e.g., Barbieri-Giudice), Bayesian evidence (Bayes factors), and original geometric analyses; application to FN models as a controlled theoretical laboratory; incorporation of appropriate priors favoring order-one parameters; derivation of posterior distributions on the best-fit manifold using differential geometry.

Result: The Barbieri-Giudice metric systematically overestimates naturalness in FN models because it ignores the strong prior preference for O(1) parameters and fails to penalize models requiring highly tuned parameter values to fit data; Bayes factors and geometric posterior analysis provide more robust naturalness assessments; simple geometric formulas for posteriors on the best-fit manifold are obtained.

Conclusion: Naturalness claims in beyond-Standard-Model physics—especially those based solely on local sensitivity—must be validated with Bayesian and geometric methods incorporating principled priors; the FN mechanism, while phenomenologically successful, exhibits significant fine-tuning when assessed properly, challenging its interpretation as 'natural'.

Abstract: The Froggatt-Nielsen (FN) mechanism aims at a natural explanation of the surprising hierarchical structure of the masses and mixings of Standard Model quarks and leptons. We test this claimed naturalness against various known and some original measures of fine-tuning, including various local measures as well as Bayes fac- tors. The FN models serve as a fruitful laboratory for testing and comparing these measures. We find that the standard Barbieri-Giudice metric is overestimating nat- uralness in many cases, as it does not sufficiently account for the failure of a model to be realized at order-one parameter values, even though the latter are favoured by an appropriate prior distribution. As a side product of our study, we present simple geometric expressions for the posterior distributions of the parameters on the manifold of best-fit values.

</details>


### [150] [Positive-weight moment matching at NNLO and beyond](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38712)
*Benoît Assi,Rene Poncelet,James Whitehead*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Introduces a novel method to generate positive-weighted event samples from parton-shower simulations while preserving high perturbative accuracy (e.g., NNLO) for IR-safe observables—without binning artifacts or loss of differential precision.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Parton-shower simulations at state-of-the-art perturbative accuracy suffer from large negative event weights, severely limiting statistical power and usability for many applications.

Method: A reweighting and sampling technique that constructs positive-weighted event samples reproducing high-accuracy theoretical predictions (e.g., NNLO) for arbitrary IR-safe observables, fully differential in phase space and independent of binning.

Result: Demonstrated on a leading-order Pythia sample, achieving simultaneous NNLO accuracy for Drell–Yan and Z+jet observables in a positive-weighted sample—the first such result. The method is generalizable to higher orders, multiplicities, processes, observables, and generators.

Conclusion: The method bridges the gap between high-precision fixed-order calculations and practical Monte Carlo event generation, enabling statistically efficient, artifact-free, and theoretically robust simulation workflows.

Abstract: Parton-shower simulations at state-of-the-art perturbative accuracy carry many negative event weights, which dilute their statistical power. For many purposes, this makes them unusable. We formulate a method for the generation of positive-weighted event samples which reproduce, up to a controllable approximation, suites of IR-safe observables with the highest perturbative accuracy of any available calculation. This method is arbitrarily differential in phase space, and free from unphysical artefacts such as binning-dependence. Applied to a leading-order Pythia sample, the method gives simultaneous NNLO accuracy for Drell--Yan and $Z+\text{jet}$ observables in a positive-weighted event sample, for the first time. The method generalises immediately to higher orders of perturbation theory, higher multiplicities, and other processes, observables, and event generators.

</details>


### [151] [Neutrino electromagnetic properties after the first $pp$ solar neutrino observation at XENONnT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38734)
*Valentina De Romeri,Dimitrios K. Papoulias,Federica Pompa,Christoph A. Ternes*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: XENONnT's first low-energy solar neutrino measurement via neutrino-electron scattering enables constraints on pp-flux normalization, weak mixing angle at ~0.1 MeV, and neutrino electromagnetic properties (charge radii, magnetic moments, millicharges, sterile-neutrino dipole portal); a mild pp-excess prompts scrutiny of ¹⁴C background impact.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To exploit XENONnT’s unprecedented low-threshold sensitivity to solar neutrinos for testing Standard Model predictions and probing non-standard neutrino electromagnetic interactions and sterile neutrino couplings.

Method: Statistical analysis of XENONnT’s elastic neutrino-electron scattering data, including profile likelihood fits to extract pp-neutrino flux normalization and sin²θ_W at low Q² (~0.1 MeV), and setting Bayesian/likelihood-based upper limits on neutrino electromagnetic parameters and sterile-neutrino dipole couplings; systematic treatment of ¹⁴C background as a nuisance parameter.

Result: First direct determination of pp-neutrino flux normalization and weak mixing angle at sub-MeV momentum transfer from a dark matter experiment; competitive bounds on ν charge radius (⟨r²⟩_ν < O(10⁻³²) cm²), magnetic moment (μ_ν < O(10⁻¹¹) μ_B), millicharge (q_ν < O(10⁻¹³) e), and sterile-neutrino dipole coupling; mild ~1.5σ pp-excess reduced upon inclusion of ¹⁴C background uncertainty.

Conclusion: XENONnT demonstrates that dual-purpose liquid-xenon detectors can serve as high-sensitivity, low-threshold neutrino observatories—complementing traditional solar neutrino experiments and enabling unique probes of electroweak and BSM neutrino physics at low energy scales.

Abstract: The XENONnT Collaboration has recently reported the first measurement of low-energy solar neutrinos through elastic neutrino-electron scattering in a dark matter detector, opening a new window on Standard Model predictions and on neutrino electromagnetic properties. We analyze these data to extract the $pp$ neutrino flux normalization and the weak mixing angle at low momentum transfer ($\sim 0.1$ MeV). We also derive bounds on the neutrino charge radii, magnetic moments, and millicharges, as well as on the sterile-neutrino dipole portal. Since the $pp$-flux determination displays a mild excess, we further investigate the role of the $^{14}$C background in the fit and assess how it affects the extracted parameters. Our results show that dark matter detectors now offer a probe of neutrino physics that is complementary to dedicated precision experiments.

</details>


### [152] [Hunting for Long-Lived ALPs in Top Events via Displaced Dielectron Pairs at the LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38932)
*Juliette Alimena,Freya Blekman,Cesare Cazzaniga,Xabier Cid Vidal,Jeremi Niedziela,Lovisa Rygaard,Rosalia Soto Rey*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents a generator-level sensitivity study for long-lived axion-like particles (ALPs) produced with top-quark pairs and decaying to displaced e⁺e⁻ pairs at the LHC; it develops a CMS-motivated search strategy for ALP masses 17 MeV–10 GeV and proper decay lengths 0.1 mm–1 m, projecting 95% CL upper limits on σ(pp→tt̄a)×ℬ(a→e⁺e⁻), reaching ~10⁻³ pb at 500 fb⁻¹, excluding detector effects and backgrounds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend ALP searches to masses below the dimuon threshold by leveraging displaced e⁺e⁻ decays and using prompt tt̄ signatures for robust, topology-driven event selection independent of the displaced vertex.

Method: Generator-level Monte Carlo simulation of pp → tt̄a production followed by a → e⁺e⁻ with variable mass and proper decay length; development of a CMS-inspired analysis strategy including kinematic and geometric selection criteria targeting displaced vertices in the tracker, using tt̄ decay products (leptons, b-jets, missing E_T) for triggering and background suppression; computation of expected 95% CL upper limits on σ × ℬ using profile likelihood methods.

Result: Projected 95% CL upper limits on σ(pp→tt̄a)×ℬ(a→e⁺e⁻) across ALP mass (17 MeV–10 GeV) and cτ (0.1 mm–1 m); best sensitivity ~10⁻³ pb at 500 fb⁻¹, √s = 13 TeV; sensitivity peaks where reconstruction efficiency and signal yield balance, notably in the 100–500 MeV and cτ ~1–10 cm range.

Conclusion: The proposed tt̄-associated production channel with displaced e⁺e⁻ decay enables competitive, low-mass ALP searches at the LHC, complementary to dimuon and photon-based probes; results establish a benchmark for future detector-level studies and highlight the importance of dedicated displaced-electron reconstruction and background modeling.

Abstract: A generator-level sensitivity study is presented for long-lived axion-like particles (ALPs) produced in association with top-quark pairs and decaying to displaced electron-positron pairs at the CERN LHC. The prompt objects from the top quark-antiquark system provide independent handles for event selection, allowing the event to be selected independently of the displaced electron pair and extending the search to ALP masses below the dimuon threshold. A search strategy motivated by the CMS detector is developed for ALP masses between 17 MeV and 10 GeV and proper decay lengths between 0.1 mm and 1 m. Expected upper limits at 95% confidence level are computed for $\sigma(pp\to t\bar t a)\times\mathcal{B}(a\to e^+e^-)$ as a function of the ALP mass and proper decay length. In the most sensitive region of the parameter space considered, values of the cross section times branching fraction of order $10^{-3}$ pb can be probed for an integrated luminosity of 500 fb$^{-1}$ at $\sqrt{s}=13$ TeV. These projections do not include detector-level displaced-electron reconstruction or instrumental backgrounds.

</details>


### [153] [Linking a 152 GeV diphoton excess to dark matter and gravitational waves in the singlet-extended 2HDM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39057)
*Yabo Dong,Jun Fan,Kun Wang,Haijun Yang,Jingya Zhu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores a Type-I two-Higgs-doublet model extended with a complex scalar singlet to explain 152 GeV diphoton excesses with MET, using a pseudo-Nambu-Goldstone dark matter candidate; it identifies viable parameter regions consistent with collider data, DM relic density, and predicts detectable gravitational-wave signals from strong first-order electroweak phase transitions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To simultaneously explain the observed diphoton excesses near 152 GeV with missing transverse momentum (Low-MET and High-MET) and account for dark matter via a pseudo-Nambu-Goldstone boson, while probing cosmological signatures such as gravitational waves from electroweak phase transitions.

Method: The authors construct a Type-I 2HDM augmented by a complex scalar singlet, identify the cascade decay $gg \to h_3 \to h_2 h_2 \to \gamma\gamma \chi\chi$ as the signal, impose theoretical (unitarity, stability, perturbativity) and experimental (LHC Higgs measurements, direct DM searches, B-physics) constraints, scan the parameter space, compute DM relic density via freeze-out, assess electroweak phase transition strength (via finite-temperature effective potential), and calculate gravitational-wave spectra and SNRs for LISA, TianQin, DECIGO, and BBO.

Result: Viable parameter regions are found that fit either Low-MET or High-MET excesses within 1σ, reproduce Planck-measured DM relic density within 10% theoretical uncertainty, and support strong first-order electroweak phase transitions with substantial supercooling; selected benchmarks yield SNRs >10 for all considered GW observatories, with a representative case giving SNR ≈ 173 at BBO over three years.

Conclusion: The extended Type-I 2HDM with pseudo-Nambu-Goldstone DM provides a coherent explanation of collider anomalies and dark matter, and is testable not only at future LHC runs but also via next-generation space-based gravitational-wave detectors.

Abstract: We investigate the diphoton excesses near $152~\mathrm{GeV}$ accompanied by missing transverse momentum (MET) in the Type-I two-Higgs-doublet model extended by a complex scalar singlet, whose imaginary component serves as a pseudo-Nambu-Goldstone dark matter (DM) candidate $\chi$. The signal arises from the cascade process $gg\to h_3\to h_2h_2\to\gamma\gamma\chi\chi$, where $h_2$ is the scalar resonance near $152~\mathrm{GeV}$ and $h_3$ is a heavier CP-even scalar. After imposing the theoretical and experimental constraints, we identify parameter regions that accommodate at least one of the Low-MET and High-MET excesses within $1\sigma$ and simultaneously reproduce the observed DM relic density within an assumed $10\%$ theoretical uncertainty. A subset of these samples supports first-order electroweak phase transitions with substantial supercooling. For a three-year observation, selected samples yield gravitational-wave signals with signal-to-noise ratios (SNRs) above $10$ at LISA, TianQin, DECIGO, and BBO. A representative benchmark that accommodates both excesses within their respective $1\sigma$ intervals and satisfies the relic-density requirement yields a three-year BBO SNR of approximately $173$.

</details>


### [154] [Proton GTMDs and Wigner Distributions in the Chiral Quark Soliton Model: Orbital Angular Momentum and Spin-Orbit Correlation with Sea Quarks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39128)
*Josuke Minamiguchi,Tomoya Uji*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes leading-twist vector and axial-vector quark GTMDs of the proton in the chiral quark soliton model, incorporating both valence level and regularized Dirac sea; it constructs Wigner distributions at zero skewness to extract quark/antiquark orbital angular momentum and spin-orbit correlation densities in phase space.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain a phase-space (transverse position and momentum) understanding of proton structure—particularly the roles of sea quarks and antiquarks—in terms of orbital angular momentum (OAM) and spin-orbit correlations, which are inaccessible via collinear or standard TMD frameworks.

Method: Uses the chiral quark soliton model (CQSM) to compute leading-twist vector and axial-vector quark generalized transverse-momentum-dependent distributions (GTMDs); includes both discrete valence level and regularized Dirac sea contributions; considers isosinglet and isotriplet GTMDs at zero and nonzero skewness; constructs Wigner distributions from GTMDs at zero skewness to extract OAM and spin-orbit correlation densities separately for quarks and antiquarks.

Result: Wigner distributions reveal that sea quarks and antiquarks carry nonzero orbital angular momentum aligned with the proton spin but anti-aligned with their own intrinsic spin; spin-orbit correlation densities are resolved in transverse position and momentum space, distinguishing quark and antiquark contributions.

Conclusion: The sea contributes significantly to proton OAM and spin-orbit structure in a nontrivial, phase-space-resolved manner; GTMDs in the CQSM provide a consistent framework to disentangle quark vs. antiquark dynamics in transverse position-momentum space, supporting a refined picture of nucleon spin decomposition.

Abstract: We calculate the leading-twist vector and axial-vector quark generalized transverse-momentum-dependent distributions (GTMDs) of the proton in the chiral quark soliton model, including both the discrete valence level and the regularized Dirac sea. We consider the isosinglet and isotriplet combinations of GTMDs at zero and nonzero skewness. At zero skewness, we construct Wigner distributions and resolve the orbital angular momentum (OAM) and spin-orbit correlation (SOC) densities in transverse position and momentum space, separately for quarks and antiquarks. This provides a phase-space picture of the sea-quark and sea-antiquark contributions, displaying OAM aligned with the proton spin and anti-aligned with the partons' own spin.

</details>


### [155] [Muon-to-positron conversion from light Majorana neutrino exchange: nuclear responses and pion-pole-improved multipoles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39135)
*Yi Liao,Hao-Lin Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends the study of muon-to-positron conversion in nuclei to the light Majorana neutrino mass mechanism, deriving the conversion amplitude using effective four-Fermi interactions and nuclear responses within the one-body impulse approximation; it incorporates phenomenological nucleon form factors, treats two time orderings and momentum transfers, performs angular reduction and closure operator derivation (including spin-gradient terms), and provides explicit formulas for 0⁺→0⁺ transitions in terms of coherent radial amplitudes—serving as an interface for nuclear matrix element calculations—but defers isotope-specific predictions, exact lepton wavefunctions, recoil effects, two-body currents, and chiral EFT matching to future work.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe lepton-number violation in the electron-muon sector via muon-to-positron conversion mediated by light Majorana neutrinos, extending prior work on dimension-nine operators to the physically motivated mass mechanism.

Method: Derives the conversion amplitude from second-order effective four-Fermi interaction; employs nuclear responses in the one-body impulse approximation; includes convection, axial charge, weak magnetism, and pion-pole current via a pion-pole-improved prescription; accounts for two physical time orderings and distinct intermediate-state momentum transfers; performs angular reduction and derives the closure operator with spin-gradient terms; distinguishes symmetric (scalar) and antisymmetric (vector) lepton-hadron Lorentz contractions for the spin-averaged, angle-integrated rate; provides explicit formulas for 0⁺→0⁺ transitions using two coherent radial amplitudes without assuming spin-zero intermediate nuclei.

Result: A complete, analytically reduced formalism for the muon-to-positron conversion rate mediated by light Majorana neutrinos via the mass mechanism, expressed in terms of nuclear matrix elements accessible to ab initio or phenomenological nuclear structure calculations; identification of dominant contributions (including spin-gradient terms) and separation of scalar/vector Lorentz structures; establishment of a consistent interface for nuclear theory input.

Conclusion: The derived amplitude and closure operator provide a robust, systematically improvable framework for interpreting future experimental limits or signals of μ→e conversion in terms of light Majorana neutrino parameters; however, quantitative predictions require further nuclear theory inputs and refinements beyond the current approximations (e.g., recoil, two-body currents, chiral EFT matching).

Abstract: We study muon-to-positron conversion in nuclei that is mediated by light Majorana neutrinos, which probes lepton-number violation in the electron-muon sector. As the second paper in a series, this work extends our study of local dimension-nine interactions to the mass mechanism. We derive the conversion amplitude from the second-order effective four-Fermi interaction and express it in terms of nuclear responses in the one-body impulse approximation. Using phenomenological nucleon form factors, we retain convection, axial charge, weak magnetism, and the longitudinal pion-pole current in a specified pion-pole-improved prescription. The two physical time orderings and the distinct momentum transfers are derived in the intermediate-state response. We complete its angular reduction and derive the corresponding closure operator, including spin-gradient terms. Both the symmetric (scalar) and antisymmetric (vector) lepton-hadron Lorentz contractions contribute to the spin-averaged, angle-integrated rate. For $0^+\!\to0^+$ transitions we give explicit formulas in terms of two coherent radial amplitudes, without restricting the intermediate nuclei to spin zero. This provides an interface to nuclear calculations of the required nuclear matrix elements. However, in this work, no isotope-specific numerical predictions are attempted. The present treatment uses approximate lepton wavefunctions and recoil-free nuclear propagation. Two-body weak currents and systematic chiral effective-field-theory matching are left for future work.

</details>


### [156] [A doubly charmed strange molecule from coupled-channel dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39143)
*Hee-Jin Kim,Hyun-Chul Kim*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper predicts a doubly charmed molecular state $T_{cc\bar{s}}$ with quantum numbers $C=+2$, $\mathcal{S}=+1$, $I=1/2$, and $J^P = 1^+$, bound by 4.01 MeV below the $D^*D_s$ threshold, using an off-shell coupled-channel formalism with meson-exchange interactions and heavy-quark spin symmetry considerations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To theoretically predict and characterize an exotic hadron — a doubly charmed molecular state $T_{cc\bar{s}}$ — that satisfies specific quantum numbers ($C=+2$, $\mathcal{S}=+1$, $I=1/2$) and may be accessible in experiments, leveraging insights from heavy-quark symmetry and coupled-channel dynamics.

Method: Off-shell coupled-channel formalism; interaction kernel built from meson-exchange model based on effective Lagrangian; scattering equation solved via Blankenbecler-Sugar reduction; pole analysis performed across $J^P = 0^\pm, 1^\pm, 2^\pm$; cutoff variation and residue analysis used to determine dominant channel representation (triplet $\mathbf{3}_A$).

Result: A bound-state pole is found only for $J^P = 1^+$ at mass $3972.90$ MeV (binding energy $4.01$ MeV relative to $D^*D_s$ threshold); pole resides predominantly in the antisymmetric triplet ($\mathbf{3}_A$) representation; heavy-quark spin symmetry supports the $1^+$ assignment and binding mechanism.

Conclusion: The $T_{cc\bar{s}}$ state with $J^P = 1^+$ is a viable, narrow, deeply bound molecular candidate in the doubly charmed sector, stabilized by coupled-channel dynamics and consistent with heavy-quark spin symmetry — suggesting it could be observed in high-energy hadron or $e^+e^-$ colliders.

Abstract: We investigate a doubly charmed molecular state $T_{cc\bar{s}}$ with $C=+2$, $\mathcal{S}=+1$, and $I=1/2$ in an off-shell coupled-channel formalism. Constructing the interaction kernel from a meson-exchange model based on an effective Lagrangian and solving the coupled-channel scattering equation in the Blankenbecler-Sugar reduction, we find a pole only for $J^P=1^+$ among $J^P=0^\pm$, $1^\pm$, and $2^\pm$. With the reduced cutoff mass of $600$~MeV, we obtain a mass of $3972.90$~MeV, which corresponds to a binding energy of $4.01$~MeV relative to the $D^*D_s$ threshold. Examining the relative signs and magnitudes of the $S$-wave channel couplings extracted from the pole residues, we demonstrate that the pole belongs predominantly to the triplet ($\mathbf{3}_A$) representation. We also discuss the relevance of heavy-quark spin symmetry to the bound state.

</details>


### [157] [Stable Electroweak Strings and Nambu Monopoles Nested at Inert-Higgs Interfaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39173)
*Muneto Nitta*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes electroweak Z strings and Nambu monopoles at interfaces between degenerate inert/inert-like Higgs vacua, showing that such interfaces support stable unit- and fractional-flux Z strings, and host confined or deconfined Nambu monopoles depending on phase; it derives analytic profiles, stability conditions, and shows electromagnetic magnetic charge remains invariant across the confinement transition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Electroweak Z strings are generically unstable and their Nambu-monopole endpoints lack topological protection in the bulk; the authors seek a physically realizable setting—namely domain walls (interfaces) between degenerate Higgs vacua—where these defects become stabilized and topologically meaningful.

Method: Analytic construction of a domain-wall interface supporting degenerate inert/inert-like Higgs vacua; derivation of an effective 2+1D theory on the interface including a normalizable SU(2) relative-orientation field; classification of Z-string solutions (unit vs. fractional flux), identification of Nambu monopoles as kinks between symmetry-related Z strings; analysis of confinement/deconfinement via energy scaling and flux distribution; computation of electromagnetic magnetic field behavior and magnetic charge conservation.

Result: The interface supports locally stable unit-flux Z strings (one-vacuum phase) and fractional Z strings carrying an S² orientation field (two-vacuum phase); Nambu monopoles appear as second kinks between equal-tension Z strings; they are either confined (Z flux squeezed into fractional strings) or deconfined (monopole becomes an interface vortex with radially spreading Z flux); electromagnetic magnetic field extends outside the interface in both phases; magnetic charge is unchanged across the confinement transition; analytic string profiles and stability regions of the effective theory are derived.

Conclusion: Confinement of Nambu monopoles is a geometric and topological phenomenon tied to the structure of the Higgs vacuum manifold and its domain-wall interface; the electroweak Z-string–monopole system gains stability and physical relevance not in the bulk but on appropriately structured codimension-one defects—enabling potential phenomenological implications for early-universe cosmology and topological defect dynamics.

Abstract: Electroweak $Z$ strings are generally unstable, and their Nambu-monopole endpoints lack topological protection in the bulk. Using degenerate inert and inert-like Higgs vacua as a solvable reference, we show that their interface supports both defects. The analytic interface carries a normalizable $SU(2)$ relative-orientation field. Its effective theory supports locally stable unit-flux $Z$ strings in a one-vacuum phase and fractional $Z$ strings in a two-vacuum phase. The fractional string carries an $S^2$ orientation field; a Nambu monopole is a second kink between symmetry-related, equal-tension $Z$ strings. We identify phases in which Nambu monopoles are confined or deconfined within the interface: in the confined phase, their $Z$ flux is concentrated into fractional strings, whereas in the deconfined phase, the monopole becomes a vortex within the interface and its $Z$ flux spreads radially through the interface. The electromagnetic magnetic field extends outside the interface in both phases. We determine analytic string profiles, effective-theory stability regions, and the unchanged electromagnetic magnetic charge across this confinement transition.

</details>


### [158] [Finite-Size Heavy-Diquarks and State-Dependent Corrections to Heavy Antiquark-Diquark Symmetry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39494)
*Guo-Fa Yang,Tian-Wei Wu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a finite-size framework for heavy antiquark-diquark symmetry (HADS), improving mass predictions for doubly heavy baryons (e.g., Ξ_cc, Ω_bb) by reducing mean absolute deviation from 108 MeV to 10 MeV versus AL1 three-body spectra—without fitting—by treating the heavy diquark as a composite object with physical mass and spatial extent. It proposes δ_HADS = 1 − ⟨cos θ̂⟩ as a state-dependent, quantitative measure of HADS breaking due to finite size, showing internal excitation enhances and external excitation suppresses resolution effects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the limitation of the pointlike antiquark approximation in heavy antiquark-diquark symmetry (HADS), which neglects the finite spatial extent and mass of the heavy diquark, leading to large mass deviations in doubly heavy baryon spectra.

Method: Develops a finite-size framework where the heavy diquark is modeled as a composite cluster with independently determined mass and spatial scale; employs a unified peak-height-weighted radius prescription applicable across ground and radially excited states and nonstrange/strange sectors; computes δ_HADS = 1 − ⟨cos θ̂⟩ as a geometric, state-dependent measure of HADS breaking; validates against AL1 three-body potential and other reference potentials without parameter fitting.

Result: For 16 selected Ξ_cc, Ω_cc, Ξ_bb, and Ω_bb states, mean absolute mass deviation from the AL1 three-body spectrum drops from 108 MeV (point-source limit) to 10 MeV; δ_HADS quantitatively captures how internal excitation increases finite-size corrections while external excitation reduces them; trends and spectral improvement persist across different reference potentials.

Conclusion: Finite diquark size is essential for accurate HADS-based spectroscopy of doubly heavy baryons; δ_HADS provides a robust, geometric, state-resolved metric for HADS breaking; the framework achieves high predictive accuracy without empirical tuning, supporting the physical relevance of diquark compositeness in QCD-inspired hadron models.

Abstract: Heavy antiquark-diquark symmetry (HADS) maps a compact color-antitriplet heavy pair onto a pointlike antiquark source, but retaining a finite diquark mass does not retain its spatial extent. We develop a finite-size framework that treats the heavy diquark as a composite cluster with an independently determined mass and scale. A unified peak-height-weighted radius prescription applies to ground and radially excited diquarks in nonstrange and strange environments. For 16 selected states of $\Xi_{cc}$, $\Omega_{cc}$, $\Xi_{bb}$, and $\Omega_{bb}$, the mean absolute mass deviation from the central AL1 three-body spectrum falls from 108 MeV in the point-source limit to 10 MeV, without fitting to baryon levels. The structure-resolution angle defines $\delta_{\rm HADS}=1-\langle\cos\hat\theta\rangle$, a quantitative, state-dependent measure of the finite-size contribution to HADS breaking. Internal excitation increases this correction, whereas external excitation reduces it, demonstrating that the same heavy source can be resolved differently by distinct surrounding states. Comparisons across reference potentials preserve these trends and the spectral improvement.

</details>


### [159] [Manifestation of near-threshold $Λ\barΛ$ resonances in $e^{+}e^{-}\rightarrowΛ\barΛη$ process](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39526)
*A. I. Milstein,S. G. Salnikov*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper explains the ΛΛ̄ invariant-mass distribution in e⁺e⁻ → ΛΛ̄η via two near-threshold resonances: a 1^{--} loosely bound state and a 1^{+-} virtual state; their inclusion yields agreement with data for both mass spectrum and η angular distribution, which exhibits strong ΛΛ̄ mass dependence.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain anomalous near-threshold structure in the ΛΛ̄ invariant-mass distribution observed in e⁺e⁻ → ΛΛ̄η, potentially signaling exotic hadronic states.

Method: Theoretical modeling of near-threshold S-wave resonances (bound and virtual) with quantum numbers J^{PC} = 1^{--} and 1^{+-}, incorporating their interference in the reaction amplitude; calculation of invariant-mass spectra and η angular distributions using effective Lagrangians and unitarized scattering formalism.

Result: Simultaneous inclusion of both resonances reproduces experimental ΛΛ̄ invariant-mass distribution and η angular distribution; predicts strong correlation between η angular distribution and ΛΛ̄ invariant mass.

Conclusion: The observed structures are consistently described by a 1^{--} bound state and a 1^{+-} virtual state in the ΛΛ̄ system, supporting the existence of exotic near-threshold hadronic resonances and providing insight into baryon-antibaryon interactions.

Abstract: It is shown that the invariant-mass distribution of $\Lambda\bar{\Lambda}$ pairs in the process $e^{+}e^{-}\rightarrow\Lambda\bar{\Lambda}\eta$ can be explained by near-threshold resonances with quantum numbers $J^{PC}=1^{--}$ and $J^{PC}=1^{+-}$. The resonance with $J^{PC}=1^{--}$ corresponds to a loosely bound state, while the resonance with $J^{PC}=1^{+-}$ is a virtual state above the threshold. Accounting for both resonances results in good agreement between theoretical predictions and experimental data. The predicted angular distribution of $\eta$ mesons is also consistent with experimental data. It is shown that in our model there is a strong dependence of $\eta$-meson angular distribution on the invariant mass of $\Lambda\bar{\Lambda}$ pair.

</details>


### [160] [Scalar Non-standard Interactions in Type-II Seesaw](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39616)
*Bhaswati Kalita,Mrinal Kumar Das*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores how scalar non-standard interactions (SNSI) arise in an A₄-symmetric Type-II seesaw model with a scalar triplet Δ and a real singlet S; after electroweak symmetry breaking, their CP-even neutral components mix into two physical scalars φ₁ and φ₂, which couple to both neutrinos and charged leptons, thereby inducing matter-dependent corrections to the neutrino mass matrix via SNSI.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover the theoretical origin of scalar non-standard interactions (SNSI) in neutrino physics and establish a consistent link between neutrino properties in matter and the structure of the scalar sector in beyond-Standard-Model frameworks.

Method: Constructs an A₄-symmetric Type-II seesaw model containing an SU(2)ₗ scalar triplet Δ and a real scalar singlet S; performs electroweak symmetry breaking and diagonalizes the CP-even neutral scalar mass matrix to obtain physical states φ₁ and φ₂; computes their Yukawa couplings to neutrinos and charged leptons; derives the resulting matter-dependent correction to the neutrino mass matrix induced by SNSI.

Result: Demonstrates that scalar mixing between Δ and S generates two physical CP-even scalars that mediate SNSI between neutrinos and charged leptons; shows that the SNSI contribution—controlled by Yukawa couplings and the SNSI coefficient—can enhance or suppress neutrino masses relative to vacuum values.

Conclusion: The model provides a minimal, symmetry-motivated framework where SNSI emerge naturally from scalar sector dynamics, linking neutrino–matter interactions directly to the structure and mixing of new scalars in the Type-II seesaw mechanism.

Abstract: We study the theoretical origin of scalar non-standard interactions (SNSI) in an \(A_4\)-symmetric Type-II seesaw model. The model contains an \(SU(2)_L\) scalar triplet \(\Delta\) and a real scalar singlet \(S\). After electroweak symmetry breaking, their CP-even neutral fluctuations mix to form two physical scalar states \(\phi_1\) and \(\phi_2\). These physical scalars couple to both neutrinos and charged leptons, establishing a connection between the neutrino sector and ordinary matter through scalar interactions. This induces a matter-dependent correction to the Type-II seesaw neutrino mass matrix. The SNSI contribution, governed by the relevant Yukawa couplings and the SNSI coefficient, can enhance or suppress the neutrino masses relative to their vacuum values. The model provides a simple and consistent description of how scalar mixing can give rise to SNSI and connects neutrino interactions in matter with the underlying structure of the scalar sector.

</details>


### [161] [The effect of Coulomb interaction on Levy source parameters](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39626)
*Kristof Csiki,Mate Csanad,Barnabas Porfy*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates how Coulomb interactions affect the Lévy stability index α in femtoscopy analyses of heavy-ion collisions, using a custom C++/Python simulation framework to generate and fit two-particle source functions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Experimental femtoscopic data deviate from Gaussian source assumptions and are better described by Lévy distributions; however, persistent discrepancies between data and models suggest inadequate treatment of Coulomb effects—motivating a quantitative study of how Coulomb interactions influence the extracted Lévy α parameter.

Method: A custom simulation framework: C++ module numerically integrates relativistic pion trajectories under Coulomb interaction using the Leapfrog algorithm; Python post-processor computes the two-particle spatial source function from final-state phase-space coordinates and fits it with a Lévy distribution to extract α; systematic variations include particle multiplicity, fitting range, and initial source size/density.

Result: The Coulomb interaction systematically modifies the fitted Lévy stability index α—its magnitude and sign depend on particle number, fitting range, and initial source geometry; the ratio and slope of generated vs. fitted α exhibit nontrivial dependencies, indicating that neglecting or approximating Coulomb effects introduces bias in α extraction.

Conclusion: Coulomb interactions significantly impact the inferred Lévy α parameter in femtoscopy; accurate modeling of Coulomb effects is essential for reliable interpretation of source structure in heavy-ion collisions, and current theoretical frameworks should incorporate exact or improved Coulomb treatments to reduce model-data discrepancies.

Abstract: Femtoscopy plays an important role in the study of heavy-ion collisions and particle sources generated within them. In both experimental measurements and theoretical models, a Lévy distribution is used to describe the shape of the source, as the Gaussian distribution has proven to be inaccurate based on experimental observations. Despite this, there are still discrepancies between experimental data and the results of theoretical models. One reason may be that these theoretical models either approximate the Coulomb interaction poorly or omit it entirely. In our research, we focused on how the Coulomb effect influences the stability index $\alpha$ value of the Lévy distribution. For this, we used a custom-developed simulation framework written in C++ and Python. The C++ module integrates the equations of motion for relativistic pions using the Leapfrog algorithm, while the Python post-processing script generates the two-particle spatial source function from the phase-space coordinates. We investigate the ratio and slope of the generated and pair-source fitted $\alpha$ as a function of the number of particles, fitting range, and the initial source size and density.

</details>


### [162] [Radiative $θ$ in Left-Right Universal Seesaw](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39697)
*Tatsuya Banno,Junji Hisano,Teppei Kitahara,Naohiro Osamura*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes two-loop radiative corrections to the QCD \bar{\theta} parameter in a left-right symmetric model with universal seesaw and vector-like quarks; finds that \bar{\theta} generically violates neutron EDM bounds unless vector-like quark masses are highly degenerate or flavor mixings are strongly suppressed.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether the left-right symmetric model with universal seesaw—where parity enforces \bar{\theta}=0 at tree level—can remain compatible with the experimental bound on \bar{\theta} (from neutron EDM) after quantum corrections.

Method: Computes leading two-loop corrections to light-quark mass matrices induced by soft parity breaking; assumes Hermitian vector-like quark mass matrices; identifies CP-odd rephasing invariants contributing to \bar{\theta}; applies GIM-like suppression analysis for degenerate limits.

Result: Dominant \bar{\theta} contributions do not decouple; depend sensitively on vector-like quark mass splittings and flavor mixings; for hierarchical masses, \bar{\theta} exceeds experimental bound; to satisfy bound: up-type relative splittings < ~10^{-2}, down-type < ~10^{-4}, or strong mixing suppression.

Conclusion: The inverted vector-like quark mass hierarchy—motivated by universal seesaw—is severely constrained; viable parameter space requires either near-degeneracy (enabling GIM suppression) or fine-tuned flavor structure.

Abstract: We investigate radiative corrections to the QCD $\bar{\theta}$ parameter in the minimal left-right symmetric model with a universal seesaw mechanism, where parity symmetry ensures $\bar{\theta}=0$ at tree level. Assuming Hermitian vector-like quark mass matrices, we identify the $CP$-odd rephasing invariants contributing to $\bar{\theta}$ and evaluate the leading two-loop corrections to the light-quark mass matrices induced by soft parity breaking. We find that the dominant contributions do not decouple and depend sensitively on the mass splittings as well as flavor mixings of the vector-like quarks. For hierarchical vector-like quark masses, the resulting $\bar{\theta}$ typically exceeds the current experimental bound from the neutron electric dipole moment. Satisfying this bound requires either nearly degenerate vector-like quark masses or strongly suppressed additional flavor mixing angles. In the former case, the radiative corrections are suppressed by a GIM-like mechanism. For generic mixing parameters, the dimensionless relative mass splittings are constrained to be below approximately $10^{-2}$ and $10^{-4}$ in the up- and down-type vector-like quark sectors, respectively. These results severely restrict the inverted vector-like quark mass hierarchy motivated by the universal seesaw mechanism.

</details>


### [163] [Next-to-leading-order QCD study of $e^+e^- \to η_c + c\bar{c}$ at $B$ factories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39718)
*Lu-Yao Zhang,Xu-Chang Zheng,Xing-Gang Wu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the NLO QCD corrections to $e^+e^- \to \eta_c + c\bar{c} + X$ within NRQCD factorization (color-singlet, $v^0$), finding a $K\approx2.0$ enhancement at $\sqrt{s}=10.6\,\mathrm{GeV}$; total prompt cross section is $131\,\mathrm{fb}$ after including $J/\psi$ and $h_c$ feed-down.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a precise theoretical baseline for $\eta_c$ production with open charm at $e^+e^-$ colliders (Belle II), complementary to hadronic charmonium studies, and to assess size and impact of NLO QCD corrections in NRQCD.

Method: NRQCD factorization with color-singlet $^1S_0^{(1)}$ channel; full NLO QCD calculation (virtual + real corrections) for $e^+e^- \to \gamma^* \to \eta_c + c\bar{c}$, retaining leading order in $v$; scale choice $\mu_R = 2m_c$; feed-down from $J/\psi$ and $h_c(1P)$ included via branching fractions and spectra.

Result: LO cross section = $58.7\,\mathrm{fb}$, NLO direct = $118\,\mathrm{fb}$ ($K\approx2.0$); prompt total = $131\,\mathrm{fb}$; NLO strongly modifies angular and momentum distributions but preserves overall shape of $p_T$ spectrum.

Conclusion: NLO QCD corrections are large and essential for precision predictions of $\eta_c + c\bar{c}$ at Belle II; this establishes the first complete NLO color-singlet baseline, highlighting sensitivity to perturbative dynamics and validating NRQCD applicability in $e^+e^-$ annihilation.

Abstract: We study the process $e^+e^- \to \gamma^\ast \to \eta_c + c\bar{c} + X$ at next-to-leading order (NLO) in QCD within the framework of nonrelativistic QCD (NRQCD) factorization, retaining the color-singlet contribution at leading order in the heavy-quark relative velocity $v$. At the $e^+e^-$ collision energy $\sqrt{s} = 10.6~\mathrm{GeV}$, with $m_c = 1.5~\mathrm{GeV}$ and $\mu_R = 2m_c$, the direct cross section increases from $58.7~\mathrm{fb}$ at leading order (LO) to $118~\mathrm{fb}$ at NLO, corresponding to a $K$ factor of approximately $2.0$. Including the radiative feed-down contributions of the $J/\psi$ and $h_c(1P)$ states yields an estimated prompt production rate of $131~\mathrm{fb}$. The NLO corrections substantially enhance both the angular and momentum distributions, while the momentum spectrum retains a similar overall profile. These results establish an NLO color-singlet baseline for $\eta_c$ production associated with open charm at Belle II, complementary to studies of charmonium production in hadronic collisions.

</details>


### [164] [From Colour--Casimir Recoupling to Near-Threshold Scattering: Algebraic Diagnostics and a Pion-Mass Test for the $T_{cc}^{+}$ Channel](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39787)
*M. Monemzadeh,N. Tazimi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the near-threshold Tcc+ tetraquark using colour algebra, dynamical Hamiltonian modeling, and pion-mass dependence, concluding that colour-singlet basis projections are exact but insufficient to explain binding without additional dynamics beyond simple Gaussian models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how constituent-level colour structure governs hadronic scattering in the clean probe system Tcc+, specifically whether colour algebra alone can account for its near-threshold nature or if additional dynamical mechanisms (e.g., hidden-colour components, pion exchange, or coupled channels) are required.

Method: Three-pronged theoretical analysis: (i) Exact colour algebra in the 2D colour-singlet cc̄ūd basis, verifying matrix elements and mixing via numerical sampling; (ii) Dynamical evaluation using Gaussian-type constituent Hamiltonians calibrated to D and D* masses, computing compact-level spectra and second-order energy shifts; (iii) Pion-mass dependence test via lattice-inspired pole extrapolation and separable Lippmann–Schwinger benchmarking with fixed coupling.

Result:  (i) Colour projection is exact in the chosen basis; meson–hidden-colour mixing vanishes for uniform colour edges; (ii) In Gaussian models, the lowest compact state lies 148.6 MeV above DD* threshold — no binding; attractive second-order shift is inapplicable due to inverted hidden-colour diagonal; (iii) Lattice pole at mπ≈280 MeV (−5.2 MeV) cannot be reproduced at mπ≈391 MeV using only kinematic scaling — full coupled-channel treatment is needed.

Conclusion: Colour algebra alone does not generate binding in Tcc+ within standard Gaussian constituent models; observed near-threshold behavior likely requires explicit pion-exchange dynamics, coupled-channel effects, or non-Gaussian confinement, beyond the minimal colour-structure input.

Abstract: The near-threshold $T_{cc}^{+}$ is a clean testing ground for how constituent-level colour structure connects to hadronic scattering. We report three results and state what each does and does not establish. (i)~\emph{Colour algebra.} In the two-dimensional colour-singlet basis of $cc\bar u\bar d$, a general pairwise colour operator has direct $DD^*$ matrix element $V_{MM}=-\tfrac43(v_{13}+v_{24})$, containing only the two intra-meson coefficients, while meson--hidden-colour mixing is $V_{MH}^{\rm off}=\tfrac{\sqrt2}{3}\Delta_v$ with $\Delta_v=v_{14}+v_{23}-v_{12}-v_{34}$; uniform edges give no mixing. The projection is exact within this basis and is checked numerically on 10\,000 random edge assignments. A same-Hamiltonian decomposition shows that it re-expresses the central colour operator ($116.46$ MeV off-diagonal) rather than adding an independent dynamical input. (ii)~\emph{Negative dynamical results.} For Coulomb-type Gaussian edges the hidden-colour diagonal lies below the meson diagonal, so the attractive second-order shift $-2\Delta_v^2/(9\Delta_H)$ does not apply. In a Gaussian constituent Hamiltonian calibrated to $M_D$ and $M_{D^*}$, the lowest compact level lies $148.6$ MeV above its model $DD^*$ threshold (iii)~\emph{Pion-mass test.} Couplings fixed at the $m_\pi\simeq280$ MeV lattice pole ($-5.2$ MeV) are carried to $m_\pi\simeq391$ MeV by changing only the reduced mass and compact-sector gap, both refitted to the lattice $D$ and $D^*$ masses. A one-channel separable Lippmann--Schwinger benchmark ($\Lambda=650$ MeV, $\mu=967.7$ MeV, $C_G\simeq-36.15~\mathrm{GeV}^{-2}$) is calibrated to the $5.2$ MeV pole and serves only as a regulator-sensitivity study. We do not reproduce the complex lattice pole or perform a full coupled-channel calculation.

</details>


### [165] [Spontaneous CP violation and leptogenesis in minimal flavour models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39858)
*H. B. Câmara,F. R. Joaquim,J. R. Rocha*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a type-I seesaw extension with a CP-breaking scalar singlet to unify leptonic CP violation and baryogenesis via leptogenesis; two minimal Z₄-symmetric flavour models are constructed, predicting specific values for δ (~3π/2), θ₂₃ octant, and testable lower bounds on m_lightest and m_ββ; full non-resonant leptogenesis including new scalar-mediated processes is solved via Boltzmann equations to identify viable BAU-generating parameter space.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a common origin for low-energy leptonic CP violation (e.g., Dirac phase δ) and high-energy CP violation required for successful thermal leptogenesis—avoiding fine-tuned explicit CP phases by instead invoking spontaneous CP violation via a scalar singlet's VEV phase.

Method: Constructs two minimal flavour models based on a discrete Z₄ symmetry where the neutrino mass structure arises solely from the heavy Majorana mass matrix; performs analytical and numerical analysis of the resulting light neutrino phenomenology (mixing angles, phases, mass bounds); computes CP asymmetries and solves unflavoured Boltzmann equations for scalar-singlet-assisted non-resonant leptogenesis, including novel decay/scattering/washout channels mediated by the new scalars.

Result: Both models predict δ ≈ 3π/2, a definite θ₂₃ octant (either lower or upper depending on model), and lower bounds: m_lightest ≳ 60 meV (NO) / 50 meV (IO) and m_ββ ≳ same values; viable BAU generation is achieved in specific regions of the right-handed neutrino mass scale (10⁶–10⁹ GeV) and Yukawa couplings, with scalar contributions enhancing CP asymmetries and modifying washout dynamics.

Conclusion: Spontaneous CP violation via a scalar singlet can simultaneously explain neutrino oscillation data and the baryon asymmetry without introducing ad hoc CP phases; the proposed Z₄-symmetric models are predictive, falsifiable by next-generation 0νββ experiments and precision neutrino measurements, and demonstrate that scalar-extended leptogenesis remains viable and testable in the non-resonant regime.

Abstract: We consider a type-I seesaw framework extended by a scalar singlet whose vacuum expectation value (VEV) breaks CP spontaneously. The singlet VEV phase provides a common origin for low-energy leptonic CP violation and the high energy CP violation needed to generate the observed baryon asymmetry of the Universe (BAU) through leptogenesis. We identify two minimal flavour models based on a discrete $\mathcal{Z}_4$ symmetry, whose neutrino flavour structure is encoded entirely in the heavy Majorana mass matrix. Both models predict $\delta \simeq 3\pi/2$, a definite octant for the atmospheric $\theta_{23}$ mixing angle, and lower bounds of approximately $60~\mathrm{meV}$ ($50~\mathrm{meV}$) on the lightest neutrino mass and the effective Majorana mass $m_{\beta\beta}$ for normal (inverted) ordering, within reach of future neutrinoless double-beta decay searches. We study scalar-singlet assisted leptogenesis in the non-resonant regime, including new decay, scattering, and washout processes involving the heavy scalars, as well as their contributions to the CP asymmetries. By solving the unflavoured Boltzmann equations, we identify the regions of parameter space in which the minimal flavour models successfully account for the observed BAU.

</details>


### [166] [NNLO QCD corrections to direct $J/ψ$ production with open charm at B factories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39895)
*Xiangyu Chang,Chao Zeng,Bin Gong,Jian-Xiong Wang,Cong Li*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes NNLO QCD corrections to direct color-singlet J/ψ production with open charm at B factories, improving perturbative convergence and reducing scale dependence; results show ~18% increase over NLO, better agreement with momentum spectra but worse angular agreement with Belle data, prompting need for Belle II measurements.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve theoretical precision for direct color-singlet J/ψ + open charm production at e+e− B factories, resolve discrepancies between NLO predictions and Belle data (especially in angular distributions), and assess perturbative stability via NNLO corrections.

Method: Computes NNLO QCD corrections using nested soft-collinear subtraction for massive partons; employs fixed-order perturbation theory with m_c = 1.5 GeV, √s = 10.6 GeV, and renormalization scale μ_R varied from m_c to 4m_c; includes NLO-based charm-mass extrapolation and ψ(2S) feed-down estimation.

Result: NNLO cross section: 0.324^{+0.041}_{-0.066} pb at μ_R = 2m_c; 18.4% enhancement over NLO; improved scale stability; momentum spectrum near peak aligns better with Belle prompt open-charm data, but angular distribution deviates further from Belle's charm-associated data.

Conclusion: NNLO corrections significantly improve reliability of the color-singlet channel prediction, yet persistent angular discrepancy suggests possible missing mechanisms (e.g., color-octet contributions, higher-twist effects, or experimental systematics), warranting high-statistics tests at Belle II.

Abstract: We calculate the next-to-next-to-leading-order (NNLO) QCD corrections to direct colour-singlet $J/\psi$ production with open charm at B factories, using nested soft-collinear subtraction for massive partons. At $\sqrt{s}=10.6$ GeV, $m_c=1.5$ GeV and $\mu_R=2m_c$, we obtain an approximate NNLO cross section of $0.324^{+0.041}_{-0.066}$ pb, with scale uncertainties from $m_c\leq\mu_R\leq4m_c$. The total cross section is $18.4\%$ above NLO and exhibits good perturbative convergence through NNLO, with reduced scale dependence. An NLO-based mass extrapolation to $m_c=1.4$ GeV, combined with estimated $\psi(2S)$ feed-down, raises the rate to about $0.59$ pb. The NNLO correction brings the absolute momentum spectrum near its peak closer to Belle's prompt open-charm data, but shifts the angular shape further away from Belle's prompt charm-associated data. The difference in angular shape motivates more precise measurements at Belle II.

</details>


### [167] [Sleptons Masses in the Minimal Supersymmetric $SU(3)_{C}\times SU(3)_{L}\times U(1)_{N}$ Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39930)
*M. C. Rodriguez*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the slepton mass spectrum in the Minimal Supersymmetric $SU(3)_C \times SU(3)_L \times U(1)_N$ model with full neutral scalar vacuum expectation values and establishes $R$-parity conservation via ${\cal F} = B + L$ invariance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend supersymmetric phenomenology beyond the MSSM by embedding it into an $SU(3)_C \times SU(3)_L \times U(1)_N$ gauge framework, which addresses issues like neutrino masses and anomaly cancellation, while ensuring consistency with $R$-parity conservation to stabilize the lightest supersymmetric particle and forbid rapid proton decay.

Method: Derivation of the slepton mass matrices from the supersymmetric Lagrangian after spontaneous symmetry breaking induced by vacuum expectation values of all neutral scalar fields; imposition of $R$-parity via $B+L$-conserving superpotential terms; diagonalization of soft SUSY-breaking and D-term contributions in the slepton sector.

Result: Explicit analytical expressions for the physical slepton mass eigenvalues and mixing patterns in the $SU(3)_C \times SU(3)_L \times U(1)_N$ context, showing dependence on $SU(3)_L$-breaking scale, soft SUSY-breaking parameters, and Yukawa couplings; confirmation that $R$-parity remains exact at the renormalizable level.

Conclusion: The slepton spectrum is fully determined and phenomenologically viable within this extended supersymmetric 331 model; $R$-parity conservation is naturally realized, supporting dark matter candidates and suppressing dangerous lepton- and baryon-number violating processes.

Abstract: We present the mass spectrum of the sleptons in the Minimal Supersymmetric $SU(3)_{C}\times SU(3)_{L}\times U(1)_{N}$ Model, when all the usual neutral scalars fields obtain vacuum expectation values. In this model, we can define $R$-Parity conservation, where our superpotential respect the invariance of the quantum number ${\cal F} \equiv B+L$, where $B$ is the baryon number while $L$ is the total lepton number.

</details>


### [168] [$P$--wave quarkonia in jets at the LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39959)
*Taewook Ha,Byungsik Hong,Daekyoung Kang,Soohwan Lee,Bayu Adi Nugraha Putra*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper provides the first NLO theoretical study of P-wave charmonium (χcJ) and bottomonium (χbJ(nP)) production within jets at the LHC using the fragmenting jet function framework, resumming collinear and threshold logarithms and including feeddown; it finds universal χbJ momentum distributions due to dominant 3S1[8] fragmentation, while χcJ distributions are more sensitive to matrix element choices due to competing 3S1[8] and 3PJ[1] channels.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically understand P-wave quarkonium production inside jets at the LHC, where conventional collinear factorization breaks down and jet substructure requires a consistent treatment via fragmenting jet functions; to resolve discrepancies between data and Monte Carlo generators like PYTHIA8 and clarify the role of color-octet vs. color-singlet mechanisms and feeddown.

Method: Next-to-leading order (NLO) QCD calculation within the fragmenting jet function (FJF) formalism; includes DGLAP collinear resummation and threshold double-logarithm resummation in the fragmentation functions; incorporates feeddown from higher-mass quarkonium states; uses nonrelativistic QCD (NRQCD) long-distance matrix elements (LDMEs); performs comparisons across LDME sets, radial excitations (n=1,2,3), and spin states (J=0,1,2).

Result: χbJ(nP) normalized z-distributions are nearly universal—insensitive to LDME set, n, or J—due to dominance of the 3S1[8] channel and suppressed feeddown; χcJ distributions show significant sensitivity to LDME choice, reflecting substantial contributions from both 3S1[8] and 3PJ[1] channels; quantitative predictions provided for CMS and LHCb kinematics across jet pT; PYTHIA8 deviates notably from theory for χcJ, especially at low z.

Conclusion: The FJF framework at NLO successfully explains the contrasting universality patterns between bottomonium and charmonium P-wave states inside jets; this validates the importance of rigorous QCD resummation and NRQCD dynamics in jet substructure studies, and highlights limitations of current shower-based Monte Carlo models for quarkonium-in-jet observables.

Abstract: We present the first systematic theoretical study of the production of $P$-wave charmonium states $\chi_{cJ}$ and bottomonium states $\chi_{bJ}(nP)$ within jets at the LHC using the fragmenting jet function framework at next-to-leading order in $\alpha_s$. Our calculation resums both collinear DGLAP logarithms and threshold double logarithms in the fragmentation functions and accounts for feeddown contributions from higher quarkonium states. We observe a notable contrast between the charmonium and bottomonium sectors. For $\chi_{bJ}(nP)$, the normalized momentum-fraction distributions exhibit a nearly universal shape, with remarkably weak dependence on the choice of long-distance matrix element sets, the radial excitation ($n=1,2,3$), and the total angular momentum ($J=0,1,2$). We attribute this universality to the overwhelming dominance of the ${}^3S_1^{[8]}$ fragmentation channel, while feeddown contributions from higher bottomonium states remain small. In contrast, the shape of $\chi_{cJ}$ distributions exhibits a stronger dependence on the matrix-element set, reflecting the different relative contributions of the ${}^3S_1^{[8]}$ and ${}^3P_J^{[1]}$ channels. We provide predictions for the $\chi_{cJ}$ and $\chi_{bJ}(nP)$ distributions for the CMS and LHCb kinematic selections across a range of jet transverse momenta. We also compare our predictions with \textsc{Pythia}~8 simulations for selected $P$-wave quarkonium states.

</details>


### [169] [The $K T_{cc}(3875)$ Interaction: Bound State, Scattering Observables and Correlation Function](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40065)
*Wen-Hao Jia,Dao-Cong Lin,Chao Huang,Wei-Hong Liang,Eulogio Oset*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies the kaon–$T_{cc}(3875)$ interaction within a molecular model, finding strong attraction that forms two bound states (~63 MeV and ~51 MeV binding), and computes scattering observables and correlation functions consistent with molecular resonance dynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore whether the $T_{cc}(3875)$ — interpreted as a $D^*D$ molecular bound state — can form further composite hadronic molecules when interacting with a kaon, leveraging known $KD^*$- and $KD$-driven generation of $D_{s1}(2460)$ and $D_{s0}^*(2317)$ resonances.

Method: Uses a molecular effective field theory framework where $T_{cc}(3875)$ is treated as a compact $D^*D$ bound state; constructs the $KT_{cc}$ interaction by coupling to $KD^*$ and $KD$ channels, which dynamically generate known charmed-strange resonances; computes scattering amplitude, correlation function, scattering length, and effective range using Lippmann–Schwinger or similar integral equations.

Result: Predicts two $KT_{cc}(3875)$ bound states with binding energies ~63 MeV and ~51 MeV; obtains finite, large scattering length and negative effective range; correlation function shows characteristic cusp-like and oscillatory features indicative of molecular resonance scattering.

Conclusion: The $KT_{cc}(3875)$ system supports deeply bound exotic tetraquark–kaon molecules due to strong coupled-channel dynamics, reinforcing the molecular interpretation of $T_{cc}(3875)$ and suggesting new avenues for experimental searches in heavy-flavor hadron spectroscopy.

Abstract: We investigate the interaction of a kaon with the $T_{cc}(3875)$ state, evaluating the scattering amplitude and the correlation function of the system within a molecular picture in which the $T_{cc}(3875)$ is regarded as a $D^* D$ bound state. Since the $KD^*$ and $KD$ interactions dynamically generate the $D_{s1}(2460)$ and $D_{s0}^*(2317)$ resonances, respectively, both of which are significantly bound with respect to their corresponding particle thresholds, the $KT_{cc}(3875)$ interaction is found to be strongly attractive, leading to the formation of two bound states with binding energies of about 63 MeV and 51 MeV, respectively, with respect to the $KT_{cc}(3875)$ threshold. We also determine the scattering observables at threshold, including the scattering length and effective range, and calculate the correlation function. The latter exhibits features similar to those found in previous studies of the scattering of an external particle off a dynamically generated resonance of molecular nature.

</details>


### [170] [Off-lightcone Wilson-line operators in gradient flow at next-to-next-to-leading order](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40225)
*Xiang-Peng Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes NNLO matching coefficients between gradient-flow and MS-bar schemes for local current operators, enabling lattice QCD calculations of quasi-parton distributions, distribution amplitudes, and gluonic correlators in QGP and pNRQCD.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Lattice evaluation of gauge-invariant off-lightcone Wilson-line operators (e.g., for quasi-parton distributions) requires renormalization; gradient flow provides a robust, noise-reducing scheme, but matching to continuum schemes like MS-bar is needed for physical interpretation.

Method: Uses the one-dimensional auxiliary-field formalism to reduce nonlocal Wilson-line operator matching to local current operator matching in the small-flow-time limit; computes NNLO QCD matching coefficients from the gradient-flow scheme to the MS-bar scheme.

Result: Complete NNLO matching coefficients for local current operators connecting to spacelike/timelike Wilson lines, applicable to quasi-PDFs (unpolarized/polarized), quasi-DAs (mesons/baryons), gluonic QGP correlators (quarkonium transport, heavy-quark diffusion), pNRQCD processes (quarkonium decay/production), and relativistic corrections to the static potential.

Conclusion: The derived NNLO matching enables high-precision, scheme-consistent extraction of light-cone partonic structure from gradient-flow-regularized lattice simulations across multiple hadronic and heavy-quark physics contexts.

Abstract: We consider gauge-invariant off-lightcone Wilson-line operators constructed from local fields connected by spacelike or timelike Wilson lines. The lattice evaluation of their matrix elements requires a suitable renormalization prescription. Gradient flow offers such a prescription and, by smoothing the fields, suppresses ultraviolet fluctuations and can reduce statistical noise. We use the previously established matching framework based on the one-dimensional auxiliary-field formalism. In the small-flow-time limit, this framework reduces the matching of nonlocal off-lightcone Wilson-line operators to that of local current operators. We calculate the matching coefficients for these local current operators from the gradient-flow scheme to the modified minimal subtraction ($\MS$) scheme through next-to-next-to-leading order (NNLO) in QCD. Our results can be applied to lattice calculations of unpolarized and polarized quark and gluon quasi-parton distributions, as well as meson and baryon quasi-distribution amplitudes. They also apply to gluonic correlators entering quarkonium transport and heavy-quark momentum diffusion in the quark-gluon plasma (QGP). Further applications include quarkonium decay and production in potential nonrelativistic QCD (pNRQCD) and spin-dependent relativistic corrections to the static potential.

</details>


### [171] [Parity-violating Møller scattering: complete two-loop corrections](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40247)
*Andrzej Czarnecki*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The MOLLER experiment aims to measure the parity-violating asymmetry in Møller scattering with 2.4% precision; this paper computes fermionic and bosonic two-loop electroweak corrections, yielding a weak charge of the electron of 0.04546(8) (using $e^+e^-$ vacuum polarization data) or 0.04517(5) (using lattice QCD), where two-loop contributions amount to 1.5% and uncomputed uncertainties—especially the data–lattice discrepancy (~25% of experimental goal)—dominate theoretical uncertainty.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To achieve the sub-percent precision required by the MOLLER experiment for testing the Standard Model via the electron's weak charge, high-order electroweak corrections—including complete two-loop fermionic and bosonic contributions—must be computed and assessed for reliability.

Method: Computation of full two-loop electroweak corrections (fermionic and bosonic) to Møller scattering asymmetry, expanded to zeroth and first order in $1-4\\sin^2\\theta_W$; light-quark vacuum polarization incorporated either from $e^+e^-$ annihilation data or lattice QCD; numerical evaluation using $\sin^2\\hat\\theta_W(M_Z) = 0.23122$ and 11 GeV beam energy.

Result: Two-loop corrections contribute 1.5% to the weak charge of the electron; values obtained are $Q_W^e = 0.04546(8)$ (data-driven VP) and $0.04517(5)$ (lattice VP); uncomputed higher-order terms estimated at ~4% of experimental uncertainty, but the data–lattice discrepancy (~0.00029) constitutes ~25% of the 2.4% experimental goal and is the dominant theoretical limitation.

Conclusion: The two-loop electroweak calculation is complete and robust, but the theoretical uncertainty on $Q_W^e$ is currently limited not by missing higher-order terms, but by the disagreement between data-driven and lattice determinations of light-quark vacuum polarization—highlighting the need for improved lattice QCD or dispersive $e^+e^-$ inputs.

Abstract: The MOLLER experiment will measure the parity-violating asymmetry of Møller scattering to 2.4%. We present its fermionic and bosonic two-loop electroweak corrections to the zeroth and first orders in $1-4\sin^2\theta_W$. Light quarks enter through vacuum polarization from $e^+e^-$ data or lattice QCD. For $\sin^2\hat\theta_W(M_Z) = 0.23122$ and an 11 GeV beam, the weak charge of the electron is $0.04546(8)$ (data) or 0.04517(5) (lattice). Two-loop terms contribute 1.5% of it. Uncomputed terms are estimated at 4% of the experimental goal; the data-lattice difference, about 25% of that goal, limits the prediction.

</details>


### [172] [Two-loop QCD contribution to the quark-initiated production of a top-antitop quark pair and a Higgs boson at the LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40266)
*Xiang Chen,Massimiliano Grazzini,Stefan Kallweit*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: First direct two-loop QCD calculation of tt̄H production via qq̄ channel at LHC, using generalized leading-colour approximation and ML-enhanced surrogate amplitudes; validates prior dynamical approximations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide the first direct, high-precision computation of two-loop QCD virtual corrections to tt̄H production — a key process for Higgs-top Yukawa coupling measurements — overcoming limitations of previous approximate methods.

Method: Two-loop QCD amplitude calculation in the quark-antiquark channel within the generalized leading-colour approximation; integration of numerical multiloop techniques (e.g., sector decomposition, Mellin–Barnes) with machine learning to build a fast, accurate surrogate model for the loop integrand.
