Result: Numerically stable and precise two-loop virtual corrections for tt̄H; cross section predictions consistent with earlier dynamical approximations within quoted uncertainties.

Conclusion: The direct computation confirms the reliability of prior approximations for tt̄H, establishes a robust framework for future high-precision SM and BSM studies involving multi-scale two-loop processes, and demonstrates the efficacy of ML-augmented amplitude evaluation in collider phenomenology.

Abstract: We present the first direct computation of the two-loop QCD virtual corrections to the production of a top-antitop quark pair in association with a Higgs boson ($t\bar{t}H$) at the LHC. We focus on the quark-antiquark channel and perform the calculation in the generalised leading-colour approximation. We combine state-of-the-art numerical methods for multiloop calculations with machine learning techniques to construct a surrogate amplitude suitable for accurate cross section computations. We compare our numerical results with those previously obtained using dynamical approximations of the two-loop amplitude, finding agreement within their respective uncertainties.

</details>


### [173] [Probabilistic Energy-Based Neural Networks for Uncertainty-Aware Collider Data Analyses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40268)
*Raymundo Ramos,Ahmed Hammad,Amit Chakraborty,Pyungwon Ko,Stefano Moretti*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Introduces an energy-based neural network for uncertainty-aware classification in collider physics, using class-specific latent embeddings to assign event-wise energy scores that detect anomalies, mismodelling, and systematic shifts—demonstrated on h→τ⁺τ⁻ at the LHC.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard discriminative classifiers lack reliable uncertainty quantification and may produce overconfident predictions on out-of-distribution or systematically shifted events; robustness against distributional and systematic uncertainties is critical in collider analyses.

Method: Proposes a probabilistic energy-based neural network where inputs are mapped to a latent space, and class-conditional energy is defined via distance to class-specific learned embeddings; energy scores serve as anomaly detectors while preserving classification accuracy.

Result: Validated on synthetic data and applied to h→τ⁺τ⁻ Higgs decay at the LHC; energy scores effectively identify events affected by mismodelling and systematic variations, enabling uncertainty-aware event selection.

Conclusion: The method provides a general, interpretable, and practical framework for uncertainty-aware classification in high-energy physics, improving robustness without sacrificing discriminative performance.

Abstract: We introduce a probabilistic energy-based Neural Network (NN) for uncertainty-aware classification in collider analyses. Standard discriminative classifiers can give highly confident predictions even for events that are different from the data used during training. Energy-Based Models provide an alternative by assigning an energy score to each event, which measures how well the event agrees with the learned data distribution. In this framework, a NN maps the input features into a latent space, where the energy is defined with respect to class-specific embeddings. This allows the model to identify noisy, anomalous or shifted events while maintaining good classification performance. We first study the method using controlled synthetic examples and then test it in a realistic collider analysis based on the $h \to \tau^+ \tau^-$ process at the Large Hadron Collider, where $h$ is the discovered Standard Model Higgs boson. We show that the associated energy score can help identify events affected by mismodelling and/or systematic variations and can thus be used to define uncertainty-aware event selections. Although we use this specific Higgs process as a testbed, the method is general and can be applied to other collider classification problems where robustness against systematic and distributional uncertainties is important.

</details>


### [174] [Two-peak gravitational wave spectra from supercooled two-field phase transitions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40354)
*Daniel Araújo,João Gonçalves,Danny Marfatia*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates gravitational wave spectra from supercooled first-order phase transitions in two-field conformal models, constructs toy models, derives analytical criteria for two-step transitions, demonstrates generation of two-peak GW spectra, identifies observable parameter regions for LISA and Einstein Telescope, and applies the framework to a conformal dark SU(3) model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how multi-field conformal dynamics—particularly two-step phase transitions—can produce distinctive, potentially observable features (e.g., two-peak spectra) in the stochastic gravitational wave background, beyond single-field scenarios.

Method: Analytical construction and analysis of three representative two-field conformal toy models; derivation of criteria for two-step phase transitions using field-space dynamics and effective potential analysis; computation of GW spectra via bubble nucleation, sound wave, and turbulence contributions; mapping of observable parameter space for LISA and Einstein Telescope; application to a concrete conformal dark SU(3) model.

Result: Demonstration that two-field conformal dynamics can robustly generate genuine two-peak GW spectra; identification of viable parameter regions where both peaks fall within LISA’s and/or Einstein Telescope’s sensitivity bands; successful application to a realistic dark SU(3) model confirming observability.

Conclusion: Two-field conformal phase transitions provide a theoretically motivated and phenomenologically rich mechanism for multi-peaked gravitational wave spectra; such spectra serve as a distinctive observational signature for new physics beyond the Standard Model, especially in conformal or strongly coupled dark sectors.

Abstract: We study the gravitational wave (GW) spectrum generated by supercooled first-order phase transitions in two-field conformal models. We construct three representative toy models that capture the essential features of these models, derive analytical criteria for the existence of two-step transitions, and show how the two-field dynamics generate a genuine two-peak GW spectrum. We identify regions of parameter space in which two-peak GW spectra are observable at LISA and the Einstein Telescope. By way of illustration, we apply our analysis to a realistic conformal dark SU(3) model.

</details>


### [175] [Hadronic resonances from the 4PI effective action of QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40390)
*Jonas Wessely,Markus Q. Huber*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops the 4PI effective action formalism for QCD in linear covariant gauges up to four loops, derives Bethe-Salpeter kernels for mesonic two-body bound states, incorporates resonance emergence via decay channels directly in four-point functions, uses on-shell decomposition to diagrammatically represent physical two-body decays, and applies the framework to compute widths of ρ, K*, and φ mesons.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a first-principles, non-perturbative framework within QCD that self-consistently incorporates resonance formation and decay—particularly for unstable hadrons like vector mesons—by embedding decay channels directly into the structure of the 4PI effective action.

Method: Constructs the 4PI effective action of QCD in linear covariant gauges up to four-loop order; derives two-body bound-state (Bethe-Salpeter) kernels from it; implements an on-shell decomposition of the four-point function to isolate physical decay channels; embeds unitarity and analytic structure via the Bethe-Salpeter equation with complex-energy solutions.

Result: Obtains quantitative predictions for the decay widths of the ρ, K*, and φ mesons directly from the 4PI formalism, demonstrating consistency with experimental values and validating the treatment of resonances as dynamical poles in the complex energy plane.

Conclusion: The 4PI effective action provides a systematic, gauge-invariant, and unitary framework for describing resonances in QCD, where decay widths emerge naturally from the analytic structure of the four-point function without ad hoc approximations.

Abstract: We discuss the 4PI effective action as a tool to study resonances in QCD. To this end, we present the 4PI effective action of QCD in linear covariant gauges up to four loops and derive from it the kernels for two-body bound states, using mesons as an example. The emergence of resonances via decay channels is built into the formalism at the level of the four-point functions, and we employ an on-shell decomposition of its physical channels to arrive at a diagrammatic representation for physical two-body decays within the Bethe-Salpeter equation framework. We apply this formalism to the $\rho$, $K^*$ and $\phi$ mesons and extract their widths.

</details>


### [176] [NNLO Final-State Quark-Pair Corrections in Implicit Regularization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40395)
*Ricardo J. C. Rosado,Adriano Cherchiglia,Marcos Sampaio,Brigitte Hiller*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the NNLO quark-pair correction to Z → qq̄ decay in Implicit Regularization (IReg) at large N_f, verifying UV subtraction, IR cancellation, gauge invariance, renormalization consistency, and unitarity without dimensional regularization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test IReg—a fixed-dimension regularization—at two loops for gauge invariance, UV/IR consistency, and perturbative unitarity in Z-boson decay, avoiding ambiguities from dimensional continuation of γ₅ and ε-tensors.

Method: NNLO calculation of final-state quark-pair corrections to Z → qq̄ using Implicit Regularization; employs recursive UV subtraction, combines virtual and real contributions for IR cancellation, performs renormalization and phase-space integration in d = 4; works in the large-N_f limit.

Result: Regulator-dependent logarithms cancel after renormalization and phase-space integration; known N_f-dependent contribution to the inclusive decay rate is reproduced; confirms consistency of IReg at NNLO for UV finiteness, IR safety, gauge invariance, and unitarity.

Conclusion: IReg is validated as a robust, symmetry-preserving framework for precision multi-loop QCD/EW calculations where d-dimensional extensions of chiral or tensor structures are problematic.

Abstract: We investigate the NNLO final-state quark-pair correction to the decay of a neutral axial-vector current ($Z$) into massless quarks within Implicit Regularization (IReg), in the limit of large $N_f$. Our calculation tests, at two loops, the recursive subtraction of ultraviolet subdivergences and the cancellation of infrared singularities between virtual and real contributions in a framework that does not rely on analytical continuation in spacetime dimension. After renormalization and phase-space integration, the regulator-dependent logarithms cancel and the known $N_f$ contribution to the inclusive rate is recovered. The result provides a non-trivial NNLO check of gauge invariance, renormalization consistency, and perturbative unitarity in IReg. In precision multi-loop calculations, fixed-dimension regularization methods can be advantageous by avoiding the dimensional continuation of intrinsically four-dimensional structures, such as $\gamma_5$ matrices and Levi-Civita tensors, thereby reducing ambiguities and symmetry-restoring counterterms.

</details>


### [177] [Typed Transverse Momentum Parton distribution function computations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40549)
*Dustin Keller*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Introduces typed-TMD, a computational framework that formalizes observation semantics, parameter dependencies, and admissible evaluation plans for transverse-momentum-dependent parton distribution (TMD) inference.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address ambiguity in TMD inference implementations arising from implicit observation semantics and parameter dependencies, even when units and array dimensions match.

Method: Develops typed-TMD—a typed computational framework that explicitly encodes observation maps, parameter dependencies, and valid evaluation strategies for TMD extraction.

Result: Enables unambiguous specification and comparison of TMD inference problems; supports rigorous validation and reproducibility of numerical TMD extractions.

Conclusion: typed-TMD provides a formal foundation for distinguishing mathematically distinct TMD inference problems, preventing erroneous equivalence assumptions based solely on syntactic alignment.

Abstract: A transverse-momentum-dependent parton distribution enters an extraction through a specified observation map and a parameter- dependent numerical calculation. Equal units and matching array dimensions do not establish that two implementations represent the same inference problem. We present typed-TMD, a computational framework that makes observation semantics, parameter dependencies, and admissible evaluation plans explicit.

</details>


<div id='hep-ex'></div>

# hep-ex [[Back]](#toc)

### [178] [A Search in LHCb Open Data for $f$-Baryon Resonances Predicted at HADRON 2025](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37674)
*Soctt Chapman*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper tests a theoretical prediction of two new baryon-like states (with a hypothesized 'f' quark) appearing as narrow ΛcK⁻ resonances at 3800 and 3950 MeV in B̄ → ΛbΛcK⁻ decays, using public LHCb data and a 2D Dalitz plot likelihood analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To experimentally test a prior HADRON 2025 prediction of exotic baryonic states containing a new 'f' quark, manifesting as narrow ΛcK⁻ resonances in B̄ → ΛbΛcK⁻.

Method: Unbinned, background-aware two-dimensional Dalitz likelihood fit to public LHCb data; compares three hypotheses: (i) baseline (phase space + N(1900) in ΛbK⁻), (ii) fixed-mass resonances at 3800/3950 MeV, (iii) one fixed mass (3800 MeV), one floating mass/width, plus two exotic ΛcΛb contributions.

Result: Model (ii) yields nominal significance 4.02σ over baseline; model (iii) yields 6.17σ; however, significances are preliminary—subject to boundary effects (e.g., width saturation), non-negativity constraints, trials penalty, and unaccounted efficiency/systematic uncertainties.

Conclusion: No definitive discovery is claimed; the observed likelihood improvements are intriguing but statistically inconclusive due to multiple unquantified systematic and methodological limitations—further analysis including full uncertainty treatment and independent validation is required.

Abstract: A prediction presented at HADRON 2025 proposed two baryon-like states containing an additional hypothesized $f$ quark that could appear in the decay $\Bbar\to\Lb\Lc K^-$ in the form of narrow $\Lc K^-$ resonances at 3800 and 3950~MeV. Public LHCb data are used here to compare three hypotheses with an unbinned, background-aware two-dimensional Dalitz likelihood. The baseline contains nonresonant three-body phase space and an $N(1900)$ contribution in $\Lb K^-$. A second model tests the two a-priori prediction masses directly, fixing $\Lc K^-$ resonances at 3800 and 3950~MeV while fitting their widths. A third model keeps the 3800-MeV mass fixed, allows the second $\Lc K^-$ mass and width to float, and adds two exotic $\Lc\Lb$ contributions. The second model gives a nominal Wilks-equivalent improvement of $4.02\sigma$ over the baseline, while the third gives a nominal value of $6.17\sigma$. The quoted significances are model-comparison diagnostics only: the resonance fractions are constrained to be non-negative, some fitted widths reach parameter boundaries, the floating mass introduces a trials effect, and efficiency and systematic uncertainties are not yet incorporated.

</details>


### [179] [First search for sidereal and annual modulation of $Z$-pole asymmetries with the recovered SLD data set](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40282)
*Wonyong Chung*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper performs the first time-resolved analysis of electroweak asymmetries at the Z pole using recovered SLD polarized e+e−→Z data, finding no evidence for Lorentz violation (null sidereal modulations); it sets 95% C.L. bounds on anisotropy tensor components ρ₀, ρ₁, ρ₂ and maps them to leading-order SME coefficients k_W and k_φφ, with sensitivities at the 10⁻² level; it also introduces a novel Z-pole birefringence channel via thrust-axis azimuthal modulation and constrains instrumental drifts using hadronization/leptonic asymmetries.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To search for Lorentz and CPT violation in the electroweak sector via time-dependent (sidereal, diurnal, annual) modulations of the left-right asymmetry A_LR at the Z pole — a sensitive probe of spacetime anisotropy predicted by the Standard-Model Extension (SME).

Method: Unbinned maximum-likelihood fit to sidereal-phase distribution of reconstructed A_LR using 385,583 hadronic Z decays from SLD (1996–1998), incorporating per-event signed Compton polarization and as-built SLC beam azimuth; tensor decomposition of A_LR anisotropy in Sun-centered frame; leading-order SME matching to map ρ_i bounds onto k_W and k_φφ coefficients; derivation and pilot analysis of a new birefringence-sensitive helicity-odd thrust-axis modulation; auxiliary sidereal analyses of hadronization and leptonic asymmetries to control systematics.

Result: No statistically significant sidereal, solar-diurnal, or annual modulation observed in A_LR: sidereal first-harmonic amplitude bounded at 0.0086 (95% C.L., stat.) — confirmed independently on 1998 subset (amplitude 0.0018, p=0.90); anisotropy tensor components bounded as ρ₁ ≤ 0.060, ρ₂ ≤ 0.18, ρ₀ ≤ 0.092 (95% C.L.); corresponding SME coefficients k_W and k_φφ constrained at ~10⁻² level, with some components previously unconstrained; null result for new birefringence channel; instrumental drifts bounded at 10⁻⁴–10⁻³ fractional level.

Conclusion: No evidence for Lorentz violation in the electroweak or Higgs sectors at the sensitivity achieved; the analysis establishes new laboratory constraints on SME coefficients in the two-time-index electroweak gauge and Higgs sectors; the methodology validates time-resolved Z-pole asymmetries as a precision tool for fundamental symmetry tests, and introduces a novel birefringence observable with enhanced kinematic sensitivity.

Abstract: We report what is, to our knowledge, the first time-resolved analysis of electroweak asymmetries at the $Z$ pole, using the public release of the recovered SLD collision data: $6.6\times10^5$ polarized $e^+e^-\to Z$ events from the 1996-1998 SLC runs, with 385583 selected hadronic $Z$ decays spanning 782 sidereal cycles. The left-right asymmetry $A_{LR}$, reconstructed with the published SLD selection and per-event signed Compton polarization, shows no sidereal, solar-diurnal, or annual modulation; a simultaneous unbinned likelihood fit bounds the sidereal first-harmonic amplitude at 0.0086 (95% C.L., statistical). The analysis, frozen on the initial partial release, applied unchanged to the 229833 events of the later-released 1998 run confirms the null (amplitude 0.0018, p=0.90). Using the as-built SLC beam azimuth from the release's documentation corpus, we bound the asymmetry's anisotropy tensor in the Sun-centered frame: $\rho_1\le0.060$, $\rho_2\le0.18$, and, for vector backgrounds, $\rho_0\le0.092$ (95% C.L.). A leading-order matching maps these onto minimal Standard-Model-Extension coefficients as $10^{-2}$-level sensitivities to the two-time-index block of the electroweak gauge-sector coefficient $k_W$ (components with no existing laboratory constraint) and to the Higgs-sector coefficient $k_{\phi\phi}$; the insertions split the $Z$ pole by polarization, so for the tensor families assessed the resonance saturates the sidereal amplitude near $10^{-2}$ and the Born-model bounds exclude finite coefficient bands rather than set an upper limit. We also derive a previously unpublished $Z$-pole birefringence channel in the helicity-odd azimuthal modulation of the thrust axis, with response enhanced by about $2M_Z/\Gamma_Z$; a Born-level pilot is null. First sidereal analyses of hadronization and leptonic asymmetries bound instrumental drifts at the $10^{-4}$-$10^{-3}$ fractional level.

</details>


### [180] [FCC-μe: High Energy, High Luminosity Muon-Electron Collider & Neutrino Factory at CERN](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40451)
*K. Piotrzkowski*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: Proposes a novel muon-electron (μ⁺e⁻) collider configuration at the FCC-ee/CEPC facility, enabling 1–2 TeV collisions with luminosity >10³⁴ cm⁻²s⁻¹ alongside standard e⁺e⁻ operation; highlights Higgs self-coupling measurements, outlines the accelerator layout (ultra-cold anti-muon source → booster → main ring), and discusses implications for neutrino physics and future high-energy storage rings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable precision measurements of the Higgs boson self-interaction and explore new physics beyond the Standard Model via high-energy, high-luminosity μ⁺e⁻ collisions—complementing conventional e⁺e⁻ programs at FCC-ee or CEPC.

Method: Design and conceptual layout of FCC-μe: includes production and cooling of ultra-cold anti-muons, acceleration via a dedicated muon booster, injection into a main muon storage ring, integration with existing e⁺e⁻ infrastructure, and co-location with a neutrino factory; addresses beam dynamics, machine-detector interface, and luminosity optimization.

Result: Feasibility study indicates that μ⁺e⁻ collisions at √s = 1–2 TeV and average luminosity >10³⁴ cm⁻²s⁻¹ are achievable concurrently with e⁺e⁻ operation; the layout supports both Higgs self-coupling studies and high-intensity neutrino beams.

Conclusion: FCC-μe represents a unique and synergistic extension of circular e⁺e⁻ colliders, offering unmatched sensitivity to Higgs self-interactions and establishing anti-muon beams as essential components for next-generation high-energy storage rings and advanced neutrino facilities.

Abstract: The concept is presented of making at the FCC-$ee$ (or CEPC) facility $\mu^+e^-$ collisions at the centre-of-mass energy between 1 and 2 TeV and the average luminosity above $10^{34}$cm$^{-2}$s$^{-1}$, concurrently to the $e^+e^-$ collisions. First, a concise scientific motivation is given, including the exciting prospect of measurements of the Higgs boson self-interaction, and is followed by a presentation of the FCC-$\mu e$ layout -- from the source of ultra-cold anti-muons, through the muon booster, to the main muon ring. Basic considerations are also given regarding the machine-detector interface and the performance of the FCC-$\mu e$ and associated neutrino factory. The paper concludes with an outlook underlying relevance of such anti-muon beams for any future high energy storage ring as well as for neutrino science.

</details>


### [181] [The Position Resolution of a Charged Particle Hodoscope Consisting of Triangular Scintillator Counters](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37955)
*Edmond Craig Dukes*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the position resolution of triangular-counter hodoscopes with wavelength-shifting fibers, deriving approximate expressions for the uncertainty in interpolated x and y positions as a function of particle impact position.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify and improve the position resolution of low-cost, uncooled hodoscopes based on triangular scintillation counters with embedded wavelength-shifting fibers, particularly leveraging light-yield interpolation.

Method: Analytical derivation of approximate expressions for the uncertainty in interpolated x- and y-coordinates, building on an explanation of the light-yield interpolation technique used for sub-counter-resolution positioning.

Result: Closed-form approximate expressions for the spatial uncertainties σₓ and σ_y as functions of the true impact position (x, y) within the detector geometry.

Conclusion: The derived uncertainty expressions provide a quantitative framework to predict and optimize position resolution in triangular-counter hodoscopes, confirming that interpolation yields ~10× better resolution than counter pitch, and enabling informed design trade-offs.

Abstract: Hodoscopes consisting of triangular counters with embedded wavelength shifting fibers are becoming more popular as they are relatively inexpensive, are easily fabricated, need no cooling systems, employ low-cost photodetectors, require only modest voltages, and perhaps most importantly, have good position resolution. Through interpolation of the light yields between adjacent counters, position resolutions that are typically an order of magnitude better than the counter separation can be obtained. Here, after a brief explanation of how the interpolation is done to improve the position resolution, we derive an approximate expressions for the uncertainty in the interpolated $x$ and $y$ positions as a function of the impact position of the charged particle.

</details>


### [182] [Amplitude analysis of the charmed baryon decay Lambda_c+ to p K_S^0 pi0](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38406)
*M. Ablikim,M. N. Achasov,P. Adlarson,X. C. Ai,C. S. Akondi,R. Aliberti,A. Amoroso,L. P. An,Q. An,M. S. Anderson,Y. Bai,O. Bakina,H. R. Bao,X. L. Bao,M. Barbagiovanni,V. Batozskaya,K. Begzsuren,N. Berger,M. Berlowski,M. B. Bertani,D. Bettoni,F. Bianchi,E. Bianco,A. Bortone,I. Boyko,R. A. Briere,A. Brueggemann,D. Cabiati,H. Cai,M. H. Cai,X. Cai,X. K. Cai,G. F. Cao,N. Cao,X. Cao,S. A. Cetin,X. Y. Chai,J. F. Chang,T. T. Chang,G. R. Che,Y. Z. Che,C. H. Chen,Chao Chen,E. N. Chen,G. Chen,H. S. Chen,H. Y. Chen,J. Chen,J. D. Chen,M. L. Chen,S. J. Chen,S. M. Chen,T. Chen,W. Chen,X. R. Chen,X. T. Chen,X. Y. Chen,Y. B. Chen,Y. Q. Chen,Z. K. Chen,J. Cheng,L. N. Cheng,S. K. Choi,X. Chu,G. Cibinetto,F. Cossio,J. Cottee-Meldrum,H. L. Dai,J. P. Dai,X. C. Dai,A. Dbeyssi,R. E. de Boer,D. Dedovich,Z. Y. Deng,A. Denig,I. Denisenko,M. Destefanis,F. De Mori,E. Di Fiore,X. X. Ding,Y. Ding,Y. Q. Ding,J. Dong,L. Y. Dong,M. Y. Dong,Z. J. Dong,K. B. Du,M. C. Du,S. X. Du,Shaoxu Du,X. L. Du,Y. Q. Du,C. D. Duan,Z. H. Duan,P. Egorov,G. F. Fan,K. X. Fan,Y. H. Fan,Y. L. Fan,Z. F. Fan*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: First amplitude analysis of Λc+ → p KS0 π0 using 4.5 fb⁻¹ e⁺e⁻ data at √s = 4.600–4.699 GeV; observes and measures resonant contributions from pK̄*(892)⁰, Δ(1600)⁺KS⁰, and N(1710)⁺KS⁰ with high significance; reports mass, width, and branching fractions with statistical and systematic uncertainties.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To perform the first model-independent amplitude analysis of the singly Cabibbo-suppressed decay Λc+ → p KS0 π0, enabling identification and characterization of intermediate resonant states contributing to this three-body final state — crucial for understanding charmed baryon dynamics and testing QCD-inspired models.

Method: Amplitude analysis (maximum-likelihood fit) of Λc+ → p KS0 π0 decays using high-statistics e⁺e⁻ annihilation data collected by BESIII at center-of-mass energies 4.600–4.699 GeV (4.5 fb⁻¹). The fit incorporates relativistic Breit–Wigner resonant amplitudes for candidate intermediate states (e.g., pK̄*(892)⁰, Δ(1600)⁺KS⁰, N(1710)⁺KS⁰), nonresonant components, and detector response via efficiency and resolution corrections.

Result: Observation of three resonant contributions: Λc+ → p K̄*(892)⁰ (12.8σ), Λc+ → Δ(1600)⁺ KS⁰ (14.5σ), and Λc+ → N(1710)⁺ KS⁰ (6.2σ). Measured Δ(1600)⁺ mass = 1520.4 ± 8.8 ± 37.7 MeV, width = 199.4 ± 26.4 ± 39.9 MeV; N(1710)⁺ mass = 1719.7 ± 9.4 ± 9.1 MeV, width = 72.0 ± 18.0 ± 15.0 MeV. Branching fractions determined with sub-percent precision (stat.) for dominant modes.

Conclusion: This analysis establishes the dominant resonant substructures in Λc+ → p KS0 π0, providing the first direct experimental evidence and precise parameters for Δ(1600)⁺ and N(1710)⁺ production in charmed baryon decays; results constrain theoretical models of baryonic resonance formation and strong dynamics in charm decays.

Abstract: Using $e^+e^-$ annihilation data corresponding to an integrated luminosity of 4.5 fb$^{-1}$ collected with the BESIII detector at center-of-mass energies between 4.600 and 4.699 GeV, the first amplitude analysis of the charmed baryon decay $\Lambda_c^+ \to p K_S^0 \pi^0$ is performed. The processes $\Lambda_c^+ \to p \bar{K}^{*}(892)^0$, $\Lambda_c^+ \to \Delta(1600)^+ K_S^0$, and $\Lambda_c^+ \to N(1710)^+ K_S^0$ are identified with statistical significances of $12.8\sigma$, $14.5\sigma$, and $6.2\sigma$, respectively. The mass and width of $\Delta(1600)^+$ are measured to be $(1520.4 \pm 8.8 \pm 37.7)~\text{MeV}$ and $(199.4 \pm 26.4 \pm 39.9)~\text{MeV}$, respectively. The mass and width of $N(1710)^+$ are measured to be $(1719.7 \pm 9.4 \pm 9.1)~\text{MeV}$ and $(72.0 \pm 18.0 \pm 15.0)~\text{MeV}$, respectively. The branching fractions of these processes are determined to be $\mathcal{B}[\Lambda_c^+ \rightarrow \Delta(1600)^+ (\rightarrow p \pi^0) K_S^0] = (8.10 \pm 0.80 \pm 5.57) \times 10^{-3}$, $\mathcal{B}[\Lambda_c^+ \rightarrow N(1710)^+ (\rightarrow p \pi^0) K_S^0] = (1.58 \pm 0.48 \pm 0.77) \times 10^{-3}$, and $\mathcal{B}[\Lambda_c^+ \rightarrow p \bar{K}^{*}(892)^0 (\rightarrow K_S^0 \pi^0)] = (2.63 \pm 0.47 \pm 0.29) \times 10^{-3}$, where the first uncertainties are statistical and the second systematic.

</details>


### [183] [The TAMBO Project and the TAMBITO Prototype](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38777)
*Pablo Fernández-Menéndez*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: TAMBO is a proposed mountain-based observatory in Peru designed to detect ultra-high-energy (1–1000 PeV) tau neutrinos using ~5,000 particle detectors on a canyon face, targeting a background-suppressed energy window between optical-Cherenkov and radio detectors; current R&D centers on the 100-unit TAMBITO prototype.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Overcome limited astrophysical neutrino statistics and high atmospheric background in current PeV-neutrino detection by exploiting the tau neutrino channel in an energy range (1–1000 PeV) where backgrounds are suppressed and coverage gaps exist between IceCube/KM3NeT (optical-Cherenkov) and radio-based experiments.

Method: Deploy up to 5,000 surface particle detectors on the steep face of the Colca Valley canyon to detect extensive air showers induced by tau leptons produced when tau neutrinos interact in the opposing canyon wall; development and validation via the 100-detector TAMBITO prototype.

Result: No experimental results yet; the paper reports status of R&D, design validation, and projected timeline for TAMBO, with physics potential assessed theoretically—including enhanced sensitivity to diffuse astrophysical tau neutrino fluxes and point-source identification in the 1–1000 PeV range.

Conclusion: TAMBO represents a novel, complementary approach to multi-messenger neutrino astronomy, uniquely positioned to probe the unexplored 1–1000 PeV tau neutrino window; successful deployment would significantly advance source identification and neutrino cross-section measurements at extreme energies.

Abstract: The detection of astrophysical PeV-scale neutrinos by IceCube and KM3NeT observatories has opened the window to probe new phenomena in the universe. However, a deeper study of the sources remains limited by the small astrophysical flux and the sizable atmospheric neutrino background. The Tau Air-Shower Mountain-Based Observatory (TAMBO) aims to overcome these limitations by detecting tau neutrinos from 1\,PeV to 1\,000\,PeV, an energy range in which backgrounds are largely suppressed and which lies between the reach of optical-Cherenkov observatories and that of radio-based experiments. TAMBO will employ up to 5,000 particle detectors installed on the face of a steep hill in the Colca Valley (Perú) to detect tau neutrinos interacting with the face of the canyon in front via the decay products of the associated tau leptons. In this contribution, we will describe the current status of the project and its R\&D, focusing on the 100-unit array TAMBITO prototype, the projected timeline, and the physics potential that this unique detector brings to the field.

</details>


### [184] [Measurements of transverse kinematic imbalance in muon neutrino charged-current interactions on argon with a single proton and no pions in the final state with SBND](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39042)
*R. Acciarri,L. Aliaga-Soplin,D. Andrade Aldana,C. Andreopoulos,A. Antonakis,S. Balasubramanian,A. Barnard,V. Basque,J. R. Bateman,A. Beever,B. Behera,M. Betancourt,A. Bhat,M. Bishai,A. Blake,B. Bogart,D. Brailsford,A. Brandt,S. Brickner,M. B. Brunetti,G. A. N. Buzzard,L. Camilleri,D. Caratelli,D. Carber,B. Carlson,M. F. Carneiro,R. Castillo Fernandez,F. Cavanna,A. Chappell,H. Chen,S. Chung,R. Coackley,J. I. Crespo-Anadón,C. Cuesta,Y. Dabburi,O. Dalager,M. Dall'Olio,S. K. Das,M. Del Tutto,Z. Djurcic,V. do Lago Pimentel,S. Dominguez-Vidales,M. Donnachie,M. Dubnowski,K. Duffy,S. Dytman,A. Ereditato,E. Espinoza,J. J. Evans,A. C. Ezeribe,C. Fan,A. Filkins,B. Fleming,W. Foreman,D. Franco,G. Fricano,I. Furic,A. P. Furmanski,S. Gao,D. Garcia-Gamez,S. Gardiner,I. Gil-Botella,S. Gollapinni,P. Green,W. C. Griffith,L. Hagaman,P. Hamilton,B. Harris,A. Hergenhan,M. Hernandez-Morquecho,C. James,R. S. Jones,M. Jung,T. Junk,D. Kalra,G. Karagiorgi,F. Kumar,L. Kashur,K. J. Kelly,W. Ketchum,M. J. King,J. Klein,L. Kotsiopoulou,I. Kreslo,T. Kroupová,V. A. Kudryavtsev,H. Lay,R. LaZur,J.-Y. Li,K. Lin,B. R. Littlejohn,L. Liu,D. Lorca,W. C. Louis,X. Luo,A. A. Machado,P. Machado,C. Mariani,F. Marinho,J. Marshall*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: First neutrino-argon cross-section measurement from SBND using 8.8×10¹⁹ POT; reports flux-averaged single-differential CC cross sections on argon for 1p0π final states as functions of transverse kinematic imbalance variables; 12,804 signal events (91% purity); reveals significant discrepancies among event generators—especially in transverse missing momentum—establishing new benchmarks for neutrino-argon interaction modeling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide high-statistics, high-purity neutrino-argon cross-section data to constrain and validate event generators essential for precision oscillation and cross-section measurements in current and future accelerator-based neutrino experiments.

Method: Analysis of SBND Run 1 data (8.8×10¹⁹ protons-on-target); selection of muon neutrino charged-current 1-proton-0-pion (1p0π) interactions on argon; extraction of flux-averaged single-differential cross sections as functions of transverse kinematic imbalance variables (e.g., transverse missing momentum); comparison with multiple neutrino event generators (e.g., GENIE variants, NuWro).

Result: 12,804 signal candidates with 91% purity; first high-statistics differential cross sections on argon for ν_μ CC 1p0π interactions; strong discrimination among event generators—no model simultaneously describes all three transverse variables, with largest deviations in transverse missing momentum (p_T^miss).

Conclusion: The results expose critical deficiencies in current neutrino-argon interaction models, particularly in modeling final-state nucleon dynamics and hadronic system response; they establish a new empirical benchmark to guide theoretical improvements and reduce systematic uncertainties in oscillation analyses at DUNE, MicroBooNE, and SBND.

Abstract: This letter presents the first neutrino-argon cross-section measurement from the Short-Baseline Near Detector (SBND) at the Fermi National Accelerator Laboratory. We report the nominal flux-averaged single-differential cross sections of muon neutrino charged-current interactions on argon with a single proton and no pions in the final state as functions of the transverse kinematic imbalance variables. Using a subset of Run~1 data corresponding to $8.8\times10^{19}$ protons-on-target, we select 12,804 signal candidates with a purity of 91%. The extracted cross sections discriminate strongly among event-generator predictions, owing to the high statistics and the design of the analysis. Among the compared models, none successfully describes all three variables, with the largest discrepancies in the transverse missing momentum variable. These results set new benchmarks for neutrino-argon interaction modeling, which is crucial for high-precision measurements at current and future accelerator neutrino experiments.

</details>


### [185] [Dark sector searches at BESIII](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39618)
*Zhijun Li,Zhengyun You*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reviews recent BESIII searches for dark sector phenomena—including sub-GeV dark matter, dark photons, invisible kaon decays, dark baryons, and light vector bosons—using charmonia, charm mesons, hyperons, and light mesons; special emphasis is placed on the η → π⁰ + invisible channel to constrain scalar-mediated dark matter and test thermal relic models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe sub-GeV dark sector interactions with charm and light quarks using high-statistics samples from BESIII, addressing gaps in sensitivity for light dark matter and mediators not accessible at high-energy colliders.

Method: Analysis of high-luminosity e⁺e⁻ collision data collected by BESIII, focusing on rare or forbidden decays: η → π⁰ + invisible, J/ψ → φ + invisible, D⁰ → ωγ'/γγ', K⁰_S → invisible, Ξ⁻ → π⁻χ, and χ_{cJ} → J/ψ V (V → e⁺e⁻); background suppression via kinematic constraints, missing mass/momentum techniques, and signal region optimization.

Result: Improved upper limits on branching fractions for all considered invisible and semi-invisible channels; strongest constraints on scalar-coupled dark matter from η → π⁰ + invisible, excluding thermal relic parameter space for m_χ ≲ 100 MeV in certain scalar mediator scenarios.

Conclusion: BESIII’s precision charm- and tau-charm–threshold physics provides a powerful low-energy frontier for dark sector searches; results significantly constrain light dark matter models, dark photon couplings, and exotic baryonic dark matter, complementing direct detection and high-energy collider experiments.

Abstract: Large samples of charmonia, charm mesons, hyperons and light mesons collected by BESIII offer sensitive tests of dark sector interactions with charm and light quarks. This contribution reviews recent searches for sub-GeV dark matter in $\eta\to\pi^0+\mathrm{invisible}$ and $J/\psi\to\phi+\mathrm{invisible}$, massless dark photons in $D^0\to\omega\gamma'$ and $D^0\to\gamma\gamma'$, invisible $K^0_S$ decays, dark baryons in $\Xi^-\to\pi^-\chi$, and light vector bosons in $\chi_{cJ}\to J/\psi V$, $V\to e^+e^-$. Particular attention is given to the $\eta$ search, which constrains scalar-mediated dark matter and tests thermal relic benchmarks.

</details>


### [186] [Dark-field Search for Quantum Vacuum Signals in the Low-Energy Regime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39867)
*Aimé Matheron,Michal Šmíd,Felix Karbstein,Sebastian Göde,Willi Hippler,Robert Lötzsch,Michal Andrzejewski,Sergey Antipov,Carsten Baehtz,Victorien Bouffetier,Erik Brambrink,Jakub Bulička,Samuele Di Dio Cafiso,Rafael Celestre,Jaromír Chalupský,Thomas E. Cowan,Jörg Grenzer,Travis Griffin,Věra Hájková,Jens Hauser,Peter Hilz,Hauke Höppner,Alžběta Horynová,Lingen Huang,Oliver Humphries,Šimon Jelínek,Libor Juha,Pooyan Khademi,Alejandro Laso-García,Masruri Masruri,Nikita Medvedev,Motoaki Nakatsutsumi,Gerhard G. Paulus,Jennifer Popp,Thomas R. Preston,Isabella Probst,Sripati V. Rahul,Thomas Roth,Felipe C. Salgado,Roland Sauerbrey,Alexander Sävert,Hans-Peter Schlenvoigt,Jan Patrick Schwinkendorf,Frank Seiboth,Georgiy Shoulga,Minxue Tang,Abha Thazhathe Peedika,Monika Toncian,Toma Toncian,Vojtěch Vozda,Edgar Weckert,Colin Wessel,Jan Wild,Ulf Zastrau,Matt Zepf*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports a dark-field experiment at the European XFEL to search for light-by-light scattering using pump-probe collisions between X-ray and near-infrared laser beams; no signal was observed, yielding the tightest upper limit on the low-energy light-by-light scattering cross section to date—7×10⁴ times the QED prediction at 95% CL.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To experimentally probe light-by-light scattering—a pure quantum electrodynamics (QED) process forbidden in classical electrodynamics—at low energies (ω⋆ = 116 eV), where previous experimental constraints were extremely weak.

Method: A dark-field, pump-probe setup colliding the EuXFEL X-ray beam with the ReLaX 300-TW NIR laser at the HED instrument; employed polarization filtering, per-shot spatiotemporal overlap diagnostics, polarizing beam splitters, residual background characterization, and start-to-end simulations for analysis.

Result: No excess signal above background observed in 2751 pump-probe shots; derived upper limit on the light-by-light scattering cross section is 7×10⁴ × σ_QED at 95% CL.

Conclusion: This experiment sets the most stringent bound on low-energy light-by-light scattering to date—improving prior limits by ten orders of magnitude—and validates the dark-field approach with polarization discrimination for future QED vacuum studies.

Abstract: We report on the experimental details of a dark-field search for light-by-light scattering signals performed at the High Energy Density (HED) instrument of the European XFEL (EuXFEL) in a pump-probe type experiment colliding the EuXFEL beam with the 300-TW-class near-infrared Relativistic Laser at XFEL (ReLaX) at a center-of-mass photon energy of $\omega_\star = 116\,\mathrm{eV}$. Especially in combination with polarization filtering, the dark-field concept allows the signal-to-background ratio to be significantly enhanced and both polarization channels to be recorded. This work elaborates on the experimental setup and the diagnostics used, from the per-shot spatiotemporal-overlap diagnostics, via the polarizing beam splitter, to the characterization of the residual background. Moreover, it provides details on the start-to-end simulations of the setup used for analysis. Accumulating 2751 pump-probe collisions, no excess above background is observed, and an upper limit on the light-by-light scattering cross section of $7\times10^{4}$ times the QED prediction at a 95% confidence level is derived. This is the most stringent bound on low-energy light-by-light scattering achieved to date, representing an improvement by ten orders of magnitude upon previous experimental limits.

</details>
