# math-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [187] [Generalized Dirac Equation in Metric-Affine Spacetimes: Consistency, Effective Mass, and Spinor Curvature](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.23609)
*Muzaffer Adak,Ali Bagci,Caglar Pala,Ozcan Sert*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper formulates a generalized Dirac equation in metric-affine spacetimes (with curvature, torsion, and non-metricity), derives consistency conditions from Lagrangian symmetry and Hamiltonian Hermiticity, identifies scalar/pseudoscalar mass terms from coframe couplings, and reveals an algebraic link between effective mass and residual spinor curvature—even in the absence of affine curvature, torsion, or non-metricity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To consistently extend the Dirac equation to metric-affine spacetimes incorporating all geometric degrees of freedom (curvature, torsion, non-metricity) while preserving fundamental physical principles: Lagrangian symmetrization, covariance, and Hamiltonian Hermiticity with respect to the curved-space Dirac inner product.

Method: Covariantization of the Dirac equation using a Clifford-algebra-expanded spinor connection; variation of a symmetrized Lagrangian; imposition of consistency between the two approaches; analysis of Hamiltonian Hermiticity in the static isotropic symmetric teleparallel sector; Clifford decomposition of generalized spinor curvature.

Result: Eight real consistency conditions reduce 16 real coupling parameters to 8 independent ones; coframe-dependent terms generate persistent scalar and pseudoscalar mass contributions; for constant coefficients and m=0, nonzero effective mass m_eff = sqrt(16(b_3^2 + b_4^2)) arises; generalized spinor curvature can be nonzero even when affine curvature, torsion, and non-metricity vanish; when m=0, b_3^2 + b_4^2 simultaneously determines m_eff^2 and the surviving spinor curvature.

Conclusion: The generalized Dirac framework reveals intrinsic algebraic connections between geometry-induced mass generation and residual spinor curvature, demonstrating that nontrivial quantum field behavior can emerge purely from the structure of the spinor connection—irrespective of classical spacetime curvature, torsion, or non-metricity.

Abstract: We formulate a generalized Dirac equation in four-dimensional metric-affine spacetimes with curvature, torsion, and non-metricity. The spinor connection is expanded over the complete basis of the Clifford algebra $cl(1,3)$ and contains eight complex coupling constants. Requiring agreement between direct covariantization of the Dirac equation and variation of the corresponding symmetrized Lagrangian imposes eight real consistency conditions, reducing the number of independent real parameters from sixteen to eight. In the static isotropic symmetric teleparallel sector, formal Hermiticity of the Hamiltonian with respect to the curved-space Dirac inner product reproduces the corresponding restrictions on the non-metricity and coframe couplings. The coframe-dependent terms introduce scalar and pseudoscalar mass contributions that persist in Minkowski spacetime. For a single free Dirac field with constant coefficients, they yield one effective mass, $m_{\mathrm{eff}}=\sqrt{(m-4b_3)^2+16b_4^2}$, and a chiral phase removable by a constant chiral field redefinition. A nonzero effective mass is therefore possible even when the original mass parameter vanishes. We also derive the Clifford decomposition of the generalized spinor curvature and show that it can remain nonzero when affine curvature, torsion, and non-metricity all vanish. When $m=0$, the combination $b_3^2+b_4^2$ determines both the effective mass squared and the surviving spinor curvature, establishing an algebraic link between these contributions.

</details>


### [188] [Twinor Theory: Field Equations from $(A)dS_4^{\mathbb C}$ Spin-Tractors: A Twin to Twistor Theory Modelled on $(A)dS_4^{\mathbb C}$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40435)
*Chen-Hsu Chien*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces 'twinor theory' as a spacetime-based extension of twistor theory, built on holomorphic reductive Cartan geometry over complexified (A)dS₄; it defines spin-tractor bundles (Dirac, ladder, chiral twinors), derives a fundamental field equation D𝒴 = 𝒥 with constrained contractions, recovers massive Dirac–Fierz–Pauli and massless zero-rest-mass equations on flat Lorentzian backgrounds, identifies the anti-de Sitter reality condition as essential for positive real mass², incorporates mass-coupling phase classically, and formulates nonlinear gravity via adjoint curvature and Einstein–Cartan dynamics without an action.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a geometric, background-flexible, and inherently chiral framework unifying massive/massless field equations, incorporating spin and gravity on equal footing via Cartan geometry—bypassing reliance on Lagrangians or fixed backgrounds while preserving both chiralities and enabling higher-spin consistency.

Method: Constructs twinor theory using holomorphic reductive Cartan geometry modelled on complexified (A)dS₄; defines spin-tractor bundles (Dirac, ladder, chiral twinors); derives the twinor field equation D𝒴 = 𝒥 and analyzes its contractions, divergences, and source constraints; imposes reality conditions to fix mass normalization; formulates gravity via full adjoint curvature and Einstein–Cartan dynamics from energy–momentum and spin currents; uses adjoint Bianchi identity and BGG-type sequence for compatibility and deformations.

Result: Reproduction of the massive Dirac–Fierz–Pauli system and zero-rest-mass equations on flat torsion-free Lorentzian backgrounds; identification of the anti-de Sitter reality condition as necessary and sufficient for positive real mass squared; classical emergence of mass-coupling phase in the connection; action-free formulation of nonlinear gravity with torsion-free infinitesimal deformations organized by a BGG-type sequence; explicit coupling mechanism for matter via D𝒥 = ℱ_D 𝒴 and contracted source relations.

Conclusion: Twinor theory provides a coherent, geometric, and pre-quantum foundation for relativistic fields—including massive and massless, chiral and non-chiral, spin-½ to higher-spin—with intrinsic gravity coupling, independent of variational principles, and with mass arising from a background-independent reality condition tied to the particle scale.

Abstract: Twinor theory is formulated as a spacetime-based counterpart to twistor theory using holomorphic reductive Cartan geometry modelled on complexified $(A)dS_4$. The fundamental spin-tractor bundle defines Dirac twinors, its symmetric powers define ladder twinors, and their extremal Lorentz components define chiral twinors. The general \textit{twinor field equation} is $D\mathbf Y=\mathbf J$. Its adjacent contractions and mixed divergence are constrained by source relations; no additional equation is imposed on its completely symmetric projection. On a flat torsion-free Lorentzian background, the adjacent equations with the mass-normalization source and vanishing additional contracted sources, together with vanishing mixed divergence, reproduce the massive \textit{Dirac--Fierz--Pauli system}; the massless chiral realization gives the zero-rest-mass equations. Within the fixed mass normalization, the reality condition yielding a positive real mass squared is the \textit{anti-de Sitter reality condition} on the particle scale, independently of the cosmological scale and background curvature. The mass-coupling phase is therefore present in the classical connection before quantization. Nonlinear gravity is formulated through the full adjoint curvature, with energy--momentum and spin currents specifying Einstein--Cartan dynamics without requiring an action. The adjoint Bianchi identity constrains source compatibility, while a BGG-type sequence organizes torsion-free infinitesimal deformations. Matter interactions enter through the coupled connection and contracted source relations, with curvature compatibility recorded by $D\mathbf J=\mathcal F_D\mathbf Y$. Both chiralities are retained, and the additional consistency requirements for interacting higher-spin fields are distinguished from the underlying bundle construction.

</details>


### [189] [Exact Solution of the $(2+1)$-Dimensional Dunkl-Klein-Gordon Oscillator with a Uniform Magnetic Field in Non-Commutative Phase Space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37820)
*R. D. Mota,D. Ojeda-Guillén*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper solves the Klein-Gordon oscillator in a non-commutative phase space generalized via Dunkl derivatives, with and without a uniform magnetic field; it derives exact eigenfunctions and energy spectra (generalized Landau levels), analyzes parity symmetry, takes the non-relativistic limit, and confirms consistency with prior commutative Dunkl models in appropriate limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the combined effects of non-commutativity, Dunkl calculus (encoding reflection symmetries), and external magnetic fields on relativistic quantum systems—specifically the Klein-Gordon oscillator—and to understand how these deformations modify spectral structure and parity properties.

Method: Introduces Dunkl derivatives into commutative phase space to study reflection parity; applies the Seiberg-Witten map to lift the system to non-commutative phase space; solves the resulting deformed Klein-Gordon equation exactly for eigenfunctions and energy spectrum; computes the non-relativistic limit; performs graphical analysis of energy dependence on deformation parameters; verifies consistency via limiting cases.

Result: Exact analytical solutions for eigenfunctions and energy spectra are obtained in both cases (with/without magnetic field); energy levels exhibit parity-dependent splitting due to interplay of Dunkl parameters, non-commutativity, and magnetic field; generalized Landau levels emerge under magnetic field; non-relativistic limit is derived; numerical/graphical behavior of spectra is analyzed.

Conclusion: The synergy of Dunkl deformation, non-commutative geometry, and magnetic field induces parity-sensitive spectral modifications in the Klein-Gordon oscillator; the framework consistently reduces to known commutative Dunkl models when non-commutativity or magnetic field is switched off, validating its theoretical coherence.

Abstract: We solve the Klein-Gordon oscillator in a non-commutative phase space, generalized using Dunkl derivatives, both with and without interaction with a uniform magnetic field. For each of these cases, we begin by introducing the Dunkl derivative into the standard commutative phase space to evaluate the reflection parity symmetries. Then, using the Seiberg-Witten map, we extend the generalized system to a non-commutative phase space. In both cases, we determine the eigenfunctions and the energy spectrum exactly (which correspond to generalized Landau levels when a magnetic field is present). Furthermore, we compute the non-relativistic limit of the system and graphically analyze the behavior of the energy spectra. Our results demonstrate that the combination of the Dunkl deformation parameters, the non-commutative phase space geometry, and the magnetic field produces a parity-dependent modification of the energy levels. Finally, we verify that when the external magnetic field or the non-commutative parameters vanish, our exact solutions correctly reduce to the commutative Dunkl models established in previous works.

</details>


### [190] [Algebraic Quantum Field Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37904)
*Kasia Rejzner*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces algebraic quantum field theory (AQFT), emphasizing its structural foundations—observables, spacetime localization, and state-independent representation theory—and highlights conceptual distinctions from ordinary quantum mechanics, including the Reeh–Schlieder theorem, local von Neumann algebras, superselection sectors, and extensions to curved spacetimes, interactions, and gauge theories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a rigorous, state-independent framework for quantum field theory that accommodates diverse physical situations (vacuum, thermal, charged states) without presupposing a common Hilbert space, while clarifying foundational differences from finite-dimensional quantum mechanics and enabling generalizations to curved spacetimes and interacting/gauge theories.

Method: Develops AQFT axiomatically from quantum mechanical principles; constructs the free scalar field explicitly; applies operator-algebraic methods (von Neumann algebras, split property); uses representation theory for superselection sectors; extends via local covariance, perturbative renormalization, and homological techniques.

Result: Clarifies how causality, positive energy, and locality constrain measurements; establishes structural distinctions between QFT and quantum mechanics (e.g., type III local algebras, Reeh–Schlieder cyclicity); links charges to statistics and symmetries; enables consistent formulations in curved spacetimes and interacting/gauge theories.

Conclusion: AQFT provides a robust, flexible, and conceptually coherent foundation for quantum physics beyond flat-space, free-field limits—unifying measurement theory, quantum information, and modern theoretical challenges like gravity and renormalization.

Abstract: Algebraic quantum field theory describes a quantum system through its observables, their localisation in spacetime, and the states that assign them expectation values. The separation of the specification of observables from the choice of a state makes it possible to compare vacuum, thermal and charged representations without assuming that they share a preferred Hilbert space. We develop the framework from familiar quantum mechanics, construct the free scalar field, and explain how causality and positive energy constrain local measurements. The Reeh--Schlieder theorem, the structure of local von Neumann algebras and the split property reveal key differences between quantum theory in the continuum (i.e. QFT) and ordinary quantum mechanics with finitely many degrees of freedom. Superselection theory relates observable charges to statistics and internal symmetries. Finally, local covariance and perturbative and homological methods extend the discussion to curved spacetime, interactions and gauge theories. We also discuss measurement theory and quantum information aspects of AQFT.

</details>


### [191] [From gauge-covariant Noether invariants to electromagnetic velocity-dependent mechanics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38128)
*A. S. de Castro*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs a systematic reduction from relativistic gauge-invariant Noether charges (derived via Noether–Bessel–Hagen formalism) to nonrelativistic, velocity-dependent conserved quantities—such as generalized linear and angular momentum and energy—while preserving gauge quasi-invariance. It clarifies how Killing vectors generating symmetries of the electromagnetic potential (up to gauge transformations) yield worldline charges whose low-velocity limits recover standard nonrelativistic Noether charges; applies this to uniform magnetic fields and Darwin interaction; and distinguishes conserved quantities across relativistic particle, effective many-particle, and closed particle–field descriptions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile and explicitly link three distinct physical descriptions—relativistic charged-particle mechanics with gauge-invariant symmetries, nonrelativistic velocity-dependent mechanics (e.g., with Darwin interaction), and closed particle–field systems—by tracing how conserved quantities descend from spacetime symmetries under gauge-quasi-invariant conditions, without conflating their physically distinct interpretations.

Method: Uses the Noether–Bessel–Hagen framework for gauge-covariant symmetries of the relativistic worldline action S_p = −mc∫ds + q∫A; derives the canonical one-form P = −m u^♭ + qA; identifies conserved charges C_X = P(X) − qχ_X associated with Killing vectors X satisfying £_X A = dχ_X; computes their nonrelativistic (low-velocity) limit to recover generalized Noether charges; applies to uniform B-field and Darwin Lagrangian; and invokes total stress-energy tensor for closed particle–field systems.

Result: An explicit, gauge-respecting reduction map from relativistic Killing charges to nonrelativistic generalized momenta/energy; demonstration that uniform magnetic field yields gauge-dependent translational charge but gauge-invariant physical content; derivation of Darwin interaction as the O(c⁻²) conservative many-particle limit after electromagnetic field elimination; and identification of total stress-energy Killing charge as the conserved quantity for closed particle–field systems.

Conclusion: The paper establishes a rigorous, hierarchy-preserving correspondence between relativistic gauge-invariant symmetries and their nonrelativistic mechanical counterparts, showing that conserved quantities are not universally identical across descriptions but are systematically related through a well-defined reduction that respects both spacetime geometry and electromagnetic gauge structure.

Abstract: For a charged particle, a spacetime symmetry need preserve the electromagnetic potential only up to a gauge transformation. Starting from the standard Noether--Bessel-Hagen construction, we make explicit the reduction from the relativistic gauge-invariant worldline charge to the generalized constants of nonrelativistic velocity-dependent mechanics, retaining the quasi-invariance boundary term throughout. With the convention $S_p=-mc\int ds+q\int A$, the canonical worldline one-form is $P=-m u^\flat+qA$, and a Killing vector satisfying $\mathcal L_XA=d\chi_X$ generates $C_X=P(X)-q\chi_X$. Its low-velocity limit is the Noether--Bessel-Hagen charge of the coordinate-time Lagrangian, from which generalized linear momentum, angular momentum and energy follow generator by generator. A uniform magnetic field gives an elementary gauge-dependent representative but gauge-invariant translational charge. The Darwin interaction then supplies the conservative $O(c^{-2})$ many-particle realization after field elimination, while a closed particle--field system is governed by the total stress-energy Killing charge. The result is an explicit reduction map linking three established descriptions without identifying their distinct conserved quantities.

</details>


### [192] [Homogenization theory of topological Anderson insulators: genuine gaps and pseudo-gaps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38150)
*Guillaume Bal*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a homogenization theory for topological Anderson insulators, showing how disorder—especially periodic or random fluctuations at small scale ε—induces non-trivial topology via a second-order (Born) correction M; it establishes norm-resolvent convergence to a homogenized operator, proves bulk-interface correspondence for half-plane fluctuations, and introduces a robust cut-based index for random cases where rare regions destroy the spectral gap.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously understand how disorder induces non-trivial topology in topological Anderson insulators—specifically, how zero-mean electric/magnetic perturbations, typically considered topologically trivial, can drive topological phase transitions via homogenization effects.

Method: Homogenization theory for elliptic systems with ε-scale fluctuations; norm-resolvent convergence analysis; Born approximation for second-order effective corrections; interface index construction using deformed cuts to avoid rare regions in random settings; spectral and topological analysis of homogenized operators including bulk-difference invariants.

Result: Periodic fluctuations generate order-one topological corrections M even when mean-free; half-plane fluctuations yield stable interface states carrying the bulk-difference invariant; for random fluctuations, the spectral gap becomes a pseudo-gap, but a cut-deformed index is well-defined and equals almost surely the bulk-difference invariant of the Born-homogenized operator—confirmed for first-order alloys with vanishing low moments and critical scalar-disordered BHZ-type models.

Conclusion: Disorder-induced topology in topological Anderson insulators is governed by homogenized effective operators, not just local symmetries or averages; the bulk-interface correspondence persists robustly under structured and certain random fluctuations, provided appropriate topological indices are defined via deformation to avoid spectral contamination from rare events.

Abstract: A topological Anderson insulator is an insulator whose non-trivial topological phase is induced by disorder. We develop a homogenization theory of this phenomenon for constant-coefficient elliptic systems perturbed by fluctuations oscillating at scale $\varepsilon$. Periodic fluctuations of every order below that of the operator, with appropriate amplitudes, do not average out: they produce an order-one correction $M$ given by a second-order (Born) formula, and the operator converges in the norm-resolvent sense to its homogenized limit. In particular, electric potentials and magnetic fields of zero mean, usually regarded as topologically trivial perturbations, generate corrections $M$ that change the topological phase. Fluctuations confined to a half-plane create an interface that carries the bulk-difference invariant of the homogenized operator for all $\varepsilon$ sufficiently small, a property that is stable under local defects. For random fluctuations, rare regions typically fill the homogenized gap, which becomes a pseudo-gap, and the standard interface index need no longer be defined. We define an index along cuts deformed so as to avoid the rare regions, and prove that it equals almost surely the bulk-difference invariant of the operator homogenized in the Born approximation, for first-order alloys with vanishing low moments and for a class of second-order systems with scalar disorder at criticality that includes the BHZ model.

</details>


### [193] [Nonequilibrium Thermodynamics of Semilinear Stochastic Partial Differential Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38166)
*Zelin Liu,Hao Ge*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a rigorous mathematical framework for nonequilibrium thermodynamics of semilinear stochastic PDEs, using a non-time-reversible Gaussian invariant measure as reference; it defines entropy production via Radon–Nikodym derivatives, proves its equivalence to time reversibility in stationarity, derives fluctuation theorems, and decomposes entropy production into housekeeping heat and free-energy dissipation—both satisfying integral fluctuation theorems and nonnegativity—then applies the theory to several physically relevant SPDE models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend nonequilibrium thermodynamic concepts—especially entropy production, time reversibility, and fluctuation theorems—to infinite-dimensional stochastic systems governed by semilinear SPDEs, where standard finite-dimensional or Markov chain frameworks fail and time-reversibility of the reference measure cannot be assumed.

Method: Constructs a thermodynamic formalism based on the invariant Gaussian measure of the linear (Ornstein-Uhlenbeck) part as reference; defines path-space measures, Radon–Nikodym derivatives for time reversal, entropy production rate via Girsanov-type analysis; employs stochastic calculus for SPDEs, relative entropy (Kullback–Leibler divergence), and spectral theory for covariance operators; derives balance equations and proves fluctuation theorems using measure-theoretic and functional-analytic tools.

Result: Derives an exact entropy balance equation; proves entropy production rate vanishes iff the process is time-reversible in stationarity; identifies sample-path entropy production with log Radon–Nikodym derivative between forward and reversed path measures; establishes detailed and integral fluctuation theorems; obtains decomposition into housekeeping heat and free-energy dissipation, both satisfying integral fluctuation theorems and having nonnegative expected values; shows mean free-energy dissipation equals the decay rate of relative entropy toward stationarity.

Conclusion: A consistent, mathematically rigorous nonequilibrium thermodynamic framework for semilinear SPDEs is achieved, unifying concepts from large deviations, stochastic analysis, and statistical physics; the results generalize finite-dimensional stochastic thermodynamics to infinite dimensions and accommodate non-reversible references, enabling thermodynamic analysis of spatially extended noisy systems far from equilibrium.

Abstract: We develop a rigorous mathematical framework for the nonequilibrium thermodynamics of semilinear stochastic partial differential equations. The invariant Gaussian measure of the associated Ornstein-Uhlenbeck process, which need not be time-reversible, serves as the reference measure. We define thermodynamic quantities in terms of probability densities and derive the entropy balance equation. We obtain explicit expressions for the entropy production rate and prove that its vanishing in the stationary state is equivalent to time reversibility. Sample-path entropy production is identified with the logarithm of the Radon-Nikodym derivative of the forward path measure with respect to the time-reversed path measure and is shown to satisfy fluctuation theorems. For nonstationary systems, sample-path entropy production decomposes into housekeeping heat and free-energy dissipation, each satisfying an integral fluctuation theorem. Both have nonnegative expectations, and the mean free-energy dissipation equals the decrease in relative entropy with respect to the stationary distribution. The theory is applied to semilinear stochastic heat equations with spatially correlated noise, a random polymer model, a nonlinear model with a divergence-free drift, and two coupled stochastic heat equations in contact with heat baths at different temperatures.

</details>


### [194] [Finite-volume and thermodynamic-limit lower bounds for the coherently driven Bose--Hubbard model via the Lieb--Yamazaki method](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38464)
*Maciej Janowicz,Joanna Kaleta,Filip Krzyżewski,Andrzej Zembrzuski*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives rigorous lower bounds on the ground-state energy of the coherently driven Bose--Hubbard model using a Lieb--Yamazaki positive-operator decomposition, leading to a quadratic bounding Hamiltonian that is analytically diagonalized; it establishes thermodynamic-limit energy density, excitation spectrum, Gaussian-state observables, and numerically evaluates bound tightness.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain rigorous, analytic lower bounds on the ground-state energy of the coherently driven Bose--Hubbard model—a non-integrable, interacting quantum many-body system—where exact solutions are intractable.

Method: Lieb--Yamazaki positive-operator decomposition to construct a quadratic bounding Hamiltonian; followed by analytical diagonalization via displacement and Bogoliubov transformations; derivation of Gaussian-state expectation values for observables; numerical benchmarking of bound tightness.

Result: Explicit stability conditions and finite-volume energy bounds in arbitrary dimension; thermodynamic-limit energy density; full characterization of the excitation spectrum; closed-form expressions for filling, number fluctuations, and g^(2) correlations; identification of antibunched and sub-Poissonian regimes.

Conclusion: The Lieb--Yamazaki decomposition provides a powerful, tractable framework for deriving rigorous, physically interpretable energy bounds and Gaussian observables for driven-dissipative or coherent many-body bosonic systems, with quantified accuracy across parameter space.

Abstract: We derive rigorous lower bounds on the ground-state energy of the coherently driven Bose--Hubbard model using a Lieb--Yamazaki positive-operator decomposition. The resulting quadratic bounding Hamiltonian is diagonalized by displacement and Bogoliubov transformations, yielding explicit stability conditions and finite-volume bounds in arbitrary dimension. We establish the thermodynamic-limit energy density and characterize the excitation spectrum of the bounding Hamiltonian. We also derive its Gaussian-state observables, including filling, number fluctuations, and second-order correlations, and identify regimes of antibunching and sub-Poissonian statistics. Numerical calculations assess the tightness of the bounds across representative parameter regimes.

</details>


### [195] [A Primer on Instanton Physics - A Short Course](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38717)
*Pervez Hoodbhoy*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: A six-lecture pedagogical introduction to instantons in quantum mechanics and quantum field theory, targeting advanced undergraduates and early graduate students; first four lectures focus on quantum mechanics (particle mechanics), last two extend to semi-classical field theory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide accessible, foundational instruction on instantons—a non-perturbative concept critical for understanding tunneling, vacuum structure, and anomalies—for students with minimal prior exposure.

Method: Lecture-based pedagogical exposition, progressing systematically from quantum mechanical instantons (e.g., double-well potential, WKB, path integrals) to their generalization in 1+1D and higher-dimensional quantum field theories, emphasizing semi-classical approximation and Euclidean path integrals.

Result: Students acquire conceptual and calculational fluency with instanton solutions, their actions, prefactors, and physical implications—including vacuum decay, level splitting, and topological aspects—within both quantum-mechanical and field-theoretic contexts.

Conclusion: Instantons serve as a unifying bridge between quantum mechanics and quantum field theory; mastering them equips students with essential tools for modern theoretical physics, particularly in non-perturbative phenomena and topological field theory.

Abstract: These six lectures, delivered at the University of New Brunswick 6-21 October 2026, are an introduction to instantons in quantum mechanics and quantum field theory for senior undergraduate and beginning graduate students. The first four lectures deal solely with particle mechanics, building the necessary background for the last two that cover applications in semi-classical field theory.

</details>


### [196] [From Entanglement Bootstrap to DHR Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38821)
*Kohtaro Kato,Yoshiko Ogata*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper bridges the entanglement bootstrap framework and the Doplicher–Haag–Roberts (DHR) theory for 2D quantum spin systems, proving Haag duality and establishing a rigorous one-to-one correspondence between entanglement-bootstrap anyons and irreducible superselection sectors; it further constructs affine bijections linking entanglement-based fusion-state density matrices to operator-algebraic intertwiners, bypassing exactly solvable models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify entropic (entanglement-bootstrap) and operator-algebraic (DHR) characterizations of anyons in two-dimensional quantum spin systems without relying on exactly solvable lattice models.

Method: Mathematical analysis within the framework of algebraic quantum field theory and quantum many-body theory: assuming entanglement-bootstrap axioms, the authors prove Haag duality, construct superselection sectors from anyon types, and build affine bijections between density matrices on fusion spaces and normalized positive intertwiners in operator-algebraic fusion channels.

Result: 1. Haag duality is proven under entanglement-bootstrap assumptions; 2. A bijective correspondence between simple entanglement-bootstrap anyon types and irreducible DHR superselection sectors is established; 3. For each fusion channel, an affine bijection is constructed between density matrices on the entanglement-bootstrap fusion space and normalized positive intertwiners in the corresponding operator-algebraic fusion channel.

Conclusion: The entanglement bootstrap and DHR theory are equivalent descriptions of anyonic excitations in 2D quantum spin systems at the level of superselection structure and fusion, providing a model-independent, intrinsic connection between entanglement-based and operator-algebraic approaches.

Abstract: We establish a direct connection between the entanglement bootstrap and the Doplicher--Haag--Roberts theory of superselection sectors for two-dimensional quantum spin systems. Under the entanglement-bootstrap assumptions, we prove Haag duality and establish a one-to-one correspondence between simple entanglement-bootstrap anyon types and irreducible superselection sectors. For each fusion channel, we further construct an affine bijection from density matrices on the entanglement-bootstrap fusion space to normalized positive intertwiners in the corresponding operator-algebraic fusion channel. These results connect the entropic and operator-algebraic descriptions of anyons directly, without passing through exactly solvable lattice models.

</details>


### [197] [The geometry of Floquet states](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38845)
*Vladimir Y. Cherynak,John R. Klein*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the moduli space of nondegenerately Floquet-spectra periodic Hamiltonians, models it geometrically via configuration spaces, flag manifolds, and principal bundles, computes its rational cohomology ring, and proposes Floquet analogues of the adiabatic theorem for 1-parameter families.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the topological and geometric structure of the space of periodic Hamiltonians with nondegenerate Floquet spectra ('good' Hamiltonians), and to extend adiabatic quantum evolution principles to the Floquet (time-periodic) setting.

Method: Geometric topology and differential geometry: construction of a geometric model using configuration spaces, flag manifolds, and principal bundles; computation of rational cohomology via this model; formulation of asymptotic conjectures based on adiabatic limiting analysis.

Result: Complete determination of the rational cohomology ring of the moduli space of good periodic Hamiltonians; geometric characterization of the moduli space; two precise conjectures on asymptotic quantum evolution under slow adiabatic driving of Floquet systems.

Conclusion: The moduli space admits a rich geometric structure amenable to cohomological classification, and adiabatic Floquet dynamics exhibits novel universal asymptotics captured by the proposed conjectures.

Abstract: This paper studies the moduli space of "good" periodic Hamiltonians, i.e., periodic Hamiltonians with nondegenerate Floquet spectrum. The corresponding Floquet states determine a geometric model for the moduli space of such Hamiltonians. The model is expressed in terms of configuration spaces, flag manifolds, and principal bundles. A corollary of the main result is the complete determination of the rational cohomology ring of the space of good periodic Hamiltonians. The paper also considers the adiabatic driving of a 1-parameter family of good periodic Hamiltonians. We formulate two conjectures describing the resulting asymptotic quantum evolution. These are Floquet analogues of the adiabatic theorem in quantum mechanics.

</details>


### [198] [A First-Order Phase Transition in Spin-Imbalanced BCS Theory of Superconductivity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38961)
*Marius Lemm,Barbara Roos*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves the existence of a first-order phase transition in spin-imbalanced translation-invariant BCS superconductivity models, characterized by a discontinuous jump in Cooper pair density and a kink in energy at a coupling strength strictly below the linear stability threshold.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously confirm the unusual first-order phase transition suggested by prior numerical (Caldas–Mota) and analytical (Freiji–Hainzl–Seiringer) studies in spin-imbalanced BCS systems where μ₊ ≠ μ₋.

Method: Rigorous analysis of the translation-invariant BCS functional with spin-dependent chemical potentials; key technique is a coercivity estimate via decomposition of the energy minimization—solving the 'inner' problem (minimization over one-particle density matrices at fixed pairing) exactly using Legendre–Fenchel transform.

Result: For sufficiently small spin imbalance and low temperature, there exists a coexistence coupling λₚₒ < λₗᵢₙ at which the global minimizer jumps from zero to nonzero Cooper pair density, and the minimized energy exhibits a non-differentiable kink—establishing a genuine first-order phase transition.

Conclusion: The normal state remains locally stable but loses global stability discontinuously below λₗᵢₙ, confirming that spin imbalance induces a true first-order superconducting phase transition in the BCS framework.

Abstract: We study a translation-invariant BCS functional of superconductivity for a system of spin-$1/2$ fermions with spin-dependent chemical potentials, $\mu_+\neq\mu_-$. Numerical work of Caldas and Mota and analytical work of Freiji, Hainzl, and Seiringer has suggested an unusual first-order phase transition in spin-imbalanced BCS models, in which the normal state becomes globally unstable while remaining locally stable. Here, we prove that a first-order phase transition occurs in such spin-imbalanced BCS functionals. For each sufficiently small fixed spin imbalance and sufficiently low temperature, there is a coexistence coupling $\lambda_{\mathrm{fo}}$ strictly below the linear-stability threshold $\lambda_{\mathrm{lin}}$ of the normal state at which the Cooper pair density exhibits a nonzero jump and the minimized energy has a kink. The methodological insight is a coercivity estimate obtained by decomposing the BCS energy minimization into two parts, where the ``inner'' problem minimizes over the one-particle density matrices at fixed pairing and can be exactly solved by a Legendre--Fenchel transform.

</details>


### [199] [Minimally-coupled Quasilinear Symmetric Hyperbolic Systems, Naturally](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39107)
*Eleanor March,James Owen Weatherall*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a rigorous framework for symmetric hyperbolicity and well-posedness of field equations formulated as natural equations on jet bundles over natural bundles, introduces 'naturalization' of non-natural equations, proves uniqueness (up to isomorphism) of minimally coupled natural equations, and derives strict geometric constraints—causal cones must align with metric lightcones and a hyperbolization-associated tensor must be divergence-free—for first-order quasilinear minimally coupled natural symmetric hyperbolic systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a geometrically precise, background-independent foundation for well-posedness of relativistic field theories by unifying naturality (covariance under diffeomorphisms), symmetric hyperbolicity (for local existence/uniqueness), and minimal coupling (for physical consistency with gravity).

Method: Uses category-theoretic and differential-geometric methods: natural bundles and jet bundles as the domain for PDEs; defines natural equations as closed subobjects; constructs naturalizations and minimal couplings via universal properties; defines symmetrizers, hyperbolizations, and constraints intrinsically; analyzes causal structure via cone geometry and tensor calculus relative to Levi-Civita connections.

Result: 1) Non-natural equations admit naturalizations; 2) Minimal coupling yields a unique (up to isomorphism) natural equation from a given equation on a fixed manifold; 3) Symmetric hyperbolicity and well-posedness extend to natural equations with arbitrary background metric; 4) For first-order quasilinear minimally coupled natural symmetric hyperbolic equations: causal cones = metric lightcones, and a rank-2 tensor derived from the natural hyperbolization is divergence-free w.r.t. the Levi-Civita derivative.

Conclusion: Naturality and minimal coupling are not merely heuristic principles but impose strong, testable geometric constraints on physically admissible hyperbolic field equations—specifically fixing causal structure and enforcing a conservation law for the hyperbolization tensor—thereby bridging foundational differential geometry with mathematical relativity and PDE theory.

Abstract: We consider symmetric hyperbolicity and well-posedness in the context of natural equations, which are closed subobjects of jet bundles over natural bundles. We show how non-natural equations can be "naturalized", and we discuss how a precise version of "minimal coupling" offers a (provably unique, up to isomorphism) method for constructing natural equations from certain equations defined on a single manifold. We discuss how to define symmetrizers, hyperbolizations, and constraints for minimally coupled natural equations, and we show how these can be used to recover standard existence and uniqueness results, relative to any choice of background structure (a metric, for minimally coupled systems). Additionally, we show that minimal coupling and naturality together imply that the causal cones associated with a (first order, quasilinear, minimally coupled) naturally symmetric hyperbolic natural equation must always coincide with the metric lightcones, and that a certain rank-2 tensor, associates with a natural hyperbolization, must always be divergence free relative to the associated Levi-Civita derivative operator.

</details>


### [200] [Higher genus polylogarithms associated with the D'Hoker-Hidding-Schlotterer connection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39382)
*Takashi Ichikawa*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs higher-genus polylogarithms via the DHS connection on Riemann surfaces of arbitrary genus, showing that under separating degenerations, the DHS connection glues BL (elliptic) and KZ (spherical) connections; resulting polylogarithms admit explicit power-series expansions in deformation parameters and logs, with coefficients in multiple zeta values (MZVs) and elliptic MZVs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the theory of polylogarithms and their associated connections from genus 0 (KZ) and genus 1 (BL) to arbitrary genus, leveraging modular and degeneration structures of moduli spaces of Riemann surfaces.

Method: Analyzing the behavior of the D'Hoker-Hidding-Schlotterer (DHS) connection under separating nodal degenerations; using gluing constructions to relate it to Brown-Levin (genus-1) and Knizhnik-Zamolodchikov (genus-0) connections; deriving asymptotic expansions via formal deformation theory and iterated integrals.

Result: The DHS connection is shown to be reconstructible by gluing BL and KZ connections across separating degenerations; higher-genus polylogarithms are expressed as convergent power series in deformation parameters and their logarithms, with coefficients lying in the algebra generated by multiple zeta values and elliptic multiple zeta values.

Conclusion: Higher-genus polylogarithms associated with the DHS connection are controlled by genus-0 and genus-1 data via degeneration/gluing, enabling explicit, computable descriptions in terms of classical and elliptic periods—establishing a bridge between higher-genus motivic Hodge theory and quantum field theoretic amplitudes.

Abstract: We study higher genus polylogarithms associated with the D'Hoker-Hidding-Schlotterer (DHS) connection defined on families of Riemann surfaces of any genus with marked points. Under separating degenerations of Riemann surfaces, we show that the DHS connection can be constructed by gluing the Brown-Levin (BL) connections on elliptic curves and the Knizhnik-Zamolodchikov (KZ) connections on pointed Riemann spheres. Therefore, the associated higher genus polylogarithms can be described explicitly as power series in deformation parameters and their logarithms for the families of Riemann surfaces whose coefficients are expressed by multiple zeta values and elliptic multiple zeta values.

</details>


### [201] [Generalized Frobenius Structures on Hurwitz Spaces, Their Open Extensions and Associated Principal Hierarchies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39411)
*Shilin Ma*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs generalized Frobenius structures on Hurwitz spaces of arbitrary genus using extended primary differentials, derives dispersionless Lax representations via Cauchy kernel contour integrals, solves open WDVV equations for both original and almost-dual structures, builds rank-one extended hierarchies, and shows compatibility with Shramchenko deformations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize Frobenius manifold structures to Hurwitz spaces of arbitrary genus and unify them with integrable hierarchies, open WDVV geometry, and deformation theory.

Method: Use of extended primary differentials on Hurwitz spaces; contour integration with the Cauchy kernel; construction of dispersionless Lax operators; derivation of principal and almost-dual hierarchies; solving open WDVV equations; rank-one extension formalism; analysis of Shramchenko-type deformations.

Result: Explicit dispersionless Lax representations for principal and almost-dual hierarchies on arbitrary-genus Hurwitz spaces; full solutions to open WDVV equations for both structures; concrete principal hierarchies for their rank-one extensions; proof that all constructions are compatible with Shramchenko deformations.

Conclusion: The framework unifies Frobenius geometry, dispersionless integrable systems, open WDVV theory, and deformation theory on Hurwitz spaces, extending prior results from genus zero to arbitrary genus and enabling new applications in topological field theory and enumerative geometry.

Abstract: We construct generalized Frobenius structures on Hurwitz spaces of arbitrary genus using extended primary differentials. Through contour integrals involving the Cauchy kernel, we derive explicit dispersionless Lax representations for the principal hierarchies of these structures and their almost-dual counterparts. We also construct solutions to the open WDVV equations for both the original and almost-dual structures, and derive explicit principal hierarchies for the resulting rank-one extensions. Finally, we show that these constructions admit Shramchenko deformations.

</details>


### [202] [Arithmetic localization for quasiperiodic long-range operators with monotone potentials](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39448)
*Hongyi Cao*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves uniform Anderson localization for 1D quasiperiodic operators with α-Hölder monotone potentials and exponentially decaying long-range hopping, under a smallness condition on the coupling and the resonance condition ρ > αβ.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend arithmetic localization methods beyond nearest-neighbor Schrödinger operators to long-range quasiperiodic models, bypassing Lyapunov exponent-based techniques.

Method: Adaptation of Jitomirskaya–Kachkovskiy’s arithmetic localization strategy—using Diophantine approximation, resonant phase elimination, and quantitative bounds on Green’s function—to long-range operators with Hölder monotone potentials and exponential decay in hopping.

Result: Uniform Anderson localization holds for all phases, provided ε is sufficiently small and the decay rate ρ of hopping exceeds αβ, where β measures frequency resonance strength.

Conclusion: Arithmetic methods can successfully replace Lyapunov exponent arguments in establishing localization for long-range quasiperiodic operators with Hölder monotonicity, broadening the scope of rigorous localization results.

Abstract: We study one-dimensional quasiperiodic operators with $\alpha$-Hölder monotone potentials and long-range hopping satisfying $|T(x,y)|\leq \varepsilon e^{-\rho |x-y|}$. Under an explicit smallness condition on $\varepsilon$, we establish uniform Anderson localization for such operators in the regime $\rho>\alpha\beta $, where $\beta$ is the exponential strength of frequency resonances. Our proof extends the arithmetic localization strategy for monotone quasiperiodic Schrödinger operators by Jitomirskaya and Kachkovskiy to the long-range setting, without relying on the Lyapunov exponent.

</details>


### [203] [A Combinatorial Formula and a Hilbert Scheme Realization of a Rank-One Virasoro Irregular Conformal Block](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39786)
*Hajime Nagoya*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper provides a combinatorial and geometric description of the rank-one Virasoro irregular conformal block of type (0,0,1), realizing it as a matrix element on an equivariant Hilbert–Fock space from Hilbert schemes of points on C²; at central charge c=1, it proves a conjecture linking coefficients to skew-tableau numbers and Littlewood–Richardson coefficients, using asymptotics of screening integrals and Laguerre–Jack evaluations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the structure and properties of irregular Virasoro conformal blocks—particularly their formal expansions near irregular singular points—via combinatorial, geometric, and representation-theoretic methods, with emphasis on integrality and positivity at special central charge.

Method: Combines sectorial asymptotics of screening integrals, Laguerre–Jack polynomial evaluations, realization on localized equivariant Hilbert–Fock space associated with Hilbert schemes of points on C², flow representation of the screening kernel via a degree-raising differential operator, and application of the one-box Pieri rule and Okounkov–Olshanski shifted Jack coefficient formula.

Result: A combinatorial formula and geometric realization of the rank-one Virasoro irregular conformal block of type (0,0,1); at c=1, parameter-independent coefficients are expressed explicitly in terms of skew-tableau numbers and Littlewood–Richardson coefficients—proving their nonnegativity and integrality.

Conclusion: The irregular conformal block admits a natural geometric interpretation and combinatorial structure; its coefficients at c=1 are fundamentally governed by classical symmetric function theory, confirming deep connections between irregular conformal field theory, Hilbert schemes, and representation theory of the symmetric group.

Abstract: For general central charge, we give a combinatorial formula and a geometric realization of the formal expansion of the rank-one Virasoro irregular conformal block of type $(0,0,1)$ at its irregular singular point. We realize the normalized block as a coefficientwise matrix element on the localized equivariant Hilbert--Fock space associated with Hilbert schemes of points on $\mathbb C^2$. At $c=1$, we prove the author's conjecture and express the parameter-independent coefficients in terms of skew-tableau numbers and Littlewood--Richardson coefficients, establishing their nonnegativity and integrality. The proof combines sectorial asymptotics of screening integrals with Laguerre--Jack evaluations. We express the screening kernel using the flow of a degree-raising differential operator and compute the Jack coefficients of its exponential from the one-box Pieri rule and the shifted Jack coefficient formula of Okounkov and Olshanski.

</details>


### [204] [An Egorov-type Theorem for Analytic Wick Observables in Quantum Field Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39806)
*Julien Malartre*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives an exact Egorov-type formula via a convergent semiclassical expansion for analytic Wick observables with super-exponential coefficient decay under time-dependent quadratic Hamiltonians, extending Breteaux's polynomial-symbol result to include $P(\varphi)_2$ and a variant of the Höegh-Krohn model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend Breteaux's Egorov-type result—previously valid only for polynomial symbols—to a broader class of analytic Wick observables with super-exponential decay, enabling treatment of physically relevant quantum field models such as spatially cutoff $P(\varphi)_2$ and a Höegh-Krohn variant.

Method: Constructs a convergent expansion in the semiclassical parameter $\varepsilon > 0$ for the Heisenberg evolution of analytic Wick observables under time-dependent quadratic Hamiltonians, leveraging analyticity and super-exponential decay of symbol coefficients to ensure convergence.

Result: An exact, convergent Egorov-type formula for the semiclassical propagation of analytic Wick observables; applicability confirmed for the spatially cutoff $P(\varphi)_2$ model and a variant of the Höegh-Krohn model.

Conclusion: The generalized Egorov theorem provides a rigorous semiclassical framework for non-polynomial, highly regular observables in quantum field theory settings governed by quadratic dynamics, bridging microlocal analysis and constructive QFT.

Abstract: We provide an exact Egorov-type formula, given by a convergent expansion in the semi-classical parameter $\varepsilon > 0$, for the propagation of a certain class of analytic Wick observables with super-exponentially decaying coefficients, under the action of a time-dependent quadratic Hamiltonian. This work generalizes a result established by S. Breteaux for polynomial symbols, and includes the spatially cutoff $P(\varphi)_2$ model, as well as a variant of the Höegh-Krohn model.

</details>


### [205] [Three-dimensional stress-gradient non-locality problem: Displacement field for an arbitrary and a conservative force](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39888)
*Slađan Jelić,Dušan Zorica*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analytically solves the statics problem for a 3D stress-gradient non-local elastic medium under arbitrary and conservative body forces on an unbounded domain, deriving six distinct analytical displacement field expressions—some shared across scalar- and tensor-type Eringen models—and visualizing them for gravitational loading.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain analytical displacement fields for 3D stress-gradient non-local elasticity under general and conservative body forces, bridging local and non-local contributions in a unified framework.

Method: Analytical solution of a linear system comprising equilibrium equations, infinitesimal strain–displacement relations, and both scalar- and tensor-type stress-gradient Eringen non-local constitutive models on an unbounded domain; solutions expressed as superpositions of local and model-specific non-local displacement fields.

Result: Six distinct closed-form displacement field expressions derived: one for a scalar-type model, three for individual tensor-type models, and two shared between one scalar- and one tensor-type model each; graphical representation confirms five distinct fields under gravitational (conservative) loading due to equivalence of two tensor models.

Conclusion: The superposition principle applies to linear stress-gradient non-local models, enabling systematic classification of displacement responses by non-local operator type; conservative body forces reduce model distinguishability, simplifying practical application.

Abstract: In order to obtain the displacement field, both generally in the case of an arbitrary body force and specifically in the case of a conservative body force, the mechanical problem of statics of three-dimensional stress-gradient non-local medium is analytically solved on unbounded domain by considering a system of equations consisting of an equilibrium equation, infinitesimal strain, and both scalar- and tensor-type stress-gradient Eringen three-dimensional models. Since the system of equations is linear, for each model the solution is obtained in the form of a superposition of the local-type displacement field, common to each model, and the non-local-type displacement field, specific to the non-locality operator of the model. Among the three scalar-type and five tensor-type considered models, six distinct analytical displacement field expressions are obtained for an arbitrary body force: one expression specific to a scalar-type model, three specific to individual tensor-type models, while each of the remaining two expressions are common to one scalar- and one tensor-type model. The displacement vector fields are graphically represented in the case of gravitational body force, yielding five different models, since two tensor-type models become the same when the force is conservative.

</details>


### [206] [Optimization Inverse Spectral Problems for Sturm--Liouville Operators with Potentials in $L^p(0,1)$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40231)
*Nur Valeev*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper addresses an optimization inverse spectral problem (OISP) for a 1D Sturm–Liouville operator: find the potential V in L^p(0,1) closest to a given V₀ that yields prescribed eigenvalues; it establishes existence, regularity, analytic representation of optimal potentials via squared eigenfunctions, uniqueness conditions, nondegeneracy and isolation results for critical points, and proposes numerical methods with stated convergence guarantees, plus applications to resonator design and nonlinear BVPs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To solve a constrained optimization variant of the classical inverse spectral problem—where instead of exact spectral matching, one seeks the potential closest (in L^p norm) to a priori given V₀ while achieving prescribed eigenvalues—motivated by applications in resonator design, diagnostics, and control of spectral properties.

Method: Uses functional-analytic and differential-algebraic techniques: analysis of isospectral manifolds in L^p, power-map representation of optimal potentials via linear combinations of squared eigenfunctions, implicit function theorem and KKT theory for constrained optimization, Newton-type methods with continuation in spectral data, and Baire category arguments for generic uniqueness.

Result: Proves existence and C¹-regularity of the isospectral manifold; derives explicit analytic formula for optimal V as a power map of squared eigenfunctions; shows analyticity of V when squared eigenfunctions are linearly dependent; for p=2, proves isolation of nondegenerate stationary points and finiteness of global minima; establishes uniqueness for λ₁* ≥ λ₁(V₀) (p ∈ (1,∞)) and generic global uniqueness (Baire sense); constructs explicit examples with multiple global minima for m=1,2 under symmetric barriers.

Conclusion: The OISP is well-posed in appropriate settings; optimal potentials admit constructive, eigenfunction-based representations; uniqueness holds under natural spectral ordering or generically; numerical methods are justified with quantified limitations; the framework bridges abstract spectral theory with applied design problems in acoustics and electromagnetics.

Abstract: We study an optimization inverse spectral problem (OISP) for a one-dimensional Sturm--Liouville operator on a finite interval. The problem is to find a potential $V \in L^p(0,1)$ that realizes a prescribed finite set $\lambda_1^* < \lambda_2^* < \dots < \lambda_m^*$ and has the smallest $L^p(0,1)$-distance from an a priori given potential $V_0 \in L^p(0,1)$. We formulate and prove results on the regularity of the isospectral manifold, the existence of an OISP solution, and an explicit analytic representation of the optimal potential by means of a power map applied to a linear combination of squared eigenfunctions. Using a unified differential-algebraic approach to squared eigenfunctions, we show that whenever these squares are linearly dependent, the corresponding potential can be expressed in terms of them and is an analytic function. For $p=2$, we prove that nondegenerate stationary points are isolated and that the number of global minima is finite provided that each of them is nondegenerate. For every $1<p<\infty$, we establish uniqueness for the first eigenvalue when $\lambda_1^*\ge\lambda_1(V_0)$ and generic global uniqueness in the sense of Baire category in $L^p(0,1)$. A separate section provides examples with at least two global minima for $m=1,2$ in the presence of a sufficiently high finite symmetric barrier. We describe an iteration with frozen eigenfunctions and a local Newton--KKT method with continuation in the spectral data, and state explicitly the limits of their guarantees. The final part discusses applications of OISP theory to nonlinear boundary value problems and to applied problems of diagnostics and optimal spectral design of acoustic and electromagnetic resonators.

</details>


### [207] [Three-dimensional QCA that are not stably circuits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40235)
*Bowen Yang*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves that certain 3D chiral Z_p Clifford quantum cellular automata (QCA) are not implementable by finite-depth quantum circuits—even with ancillas and translations—for all odd primes p; it introduces a new invariant of invertible subalgebras conjectured to be the square of the total quantum dimension modulo rational squares, implying the QCA group modulo stabilized circuits and translations is not finitely generated.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve whether chiral 3D Z_p Clifford QCAs admit finite-depth circuit implementations—a fundamental question for classification of quantum dynamics and topological order—and to understand the algebraic structure of the QCA group beyond the Clifford restriction.

Method: Develops and applies a novel invariant of invertible subalgebras (derived from operator-algebraic and categorical considerations), extends prior Clifford-only obstructions to the full unitary setting via stabilization and translation equivalence, and leverages representation-theoretic and modular tensor category arguments.

Result: For every odd prime p, the 3D chiral Z_p Clifford QCAs of Haah and of Sun–Yang–Wang–Tantivasadakarn–Chen are provably not finite-depth quantum circuits, even after arbitrary stabilization and translations; consequently, the quotient group of 3D QCA modulo stabilized circuits and translations is not finitely generated.

Conclusion: The non-circuit realizability of these chiral QCAs persists beyond the Clifford framework, revealing intrinsic long-range entanglement and establishing a structural limitation on the algebraic generation of the 3D QCA group; the new subalgebra invariant provides a candidate for quantifying chiral edge modes in higher dimensions.

Abstract: We prove that the three-dimensional chiral $\Z_p$ Clifford quantum cellular automata of Haah and of Sun--Yang--Wang--Tantivasadakarn--Chen are, for every odd prime $p$, not finite-depth quantum circuits, even after tensoring with arbitrary ancillary degrees of freedom and composing with translations. Previously such statements were known only inside the Clifford category. As a consequence, the group of three-dimensional QCA modulo stabilized circuits and translations is not finitely generated. The key ingredient is a new invariant of invertible subalgebras, which we conjecture to be the square of the total quantum dimension of the hosted anyon theory, modulo rational squares.

</details>


### [208] [Bulk gap stability and spectral flow for fermions with weak long-range interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40299)
*Stefan Teufel*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves that weak long-range many-body interactions preserve the spectral gap of a free fermionic system and constructs a quasi-local spectral flow that unitarily relates the free and interacting ground states, with explicit gap stability bounds and locality estimates for polynomially or stretched-exponentially decaying interactions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously establish spectral gap stability under weak long-range interactions in fermionic quantum lattice systems — a foundational question for topological phases, adiabatic continuity, and robustness of gapped ground states beyond short-range perturbations.

Method: Constructs a quasi-local automorphic spectral flow via Hastings' generator method; employs excitation-number comparison and polynomial propagation bounds (from a companion paper) to control norm growth and locality; uses Lieb–Robinson-type estimates adapted to fermionic Majorana operators and general interaction norms.

Result: For sufficiently small |λ|, the interacting Hamiltonian Φ_λ = Φ_h + λ𝒱 has pure ground states ω_λ related to the free ground state ω₀ by a quasi-local automorphism β_{0,λ}; the GNS Hamiltonian remains gapped with lower bound g − C_*|λ|‖𝒱‖_*, and β_{0,λ} inherits the spatial decay exponent (polynomial or stretched-exponential) of h and 𝒱.

Conclusion: Weak long-range even many-body interactions do not close the bulk gap of gapped free-fermion systems; the ground-state phase is adiabatically connected via a quasi-local automorphism, preserving locality structure and enabling rigorous stability analysis of topological invariants under realistic interaction models.

Abstract: We prove that weak long-range many-body interactions preserve the bulk gap of a free fermion system and that a quasi-local spectral flow transports the free ground state to ground states of the interacting system. Let $\Phi_h$ be a free fermionic interaction, possibly including pairing terms, whose infinite-volume Majorana coefficient operator $h$ has no spectrum in $(-g,g)$, and let $\mathcal V$ be an even self-adjoint many-body interaction. For sufficiently small $|\lambda|$, we construct pure ground states $\omega_{\lambda}$ of the interacting bulk system $\Phi_{\lambda}=\Phi_h+\lambda\mathcal V$ and quasi-local automorphisms $\beta_{0,\lambda}$ such that $\omega_{\lambda}=\omega_0\circ\beta_{0,\lambda}$, where $\omega_0$ is the free ground state of $\Phi_h$. Each $\omega_\lambda$ has a GNS Hamiltonian with gap at least $g-C_*|\lambda| \|\mathcal V\|_*$, where $\|\cdot\|_*$ is the relevant interaction norm. Our result allows polynomial decay of both the free coefficients and the many-body interaction. For two-site interactions on a uniformly discrete point set $\Gamma\subset\mathbb{R}^d$, a sufficient condition is decay as $(1+|x-y|)^{-\varpi}$ with $\varpi>2d$, for both the hopping and pairing coefficients and the interaction terms. The spectral-flow generator preserves the spatial exponent of the polynomial interaction norm, and its automorphisms satisfy locality bounds at that exponent. If $h$ and $\mathcal V$ have stretched-exponential decay, so does the spectral-flow generator. The proof constructs the spectral flow and establishes the gap through an excitation-number comparison, using polynomial propagation bounds for evolved interactions proved in a companion paper.

</details>


### [209] [Symmetries and exact solutions of stochastic differential equations: the CIR model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40316)
*Miguel A. Rodríguez*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper explains the critical parameter relation in the Cox-Ingersoll-Ross (CIR) interest rate model via Lie symmetries of its stochastic differential equation (SDE), and recovers prior results linking SDE symmetries to those of the forward Kolmogorov equation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the well-known critical parameter relation in the CIR model—often required for boundary behavior and positivity—by leveraging symmetry analysis of its underlying SDE, rather than relying solely on probabilistic or analytic methods.

Method: Lie symmetry analysis of the CIR stochastic differential equation; derivation of symmetry generators; use of symmetries to reduce and solve the SDE; comparison with symmetries of the associated forward Kolmogorov (Fokker–Planck) equation.

Result: The critical parameter condition arises naturally as a necessary requirement for the existence of a nontrivial Lie symmetry group of the CIR SDE; the symmetry-based integration recovers the known closed-form solution; consistency with recent work on SDE–Kolmogorov symmetry correspondence is established.

Conclusion: The critical parameter relation in the CIR model is fundamentally tied to the Lie symmetry structure of the SDE; symmetry methods provide both conceptual insight and an alternative constructive route to the solution, reinforcing the deep connection between SDE symmetries and their associated PDEs.

Abstract: It is well known that in the celebrated Cox-Ingersoll-Ross process for interest rates there exists a critical relation among the model parameters. In the present work, we explain this critical relation by studying the symmetries of the stochastic differential equation and compute the solution of the model using this symmetry. In this way we also recover results of a recent paper where this property has been studied within a broader context by analyzing the relationships among the symmetries of stochastic differential equations and their corresponding forward Kolmogorov equations.

</details>


### [210] [Local determination of low-energy states in gapped fermion systems with weak long-range interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40337)
*Stefan Teufel*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves that low-energy states of weakly interacting gapped fermionic systems are locally determined by the interaction within a region U, with exponentially decaying errors away from U's boundary; this implies uniqueness of the ground state, locality of response to perturbations, boundary-localization of finite-sample excitations, and decay of long-range correlations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a rigorous, quantitative foundation for locality and stability in interacting gapped fermionic systems—particularly how local interactions determine local properties of low-energy states, independent of distant details.

Method: Combines quasi-adiabatic continuation (from free to interacting ground state) with a newly introduced condition—Local Excitation Detectability (LED)—to prove local determination of low-energy states; relies on gap stability (established in companion work) and nonperturbative analysis valid for sufficiently weak even many-body interactions.

Result: Quantitative local determination: for any region U, low-energy states (<3g/4) of Hamiltonians matching the bulk interaction inside U have local expectations converging to those of the bulk ground state inside U, with error decaying with distance to U^c, uniformly over external terms and states; yields four key consequences: (i) unique algebraic ground state, (ii) LPPL principle, (iii) dynamical boundary localization of excitations, (iv) quantitative correlation decay.

Conclusion: Local determination of low-energy states is a robust, nonperturbative property of weakly interacting gapped fermion systems, enabled by quasi-adiabatic transport and LED; it unifies and generalizes locality principles across equilibrium and dynamical settings, with implications for topological order, quantum memory, and adiabatic quantum computation.

Abstract: We consider bulk-gapped fermion systems whose infinite-volume bulk is obtained by adding sufficiently weak even many-body interactions to a free Hamiltonian with spectral gap $g>0$, allowing polynomial spatial decay of both parts. We prove quantitative local determination of low-energy states: whenever another interaction agrees with the bulk interaction inside a finite or infinite region $U$, with bulk terms crossing its boundary unchanged or removed, all its states with excitation energies in $[0,3g/4]$ have local expectations close to those of the bulk ground state deep inside $U$. The error decays with the distance to the complement of $U$, independently of the interaction terms supported outside $U$ and of the chosen low-energy state. Quantitative local determination has strong consequences. (i) The bulk interaction has a unique algebraic ground state. (ii) A strong "local perturbations perturb locally" (LPPL) principle holds: arbitrary distant defects change local ground-state expectations only by a spatially decaying error. (iii) Low-energy excitations in finite samples remain localized near the boundary for all times, up to spatially decaying tails, uniformly over arbitrary even boundary terms. (iv) Correlations from deep inside $U$ to outside $U$ are small, with quantitative bounds in terms of spatial separation and distance to $U^c$. To prove local determination of low-energy states, we combine quasi-adiabatic transport of the free ground state to the interacting bulk ground state with a new condition, local excitation detectability (LED). A companion paper establishes gap stability and constructs the transport for weak interactions, while we prove LED at sufficiently weak coupling. The abstract theorem is nonperturbative and applies whenever the transport and LED hypotheses hold.

</details>


### [211] [Entropic Fluctuations in Quantum Statistical Mechanics: A Review](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40463)
*Tristan Benoist,Laurent Bruneau,Vojkan Jakšić,Aannalisa Panati,Claude-Alain Pillet*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper reviews and compares quantum extensions of the classical Evans-Searles and Gallavotti-Cohen fluctuation theorems (FTs), focusing on open quantum systems interacting with infinite reservoirs. It introduces a novel algebraic two-time measurement extension of the steady-state FT, discusses four entropy production approaches (naive, two-time measurement, phase space contraction, ancilla tomography), links them to modular theory, extends the classical principle of regular entropic fluctuations to quantum settings, and connects entropy statistics to spectral resonances of quantum transfer operators—illustrated via the spin-fermion model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically extend the foundational classical fluctuation theorems—governing entropy production statistics in transient and steady states—to the quantum domain, especially for rigorously defined open quantum systems where classical notions lack direct analogues.

Method: Uses algebraic quantum statistical mechanics (C*- and von Neumann algebras), modular theory (to define time-reversal and entropy production), spectral analysis of quantum dynamical semigroups and transfer operators, and comparative analysis of four quantum entropy production protocols; includes rigorous two-time measurement schemes in the thermodynamic limit and resonance-based large-deviation analysis.

Result: A unified framework for quantum fluctuation theorems is established: (i) a previously missing algebraic two-time measurement Gallavotti-Cohen FT for steady states is proven; (ii) equivalence and hierarchy among quantum entropy production definitions are clarified; (iii) modular theory is shown to provide a natural quantum generalization of time-reversal and entropy balance; (iv) regular entropic fluctuations are extended to quantum systems; (v) spectral resonances of quantum transfer operators fully characterize large-deviation statistics of entropy production, verified in the spin-fermion model.

Conclusion: Quantum fluctuation theorems are not mere analogues but require intrinsically quantum structures—especially modular theory and spectral resonance analysis—to capture non-commutative, measurement-induced, and thermodynamic-limit effects; this work provides a rigorous, physically interpretable, and computationally tractable foundation for entropic fluctuation theory in quantum non-equilibrium statistical mechanics.

Abstract: The celebrated Evans--Searles, respectively Gallavotti--Cohen, fluctuation theorem concerns certain universal statistical features of the entropy production rate of a classical system in a transient, respectively steady, state. In this paper, we review and compare several possible extensions of these fluctuation theorems to quantum systems. We present, in an introductory way, the approach proposed in a recent series of works [Lett. Math. Phys. 114, 32 (2024): doi:https://doi.org/10.1007/s11005-024-01777-0], [Rev. Math. Phys 38, 2461006 (2026): doi:https://doi.org/10.1142/S0129055X24610063], [Commun. Math. Phys. 406, 201 (2025): https://doi.org/10.1007/s00220-025-05360-z] and [J. Math. Phys. 67, 022101 (2026): doi:https://doi.org/10.1063/5.0311182] including a previously missing extension of the Gallavotti--Cohen (steady state) version of the FT to two-time measurement entropy production in the algebraic setting of quantum dynamical systems. We focus on the open quantum systems, where a small system interacts with several (infinitely extended) reservoirs, and discuss the following approaches: naive, two-time measurement, phase space contraction and ancilla state tomography. We review the connection with modular theory. The latter provides a way to extend the classical notions to the quantum domain. We discuss the quantum version of the principle of regular entropic fluctuations, introduced in the classical context in [Nonlinearity 24, 699 (2011): doi:https://doi.org/10.1088/0951-7715/24/3/003]. Finally, we relate the statistical properties of these various notions of entropy production to spectral resonances of quantum transfer operators and apply this approach to a paradigmatic model of non-equilibrium statistical mechanics: the spin-fermion model. The results presented here shed a new light on the nature of entropic fluctuations in quantum statistical mechanics.

</details>
